## 3. Data model

### 3.1 Identities

MASTER_PLAN §2.11 asks to keep graph, current and reduction-family identity distinct.

| Identity | Object | Key | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tree current | Block | Full `CurrentIndex` (`src/pyamplicol/generation/dag_types.py:121-176` [V]), including the full `ColorState` (accuracy, `sector_id`, `line_groups`, `basis_key`; `dag_types.py:66-90` [V]). Rust: `CurrentCoreKey` (`rust/crates/rusticol-core/src/recurrence/layout.rs:497-510` [V-s]). CT order added in wave 2 | Tree engine |
| Loop prefix (generation) | Family-recursion state | (cut species, ordered position leg sets, propagator species and masses, open species, orders): Recola's (ho, hm) [V-s recola2 `skeleton_rcl.f90:876-905`] | `loops/v2/families.py` |
| **Family** (reduction identity) | Denominator set | Canonical cyclic sequence of (position leg mask, mass id), minimised over the 2N rotations and reflections | `loops/v2/families.py` |
| Skeleton | Colour-ordered ring of blocks | (family, cut species, content per position, gap species) | Implicit (trie leaves / M closures) |
| Open-loop state | B node / F prefix / M state | §3.5 | Executor |
| Seed class | Colour vector | Exact colour-flow vector (generic) or primitive id (certified) | `loops/v2/colour.py` |
| Channel | NinjaRS lane | (family, point, sector, observable) | Reduction adapter |
| Graph | Feynman diagram | FeynKit | **Oracle only** |

MASTER_PLAN §2.11 also says "use FeynKit for canonicalization, routing, signs and symmetry factors". The 2026-10-20 user clarification (`MASTER_PLAN.md:1496-1501` [V]) supersedes this for production: families, insertions, colour and signs come from the recursion. FeynKit `IntegralFamily` and graph overall factors stay the small-process oracle (§14).

### 3.2 Blocks: the tree engine in loop-request mode

Blocks are the tree engine's colour-ordered off-shell currents. A loop request changes the following:

1. **Every needed subset; loop-aware reachability.** The Python builder prunes in three layers, each derived from the Born's closure splits (`generation/dag_compiler.py:131-133` flags, `:341-378` use [V]):
   - closure-side mask pruning;
   - colour-order mask pruning;
   - species reachability (the "useful states" pass).
   
   All three would drop currents a loop vertex needs. Loop mode replaces them with one loop-aware reachability test. A current (leg set B, field, colour state) is kept iff either:
   - some catalogue loop vertex accepts its field and (B, field) occurs as the content of an admissible position of some family-recursion state (leg-set, order and colour-class caps applied); or
   - it is needed by the Born or CT = 1 roots.
   
   The test is evaluated from the family recursion's position table, not from a list of skeletons.
2. **Kernels: transverse where exact, full where needed.**
   - The transverse massless Yang–Mills replacement is chosen per vertex term at model compile time (`models/compiler_entry.py:726-733` [V]), applied at `models/compiler_kernels.py:326-335` and defined at `:398-425` [V].
   - It drops only terms proportional to the input currents' self-contractions p_i·J_i. Berends–Giele currents built from transverse on-shell sources are conserved, so the replacement is exact for them. Plain blocks and the Born therefore keep it.
   - Full kernels are required wherever an input current is not conserved: every loop segment (the open-loop object is not transverse), CT = 1 currents (wave 2), and blocks fed by Ward (ε → p) or non-transverse spin sources.
   - A coloured propagator that breaks the Ward identity of the gluon currents it feeds needs them too: a width (fixed-width scheme, S(q)(q̸ − m) ≠ 1) or a model-supplied propagator. The choice is per request, from the tree currents: when a block or Born current propagates such a particle (`loops/v2/blocks.py` `ward_breaking_fields`; a width counts unless it is a compile-time zero or the request fixes it to zero), the request's blocks and Born take the full kernels on the same DAGs (`with_ward_kernels`; the two variants share their kernel tables, only the eligible Yang–Mills expressions differ). A model-level predicate would not do: loop_sm's WT is a runtime input, so every loop_sm request would lose the transverse kernels. Requests without such a current (every massless QCD request) are unchanged, bitwise.
   - WP1.1 adds the full-kernel catalogue flag. The loop-mode builder selects the kernel variant per current class.
   - The replacement exists only for massless adjoint currents, so electroweak blocks use full kernels anyway.
3. **Block identity and exports.** Blocks are keyed by the full `CurrentIndex`: leg mask, field, coupling orders and the full `ColorState`. For multi-quark FC, colour word and field alone do not identify a block. Exports:
   - J[block id][h_B]: block currents per helicity row;
   - A0[b][h]: the Born per colour-basis element and helicity, which already exist internally in `recurrence/direct_plan.rs:256-263` and `direct_runtime.rs:1167` [V-s];
   - Born roots bucketed by exact Born order (needed for mixed orders).
   - A loop vertex attaches the sum of every block of one field current, colour name and chirality: the tree engine also splits a current by the flavour flow of its trees (u u~ > w+ w- g: the u current of the legs {u, W+, W-} is a u → u line through γ*/Z* and a u → d → u line through two W's). `assemble._BlockRows` sums their rows in block order, the native tree program adds the further trees as `block_row_terms` (emitted only where present), and `SeedColour` refuses such blocks with different colour flows (mq-ewjoin: keeping only the last block gave q q~ > W⁺W⁻ g a double pole of 0.45 × Catani's and broke the gluon's Ward identity; m-qcd rows D28, D29, `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_vv_jet.py`).
4. **Graded colour pieces.** The U(1)-subtraction auxiliaries and the LC Fierz singlet are separate, graded currents instead of numeric 1/3 factors (`models/compiler_color_flow.py:417-419`, `generation/dag_color.py:539, 562` [V]). This is needed for LC/NLC; FC at N_c = 3 is unaffected. Identical-flavour lines reach one colour word with several grades; LC and NLC then also put the grade into the identity of those blocks' currents (§3.3, "LC and NLC of identical-flavour lines").
5. **Counterterms (wave 2).** A CT pseudo-order capped at 1, and a unary node for two-point insertions (§6.1). `InteractionNode` is binary today (`dag_types.py:313-325` [V]).
6. **Electroweak (wave 3).** Massive vectors in Feynman gauge with explicit Goldstones. The tree model takes either gauge (`models.loading.with_model_gauge`, WP3.2 EWI-4a: unitary by default; 't Hooft-Feynman propagates −i g/(p² − M² + iMΓ) and keeps the Goldstone vertices); requests with electroweak loop species take it throughout since EWI-4b (`ew_policy.feynman_gauge_model`, §6.8). Ghosts appear only as loop lines, never as blocks.
7. **Genericity.** The hard-coded gluon gate `abs(pdg) == 21` (`generation/service.py:8467` [V]) is replaced by a model predicate.

### 3.3 Families

- **Positions.** A family is a cyclic sequence of N ≥ 1 positions. Position i has a leg set B_i; the leg sets are non-empty, pairwise disjoint, and their union is all legs. A quartic position carries two currents with B_i = B′ ∪ B″. Higher-arity loop vertices of a general model carry more currents.
- **Propagators.** Propagator i follows position i and has a mass id m_i: the exact model mass expression including its width. Two ids are equal only if the expressions are identical, so species of equal mass share families: gluon, ghost and massless quarks; at ξ = 1 a vector and its Goldstone.
- **Frame.** The cut sits before position 1 and the loop momentum q flows through propagator N.
  - The shifts are V_i = Σ_{j≤i} p(B_j), stored as integer leg masks, with D_i = (q+V_i)² − m_i² (−μ² in d dimensions). This is the contract convention (`loops/kinematics.py:72-101` [V-s contracts K4]).
  - Each schedule publishes its own frame; oracles map frames with exact affine transformations (`loops/v2/poly_affine.py`).
- **Canonical key.** The lexicographic minimum of ((mask, mass id)_i) over the 2N rotations and reflections. It equals Recola's tensor-integral identity and was validated as equal sets on 5 processes [M recola-ninjars §5.1].
- **Selection rule.** These are the semantics of `GraphSelectionConvention` and `_physical_rejection` (`loops/graphs.py:109-121, 312-335` [V]), applied structurally. A ring is never generated if it is one of:
  - an external wavefunction correction: N ≤ 2 positions with exactly two attachments, one of them a single external leg (LSZ; `graphs.py:321-327`);
  - a VEV tadpole: N = 1 with at most one attachment;
  - a colour zero: a ring with exactly one coloured attachment.
  
  Every other ring is generated. That includes two-position rings with three or more attachments, i.e. a single-leg position whose partner position is quartic. Internal self-energies and bridges are retained and live inside blocks, so nothing is restricted to 1PI (MASTER_PLAN §2.10). Massive N = 1 tadpoles with two or more attachments are retained.
- **Certified scaleless families.** A generated family is dropped only with a structural certificate:
  - the condition is that all its propagator mass ids are zero and every entry of its S-matrix is structurally zero after on-shell bindings;
  - examples: N = 1 massless tadpoles, and N = 2 massless bubbles whose single invariant is p_i² = 0 of an on-shell massless leg;
  - the certificate uses mass ids and the on-shell binding table, never numerics.
  
  For massless QCD this removes exactly the gluon bubbles {k², (k−p_i)²} with a quartic partner position. They are kept with their routings (`FamilySet.scaleless_families`) as a companion slice (`LoopSlice.scaleless`) that only the `uv` and `ir` pole selections dress: their integrals vanish, but their UV and IR poles do not separately (§12.4). The companion's seed table extends an empty table holding the slice's certificates (`LoopSlice._companion_seeds`, `SHARED_COMPANION_CERTIFICATES`; generation-scale-2, WP3.1 status item 5); that extended table records no native build stage, so the native LC/NLC prune of native-seeds' `prune_switch.patch` (generation-scale-2's next run) must route the companion's prune through the Python callback, as `test_loop_v2_native_seeds.py` does now, or record a build source on the extend path. FeynKit gives 52 gg→gg families in the control model: 4 mass classes × 12, plus 4 such bubbles, each from 3 quartic-vertex diagrams [V `revision/fam_gg_classes.py`; the control model has massive c, b, t]. The QCD counts of this document (Appendix B) are therefore unchanged. With massive loop lines and quartic vertices (electroweak, BSM) these families are non-zero and kept. Oracles O2 and O7 compare modulo certified-zero families.
- **Rank.** R_f is the max-plus sum over contents of Δ = vertex q-degree + propagator-numerator q-degree. It is ≤ N in renormalisable Feynman gauge. R_f > N + 2 fails explicitly; this is NinjaRS's domain (`ninjars:fast/engine.rs:620-632` [V]), not an arbitrary cap.
- **Leaf weight** of every skeleton, folded into its seed (judges X4, 24):

  w_s = (automorphism weight) × (fermion sign ε_s) × (product of the kernels' i-factors).

  - The automorphism weight is 1/|Stab(s)| under the ring's dihedral group restricted to maps that preserve contents and species. It is ½ for a two-position bubble with identical self-conjugate propagators, and 1 for oriented fermion or ghost loops and for N ≥ 3 rings of distinct blocks.
  - Closed ghost loops carry −1 inside ε_s, like closed fermion loops.
  - These rules are checked against FeynKit overall factors (`overall_factor_expression`) on the oracle set, and against Recola's separate factor for 1- and 2-point loops (`amplitude_rcl.f90:1471-1474` [V-s physics judge]).
- **Fermion sign ε_s.** Every skeleton carries the sign of its Wick contraction relative to the Born. It is computed with the tree engine's own conventions, so that blocks, Born and loop agree:
  1. Signs inside blocks are already in the block currents:
     - colour-singlet fermions: Koszul signs of binary merges, with source ranks = source slots (`generation/fermion_ordering.py:58-79` support sign, `:81-110` orientation sign, `:112-130` ranks, `:163` `merge_sign` [V]);
     - coloured open lines: the colour-flow construction, which owns their ordering (`fermion_ordering.py:1-16` docstring [V]).
  2. **Ring sign.** Cut the loop before position 1 and treat the skeleton as the (n+2)-point tree whose two extra legs are the cut pair. The field entering position 1 gets source rank n+1 and the field leaving position N gets rank n+2, both after all external legs. At every ring step, apply the tree engine's `merge_sign`: Koszul sign plus the orientation sign of the two-fermion kernel, merging the open-loop object's support (which includes leg n+1) with the block's support, exactly as for a tree merge.
  3. **Coloured lines.** For coloured fermion lines, multiply by the parity of the external-fermion pairing per (role, canonical id) class, as the colour plan does for identical fermions (`color/plan_replay.py:671-701` [V]), with the cut pair ranked n+1, n+2.
  4. **Sewing.** Closing the cut pair gives −1 if the cut line belongs to a closed fermion or ghost loop and +1 otherwise. No further permutation arises, because the cut pair is adjacent and ranked last.
  5. ε_s ∈ {±1} multiplies w_s. Both traversal orientations of a fermion line through the loop carry their own ε_s.
  
  `loops/v2/conventions.py` calls the tree engine's functions; there is no private re-implementation.

  **Coloured fermion lines: no cap; validated range (package fermion-lines, 2026-10-26).** The ring sign, the blocks' colour-connection signs and their identity pairings take any number of coloured fermion lines, of distinct or identical flavours: no rule, guard or table is keyed to a line count, a flavour or a process. Loop-level provider evidence (QCD corrections, `loop_sm-no_b_mass`, MadLoop 3.7.0 in quadruple precision with Recola2 and, where an installed library has the process, OpenLoops; three exact on-shell points each, all Laurent coefficients, colour-summed; `tests/fixtures/loop/required/`, `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_fermion_lines.py`):
  - two lines: uu~→dd~g, uu~→uu~g, uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g (earlier controls);
  - three distinct lines uu~→dd~ss~; two identical-flavour lines and a third uu~→uu~dd~; three identical-flavour lines uu~→uu~uu~; two identical-flavour lines and a lepton line uu~→uu~e⁺e⁻ (QED=2);
  - the double-double virtual of the production route (native DAG, counterterms from the recursion, native executor) equals MadLoop within 3.7·10⁻¹⁴ strictly relative per coefficient (Born ≤ 3.4·10⁻¹⁵) on these four, and its poles equal Catani's operator (≤ 2·10⁻²⁹ of the poles); binary64 is within 1.2·10⁻¹¹ of MadLoop with Python point inputs (the executor of the test) and 1.7·10⁻¹¹ with native point inputs (prepared artifacts with a native tree program, `point_inputs = 'native-v2'`), both the finite part of uu~→uu~uu~ at point 2 and inside its sector estimate (3.1·10⁻¹¹ and 3.2·10⁻¹¹). The per-coefficient estimate (one exact rotation plus the reduction-direction check) is below the error only for the double pole of uu~→uu~e⁺e⁻: at point 0 by 4.7× (5.3× with native inputs; error 5.6·10⁻¹³, estimate 1.2·10⁻¹³) and at point 2 by 22× (error 1.7·10⁻¹², estimate 7.6·10⁻¹⁴; 5.6·10⁻¹³ of the sector's largest coefficient), which breaks the binding per-coefficient 10× rule (an open item of the stability owner). The point-2 error is the same at the rotated point, whose invariants and hence master values are the same, and the reduction-direction check reuses the cached masters, so neither check sees rounding of the masters amplified by cancellation between families; the rescaled reduction check (masters recomputed) estimates 3.4·10⁻¹² and covers it;
  - LC and NLC of three distinct lines (uu~→dd~ss~) are the formal-N_c projections of the exact colour cells, and all N_c powers sum to full colour (nightly).
  - **LC and NLC of identical-flavour lines: exact (package identical-flavour-lc, 2026-10-27).** From uu~→uu~g up (uu~→uu~dd~, uu~→uu~uu~, uu~→uu~e⁺e⁻, gg→uu~uu~; not uu~→uu~) one colour word is reached by trees of different U(1)-subtraction grades: in uu~→uu~g the quark current of word (3, 1, 2) is derived as quark 3 + gluon (1, 2), grade 0, and as the U(1)-subtraction auxiliary (3, 1) + quark 2, grade −1 (the Fermi-exchanged pairing with the same colour flow), for the same or for different helicities of the lines' legs. A block whose trees mix grades is not exact in N_c. Before this package its grade was `None`, which every LC and NLC route projected as grade 0: generation, export, the native runtime and the SDK served those values, and only the Python per-point path refused (genericity audit 2, G2-M1).
    - Design: a post-pass on the loop-request DAG in both lanes (`generation/dag_grades.split_u1_grades`, native `loop_v2::dag::grades`, grade sets as bit sets), after the prune and before the colour-connection fold (which stays before the evaluation reuse; the colour-flow signs stay after it). The grade enters the identity (`ColorState.nc_grade`, native `ColourState::nc_grade`) of exactly the currents of a block whose trees have several grades: a current whose own derivations mix grades becomes one current per grade, each fed by the derivations of that grade (its consumers follow), and a current of one grade in such a block only takes its grade as a label. Every other current, interaction and id order is unchanged. A DAG without U(1)-subtraction auxiliaries is not scanned (one linear pass over the interactions otherwise), and a DAG whose blocks have one grade each is returned as it is (every distinct-flavour request checked, e.g. uu~→dd~g). The pass reads grades only, never a particle, flavour, line count or process, so any other block whose trees mix grades (`blocks.py` names a photon and a U(1) piece giving the same flow) is split the same way. Over graded blocks a colour state names a block by its word and grade (`colour.block_name`, `PositionColour.block_names` and `grades`; `SeedColour`, the block rows of the reference executor, and the native tree program through the DAG's colour states); over full-colour blocks it names it by its word alone, the base's code path. Every block of an LC or NLC request then has one grade, and the seeds are exact in N_c (`SeedTable.formal_nc`). The copies of a split current sum to its value, so the pass is exact at N_c = 3. Only LC and NLC requests run it (`u1_grades = accuracy != "full"` in `LoopSlice.generate`: a `GenericDAGCompiler` option and a native DAG input field written only when set, for the loop blocks and the CT program; the certified-scaleless companion and the observable columns use the slice's blocks). Full colour keeps every power at N_c = 3 and needs no grades: its DAG, digests and values are unchanged, bitwise, and so are its blocks but for the order-free grade labels below. With the split, the derivations of both colour-connection parities in uu~→uu~uu~'s CT program (previous item) fall into blocks of different grades, and nothing folds. Block grades (`BlockColourFlow.nc_grade`) come from one rule per lane, order-free: `blocks.u1_grades` is the single-grade view of `dag_grades.u1_grade_sets` (the grade sets the split reads), natively `blocks::fold_u1_grades`; a current has a grade only if every derivation gives it the same one from inputs of one grade each, whatever their order. Before package identical-flavour-lc's second fix round both lanes kept the grade of an earlier derivation when a later one read a current of mixed grade (32 currents of gg→uu~uu~'s full-colour CT program kept −1 while their grade sets are {0, −1}; `test_native_counterterm_program_grades_are_order_free`, Rust `a_derivation_from_a_mixed_input_leaves_no_grade_in_either_order`). With the split no current mixes, and values at N_c = 3 do not depend on a block's grade, so this changed only full colour's block labels and the guard of DAGs compiled without the split, which such a label could hide.
    - What stays refused: the generation-time guard of package fail-closed (second genericity audit G2-M1; merged with slice `fb54246c`) refuses a colour accuracy other than full whenever the slice's currents are not N_c-exact, right after the trees and before their kernels, families or any export, with the typed `LoopRequestUnsupported` of the listed rule `colour-accuracy-nc-exact` (`loops/v2/refusals.py`; loop blocks and the CT program's; the dry run lists it), and every export refuses such a slice again (`export_plan`, the Python and native seed records, the native trees). Its criterion is N_c exactness itself, so with the split no LC or NLC request of the processes below reaches it, while a DAG compiled without the pass still does (forced off, uu~→uu~g at LC and NLC is refused). Below it, a seed table over blocks that mix grades keeps the typed refusal of `SeedTable.seeds` ("<LC|NLC> seeds need N_c-exact tree currents; these blocks mix U(1)-subtraction grades …"), and `SeedColour` refuses a graded table holding a block of mixed grades (a `ValueError` naming its word) instead of building an unorderable colour state. The strict xfails pinned to the guard's refusal of identical-flavour LC and NLC pass with the split and are removed: `test_loop_v2_merged_controls.py::test_m_equals_b_on_every_control[uux_uuxg-lc]`, `[uux_uuxg-nlc]` and fail-closed's version of `test_colour_projections_are_formal_nc_projections[*-uux_uuxuux]` (this package rewrote that test).
    - Validation (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_fermion_lines.py`, `test_loop_v2_native_dag.py`, `test_loop_v2_slice.py`, `test_loop_v2_artifact.py`):
      - Both lanes give the same DAG, digests and blocks with the split: uu~→uu~g loop blocks; uu~→uu~uu~ loop blocks (grades 0, −1 and −2, nightly) and CT program; uu~→dd~g unchanged.
      - Parts check: for uu~→uu~g, uu~→uu~dd~, uu~→uu~uu~, uu~→uu~e⁺e⁻, gg→uu~uu~ and uu~→dd~ss~ the parts of the exact colour cells at every N_c power sum to full colour, and the LC (NLC) slice equals the sum of its kept powers, for the Born, every component and every Laurent coefficient at the first point. Double-double holds within 10⁻²⁴ of each sector's largest coefficient (native executor). Binary64 holds strictly within 10⁻¹² for every process but gg→uu~uu~ (uu~→dd~ss~ as at the base, where E = 1.28·10⁻¹¹ but the deviations are at most 1.6·10⁻¹⁴). gg→uu~uu~ is held to its own estimate E (the O1 rule, `PROJECTION_O1`): its first three exact points are outside the O1 scope (E above the 10⁻⁹ cap), so binary64 takes the fourth (E = 1.87·10⁻¹¹), where the sum over the powers is within 1.33·10⁻¹² of full colour and the NLC within 1.93·10⁻¹² of its kept powers; double-double at the same point gives 9.1·10⁻²⁹ and 1.2·10⁻²⁸, so the excess over 10⁻¹² is binary64 conditioning. The default tier runs the double-double check of uu~→uu~g; every other id is nightly. The first point of uu~→uu~uu~ is outside binary64's 10⁻¹² (E = 3.9·10⁻¹¹, true binary64 error of full colour 1.95·10⁻¹² against double-double; its parts are up to six times its full-colour coefficients and cancel; there the sum over the powers is within 3.0·10⁻¹² of full colour and the NLC within 3.7·10⁻¹² of its kept powers). Binary64 therefore takes the fixture's second point (`PROJECTION_POINTS`; E = 4.0·10⁻¹², true error 2.7·10⁻¹³), where the sum over the powers is within 7.9·10⁻¹⁴ of full colour and the LC and NLC within 1.7·10⁻¹⁶ and 8.4·10⁻¹⁴ of their kept powers, strictly within 10⁻¹². This check sums over every power, so it would not see a wrong grade, which moves a block's trees between N_c powers. The next four checks are independent of the pass and would; the N_c scan of full colour uses no grade at all.
      - Legacy oracle, two identical-flavour lines: the legacy engine's formal-N_c projections (`SUNColorSpace(N_c)`, its own colour algebra; `tools/loop_reference/legacy_capture.py`, control `uux_uuxg` with LC and NLC) equal the slice's LC and NLC of uu~→uu~g at the fixture's three points. Leading degrees (3, 4), the Born and every component and Laurent coefficient agree within 10⁻¹² in binary64 (3.2·10⁻¹³ at the first point; 8.6·10⁻³⁰ in double-double) and within 10⁻⁴⁰ at 200 bits. Through the public artifact (NLC, native point inputs) they agree within 10⁻¹² (binary64) and 10⁻²⁴ (double-double).
      - N_c scan of the tree currents (`test_graded_blocks_scale_as_their_u1_pieces`): each U(1) piece carries the numeric −1/N_c of its colour weight, so a block's N_c dependence is that of its U(1) pieces. With the colour weight of every interaction producing the U(1)-subtraction auxiliary doubled, the rows of every block of grade g are 2^(−g) times its rows. This holds bitwise in binary64 (test tolerance 10⁻¹⁴ of the block's largest row) for the loop-request blocks and the CT program of uu~→uu~g (physics tier), dd~→dd~g, ug→uuu~, uu~→uu~uu~ (669 loop blocks with nonzero rows over grades 0, −1 and −2), uu~→uu~e⁺e⁻ and gg→uu~uu~ (nightly). The point has its last leg rescaled, so no block current is on shell. Without the split, each of these processes has blocks that scale by no power of 2. This checks every grade the projections use, independently of `dag_grades`; the parts check above checks the projections themselves.
      - Legacy oracle, three identical-flavour lines (`test_identical_flavour_projections_equal_legacy`, nightly): the legacy engine's LC of uu~→uu~uu~ at the first point of the provider fixture (projection-only record `tests/fixtures/loop/legacy/uux_uuxuux.lc.json`, `legacy_capture --projection`; its LC program took 3987 s and 8.4 GB and its 200-bit evaluation 543 s and 22.5 GB on one thread, against 12 s for uu~→uu~ and 81 s for uu~→uu~g) equals the slice's: leading degrees (3, 4), Born and every component and Laurent coefficient within 3.4·10⁻¹³ (binary64) and 2.4·10⁻²⁹ (double-double, native executor) of each component's largest coefficient. The base without the split serves this LC 62 % off (double-double, native executor). Its NLC has no legacy record: the legacy NLC program of three identical-flavour lines did not finish within a 2 h bound on one thread and was not relaunched (slice-status DECISIONS 2026-10-28 ~00:20 item 2). Its independent oracle is the N_c scan of full colour (next item).
      - N_c scan of full colour, the independent formal-N_c projection (`test_colour_projections_are_coefficients_of_an_nc_scan_of_full_colour`, nightly; `tests/unit/loop_nc_scan.py`): full colour has no grade split, so its value as a function of N_c needs neither the pass nor any grade. One process per N evaluates full colour (Python DAG lanes and reference path) with N_c = N wherever the engine substitutes N_c = 3 (every module constant `NC`: seed cells, the Born amplitude's grades, the counterterm and field cells and couplings of the colour declaration, literal overlaps, the Born metric) and with 1/N for the trees' U(1)-subtraction weight (the colour weight of every interaction producing the auxiliary times 3/N before the fold, the tree scan's doubling generalised: 280 interactions in uu~→uu~g, 2418 in uu~→uu~uu~). The external average stays the request's. Then FC(N) = Σ_p c_p N^p, and part p of the parts check is c_p 3^p. Eleven N, one per power of the parts (N_c^−6 to N_c^4), fix every coefficient: 3, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 1/2, 1/4, 1/8, 1/16 and 1/32 (1/N exact in binary64; more of them below 3, since the powers reach N_c^−6 but only N_c^4, so the exact fit loses about three digits instead of five). For uu~→uu~g and uu~→uu~uu~ at the binary64 and double-double points of the parts check: in binary64, FC(N) equals Σ_p part_p (N/3)^p at every N within 3.7·10⁻¹³ (uu~→uu~g) and 1.9·10⁻¹² (uu~→uu~uu~ at its second point, under its E = 4.0·10⁻¹², the O1 rule; worst at N = 1/32, where (N/3)^−4 ≈ 8.5·10⁷ weighs the low powers) of the sector's largest term. FC(N) itself is no scale: at N = 1 it cancels to rounding. At 32 digits, the coefficients solved exactly from FC(N) equal the parts within 7.8·10⁻²⁸ (uu~→uu~g) and 5.8·10⁻²⁷ (uu~→uu~uu~) of the sector's largest part, and their kept powers equal the LC slices within 8.2·10⁻³⁰ and 2.4·10⁻²⁸ and the NLC slices within 3.7·10⁻²⁸ and 2.1·10⁻²⁷ (32-digit parts and slices on the reference path; the test holds the native executor's double-double parts and slices to 10⁻²⁴). Negative control: with the grade −1 blocks read as grade 0 in the seed colour (`block_colour_network`; every value at N_c = 3 unchanged), the parts check of uu~→uu~g still passes and this check fails at N = 2, by 5 % of the largest term. So does uu~→uu~uu~ under the same mutation: the parts check passes and this check fails at N = 2, by 0.8 % of the largest term (its slice's blocks have grades 0 and −1 only; its grade −2 pieces are the Born's literal tensors and the counterterm program's). This is the independent NLC oracle of uu~→uu~uu~. Cost of one id (12 cores, the eleven N concurrently): uu~→uu~g 89 s (binary64) and 162 s (32 digits), uu~→uu~uu~ 208 s and 303 s.
      - Genericity (genericity audit 2, G2-M1 (iii); `test_loop_genericity_renamed_models.py::test_renamed_quarks_and_permuted_legs_give_the_same_projections`, physics tier): uu~→uu~g at LC and NLC on a loop_sm copy with the up quark renamed and renumbered (`uq`, PDG 9000002; harness variant `quarks`) splits the same number of blocks by grade and gives the original's decisions and values, and so does the leg order u~u→gu~u at the permuted point (the loop requests close at an antiquark; binary64, every component within 10⁻¹² of the largest coefficient).
      - Observables: the helicity- and Born-basis-resolved entries of uu~→uu~g at NLC sum to its inclusive result (binary64, native columns = Python; 200 bits; nightly `test_resolved_entries_sum_to_the_inclusive_result[uux_uuxg_nlc-*]`). Correlated calls take the same graded colour states; no identical-flavour correlator has its own oracle yet.
      - Full colour of the seven fermion-lines fixtures (uu~→uu~g, uu~→dd~g, uu~→uu~, uu~→uu~dd~, uu~→uu~uu~, uu~→uu~e⁺e⁻, uu~→dd~ss~; three points, binary64 and double-double, native executor) is bitwise that of `db1ed5cb` at the package head, also after its merges of slices `6da37352` and `fb54246c` (natives `loop-native-identical-flavour-lc-57417db9` and `-7dcebbf7`), and after the order-free grade rule (native `-7dc0ef04`): every value of the seven fixtures and of gg→uu~uu~ (three exact points; 1776 values, binary64 and double-double, each as a full-precision string) equals the `db1ed5cb` value when both run with the same `PYTHONHASHSEED` (0 and 1 checked). Unseeded, 22 double-double strings of uu~→uu~e⁺e⁻ differ in their last digits, about 10⁻⁵² relative, between two runs of the base itself (a property of the 52-digit decimal rendering, far below double-double's 10⁻³²). So are LC and NLC of uu~→dd~g and uu~→dd~ss~ (at `-7dc0ef04` through their byte pins below: identical plans). Package fail-closed's byte pins (`test_plans_the_guards_do_not_refuse_keep_their_bytes`: plan, companion and native members of uu~→uu~g and gg→uu~uu~ at full colour and of uu~→dd~g and uu~→dd~ss~ at LC and NLC, measured on `6da37352`) hold at the merged head.
      - Cost: full colour runs no pass and keeps the base's code path. FC of gg→ggggg and uu~→ggggg, base (`11542fd5`, native `loop-native-v2-slice-11542fd5`) against the package head (`loop-native-identical-flavour-lc-868f5b5d`), `tools.loop_benchmark` (native inputs), one thread each on pinned cores 230 and 236, concurrently, cores swapped between rounds, medians (27 Oct 19:42–20:42 UTC, host load about 160): gg→ggggg generation 180.3 → 180.9 s (×1.004, three rounds), export 229.1 → 228.1 s (×0.995), evaluation 7.55 → 7.45 s/pt (×0.987; batch 16, three repetitions); uu~→ggggg generation 49.8 → 49.3 s (×0.989, seven rounds), export 83.7 → 83.3 s (×0.995), evaluation 1.719 → 1.716 s/pt (×0.998). Confirmed after the merge of slice `6da37352` (base `6da37352`, native `loop-native-v2-slice-59d47cb3`, against `c3ac88ae`, native `loop-native-identical-flavour-lc-57417db9`; cores 236 and 238, 28 Oct 00:49–01:28 UTC, host load 150–245): the stored plans are bitwise equal (same plan SHA-256 for both processes); uu~→ggggg generation 48.3 → 48.5 s (×1.003, three rounds), export 86.3 → 86.3 s (×1.000), evaluation 1.849 → 1.790 s/pt (×0.968, three rounds, cores swapped); gg→ggggg generation 191.0 → 190.9 s (×1.000, one round), export 242.4 → 242.6 s (×1.001), evaluation 7.47 → 7.35 s/pt (×0.985, one round). An identical-flavour LC slice is cheaper per point than its full colour (uu~→uu~uu~: 0.69–0.73 against 2.05–2.08 s/pt, native executor with Python point inputs, one point repeated eight times) and is generated no slower (16.4–16.8 against 17.2–18.2 s). At LC the split costs what its extra blocks cost: uu~→uu~ggg (2→5, two identical-flavour lines) has 1374 instead of 934 loop blocks and generates in 42.7 against 41.4 s (×1.03; export 6.1 against 5.9 s, peak 1.00 against 0.96 GiB; the base's grade-0 projection, which is wrong; three rounds, one thread on cores 229 and 235 swapped), uu~→uu~gg in 12.4 against 12.9 s.
  
  Four coloured lines are validated at tree level against MadGraph (`tests/integration/test_tree_coupling_orders.py`: uu~→dd~ss~cc~, its relabelling u~u→cc~ss~dd~, uu~→dd~dd~ss~ and dd~→uu~ss~cc~); five W-paired lines (uu~→dd~ss~cc~bb~) only by labelling invariance, with no MadGraph reference and no test ("Five W-paired lines" below). At loop level four and more lines take the same rules, with no provider reference yet.

  **Mixed connection parities (identical flavours).** A current whose trees pair its coloured fermion endpoints in more than one way can have derivations of both colour-connection parities. In uu~→uu~uu~ the counterterm program's contact auxiliary of the four-gluon R2 that closes the gluons of the pairing (2, 4̄)(3, 6̄), and the one that takes the U(1)-subtraction auxiliary of the pairing (2, 6̄)(3, 4̄), have the same closed lines (2, 6)(3, 4). Their block's single connection sign cannot carry both, and generation refused the process ("derivations with different colour-connection signs", both lanes). It needs two identical-flavour pairs among the legs of a current without leg 1: uu~→uu~dd~ and uu~→dd~ss~ never meet it, gg→uu~uu~ does.
  - The fix: every block takes the parity of its first derivation (by result size, in DAG order), and the derivations of the other parity carry the −1 in their colour weights (`generation.fermion_ordering.apply_colour_connection_signs`; native `dag::signs::apply_connection_signs`). That is the blocks' per-tree connection sign, applied tree by tree. The fold runs before the evaluation reuse in both lanes: a parity depends on the colour state, which the reuse's evaluation contracts leave out, so the value equivalences are derived from the folded weights (the colour-flow signs stay after the reuse; signs of colour weights commute). The native CT program equals the Python one with these folds (`test_native_counterterm_program_folds_mixed_connection_parities`, which also asserts that uu~→uu~uu~ has folded derivations), and the counterterms of gg→uu~uu~ from the recursion, folds included, equal the site closure in the default tier (`test_slice_counterterms_from_recursion_equal_sites[gg_uuxuux-double]`).
  - Cost (native). The first version of the fold added two passes per interaction to the native loop-block stage (a sort, a merges vector and a hashed block key per interaction in `apply_connection_signs`, and again in `blocks.rs`): about 1.17× at g g > g g g g g, where nothing can fold (requirements audit, 2026-10-26). Now `apply_connection_signs` returns at once without coloured fermion legs or without a connecting merge (every parity is then 0), reads per-word endpoint and per-particle auxiliary flags, and groups blocks (one map entry per current) only when a merge connects; the parities are indexed by dense block id, the merges are visited in DAG order where that already is by result size (`signs::size_order`), and `blocks.rs` takes its block ids from the grouping it builds anyway and shares one size order with the U(1) grades. Measured against the slice (`005ab8f8`, native `loop-native-v2-slice-5ebcebea`) with the package head (`03e8f0dd`, native `loop-native-fermion-lines-559d2bf2`), `tools/loop_v2_dag.py --lane native --repeat 3`, `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1`, one pinned core per run, two rounds with the cores swapped (medians of six runs; `workspace/tmp/fermion-lines/perf/`): g g > g g g g g loop-block DAG + blocks 9.43 → 9.08 s; u u~ > g g g g g 0.81 → 0.74 s and CT program 3.72 → 3.56 s; u u~ > u u~ g g g (connecting merges, nothing to fold) 0.21 → 0.21 s and CT program 0.81 → 0.80 s. The S1′/S1″ digests and counts are unchanged (`9d274adf…`, `198f439b…`, `8eeca050…`, `2f53526e…`, `900f773d…`) and so are the process peaks (1.78 GB at g g > g g g g g).
  - Evidence and its limit: the provider check of uu~→uu~uu~ confirms the generation, not the folded sign. These closed-line contact currents contribute nothing at the requested orders: in uu~→uu~uu~ (504 folded derivations) the four-gluon R2 cannot enter a tree at QCD⁶, and in gg→uu~uu~ (168) zeroing every closed-line contact current leaves the counterterms unchanged. Folding, not folding or zeroing the folded derivations (uu~→uu~uu~ also tripling them) changes no counterterm, which equals the site oracle within 4·10⁻¹⁶ of the largest (binary64, Python DAG, one exact point; an independent audit measured the same for the native lane, folded, unfolded and tripled, within 3.7·10⁻¹⁵ per coefficient). The folded sign rests on the per-tree rule. Open: no process is known in which folded derivations contribute, and they are not pruned: the order limits do not make them dead in general (in gg→uu~uu~ the four-gluon R2 does enter at QCD⁶, yet zeroing its closed-line contact currents changes no counterterm, a numerical observation), so a prune needs a proof of which closed-line contact blocks the counterterm closure can read, not an order cut.

  Steps 1 and 3 describe the colour-flow convention of the loop-request **blocks**. The tree **Born** programs (`GenericDAGCompiler` in tree mode, and the Rust recurrence) instead sign coloured fermions by their Wick order at every merge, divide each root by the colour-metric parity of its sector times (−1)^(lines−1), and carry the U(1)-subtraction −1 where that current is created (`generation/fermion_ordering.py`). The colour-flow convention gives a QCD×EW interference between quark lines the wrong sign (−1 per colour-octet link); the Wick convention matches MadGraph split orders at full and leading colour. The Python Born also multiplies every root by a per-process constant `gluon_joined_phase` (±1, from one reference tree) that the Rust recurrence does not apply, so that its gluon-joined roots stay identical to the colour-flow convention; the two lanes' amplitudes can therefore differ by a global sign (−1 for two quark lines), and squared results, interferences and per-order partials agree. The loop engine takes its Born from the Python lane only: `test_loop_v2_slice.py::test_born_closure_is_the_tree_runtime_born` checks that the Born closed from colour-flow blocks equals that lane's amplitude roots, phase included (uu~→dd~g, uu~→uu~), and the slice's provider checks fix the sign of the Born–loop interference. A consumer that took its Born from the Rust recurrence instead would have to multiply it by `gluon_joined_phase` (or the recurrence would have to apply it) before interfering it with loop amplitudes.

  Tree-lane electroweak checks and defects (MadGraph 3.7 split orders, `tests/integration/test_tree_coupling_orders.py`; the open items are listed in WP2.9):

  - **Three-leg colour-singlet vertices on three lines.** A colour-singlet vertex whose three legs carry three different quark lines (WWγ/WWZ in uu~→dd~ss~) has the same colour factor in both cyclic orders of those lines, and the sector's line orders admit it once in each; the Python Born keeps the order whose smallest line labels increase cyclically (`ColorEngine._singlet_vertex_orientation_allowed` on the sector lines at full colour, `_shared_lc_singlet_vertex_orientation_allowed` on the coloured labels of the shared LC orderings).
  - **LC colour-identity closures.** At LC an electroweak boson emitted by a fermion line after a gluon exchange with other lines carries a colour-identity closure spanning several complete colour lines; `_lc_color_identity_closures_compatible` admits such a union of complete lines (before, only a closure inside one line, which dropped those roots: the uu~→dd~ss~ LC total was off by up to 5% of the summed partials and generation at QCD=2 QED=2 then failed to load). Public physics also lists the flows of sectors without roots at the requested orders (LC flows pairing d with s~ at pure EW order), which the eager runtime selects, so restricted orders load.
  - **Three-line evidence.** The Python Born then matches MadGraph in all five squared orders of uu~→dd~ss~, uu~→dd~cc~ and dd~→uu~ss~ at full colour, and at LC in their totals and in QED^8 of uu~→dd~ss~ (uu~→uu~uu~ at LC as well), including the W pairing against the neutral pairing, so the Wick signs hold for three distinct-flavour lines. The same holds for three lines whose incoming quarks a W pairs, ud→udss~, ud→udcc~ and ud~→ud~ss~, at full colour and LC in their totals (ud→udss~ also at QCD=0 and QCD≤2). MadGraph 3.7 leaves the colour-flow amplitudes no diagram feeds unassigned in these exports (the tool zeroes them), so the references are cross-checked by a second method: a separately generated QCD=0 export, whose amplitudes are all assigned, reproduces QED^8 of the all-order export, and the recurrence lane, which shares nothing with the Python colour engine, reproduces QCD^8 and QCD^6QED^2 (`tests/integration/test_tree_coupling_orders.py`).
  - **Recurrence alias collapse (fixed).** The Rust recurrence counted every vertex whose two inputs exchange with a proven factor (WWγ, WWZ, HWW, Htt) twice: both mirrored orientations, e.g. (W⁺, W⁻)→γ and (W⁻, W⁺)→γ, kept the input order (0, 1) of their kernel-class representative, so the template's alias collapse (`_canonical_transition_alias_key` in `models/recurrence_catalog_builder.py`) kept both rows, and the recurrence applies every row of a state pair to each unordered parent pair (e⁺e⁻→W⁺W⁻ was 13× the eager lane; uu~→dd~e⁺e⁻ up to 36× MadGraph in QED^8; for uu~→dd~ss~ this looked like one count per cyclic line order). The alias key now states such a transition in the input order that lists its state IDs increasingly and carries the exchange factor when that reverses the certified order. The recurrence lane then matches MadGraph in every squared order at full colour and LC for uu~→dd~ss~, ud→udss~, ud~→ud~ss~, uu~→dd~e⁺e⁻, ud→ude⁺e⁻, e⁺e⁻→uu~dd~ and uu~→e⁺ν μ⁻ν̄ (W⁺W⁻ via leptons), and for uu~→dd~e⁺e⁻g at full colour (at LC the eager lane is the second method), to ≤ 5·10⁻¹³ of the summed partials (worst 4.8·10⁻¹³, the W-paired three-line processes); pure-QCD results are bitwise unchanged (compared by script when the alias key changed; the test file checks them against MadGraph).
  - **Four-point colour-singlet contacts (fixed in the eager lane).** A four-point colour-singlet vertex (WWγγ, WWZγ, WWZZ, WWWW) whose four legs carry disjoint sets of complete quark lines is built by a partial merge into its contact auxiliary and a final merge out of it, and the full-colour traversal sectors of one line pairing (one sector per block order, all starting with the block of the distinguished, smallest coloured label) close on different sinks. Each merge keeping the three-leg cyclic orientation selected an order of the four legs that depends on the sink, so the Python Born built some such diagrams twice and their mirror images not at all: pure-EW uu~→dd~ss~cc~ was 1.48× MadGraph at full colour at the first reference point and up to 185× elsewhere, 2.5× with at most two gluons (QCD≤2) and 6 % off at all orders; LC, whose shared orderings close every traversal of a sector on one sink, was right. The partial merge now records its left input's lines in the auxiliary's basis key, and the final merge decides for the whole vertex (`ColorEngine._singlet_contact_orientation_allowed`): while the distinguished line lies on the root side, the root side is the same leg in every traversal and the three-leg rule of both merges keeps one order; otherwise the vertex lies on the path from the distinguished line to the sink, and only the order whose root side has the largest smallest line label is kept (the order the three-leg rule keeps for a three-leg vertex; one partial-merge orientation where two exist). Each such diagram is then built once over all traversal sectors, with the multiplicity of the contact lowering at a fixed root (two binary paths for WWZZ at a W root, one at a Z root): expanding the DAG of pure-EW uu~→dd~ss~cc~ into explicit trees of one helicity, each binary path of a contact weighted by the inverse of the path count at its root, gives every diagram the weight 1 at full colour and LC, also relabelled (a development check, not a committed test).
  - **Evidence for the contact rule.** With the rule, uu~→dd~ss~cc~ matches MadGraph at full colour and LC at three points, pure EW, at QCD≤2 and at all orders, to ≤ 10⁻¹³ of the summed partials, and so does the same process relabelled as u~u→cc~ss~dd~ (second method: other traversal sectors and vertex orientations build the diagrams; pure EW at full colour and LC, and once by hand QCD≤2, QCD≤4 and all orders at full colour); NLC gives the full-colour export pure EW, directly and relabelled (it moved by 5–38 % under relabelling before); uu~→dd~dd~ss~ (identical quarks) and dd~→uu~ss~cc~ match their pure-EW exports at full colour and LC (≤ 10⁻¹³; 4–13 % and 2.9–4.4× off before); MadGraph's pure-EW export assigns all its colour-flow amplitudes and reproduces the QED^12 column of its all-order export, its QCD≤2 export the QCD²QED¹⁰ and QED^12 columns, and the recurrence lane matches the all-order export in every squared order at full colour and LC. The eager lane also matches MadGraph for uc~→ds~dd~ (identical quarks paired by Ws) at full colour and LC, pure EW and at all orders. Pure-QCD results (gg→ggg, uu~→ggg, uu~→dd~g, ug→ugg, uu~→dd~ss~, uu~→uu~g, uu~→dd~gg at FC, NLC and LC, both lanes) and the two- and three-line electroweak processes of this section are bitwise unchanged (compared by script against the base; no committed golden).
  - **Five W-paired lines.** The rule decides at the contact vertex alone, from the lines of its legs, so it holds where legs carry several lines: pure-EW uu~→dd~ss~cc~bb~ with a massive b (five W-paired lines, default SM) gives the same value in four labellings at full colour (≤ 2·10⁻¹³), NLC (equal to full colour) and LC (≤ 5·10⁻¹³) at three points; it has no MadGraph reference: the 45 137-diagram standalone export had not compiled after 43 minutes and was stopped (REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM §F). It was launched without a cost estimate; the diagram count, known once MadGraph has generated the process, gives one before compilation.
  - **Larger contacts (fail closed).** Larger colour-singlet contacts (lowered to trees of auxiliaries, `models/compiler_contact_trees.py`) that join three or more quark lines at NLC or full colour fail closed with a generation error (`ColorEngine._require_four_point_singlet_contact`; open in WP2.9); the SM has none.
  - **Dirac–Weyl closures (both tree lanes fail closed; the loop Born admits them, m-qcd MQ-5F).** A fermion current whose line passed a massive fermion, an internal propagator or a massive external state, is a Dirac (chirality-0) current, and a massless external fermion is a Weyl (chirality ±1) state. Where a chiral vertex takes a line between massive and massless fermions (a massless b and the top through a W, restriction `no_b_mass` as in the providers' `loop_sm-no_b_mass`; ν_τ and the τ in the default SM), the line can reach its massless sink as a Dirac current, and the models install no Dirac–Weyl closure contraction (`models/compiler_contractions.py` installs (−1, 1), (0, 0) and (1, −1)). Measured at three on-shell RAMBO points of √s = 1 TeV (M_W = 80.419 GeV) against MadGraph 3.7 (`sm`, `sm-no_b_mass`):
    - *Silently wrong before the guard, in both lanes alike:* the diagrams of the massive fermion's exchange are dropped without an error. ν_τν̄_τ→W⁺W⁻ is 982–3030× MadGraph, ν_τν̄_τ→W⁺W⁻Z 631–2210× and ν_τν̄_τ→W⁺W⁻γ 157–4780× (default SM); with a massless b, bb~→W⁺W⁻ is 314–671×, bb~→W⁺W⁻Z 21–1460×, bb~→W⁺W⁻γ 68–685×, bb~→W⁺W⁻g 14–160×, tt~→bb~ 1.15–1.75× and e⁺e⁻→bb~W⁺W⁻ (off-shell top pairs in the five-flavour scheme) 0.24–0.74×. The recurrence lane equals MadGraph with that exchange removed to 13 digits ("vt vt~ > w+ w- / ta- ta+", "b b~ > w+ w- / t t~", "t t~ > b b~ / w+ w-"), and the eager lane before the guard (closure without the error) gave the recurrence's values. In the eager lane the drop depends on the labelling: ss~→cc~bb~ lost its top diagram in one labelling and kept it in another, and five W-paired lines with a massless b (uu~→dd~ss~cc~bb~, pure EW), whose b line closes on b~ in some sectors and not in others, changed under relabelling by 7.9 % at LC and up to 6.2× at full colour and NLC (at LC one labelling lost 625 of the 1654 top-quark diagrams of one helicity, all in the pairing where the b line closes on its own sink; with a massive b the process is labelling-invariant, above).
    - *Eager lane, fixed to fail closed for tree requests:* it raises a generation error (`dag_ordering.DiracWeylClosureUnsupported`, a `GenerationError` and `ValueError`), with the key `dirac-weyl-closure` of the recurrence lane, wherever a Dirac current would close on a Weyl sink (`generation/dag_ordering.py::_direct_contraction_ir`): on all processes above, on tW with a massless b (gb→tW⁻, bg→tW⁻, gb→W⁻t, bg→W⁻t) and on t-channel single top ub→dt and ub→td (gb→tW⁻ and ub→dt had no roots before the guard either: "no model-supported tree-level amplitudes"). Processes whose closures meet no such sink still build: bu→dt and bu→td match MadGraph `sm-no_b_mass` (≤ 10⁻¹³). Tests: `test_eager_lane_refuses_a_massless_line_through_a_massive_fermion`, `test_eager_lane_refuses_dirac_weyl_closures_with_a_massless_b`, `test_eager_lane_builds_single_top_where_no_dirac_current_meets_a_weyl_sink`.
    - *Recurrence lane (the default tree lane), fail closed since m-qcd MQ-5F:* it was silently wrong by the values above (the strict xfails `test_recurrence_lane_keeps_a_massless_line_through_a_massive_fermion` and `test_recurrence_lane_keeps_the_massive_exchange_with_a_massless_b`). Its structural-demand pass (`recurrence/construct.rs`, `DiracWeylClosureGuard` in `StructuralDemandIndex::new`) now refuses, with the listed key `dirac-weyl-closure`, a closure anchor and a structurally feasible complement state that no closure row joins when they are a Weyl and a Dirac state of a fermion pair that closes in other chirality states (the complement a Dirac current of the anchor's line) and some structural derivation reaches the complement within the request's coupling-order limits (`StructuralOrderReachability`: the minimal order vectors per support and state under the builder's own rule, zero-order sources and `combined_coupling_orders` on every internal current, filled only for such a complement; the structural-demand pass itself carries no orders, and without this the guard refused tt~→bb~ at QCD 2, QED 0 and gg→tt~bb~ at QCD 4, QED 0, which now build equal to the eager lane under both policies, `test_recurrence_lane_builds_a_massless_b_where_the_orders_keep_the_w_off`; unit test `the_order_reachability_keeps_minimal_orders_within_the_limits`): ν_τν̄_τ→W⁺W⁻, W⁺W⁻Z, W⁺W⁻γ (default SM) and, with a massless b, bb~→W⁺W⁻, W⁺W⁻Z, W⁺W⁻γ, W⁺W⁻g, tt~→bb~ and e⁺e⁻→bb~W⁺W⁻ are refusal tests (`test_recurrence_lane_refuses_a_massless_line_through_a_massive_fermion`, `test_recurrence_lane_refuses_the_massive_exchange_with_a_massless_b`, `test_recurrence_lane_refuses_the_radiative_dirac_weyl_class`). The on-the-fly lane (`evaluator.execution_mode = "on-the-fly"`), which closes its selected LC flows on the same template closures and dropped the same channels (bb~→W⁺W⁻ with a massless b: 24.0 against MadGraph's 0.060 at the first point), refuses them with the same guard where it builds a query's closures, at the query's anchor, at evaluation (`on_the_fly::sweep::build_selected_closures`; `test_on_the_fly_lane_refuses_dirac_weyl_closures`, explicit and minimal coupling-order policies). An on-the-fly artifact of the class is therefore generated and refuses at the first query whose anchor closes on the pair (a typed `EvaluationError`); queries anchored elsewhere count those diagrams at their own anchor and evaluate, so a run with LC flow selection can return values for some points before it fails. The anchor follows the query's colour components, so a refusal at generation would have to enumerate the LC selectors. Its minimal coupling-order policy (the default, resolved at the first cold query) does not know which singleton a query closes on: a Dirac-Weyl pair contributes the total orders of the diagrams it would close (with the local orders of the closures of the same two particles), and the pass refuses only where they would change the limits — a lower hierarchy degree than every viable total, or its minimum outside the envelope (`coupling_policy::refuse_dirac_weyl_orders_outside_the_envelope`): gb→tW⁻ with a massless b, whose every closure at the anchor meets a Dirac complement (`test_on_the_fly_minimal_policy_refuses_a_process_of_dirac_weyl_closures_only`). Otherwise the limits hold with or without those diagrams, and the query that closes on the pair's singleton meets its complement within them and refuses, while a query that closes elsewhere counts them at its own anchor. The refusal is an evaluation-kind error: generation wraps it as `GenerationError`, and an on-the-fly query raises the public `EvaluationError` (guard unit tests `construct.rs` `dirac_weyl_guard_tests`, the policy rule `coupling_policy` `dirac_weyl_orders_refuse_only_where_they_would_change_the_minimal_limits`). The checks read the anchor a closure is made at, so a massive line that closes at a massive leg still builds in the recurrence and in the on-the-fly lane under either policy (τ⁺τ⁻→ν_τν̄_τ and τ⁻ν_e→ν_τe⁻ equal the eager lane, `test_tree_lanes_build_a_massive_line_closing_on_no_weyl_sink`). tW and t-channel single top with a massless b are refused as before (now by this key where the guard meets them first), and bu→dt, bu→td with "no exact closure for physical LC sector" (the flows-without-roots item below, which refuses single top with a massive b as well). So no tree lane builds tW in the five-flavour scheme and single top builds only in the eager lane as bu→dt or bu→td; the tree lanes refuse where they would be wrong.
    - *Loop engine (m-qcd MQ-5F):* the loop engine needs no Dirac–Weyl contraction. It reads its Born DAG's colour plan and, per amplitude root, the coupling orders, colour sector and helicities (`blocks.TreeBlocks.born_roots`, `colour.born_basis`, `colour.leading_born_flows`, `artifact.born_amplitude_orders`), and closes the Born itself on the tree blocks at a model vertex holding leg 1 (`assemble._born_block_requests`, `ct_sites.SiteClosure`), whose vertex kernels embed Weyl currents in the Dirac basis; loop lines are full Dirac states. The decision is recorded by `test_loop_v2_dirac_weyl.py::test_loop_slices_never_read_the_root_contraction`: with a marker contraction on every direct root of the Born DAG the exported plan is byte-identical and the Born and virtual are identical. So the loop Born DAG (`compile_generic_dag(..., loop_born=True)`, `blocks.build_tree_blocks` and `artifact.born_amplitude_orders`) records a Dirac current closing on a Weyl sink as a root with the marker `dag_ordering.DIRAC_WEYL_CLOSURE` (`_direct_contraction_ir(..., loop_born=True)`, the tree DAG's own closure check; its bases name no propagator basis and its one component matches no fermion current, so no tree evaluator lowers it), where a tree request refuses. The class generates on loop_sm's own restrictions — no_b_mass gb→tW⁻, bb~→W⁺W⁻, ub→dt, bu→dt, tt~→bb~; zeromass_ckm and ckm gs→tW⁻, gd→tW⁻, us→dt, ss~→W⁺W⁻; c_mass ss~→W⁺W⁻, gs→W⁻c — with Catani's poles in double-double (nightly), the no_b_mass processes equal the default restriction with MB = ymb = 0 (binary64 ≤ 1e-13, double-double ≤ 1e-28; bb~→W⁺W⁻ in the default tier), and the m-qcd rows D17, F01, F02, F04 on no_b_mass, F03, C09, H02 (with its CKM-factor relation) and H03 pass PLAN §1.4 against MadLoop at four points (MQ-VALIDATE's campaign harness; records in `workspace/reports/engine-v2/mq-5f/`). The route equality is shown for those 2→2 processes (and gb→tW⁻g in an audit probe); it is no general substitute: bb~→W⁺W⁻g on the default restriction with MB = ymb = 0 fails Catani's poles and the Ward check in double-double where no_b_mass passes both, and uu~→W⁺W⁻g fails them on either restriction — a defect of W-pair production with a jet in the loop engine that predates MQ-5F (identical on the slice before it; open, outside this package).
    - *Controls (same points, both lanes unless stated):* with a massive b (default SM), tt~→bb~, gb→tW⁻ and bg→tW⁻ match MadGraph, and so do bu→td, ub→dt and ud~→tb~ in the eager lane (the recurrence refuses them, below). In the default SM, ν_eν̄_e→W⁺W⁻, the processes where a W turns an external τ into a ν_τ (τ⁺τ⁻→ν_τν̄_τ, τ⁻ν_e→ν_τe⁻, τ⁻ν̄_τ→e⁻ν̄_e) and e⁺e⁻→ν_τν̄_τW⁺W⁻, whose ν_τ line passes an internal τ but closes elsewhere, match MadGraph to ≤ 2·10⁻¹³: the defect needs a closure on the massless sink.
  - **Recurrence refusals (open).** The Rust recurrence fails closed with a generation error, never with a value, where it finds no exact closure for a flow without roots at the requested orders: uu~→dd~ss~, ud→udss~ and ud~→ud~ss~ at QCD=0 QED=4 ("no exact closure for physical LC sector 1"), uu~→dd~ss~cc~ at QCD=0 (sector 2) and QCD≤2 (sector 16), all at full colour and LC, uc~→ds~dd~ at all orders (sector 0), and, with a massive b (default SM), single top bu→td and ub→dt and ud~→tb~ at all orders (sector 1); tW builds and matches MadGraph in both lanes. Each named sector has no root at the requested orders in the eager lane (the u–c~ pairing of uc~→ds~dd~ at every order, the d–s~ pairing of uu~→dd~ss~ without gluons): the builder demands an exact closure in every physical LC sector, where such a sector is a zero amplitude. At full colour its helicity selector refuses uu~→dd~ss~ and ud→udss~ at QCD≤2 and uu~→dd~ss~cc~ at QCD≤4 ("requires a direct, Walsh, or symmetric-group Fourier reducer"; LC builds them). The eager lane builds and matches all of these.
  - **Oracles (O13).** uu~→uu~ and uu~→uu~g (identical-flavour pairings with relative signs), and ee→μμ and e⁺e⁻→e⁺e⁻ at NLO EW (lepton lines inside the loop), on top of the uudd, uu~→dd~g and uu~→dd~ss~ controls.
- **Majorana fermions** are rejected by the tree engine (`fermion_ordering.py:100-104`: "requires non-self-conjugate model orientations" [V]). v2 inherits this limit and states it in its scope check.
- **Per-family data:**
  - on-shell bindings (S-matrix entries replaced by exact external masses, `loops/kinematics.py:150-194` [V-s]);
  - active sectors;
  - bucket (N, R_f);
  - master keys: canonical (cut lines, invariant masks, mass ids) per cut with at most 4 lines;
  - the re-test relabelling k_f (§10.2).

### 3.4 Skeleton contents and seed classes

**Content of a position:** (vertex template, attached block current or currents with their full colour states, outgoing gap species, traversal orientation). Fermion and ghost lines are traversed in either direction with the matching oriented kernels.

**Certified planar mode.** When a colour class is certified (DDM for adjoint rings, or LC), the cubic and quartic contributions of a position with word π fold into one segment:

X(π) = V₃·J(π) + Σ_{π=π′π″} V₄(J(π′), J(π″))  (Design 1).

The selection rule (§3.3) still applies to the cubic and quartic parts separately: for a two-position ring with a single-leg partner, only the quartic part is generated. Generic mode keeps quartic splits, and blocks on the non-planar side of the colour double line, as separate contents with their own ordered-vertex kernels.

**Seed class of a skeleton.** This is the colour vector obtained by closing the cut ports of the skeleton's colour flow. It is the colour factor of the (n+2)-point tree with the cut pair closed [L-s Kälin 1712.03539 §4.1].

- **Generic** (any model whose vertices lower to colour flow): κ_s = Σ_t c_{s,t}|t⟩. The structures t are single traces, products of traces, open strings, and graded U(1) pieces. Blocks on the inner side of a gluon double line are merged into the planar skeleton only where the tree engine's reflection certificate applies (`models/external_catalog.py` `adjoint_current_reflection_phase` [V-s tree report]); otherwise they are separate contents.
- **Certified compressions.** Each is re-certified at loop level per model and order, never inherited from tree certificates (MASTER_PLAN §2.16):
  - adjoint ring Tr(F^σ) for gluon and ghost loops [L hep-ph/9910563 §3 `NewLoopColor`; verified at `fcolor.tex:845-852` by the physics judge];
  - fundamental trace Tr(T^σ) for closed quark loops (automatic in colour flow);
  - one quark line: the quark ring, one class per proportional colour vector of its generalised arc word (WP2.4, §7.2). The q̄q ring with left/right primitives [L hep-ph/9910563 `LoopColorqqNew`] would leave fewer classes but is not adopted (§7.2 states the seed-cost consequence).

  Compression changes only the seed-table size, never the dressing. It is also what makes M valid in FC/NLC.

### 3.5 Open-loop objects

All payloads use raw monomial coefficients in the NinjaRS layout. `ninjars:fast/tensor_layout.rs` is a public module with `ncoeff`, `block_offset` and `homogeneous_index` [V]. Rank r has C(r+4,4) coefficients, there are no hidden factorials (MASTER_PLAN §2.12), and the raise tables are built once from `homogeneous_index`.

- **B node** v = (f, s_cut, k, σ_{k+1..N}), where σ_j = (content of position j, gap species s_j).
  - Payload O_v[o][h][α][β][m]:
    - o: observable column;
    - h: helicity row over the undressed legs;
    - α: cut-line index (4 for vector or Dirac lines, 1 for scalars and ghosts);
    - β: current-end index;
    - m: monomials of degree ≤ r_k.
  - Leaves (k = 0) are skeletons; their seed object is w_s·U0[c(s)][o][h]·δ_{αβ}.
- **F state** p = (s_cut, (content_1…content_k), species, orders). Payload O_p[h_att][α][β][m]; observables enter at closure.
- **M state** = (routing class ρ, (B_1,s_1)…(B_k,s_k), undressed word u, open species, orders). Payload as in B; seeds per (σ, ρ, rotation) at the cut. Closures scatter into per-family accumulators. Live states are chunked by the last letters of the cut word, which loses no merge (`cft/memory_gluon.py` [M]).

### 3.6 Axes

- **Helicities.** The Born catalogue (`loops/physical_states.py:23-29, 87-89` [V-s]) with a selection mask (§8.1). B and M carry rows of undressed legs; F carries rows of attached legs.
- **Observables.** Columns o = (Born-order component a) × (inclusive | directed history and direction | spin-source class | resolved component). The closure of a skeleton of loop order b lands in sector a + b.
- **Orders.** Exact per-current and per-content order vectors. The joint MadLoop-style eligibility of `loops/orders.py` is kept.
- **ε and μ².** Production numerators are four-dimensional and μ²-free, which `TensorNumerator` covers (MASTER_PLAN §2.12; `physical_process.py:495-503` [V-s]). The monomial label (a0..a3, k_μ², e) is retained in the plan schema for the direct-R2 bonus (§6.9).

### 3.7 Kernel contracts

These are new prepared-kernel kinds in `models/prepared_catalog.py` (`PreparedKernelSpec` at `:269-310` [V-s]).

| Kind | Content | Inputs |
|---|---|---|
| `loop-segment` (split) | Vertex tensor Y_g, Z_g (and higher q-degree terms if a model has them) for (vertex template, loop-in leg, loop-out leg, attached legs, orientation), including the outgoing loop-propagator numerator; independent of the block current | P_in, block momenta, couplings, masses |
| `loop-segment` (fused) | X(J) = Y(J) + Z(J)·q for one block current | Same, plus J |
| `loop-propagator-numerator` | Numerator only, per species and orientation (denominators belong to NinjaRS) | Momentum |
| `loop-closure` | Trace over the cut index; no sign (the closed fermion- and ghost-loop −1 enters only through ε_s, §3.3) | — |
| `ct-vertex` (wave 2) | CT vertex as a tree vertex with (kind, ε) slots | As tree vertices |
| `ct-site` (slice, oracle) | CT vertex applied to m block currents at the root | Currents, momenta |

The catalogue carries a **full-kernel flag** (WP1.1): every tree kernel exists in its compile-time variant (transverse where `compiler_entry.py` chooses it) and in its full Feynman-rule variant, and §3.2 item 2 selects between them.

All kinds are compiled from the UFO Lorentz structures by Symbolica coefficient extraction in q. That works because the kernel component expressions are plain Symbolica expressions. The probe found q-degree ≤ 1 for G G G and ghost–gluon kernels and 0 for quark–gluon kernels (`probe_kernel.py`) [V-s tree report §6.2].

**Native polynomial stage (M-SM MS-GEN, HANDOFF §0.4).** The segments' coefficient extraction runs in Rust (`rusticol_core::loop_v2::generate::catalogue`, PyO3 `loop_catalogue_polynomials`): Python forms each segment's outputs (Lorentz contraction and loop-line numerator) and defers it; every 64 segments one parallel call expands the outputs, takes their coefficient lists over the current, block and loop-momentum variables, sums and expands the entries, forms their canonical strings, the inputs they read, the undeclared-input check and the candidate's signature (sha256 of its canonical JSON), each step the Python oracle's own on the same Symbolica revision. The outputs cross as Symbolica's portable text (`repr`), the variables and inputs as canonical strings. Python stays the oracle (`catalogue.native_catalogue_polynomials(False)`); the catalogue is byte-identical on loop_sm (default-tier test), on the G_μ UFO with every propagating loop species (nightly), and with its plans on the EW ladder and the M-QCD default rows (§4.4). The canonical renaming of a stage with function definitions (`assemble._canonical_stage`: the native parameter program's, whose 2640 outputs on the 2→3 EW rung print to 634 MB) renames whole name tokens of the portable text natively in parallel (`loop_v2::generate::rename`, PyO3 `loop_rename_symbols`: a head where a call follows, a variable otherwise) and parses it back; the `replace_multiple` passes with a rule per symbol and repeated call passes stay its oracle (`assemble.text_canonical_stage(False)`): 46 s → 25 s there, Python's parse of the renamed text being the rest.

- **Evaluators.** Binary64 uses SymJIT; DoubleFloat and arbitrary precision use exact `ExpressionEvaluator<Complex<Rational>>` states. As built (WP3.1 native point inputs, artifact-portability): the tree program's kernels and its parameter program are saved exact states plus one binary64 SymJIT plane application each (storage-v4 IR, the kernels at -O2 and the parameter program at level 0, compiled at generation from the state's binary64 evaluator, recompiled at load; §12.1 (a)), so binary64 touches no Symbolica state and needs no licence, and DoubleFloat and arbitrary precision decode the states under pyAmpliCol's Symbolica library key (crate `pyamplicol`, `rust/crates/pyamplicol`, §12.1 (e)); on an x86-64 CPU without AVX and FMA3 (no SymJIT, §12.1 (c)) binary64 runs the states too. Each plane application is checked against its state at generation, off the real axis and at the real points it runs at (the parameter program: the request's parameter values; WP3.2 status, ew-native). At other real parameter values the translation makes the zero imaginary part of every cut argument +0 (`symjit_plane::canonical_cut_row`, M-SM MS-MASTERS, §15 risk 13), a cut argument that is real wherever some complex-mass widths vanish reads its real projection when they are exactly zero (`ct_recursion._RealArguments.zero_width_cases`, every carrier: its zero imaginary part could otherwise come out as a rounding residue of either sign, CM01 at WZ = WT = 0 with WW ≠ 0), and it flattens the control flow, evaluating every branch of a conditional (a model helper's `recms`, `reglog`, `grreglog`) and selecting with its `Join` (`symjit_plane::is_control_flow`; SymJIT's common-subexpression elimination could define a value shared with later straight-line code inside a branch the parameters do not take), so binary64 consults no Symbolica state at run time, whatever the parameters, scale or pole selection. The plan's kernels are exact term lists interpreted natively in every carrier (§5.5).
- **Polynomial multiply.** O′ = O·Y + raise(O·Z_μ, q_μ) is one generic Rust routine over T ∈ {f64, DoubleFloat, Float}, with no per-rank kernels and no rank cap. Lanes are the innermost (structure-of-arrays) index, and the batch carries at least 16 lanes (§5.4).

### 3.8 Plan v4 and batch state

```text
LoopProgramV4 {
  process:   legs (StateLeg), helicity catalogue + selection mask, normalization divisor,
             order layout, ModelBinding::Source, flavour members
  tree:      reference to the tree recurrence plan (existing PACBIN member), block export table
             (block id -> full CurrentIndex incl. ColorState, rows, kernel variant),
             Born export (basis b x helicity), CT=1 export (slots)
  kernels:   references into the prepared model bundle (SymJIT applications, exact states),
             transverse and full variants
  families:  [key, positions (leg masks), mass ids, frame, rank, bucket, on-shell bindings,
              active sectors, master keys, re-test relabelling]; certified-zero log
  schedules: B: per family {nodes (depth, species, row legs), edges (child|leaf, content, group),
                kernel groups, roots per cut species, leaf weights incl. fermion signs}
             F: prefix graph {states, edges, closures (family, class, weight)}
             M: where adopted {chunked states, edges, seed slots (ordering, routing class),
                closures (family, weight)}
  colour:    Born basis, seed classes (generic terms | primitive ids + certificates),
             exact Laurent tables K[c,b], degrees P per sector, accuracy, FFT certificates
  counterterms: CT=1 export map, kinds/slots, Laurent coefficient columns, field rules, witness log
  observables:  sectors and pairs, directed histories, spin classes, resolved axes
  reduction:    buckets (N,R) -> families, master canonicalization rules, engine policy
  stability:    re-test mode, error budget, trigger and escalation policy defaults
}

LoopBatchStateV4<T> {
  exact inputs, invariant table s[mask], model snapshot (SourceOwner), tree arena,
  seeds U0[class][obs][h], per-thread trie workspaces (B) / chunk workspaces (M),
  bucket TensorNumerators, Laurent results and re-test results per (family, lane),
  CT amplitudes, statuses, rescue queues
}
```

As built (WP2.1, WP2.6; §12.1): the stored program holds no `tree` reference and no model-bundle `kernels`. Its kernels are exact polynomial term lists in a canonical term order, one copy per catalogue kernel and mass-sign flip of the content that binds it (a massive fermion line leaving a segment is read with its mass negated, `reference_executor.mass_flips`), interpreted natively (`loop_v2/dress.rs`). Trees, Born components and counterterm scalars are per-point inputs that native programs stored as further PACBIN members form (`point_inputs = "native-v2"`, WP3.1; the former `"regenerated-at-load"`, which rebuilt them in Python from a slice regenerated at load, is retired by generation-scale-2).
