**Verdict.**

- *Integrated slice:* G1 fails for every 2→3 and 2→4 process it generates, at 11.1–27.4 × (largest batch timed; 11.8–27.4 × at B = 1) the limit; the per-point Python inputs take 85–99 % of the time, the Born and counterterm colour projections (`_PointEvaluation._born_and_counterterms`) the largest part. It does not generate the V+jets rows: their closed-quark A g g triangles need Furry's certified zeros (`CountertermCompletenessError`, WP3.5), and their Z couplings on loop quark lines the electroweak fermion signs (WP3.2); the native executor tests generate them with a test-only Furry patch and photon exchange only.
- *Native inputs* (development, WP3.1) at B = 128: seven of nine gated processes pass, uu~→uu~g is open (1.00 of its limit, band 0.90–1.10, also after its single repeat; 0.87 at B = 32) and uu~→dd~g fails (1.35; 1.15 at B = 32). The gate holds for every B: at B = 1 with 16 lanes every gated process fails (a one-point call pays a full 16-lane chunk); with one lane per call it passes for uu~→ggg, gg→ggg, uu~→e⁻e⁺gg, gg→gggg, gg→tt̄gg and uu~→gggg, and fails for uu~→dd~g, uu~→uu~g and uu~→e⁻e⁺g; at B = 8 uu~→dd~g, uu~→uu~g and uu~→e⁻e⁺g fail and uu~→e⁻e⁺gg is open (0.62 of its limit, one of its three blocks 2.3 × slower than the other two).
- *Fastest of all tools* at B = 128 (outside the uncertainty band): uu~→e⁻e⁺g (0.82 × MadLoop), uu~→ggg (0.90 × OpenLoops, the closest call), gg→ggg, uu~→e⁻e⁺gg (0.55 × Recola), gg→gggg, gg→tt̄gg and uu~→gggg (0.35–0.51 × OpenLoops); slower than OpenLoops for uu~→dd~g (1.75 ×) and uu~→uu~g (1.30 ×).
- The 2→2 control gg→gg (no gate): 2.22 ms with native inputs against MadLoop's 2.02 ms.

**Where the time goes** (native inputs, B = 128, share of the total): native trees, Born and counterterm inputs 15–71 % (gg→gggg 71 %); seeds, factors and dressing 22–49 %; scatter into NinjaRS lanes ≤ 20 % (uu~→e⁻e⁺g); NinjaRS with masters 5–14 %; Python glue (point parameters, conversion, assembly, result export) ≤ 30 % (uu~→dd~g). The master integrals (canonical arguments, cache and oneloopmaster) are 11–45 % of the reduction stage, oneloopmaster itself 2–12 %: the cache bookkeeping costs more than the integrals.

**Recalibration** (`tools/loop_v2_costmodel.py --g1`; §1.3 Table 1c). Kernel throughput on the real plans is 1.25–1.34 G complex MAC/s per core, gate G0's rate; the NinjaRS stage with masters is 0.97–1.39 × the model R. The recipe's outer factor (trees, seeds, counterterms: 1.05–1.10) is wrong with today's native inputs: the total is 1.66–3.6 × dressing plus reduction at full colour and up to 12.7 × at LC, so measured totals are 1.2–7.1 × the recipe band where the design has a row (gg→gg, gg→gggg, gg→tt̄gg). Table 1c re-estimates every design row with the measured terms (outer factor of the class representative): every gated FC row keeps a margin with native inputs (headroom 1.55–2.9), the thinnest uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g (1.7) and gg→tt̄gg (1.55). The dressing and NinjaRS terms of every estimate stand; the tree term must be re-measured once the trees are compiled.

**B against M** (§1.6 item 5, on the schedule-dependent stages: seeds, factors, dressing, scatter and the NinjaRS reduction, "the reduction included"; the trees, Born and counterterm inputs and the glue are the same work in both schedules). M/B: gg→gggg LC 0.75 (B = 1) and 0.79 (B = 128) — adopted; gg→gggg FC 0.88 and 0.85 (± 0.09), the rule's edge — not adopted; gg→ggg LC 0.90 / 0.93 and FC 0.99 / 1.00 — not adopted. End to end the same rows give 0.86–1.11, diluted by the shared tree inputs. With a quark line M's work equals B's (DESIGN §5.6; `tests/unit/test_loop_benchmark.py`). *Decision (provisional until the trees are compiled):* schedule M for pure-gluon classes at leading colour from n = 6; schedule B for every other class at n ≤ 6 (pure-gluon FC included); M remains the planned default from n ≥ 8. At seven legs M against B is open: `measure-g1-7leg` timed schedule B only (its schedule-M run was stopped), routed to generation-scale-2 / WP3.1 I13.

**Leading colour.** LC is 0.43–0.50 × FC at B = 1 but 0.72–1.30 × at B = 128 with native inputs: the loop stage shrinks, the native trees at LC cost more than at FC (uu~→ggg 9.9 against 5.9 ms per point). The V+jets rows were not timed at LC.

**Values** (`g1-values.json`; report Table 10). The Born agrees with every provider to ≤ 1.0×10⁻¹³ on the common stream points. At each process's worst point the double-double value (one rotation and the reduction-direction check, native inputs) agrees with the closest provider to ≤ 7.3×10⁻¹² and separates the sources: the binary64 deviations above 10⁻⁹ (gg→ggg 1.7×10⁻⁶, gg→gggg 4.0×10⁻⁷, gg→tt̄gg 1.5×10⁻⁹–7.6×10⁻⁹) and uu~→gggg's 1.9×10⁻¹⁰ are pyAmpliCol's binary64 loss at ill-conditioned points, each below its returned estimate E (9.9×10⁻⁶, 4.8×10⁻⁶, 5.6×10⁻⁸, 2.7×10⁻⁹); the other deviations above 10⁻¹¹ stay in DD and are the providers' own accuracy — Recola at uu~→dd~g (3.3×10⁻¹⁰), uu~→uu~g (7.1×10⁻¹¹) and uu~→e⁻e⁺gg (4.8×10⁻¹¹), OpenLoops at uu~→ggg (8.4×10⁻¹¹), MadLoop at uu~→e⁻e⁺gg (1.1×10⁻¹⁰), and MadLoop's production values at the ill-conditioned gluon and top points (up to 2.4×10⁻⁷ from the DD value). Campaign fixtures: uu~→dd~g 3.6×10⁻¹⁰, uu~→uu~g 8.6×10⁻¹¹, gg→tt̄gg 1.1×10⁻¹¹ on both paths; gg→gggg point 1 misses the 2×10⁻⁸ tolerance in binary64 (native inputs 4.0×10⁻⁷, integrated 8.7×10⁻⁸; E = 4.8×10⁻⁶; DD agrees with Recola to 4.4×10⁻¹²). The binary64 values at gg→gggg point 1 differ between the two paths (and from the 8.4×10⁻⁹ of the WP3.1 validation table on a later generation-scale commit) because the ill-conditioned point amplifies roundoff differently in each; all lie within E.

**Levers, with measured or bounded gains.**

1. Native per-point inputs (WP3.1 G-int): measured 3.6–24.7 × at B = 1 and 12.2–55.8 × at B = 128 (2→3), integrated ÷ native at equal batch size.
2. Compiled native trees (kernels as the tree engine's SymJIT planes, batched over lanes): the trees' share is an upper bound of the gain, × 3.4 for gg→gggg.
3. Lanes by batch size in binary64 (`plan_export.lanes_for` returns 16 lanes for one point): measured 2.4–4.2 × at B = 1.
4. Finite-only result path (sum the virtual natively; no per-family lane export and Python assembly unless checks or observables ask for them) and lane-major closures: up to the glue and scatter shares; for uu~→dd~g they are 1.25 ms of 2.65, without them it would take 1.40 ms (0.71 of its limit).
5. AVX2/AVX-512 kernel selection (G0: × 1.0–1.4 on the dressing) and NinjaRS-side work (parallel reduction, transposed reduction, a cheaper master cache key) for the remaining loop stage.

Kernel specialisation (WP4.6) stays a wave-4 option: seeds, factors and dressing are 22–49 % of the native-input time, and levers 1–4 come first.

---

## 6. Counterterms, R2, UV and external fields

### 6.1 Production form: counterterms inside the tree recursion (wave 2)

**Rules are kept.** Every prepared `CountertermRule` (`loops/counterterms.py:52`, built by `prepare_counterterm_model` at `:161` [V]) carries:

- its kind (R2, UVmass, UVloop or UVtree);
- Laurent coefficients for ε powers −2..0;
- loop-species alternatives and loop group;
- an order already tagged with the internal order `PYAMPLICOL_CT_INSERTION` (`:47` [V]).

The rules are lowered into the tree model as ordinary vertices, and a counterterm contribution is an ordinary tree contribution with exactly one of them (REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM J): no counterterm is mapped onto its loop counterpart (§6.2).

- **Lorentz.** The same normalisation and oriented-kernel path as tree vertices (`models/tensors.py:72`, `compiler_kernels.py:223` [V-s]), always in the full-kernel variant. Contact CT vertices of any arity use the existing contact decomposition.
- **Order.** One insertion carries the pseudo-order `PYAMPLICOL_CT_INSERTION` twice (a CT vertex; a clone line once on each of its two vertex copies) and its limit is 2, so `CurrentIndex.coupling_orders` interns CT = 0 and CT = 1 currents in one recursion; the closure at leg 1 makes the CT amplitudes (Status below).
- **Slots.** Kind × ε power become coefficient columns on CT = 1 currents: at most 12, sparse. UV_local, R2 and the fields therefore stay separable, and the coefficients are runtime scalars from the `counterterm_scale` source view (contracts K1).
- **Two-point insertions on internal lines** are the unary step J′(S) = P(S)·Π(S)·J(S). It is realised without a new node kind, as clone lines of the tree model (Status below). Insertions on external legs are excluded and replaced by field terms.
- **Colour.** CT colour structures are rewritten into tree colour sources (`models/ct_color_flow.tree_colour_source`: the UFO colour of a CT entry mapped onto the d-symbol, structure-constant and identity forms) and compiled by the existing tree compiler; no new colour algebra.
  - Besides singlet, identity, T and f, this covers the d-symbol and δδ forms. The recurrence compiler accepts the d-symbol only on vertices whose gluon legs are projected onto SU(N) (`compile_ufo_model_ir(u1_projected_vertices=...)`: the CT vertices of the closure's lowered model); an ordinary model vertex with a d-symbol is still refused, since it would read the U(1) part of a fermion line's gluon (`test_external_compiler_lowers_the_symmetric_color_tensor_only_projected`). The summed indices of its expansion are numbered after the expression's own dummies (`models/tensors.py`).
  - δδ and d·d produce a new colour-state kind, open string ⊗ closed trace. The Rust state already has `Trace` components (`recurrence/color.rs:16` [V-s]).
  - Before any LC/NLC split, the fixed-SU(3) four-gluon R2 is replaced by the general-N_c HV asset: single trace × (r_CI V_I + r_CII V_II) + T_F δδ × (−C_A/(3N_c)) [L-s 2007.03713 eqs. 5.30–5.38].

Counterterm amplitudes then sit in the Born colour sectors (plus the few multi-trace CT states) and are contracted with the Born like the Born itself. This costs about #CT kinds × tree cost and needs no per-site colour seed. That is why the recursion beats the site closure in FC at 7 legs: site seeds would cost #sites × #Born × #h ≈ 3×10⁹ complex MAC per point [I].

**Status (WP2.2, `work/v2-ct-recursion`).** The counterterms of the slice come from the tree recursion over the CT-lowered model, closed at leg 1 like the Born; the site closure stays as the oracle (§6.6). Lowering goes through the existing tree engine rather than a CT-specific builder [V `loops/v2/ct_recursion.py`, `loops/v2/ct_closure.py`, `models/loading.py` `lower_counterterm_vertices`, `models/ct_color_flow.py`, `models/ct_line_insertions.py`]:

- **Lowered model.** Every CT rule of arity ≥ 3 whose colour, Lorentz structure and contacts the tree compiler lowers becomes an ordinary vertex of a copy of the tree model. One lowered model serves every request of the same model, veto set, colour declaration and scope (order bounds and compatibility profile): the request's own rules are lowered into a copy kept within the CT program's order limits (`ct_closure.program_order_limits`; `lower_counterterm_vertices(order_limits=...)` drops the ordinary vertex cells above them, and a rule above them is inert). At g g > g g g g 9 of loop_sm's 96 vertices remain, and the whole-model compile (twice, since the first attempt refuses the EW contacts) went from 30 s to 5 s at gg→ggg. The tree engine then builds CT = 0 and CT = 1 currents in one recursion, in both builders (Python generic DAG, Rust recurrence). No CT-specific Rust code was needed.
- **Pseudo-orders.** `PYAMPLICOL_CT_INSERTION` and one order per slot (component × ε power × N_c grade, `PYAMPLICOL_CT_{R2,UV_LOCAL}_EPS{0,1,2}[_NC{g}|_NCM{g}]`). One insertion carries each of them twice: a CT vertex itself, a clone line once on each of its two vertex copies; the insertion limit is 2.
- **Couplings** are derived model couplings, `S_slot · Σ_rules M_rule · c_rule`. Here `c_rule` is the exact Laurent coefficient of the rule's UFO coupling (fixed parameters and numeric constants substituted at the leaves, so a `cond` branch not taken is never formed; the UFO-standard `cond`/`reglog`/`complexconjugate` resolved, `ct_recursion.standard_helpers`; non-vanishing assumptions recorded and checked). `M_rule` (the number of admitted loop-content alternatives, §6.2) and `S_slot` (slot switch: 0 drops a slot, e.g. the poles in finite-only mode) are integer runtime parameters set once per process. Every carrier evaluates the couplings itself. **Electroweak CT scalars (WP3.2 EWI-3, EWI-3R).** A coupling reading a CT scalar whose body needs the model's scalar library (the G_μ and loop_qcd_qed_sm helpers `re`, `recms`, `regsqrt`, `grreglog`, `reglogp/m`, `arg` and their CMS `reglog`) is lowered on *reference parameters*: each Laurent coefficient of the coupling itself is a pair of real external parameters (real, imaginary part) of the lowered model, so the tree compiler sees one symbol per coefficient and never expands the coupling's model parameters (the G_μ couplings resolved to `Gf`, `MW`, `MZ` and the `conj` of real masses; EWI-3R option 2). The CT program binds them per card from the coupling's exact source and its CT scalars' bodies with every helper a scalar-library function (`ct_recursion.coefficient_plan`, one helper semantics with the scalar plans; `CountertermRecursion.parameter_values(card=…)`, at ≥ 32 digits plus 16 guard digits, one rounding per carrier: the plan's Symbolica evaluator orders its operations by the process's symbol registrations, and the guard digits keep the rounded values independent of that history); the coupling may be any polynomial in its CT scalars (the coefficient is the plan's exact Laurent coefficient of the product); which powers can be non-zero is read from the source (`CoefficientLowering.is_zero`: each term's power plus every sum of up to its degree of its CT scalars' non-zero powers, `_reachable_powers`), and coefficients vanishing at the fixed parameters are structural zeros, non-finite ones refuse their rules, and so does a coupling that is not a polynomial in its CT scalars or reaches a power below ε^-2 (fail closed, `CountertermCouplingError`). The per-scalar form (the NLOCT couplings are linear in their CT scalars, so a coefficient is a linear form in the scalars' Laurent coefficients `P_k^(r)`, each a reference pair) stays the exact form of the cells the lowered model leaves out (their cancellation between rules needs the shared scalars) and of couplings that read a formal-N_c continuation's colour invariants (`CoefficientLowering.exact`). loop_sm has only UFO-standard helpers, so its CT program is unchanged (plans, CT payload and native trees byte-identical). Natively the parameter program binds them (`native_trees._CountertermReferences`): per referenced (coupling or scalar, ε power) its coefficient-plan expression on the program's inputs, the helpers again the scalar library's functions, and a reference reads its real or imaginary part (the scalar library's exact projections); the plan's active diagnostic enters as a finiteness indicator (`equal(d, d)`: 1 or NaN), which the executor checks. This covers the QCD CT scalars of loop_qcd_qed_sm and G_μ too (G_UVb, tMass_UV, … call their CMS `reglog`, so QCD-only requests on those models have references): native and Python inputs agree to 3e-15 in binary64 and 3e-32 in double-double (`test_counterterm_references_are_native_parameter_program_inputs`, G_μ g g > t t~). **Branch sides.** At real masses some helper arguments lie exactly on a branch cut (at the `gg_ttx_ew` card 945 `regsqrt` arguments, e.g. `regsqrt(MH^4 - 4 MH^2 MT^2)`, and some of `reglogp` and `grreglog`), and their side matters: an exactly-on-cut argument with a -0 instead of a +0 imaginary part changes HWcft_UV_EW by 80 % and HMass2_UV_EW by 1.7 % on both electroweak UFOs (`grreglog` selects its sheet by the signs of its arguments' imaginary parts; the UFOs' ±1e-10 i regulators of near-cut arguments are another matter: flipping those as well conjugates every argument and leaves the values at real masses unchanged). The convention is **MadLoop's** (DECISIONS 2026-10-25 ~16:45): MG5_aMC's Fortran helpers `REGLOG`, `REGLOGP`, `REGLOGM`, `REGSQRT` and `GRREGLOG` (double and quadruple precision) replace a vanishing imaginary part of either sign by +0 before taking a branch, so an exactly real argument takes the +0 side; it is also the side of the UFO's own Python evaluation at the card and of mpmath's principal branch (no negative zero). Every lane takes it (`ct_recursion._RealArguments`): a helper call's, logarithm's or fractional power's argument that is a rational function of real inputs reads it through the real projection (static, +0 in every carrier and evaluator), and any other argument of those five helpers is normalised at runtime as MadLoop does (`if(imaginary_part(z) == 0, real_part(z), z)`). Without it the side of an exactly real intermediate depends on the evaluation order: SymJIT and Symbolica's evaluator gave `regsqrt(MZ^4 - 4 MW^2 MZ^2)` opposite sides, so `grreglog` differed by 2πi·LogSwitch and the binary64 plane check refused g g > g g with QCD+QED corrections. Evidence: every Laurent piece MadLoop's model library writes (143 on G_μ, 139 on loop_qcd_qed_sm, HWcft_UV_EW and HMass2_UV_EW included) equals the plan at the `gg_ttx_ew` card to 3.7e-15 in binary64 and, against MadLoop's quadruple precision, 6.0e-31 in double-double and 2.8e-33 in arbitrary precision (`test_counterterm_scalars_equal_madloop`, fixture `tests/fixtures/loop/madloop-ct-parameters.json` from `tools/loop_reference/madloop_ct_parameters.py`: MG5's model writer with the options of a MadLoop standalone output for every coupling, production flags, the toolkit's quadruple-precision repairs; its 12 pieces of the `gg_ttx_ew` standalone export's own library are bit-identical); the -0 side differs from MadLoop's values by O(1) there (discriminating). In the real-mass scheme the widths enter the CT parameters only under `recms(CMSParam == 1 and W != 0, …)`, so zero widths left to the card change nothing. MadLoop's quadruple-precision lane reads the card through a binary64 buffer (`lha_read.f`), so the comparison binds the plan at the card's binary64 values. A parameter program with references whose binary64 plane application is refused fails the generation, as any other refused plane does (no plan is stored without its native inputs). Cost (measurement M1, `workspace/reports/engine-v2/wp32/M1-ew-ct.md`): the tree compile of the lowered model dominates, through the kernel-equivalence keys and proofs of its kernels (the canonical strings of components times the resolved couplings, `compiler_entry._kernel_keys_and_proofs`). EWI-3R removed the referenced CT couplings from them (option 2 above) and runs the keys and the proofs a compile lacks natively in parallel (`rusticol_core::model_proofs`, `model_kernel_equivalence`; byte-identical, the Python stage is the oracle: `compiler_entry.native_kernel_proofs(False)`); the proofs of kernels with the same coupling and equal or exchanged components (the two input orders of one orientation, repeated kernels) share their expansions and canonical strings. The compile forms each oriented Lorentz structure's components, contact partials and finals, Horner schemes, fusions and tensor normalisations once and renames them per kernel (the networks run on canonical symbols whose order does not depend on the kernel kind): on the canonical text when every kernel symbol it holds has the one kind (Symbolica's canonical string sorts each node's children by their own canonical strings, which then first differ outside a kind; likewise a fusion's derived-coupling term ids when no id is a prefix of another and their order is kept), otherwise through Symbolica (`compiler_kernels._renamed_kernel_text`, `_fused_components`, `_canonical_product_text`). Symbolica's `expand` is not idempotent (a second expansion can expand a sum the first left in a factor), so the proofs keep the oracle's `(sign * oriented).expand()` for both signs (pinned by `test_native_proofs_expand_the_signed_components_again`: `((1-a)^(3/2)+1/2)·(1-a)^(1/2)` needs two) and the lowering's zero test of a coefficient expands twice, as `ct_sites.vanishes_identically` does. The target's CT lowering (stage `lowered_counterterm_model`) takes 287 s → 24.2-27.4 s cold and 10.0-11.1 s warm on 8 pinned cores (option 2 alone: 153 s), with byte-identical base models (loop_sm, loop_qcd_qed_sm, G_μ), CT model IR equal to the Python oracle's (G_μ, loop_qcd_qed_sm) and loop_sm's CT programs unchanged; the remainder is listed in `M1-ew-ct.md` (EWI-3R section). Hygiene (M2): the lowering forms no infinity or indeterminate; on G_μ every M1 rung logs only the 9 Symbolica infinity warnings of model preparation, all from Community-HEPKit's `Model.from_json` (3 per call; `covariant_model.build_covariant_structure` calls it twice, `counterterms.prepare_counterterm_model` once), which forms the branch not taken, `1/aEW0M1`, of `aEW0 = cond(aEW0M1 == 0, 0, 1/aEW0M1)` at the fixed aEW0M1 = 0 (no value is affected).
- **N_c grading (§6.7).** With a colour declaration each lowered term is split into its N_c powers over the formal-N_c identities of the source continuation (C_A → N_c, C_F → (N_c² − 1)/(2N_c), T_F → 1/2; `split_color_grades`), and the grade is part of the slot. Whole-rule continuations (the general-N_c four-gluon R2 asset, `QCDR2Continuation`) are lowered from their replacement terms (`whole_rule_rows`: spenso network → adjoint traces). LC and NLC therefore take their CT components from the recursion.
- **Closure at leg 1** [V `ct_closure.py`]. The CT program is the loop-request recursion of the lowered model over the leg sets without leg 1, except the set of all other legs (Python generic DAG; `RootReachability`): a root attaches leg 1 and at least two currents, so no root reads that set, which held 80 % of the program's interactions at gg→ggg (89 k of 111 k; 21.7 k remain, against 22.2 k for the slice's own blocks). The next-largest sets then feed no interaction; the runtime schema gives them their propagated value anyway (`build_runtime_expression_schema(propagated_currents=...)`, the hunk of WP3.1 I1a on `work/v2-generation-scale`, byte-identical; `ct_closure.program_propagated_currents`). A one-CT tree rooted at leg 1 has its insertion inside a block (`ROOT_VERTEX`: an ordinary root vertex with one CT = 1 current), on the line between the root and a block (`ROOT_LINE`: a clone current read by the root's ordinary vertex, with the other half of Π's orders and κ on a Weyl anti-clone) or at the root (`ROOT_COUNTERTERM`: the rule's site with leg 1 as one block). Every root term is a site term on the program's blocks, so colour (exact SU(N) at the root, any N_c grade), signs and kinematics follow the Born closure; no CT tree or placement is listed and CT × CT never arises. `SliceRequest.counterterms` selects `"recursion"` (default) or `"sites"` (the O6 oracle).
- **Multi-trace colour state.** CT currents carry closed traces and closed fundamental lines next to their open word (`BlockColourFlow.traces/lines`, `ColorState.traces/lines`, the closing rules of `generation/dag_color.py`). A trace (t₁ … tₙ) is tr(τ^{t₁} … τ^{tₙ}) while a word a₁ … a_k is the product τ^{a_k} … τ^{a₁}, so the trace of a closing, tr(M_left M_right), reads the joined word backwards; with the joined word itself (fixed 2026-10-23) traces of three or more labels had the opposite orientation, which first matters when the four-gluon R2 takes a multi-gluon current inside a block (u u~ > g g g g: R2 off by 4.6·10⁻⁵ at NLC and full colour, found by the O6 the audit asked for; Bose-symmetrised colour classes of the four-gluon R2 isolated the d·d and δδ classes). `tree_colour_source` admits d-symbols (three adjoint legs, or with one colour-singlet leg), d·d, d·f and f·f products sharing one summed index, and Identity·Identity on four adjoint legs. The U(N) contact split of d·d is d·d + (2/N)δδ; the U(1) term is subtracted in the coupling (`_without_u1_terms`, N_c formal with a continuation). New contact splits of `models/compiler_contacts.py`: `trilinear-identity` (gggV: a d or f on the pair with an identity to the remaining leg) and `identity-singlets` (ggVV, ggSS). Only the closure reads these currents; seeds refuse them.
- **U(1) projection of the gluon legs** [V `models/compiler_color_flow.py` `synthesize_u1_projections`]. The tree engine carries a gluon between fermion lines as its U(N) colour-flow current G_U plus the U(1)-subtraction auxiliary α = −tr(G_U)/N, which only fermion vertices produce and absorb. That is exact as long as every other vertex of the gluon is blind to the U(1) part (structure constants). CT stages with a d-symbol or an identity between adjoint legs are not (the four-gluon R2, gggV, ggVV, ggS, ggSS), so the closure's lowered model projects each of their gluon legs onto SU(N):
  - *absorption*: the stage with an input gluon replaced by the auxiliary, i.e. α times the stage with the identity for that leg (colour weight −c, the sign of α);
  - *production*: a gluon output replaced by the auxiliary, minus the trace of the output over N (colour weight −c and the exact 1/3 in the coupling, the numeric 1/N_c of the Fierz auxiliaries, graded like theirs);
  - *identity contact lines*: a d-symbol partial stage of a contact with both inputs replaced is the identity on its adjoint contact line; a colour-singlet clone of that line carries it, read by copies of the d-symbol final stages (d(1, C) = 2C from their two orders).
  - Vanishing terms are not generated: identity outputs (projected out together with their production) and closings of the identity alone. A closing whose left input is replaced sums to zero over the patterns of its right gluon (tr(G_U) + Nα = 0), so closings replace their right input only and the colour engine keeps the pattern whose replaced input has the larger first label.
  - The loop-block colour engine (`generation/dag_color.py`) keeps the complete strings of a colour singlet merged into an adjoint current or a closing as closed lines (the auxiliary, an identity contact line, or a boson of a quark pair entering an inherit vertex, which the word grammar previously dropped silently), with the singlet on the right except for the U(1)-projection singlets, whose two orders are distinct terms. The connection parity of blocks (`loops/v2/blocks.py`) counts only open-word endpoints and no merge of a singlet into a non-fermion current; the auxiliary's sign is the copies' weight there.
  - The projection is all or nothing per model: if a tree vertex of the model couples to the U(1) part of a gluon itself, the invariant tr(G_U) = −Nα fails and nothing is projected; requests whose CT vertices would then take the U(1) part fail closed (`ct_closure.UnprojectedU1Error`). The former per-request demotion to site closure is gone.
- **Two-point insertions** are clone lines [V `models/ct_line_insertions.py`]. A two-point term Π(X, Y) (both UFO legs incoming) sits on a line whose current enters it at its leg of X and leaves at its leg of Y; a diagonal term has Y = X̄, a mixing term two different fields of equal spin and conjugate charges. Each (X, Y, orders of Π, slot) class gets an oriented clone pair: C, read as Ȳ (a copy of its record), and its antiparticle C̄, read as X̄ (one self-conjugate clone for a self-conjugate diagonal class), plus copies of the ordinary vertices of Ȳ with C and of X̄ with C̄ on that leg. Each copy carries the original orders, half of Π's orders and the insertion and slot orders once. The closure reads a clone current as the field it is read as (`CountertermLoweredModel.line_fields`).
  - Every internal line is a current of the recursion. Clone lines therefore cover each internal line exactly once, and the external-leg lines (LSZ) never close, since the clone's id differs from the sink's.
  - Clone propagators are model-supplied: N_C(1, 2) = N_out(1, B) Π(in → A, out → B) N_in(A, 2) / (D_in D_out), with the propagator of the field the current enters by (X for C) and leaves into (Ȳ); C̄ is the reversed line. D is the denominator the engine applies, including the width of its standard propagators, p² − M² + iMW (found by O6 on gg→tt̄(g)); a mixing line has the two fields' own propagators (the photon-Z line p² (p² − M_Z² + iM_Z W_Z)). Each term's scale (coupling × colour number) is an internal model parameter, evaluated exactly at every precision. The composed numerator is expanded into single spinor chains. (The unexpanded product once seemed not to finish; that was the deadlock of Spenso's Rayon workers under the package-scoped Symbolica key, below.) A clone has no mass of its own (its record has M = W = 0): it propagates only through its own propagator, and a massive vector's clone would otherwise be a second Goldstone partner of that vector.
  - Weyl fermions (massless, `is_chiral_eligible`): when every field of a class is a Weyl field and Π is a multiple of p̸, the clones keep the field's propagator and P_Y Π P_X = κ·P with κ derived exactly from the composed Lorentz structures (the same in both directions). κ sits on the copies of C̄; at a `ROOT_LINE` it is a closure scale (`model_value` resolves its payload symbols; found by O6 on gg→uu~g). Otherwise (a chiral or chirality-flipping Π, or a Weyl field mixing with a Dirac field) the class's Weyl fields become Dirac fields of the lowered model (`CountertermLoweredModel.dirac_fields`: their standard propagators are made model-supplied, so their sources, currents and clone lines are Dirac spinors), and the clones are model-supplied lines. The closure's root kernels read Dirac blocks of a Weyl field through the catalogue's chirality-0 kernels. This needed one generic fix of the exact tree runtime: the source of a massless Dirac fermion of a crossed (initial-state) leg vanished (chirality = helicity instead of −helicity for negative energies, `runtime/symbolica_exact._massless_quark_dirac`; it now equals the massive Dirac state at zero mass, as the native `ext_quark_dirac_generic`).
  - **Spinor chains and projectors (WP3.2 EWI-4b).** The propagator reading takes UFO's own `ProjM`/`ProjP` (consistent with `Gamma5`, as at vertices since EWI-4a), and the composite puts Π between the propagators with its receiving leg on the side of the entering one, which reads Π's spinor chain from that end: Π is composed with its chain reversed (`ct_line_insertions._reversed_chain`; a single factor such as loop_sm's p̸ or identity is unchanged, so QCD plans are byte-identical). Before, the reading exchanged the two projectors, which made p̸P_L chains right by cancellation and a lone projector (the chirality-flipping electroweak mass counterterms, `l_vevexMass10/11`) the opposite chirality; O6 on the two-point synthetic model found it (a lone P_L with a complex coupling on the top of gg→tt̄g: recursion and sites differed by 10⁻⁶ relative in the R2; `test_loop_v2_ct_two_point` ids `gg_ttxg` and, for the other clone of a chiral u line, `uxu_emepa`).
  - **Gauge.** The composed propagators are those the engine applies: a standard vector in a model compiled in 't Hooft–Feynman gauge propagates with −i g (the UFO record of a massive vector holds the unitary numerator). A term on a Goldstone the unitary-gauge tree model absorbs is not lowered (no tree vertex holds the Goldstone; a clone would bring its vertices back), and clones are no Goldstones.
  - **Scalar–vector mixing is a vertex insertion** (the Goldstone–vector two-points G⁰–Z, G⁰–A, G^±–W^∓ of the 't Hooft–Feynman-gauge model, WP3.2 EWI-4b). By Lorentz covariance Π^μ = κ p^μ, so the line momentum moves into the copies of the scalar's vertices: their clone leg is a vector leg with the original Lorentz structure times P(μ_k, k), the tree momentum the engine gives that leg (minus the momentum flowing in); Π is read as the tree engine reads every vertex, on the tree momenta of its legs: its scalar leg takes −P(k), its vector leg +P(k), whichever way the current flows (`ct_line_insertions._momentum_ratio`). The reading with the momenta flowing in (+P(k) on the scalar leg, WP3.2 EWI-4b, calibrated against the site oracle while the oracle read the mixings that way too) gave every mixing the wrong sign; WP3.2 EWI-7b corrected both, decided by MadLoop: the UV counterterm component (finite part and single pole) of g t > t t t~'s QED corrections equals MadLoop's Born × CT interference without its R2 amplitudes at three points to ≤ 4.4·10⁻¹⁴ (binary64 and 32 digits), where the neutral mixings G⁰–Z and G⁰–A make 0.3, 3 and 0.45 % of it and the earlier reading missed by twice that (`tests/fixtures/loop/goldstone-mixing.json`, split by `tools/loop_reference/madloop_ct_selection.py`; nightly `test_goldstone_vector_mixings_equal_madloop`). The charged mixings G^±–W^∓ take the same reading by construction; their MadLoop component (g b > t t~ b with a massive b, where they make 150 % of the UV component at point 0) is in the same fixture, but that request is refused at the W ghost vertex [ghA ghWm~ W⁻] of its loops (not closed under conjugation, no conjugate-rule reading; `test_charged_goldstone_vector_mixings_equal_madloop` expects exactly that refusal until the request generates, then compares), and no request pyAmpliCol generates today reaches a charged mixing (g t > t t t~ reaches none: MadLoop's CT amplitudes hold none). The whole virtual of g t > t t t~'s QED corrections equals MadLoop's too (WP3.2 EWI-5, the same nightly test): the bare part equals MadLoop's virtual minus its counterterms and the single pole MadLoop's and the QED Catani operator's, within 4.6·10⁻¹⁴ of the virtual at the three points (32 digits, held to MadLoop's accuracy: 10⁻¹², or its own estimate `virtual_accuracy` where larger, the O1 rule; binary64 loses 3·10⁻¹¹ of the finite part at point 2, MadLoop's own estimate there 1.5·10⁻¹²). Open for WP3.4 (stability estimates, O1): that binary64 cancellation loss, and the 2·10⁻¹⁰ of g g > t t~ e⁻ e⁺ EW at point 0 (`required/gg_ttxemep_ew`, run in double-double), are shown by no stability estimate in these tests; their production binary64 values carry it. The miss reported by EWI-7b (bare single pole −1.07·10⁻⁷ against −3.78·10⁻⁸ at point 0, the virtual's −5.6·10⁻⁸ against Catani's 1.35·10⁻⁸) was the loop segments' reading of the Goldstone vertices, which EWI-5 fixes (`catalogue._CatalogueBuilder.reading_sign`, ew-leptons status below); the remaining R2 difference (−1.83·10⁻⁸ against −1.33·10⁻⁸ at point 0) is MadLoop's: it omits R2_G0tt_b (V_ttG02, loop label [W⁺, b]: in 't Hooft–Feynman gauge every b loop of t̄ t G⁰ holds a G^±, so MG5_aMC's label matching never adds it, as for e⁺e⁻→tt̄h), and our R2 is MadLoop's plus (R2_G0tt_b/R2_G0tt) = 0.869 times MadLoop's R2_G0tt component at every point (`omitted_r2` and `r2_g0tt` in the fixture). Both clones are vector lines (the scalar side's a copy of the vector's antiparticle) with κ N_S N_V(1, 2)/(D_S D_V). The current a vector-side vertex consumes is the vector's own (P_V Π P_S J), the one a scalar-side copy consumes is contracted with its momentum there. A clone of the scalar side is therefore no current of the scalar it is read as: the CT closure refuses a root vertex that would read it (`CloneLine.promoted`: leg 1 coupling to the Goldstone, e.g. a massive b at leg 1; not lowered yet). Tests: `test_goldstone_vector_mixing_is_a_vertex_insertion`; O6 `test_goldstone_vector_mixings_equal_the_site_oracle` (nightly): g t > t t t~ with QED corrections and a massive b, whose trees reach all four mixings, equals the site oracle in every component (≤ 10⁻¹² of each component's largest coefficient; 4·10⁻¹⁵ relative at a second point in binary64; the oracle's coverage rule certified for its electroweak g t t̄ witnesses, test only). The b is massive because the oracle admits a rule's loop species only where a divergent witness has exactly those species while the UFO names the W for its W and G^± loops: with a massless b the only divergent b–W loops of [t̄ t H] and [t̄ t G⁰] also carry a G^±, so the oracle drops `R2_Htt_b` and `R2_G0tt_b` (∝ M_t(1 + M_b²/M_W²), about half the R2 there), which production admits by coupling orders and `particle_veto` alone (§6.2); g g > t t~ e- e+ (G_μ, QED) now lowers its G⁰–Z and G⁰–A mixings, which its trees reach, and stops at B4.
  - Not lowered (the rule is residual: inert in a request none of whose trees can hold it, refused otherwise, §6.2): mixing between fields of non-conjugate charges or of different spins other than a scalar and a vector, terms on absorbed Goldstones, mixing structures that are not a multiple of the line momentum, odd orders of Π, spins other than 0, ½ and 1, and vertices with two legs of a clone's fermion.
  - Engine defects fixed generically on the way: the parity of oriented structure-constant kernels (`compiler_entry`), the U(1) auxiliary of a gluon with a model-supplied propagator (`compiler_color_flow`), fusion keys of contact kernels with different runtime couplings (`compiler_contacts`), and the exact imaginary unit of stage parameters (`generation/stage_parameters.py`; binary64 `1j` cost 200-bit runs 1e-19). The slice evaluates its couplings at 32 digits and rounds them once, with the Born couplings in a scalar program of their own (`LoopSlice.scalars`): their binary64 values no longer depend on which counterterm or pole couplings a request binds, so finite-only parts stay bitwise equal to full ones and the bare loop's Born weight is the same for either counterterm form.
- **Cancelling cells** [V `ct_recursion.lower_model_counterterms`, `uncancelled_rules`, `ct_sites.vanishes_identically`]. A colour or Lorentz cell of a UFO CT vertex that the tree compiler cannot lower does not make its rule unlowerable when it cancels exactly between the rules of that cell (the loop groups of one vertex): the sum over those rules of `M_rule` times their Laurent coefficients vanishes identically once the internal parameters take their exact definitions. In loop_sm these are the Levi-Civita cells of the R2 of the Z with gluons (R2_GGZ, and the axial cell R2_GGGVa of R2_GGGZ): their couplings are proportional to AxialZUp for the u, c, t loop group and to AxialZDown = −AxialZUp for d, s, b, so they cancel over complete quark doublets (the anomaly cancellation of the R2 part; the R2 does not depend on the quark masses). The check is made once, when the model is lowered for a veto (`M_rule` depends only on the rule, the veto and the selected corrections, §6.2): the lowered model leaves such cells out and keeps their coefficients and multiplicities (`CountertermModel.unlowered`, `weights`), and the CT-root sites leave the same cells out. Where such a cell does not cancel (an incomplete doublet; an anomalous model) it is lowered instead (WP3.2 EWI-7b, `ct_recursion._CellSettlement`): the tree compiler lowers UFO `Epsilon` as the dense `models.tensors.LEVI_CIVITA` (ALOHA's components, ε^{0123} = −1; its tree kernels equal compiled ALOHA routines, `test_model_levi_civita_kernels.py`), so the cell's vertex terms join the rows of each of its lowered rules; only an uncancelled cell of another kind makes its rules unlowerable. A cell that cancels with every flavour admitted but not under the request's `particle_veto` (a veto that breaks a doublet: `particle_veto = [t]`, two up-type against three down-type flavours, or `[d, s, b]`; the same cancellation test with the multiplicities of no veto, the vetoed-out rules included) is the user's decision [U2]: refused by default (its rules are residual with a reason naming the cell and the override, and a request reaching one raises the typed `ct_recursion.AnomalousVetoError`), lowered with the explicit override `allow_anomalous_veto` (`SliceRequest`, TOML `generation.allow_anomalous_veto`, CLI `--allow-anomalous-veto`), which the artifact records in its request record and in the generation filters next to `particle_veto` (`allow_anomalous_veto`, `counterterms_anomalous_veto`: rule → cells); without a veto the test is not run. Cells that cancel stay out, so loop_sm's lowered models and plans keep their bytes. The vector (d-symbol) cell of R2_GGGZ is lowered like the gggA R2. So no loop_sm rule is unlowerable, with or without a veto. The site oracle checks the cancellation on its own per block assignment (`SiteClosure.cancelled_cells`) and closes an uncancelled cell with the catalogue's Levi-Civita kernel. O6 on the vector-Z synthetic model of the two-point tests (`test_loop_v2_ct_cancellation.py`): uu~→e⁻e⁺g (g g Z) and uu~→e⁻e⁺gg (g g Z, and g g g Z in blocks and at the root, whose vector cell changes the R2 component) in binary64 and at 200 bits, with the cells cancelled; with the cell lowered, uu~→e⁻e⁺g with an anomalous g g Z coupling and with `particle_veto = [t]` (under the override). The sign is physics' (EW-20, `test_loop_v2_epsilon_sign.py`): u u~ > e⁻e⁺ g g with `particle_veto = [d, s, b]` (under the override), whose up-type quark triangles are anomalous, equals MadLoop `/ d s b` in its bare and R2 parts separately, every Laurent coefficient within 1e-12 of MadLoop's at each of three points on both routes (`tests/fixtures/loop/epsilon-sign.json`; points 1 and 2 in binary64, point 0 at 32 digits: in binary64 its bare double and single poles carry 3.7·10⁻¹² and 1.1·10⁻¹² of pyAmpliCol's own rounding, its double pole 3.0·10⁻¹² from the exact Catani −(α_s/2π)(2C_F + 2C_A)·Born against MadLoop's 8.3·10⁻¹³ — recorded for the stability package; at 32 digits every coefficient is within 8·10⁻¹³ of MadLoop and the double pole within 4·10⁻¹⁴ of Catani), and its R2 restores the gluon Ward identity the bare triangles break (1e-25 of the amplitude in DoubleFloat, the R2 part being 6e-2 to 1.1 of it). The root closure's reading of the R2_GGZ and R2_GGGVa vertices is the general one of "Root closure reading" below.
- **Not lowered** (`CountertermModel.residual`): colour or Lorentz cells the tree compiler cannot lower that do not cancel (Levi-Civita cells are lowered, above); two-point terms the clone lines do not lower (above; Goldstone–vector mixing is lowered since WP3.2 EWI-4b); couplings of left-out cells (or graded by a formal N_c) not linear in their referenced CT scalars, and coefficients that are not finite at the fixed parameters (an infinity in a taken branch). A request admitting such a rule is refused (`ct_recursion.UnloweredCountertermError`, naming the rule and its fields) only when the rule's vertex can enter a tree of the request's CT program; otherwise the rule is inert (§6.2, REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM J): e.g. a Goldstone rule of a unitary-gauge request (§6.2; electroweak requests take 't Hooft–Feynman gauge since WP3.2 EWI-4b, where the Goldstone–vector mixings are lowered and the request's rules on the explicit Goldstones are ordinary rules). There is no site fallback in production. Inert rules can be in no tree of the program and contribute nothing: ghost legs (the tree vertices conserve ghost number, so no current of physical legs carries a ghost), orders above the program's limits, vertices no tree of the request holds (`ct_closure.InsertionReachability`), and rules without an admitted alternative (all of their loop content vetoed). The admitted ones are recorded with their reason in `CountertermRecursion.inert` (the CT program's generation record, `LoopSlice.counterterm_program.recursion`; not persisted in the artifact, §6.2). One-point rules are refused at preparation. No rule is demoted for the U(1) part of a gluon. Chiral and mixing *external-field* factors (§6.5) have no UFO format.
- **Root closure reading** [V `ct_sites.SiteClosure._reading_sign`, `SiteClosure.value`, `native_trees` closure terms; WP3.2 EWI-7b, genericity audit 2 G2-M7]. The tree engine reads every vertex with the rows and tree momenta of its particles' currents and their outgoing colour flows. The root closure (`SiteClosure.vertex_terms`: Born roots `assemble._born_terms`, counterterm roots `ct_closure` ROOT_VERTEX/ROOT_LINE and CT-vertex roots, and the site oracle's sites) gives each kernel leg the row and the momentum of the block of its tree particle (`SiteTerm.kernel_legs`, also for the momenta since this fix; a vertex not closed under conjugation through its conjugate rule), but the momentum flowing in (the tree momentum negated) and the incoming colour. The two readings therefore differ by (−1)^(momentum degree of the kernel) times the transposition sign of the colour (−1 per `f`; `d`, fundamental generators between swapped ends, identities and two-index traces keep it), which every term carries, on every vertex. It is 1 on every QCD vertex (`f` with one momentum; `d`, generators and metrics without; a quark two-point odd in the momentum and diagonal in colour, whose two block momenta are opposite, so it keeps its value) and −1 on every colourless vertex odd in the momenta: W⁺W⁻A, W⁺W⁻Z, Z H G⁰, W G H, W G G⁰, their R2 and UV counterterms, the Goldstone–vector mixings G⁰–Z, G⁰–A and G^±–W^∓ at two-point sites, the Levi-Civita R2_GGZ (a two-index trace with one momentum) and R2_GGGVa (`f` without), and the d·f cells of loop_sm's four-gluon R2 V_R2RGA (which sum to zero: values unchanged). A kernel neither even nor odd in the momenta or a colour without a transposition sign (a trace of three octets, sextet or epsilon colour tensors) is refused with the typed `ct_sites.ReadingSignError` naming the vertex (no loop_sm or G_μ UFO vertex is). The earlier reading (sign 1 except on all-self-conjugate vertices, momenta in UFO-leg order) was wrong at every bosonic root odd in the momenta: z h > t t~ had a 't Hooft–Feynman-gauge Born 20 times its unitary-gauge one, w⁺ h > t b̄ (a W G H root) 50 times, and w⁺w⁻ > u ū (W⁺W⁻V, where rows and momenta were on swapped legs, not a sign) 177 times its time-reversed process. Tests (nightly, 32 digits): `test_born_closure_reads_the_z_h_goldstone_root_in_both_gauges`, `test_root_closure_reads_the_w_goldstone_higgs_root_in_both_gauges_and_leg_orders` (w⁺ h > t b̄ = unitary gauge, and h w⁺ > t b̄ at the same point), `test_born_closure_reads_the_w_w_vector_root_as_the_time_reversed_process`; unit: `test_loop_v2_levi_civita.test_reading_signs_of_root_vertices`. loop_sm plans and lowered models are byte-identical and values bitwise on g g > g g, u u~ > g g, g u > u g g, u u~ > e⁻e⁺g, g g > t t~ g and u u~ > d d~ (binary64, Python and native point inputs; the one exception is one ulp of g g > t t~ g's R2 with native inputs, where the d·f cells, whose sum vanishes, add with other signs). The native tree program stored in artifacts (`export_plan(..., trees=True)`) changes for every one of them: the four-gluon R2's d·f cells at a gluon root (g g > g g: 384 of 1488 CT root terms) and the momentum input of a quark two-point at a quark root (the other block's, the opposite momentum), so loop_sm artifact digests change.
- **O6 and O1.** Closure = sites of the lowered rules (the site oracle with its MadLoop-style witness admission, §6.6, so O6 also checks the plain admission of §6.2 against loop matching), and closure + remaining sites = O1 UV_local and R2, on all 8 controls in binary64 and at 200 bits on gg→gg, uu~→ggg, uu~→dd~g and DY (`test_loop_v2_ct_closure.py`). Slice level, all six components with counterterms from the recursion equal the request with every rule at sites, in binary64 and at 200 bits, on uu~→γgg (gggA), uu~→γγg (ggAA), gg→ggg, gg→uu~g, gg→tt̄g and, with the U(1) projection, uu~→dd~gg (the four-gluon R2 taking the d line's gluon), gg→uu~γg (gggA absorbing the u line's gluon), uu~→γggg and uu~→γγgg (gggA and ggAA feeding the u line), gg→tt̄gg, uu~→tt̄gg and uu~→dd~ss~ (three quark lines). Six legs with four gluons (closed traces of three labels, multi-trace CT currents merging with further gluons): uu~→gggg at the CT level (Born and counterterms, `LoopSlice._point`; physics tier) and in the slice-level set (nightly); gg→gggg generated through `LoopSlice.generate` on both routes in a fresh interpreter, O6 at the CT level, and both routes' R2 against MadLoop's (`tests/fixtures/loop/gg-gggg-r2-component-control.json`, from the existing three-provider export instrumented by `tools/loop_reference/madloop_ct_component.py`; the site closure's R2 is 1.1·10⁻¹² from MadLoop's) in the nightly tier. At the first point of that reference (binary64): R2 recursion −2.625658292295815·10⁻⁴, sites −2.625658292295828·10⁻⁴ (5·10⁻¹⁵), MadLoop −2.625658292293035·10⁻⁴ (1.1·10⁻¹²); UV_local and the field terms agree to 6·10⁻¹⁶, the Born with MadLoop's to 7·10⁻¹⁵. uu~→dd~gg at LC and NLC, and uu~→gggg at NLC (nightly), with the colour declaration at the CT level (physics tier). The slice O1 controls (`test_loop_v2_slice.py`: Born and components, massive line, LC and NLC projections) run on the recursion. Two-point forms beyond loop_sm (`test_loop_v2_ct_two_point.py`, loop_sm plus synthetic rules: photon-Z mixing, chiral and chirality-flipping u terms, d-s mixing; the test model's Z couples to quarks through FFV1 only, since axial loop couplings need the EW sign oracles): lowered as clone lines, and recursion = sites at slice level in binary64 and at 200 bits on uu~→e⁻e⁺, uu~→e⁻e⁺γ and gg→uu~g, with the synthetic rules admitted and changing the counterterms. Without synthetic rules, a lowered model with a Dirac u quark closes to the sites and to O1 on DY, uu~→ggg and uu~→uu~g. The d-s (fermion) mixing line is checked structurally only: a single flavour-changing insertion interferes only with a flavour-changing Born (the W, read through its conjugate rule since WP3.2 EWI-2, is not in that test model).
- **Coloured widths and the transverse tree kernels (decided 2026-10-23).** The public v2 path refuses coloured widths in tree currents (WP2.6 `artifact.require_zero_coloured_widths`, the scheme question below; `docs/user/configuration.md` "Widths"), so there the full-kernel choice serves model-supplied propagators only; the slice level keeps it for both, for the oracle requests at the model's widths. The slice's tree blocks and Born use the transverse Yang–Mills kernels (§3.2 item 2), which drop the p_i·J_i terms of their input gluon currents. A top width breaks the Ward identity of a gluon current with an internal top propagator (fixed-width scheme: S(q)(q̸ − m) ≠ 1), so these kernels are then not the Feynman rules, while the CT program's lowered model has the full kernels. Before the fix, at the model's top width (WT = 1.508), the recursion and the site closure (which closes the CT vertices on the slice's blocks) differed by 3.5·10⁻⁷ of the UV_local single pole of gg→tt̄gg and 5.6·10⁻⁸ for uu~→tt̄gg, and the kernel choice moved the uu~→tt̄gg Born by 4.7·10⁻⁷ and the finite part of the virtual by 3·10⁻⁴ (relative). Now a request whose trees propagate a coloured particle with a width or a model-supplied propagator takes the full kernels in its blocks and Born (§3.2 item 2, `blocks.with_ward_kernels`; decided per request from the DAG currents, since WT is a runtime input of loop_sm). Recursion and sites then agree at the model's top width on gg→tt̄g, gg→tt̄gg and uu~→tt̄gg, in binary64 (≤ 8·10⁻¹⁶) and at 200 bits (`test_slice_counterterms_from_recursion_equal_sites`). Cost on gg→tt̄gg (sites route, binary64, one process on a shared host): generation 48.4 s against 45.8 s (3.2 s for the variant's compile), peak 0.73 against 0.69 GB, and the same per-point tree rows (0.197 against 0.201 s); massless QCD requests are bitwise unchanged. Still open, as a scheme question (complex masses in loops and trees, WP3.2): the loop lines carry no widths (`assemble._mass_values`) while the trees do, so with a coloured width the double pole is not exactly −(α_s/2π) Σ C_i × Born (1.8·10⁻⁵ off at WT = 1.508, against 10⁻¹⁵ at WT = 0, the scheme of every provider reference in `tests/fixtures/loop/required`).
- **Generation cost** of the closure route, Python generic DAG (2026-10-23, host load 70–250 on 384 cores): gg→ggg CT program 18 s + bind 33 s, 1.0 GB; gg→gggg 136 s + 361 s, 11.8 GB peak (before two generic fixes: 236 s + 1540 s); gg→tt̄gg 30 s + 73 s, 2.6 GB, every rule in the recursion (before the U(1) projection 23 s + 61 s, 2.2 GB, with its two four-gluon R2 rules at sites); uu~→dd~gg 1.8 s + 8.8 s, 0.35 GB. The fixes: stage specialisation inlines only the model functions an expression reaches (`stage_parameters._specialize_stage_symbolica_functions`), and current-evaluation reuse multiplies by ±1, ±i without rational arithmetic (`dag_equivalence`). At seven legs (2026-10-23; CT program only, one process, `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1`, host load 130–150): uu~→ggggg 238 s, 336 k currents and 2.74 M interactions, 186 CT-root sites; gg→ggggg 3,210 s, 3.07 M currents and 34.8 M interactions, 847 CT-root sites, 30 GB after the build. Both have no residual rule. The peaks of the whole runs, with the lowering (24 s) and the plain blocks (48 s and 561 s, 0.55 M and 7.1 M interactions) included, are 4.9 GB and 54.6 GB, as the DAG's equivalence passes double the memory transiently (scripts and logs in `workspace/tmp/ct-recursion/s4/`). The root-closure terms are cheap: gg→gggg 76,360 terms in 13 s and uu~→ggggg 20,980 terms in 2.4 s, without tree rows; at gg→ggggg they (the bind) were not measured, so its seven-leg figure is the CT program alone. These seven-leg runs lowered the model before building the blocks (`s4/ctprog7.py`); production generation through `LoopSlice.generate` builds the blocks first, which deadlocked at gg→gggg until the Spenso fix below. Since the request-scoped lowering and the program without the set of all legs but leg 1, g g > g g g g generates on the recursion route in 257–275 s against the sites' 243–245 s, with the same 4.2 GB peak (generation split, 257 s run: the slice's own block rows 134 s, its blocks 33 s, the CT program 39 s of which its DAG 36 s and the lowering 3 s, the bind 7 s). These seven-leg figures predate both changes. Re-measured with both and the plain admission of §6.2 (ct-simplify, 2026-10-23; production `CountertermProgram.build` with the request-scoped lowering, one process, `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1`, host load 50–70; `workspace/tmp/ct-simplify/ctprog7.py`): g g > g g g g g CT program 802 s (was 3,210 s), 1.03 M currents and 9.75 M interactions (was 3.07 M and 34.8 M), 12.4 GB after the build (was 30 GB), 847 CT-root sites, no unlowerable rule, 57 vertex rules in the lowered model; the bind (root-closure terms, measured for the first time) 74.5 s for 648,000 terms; the whole run with the plain blocks (571 s) 1,457 s at a 19.4 GB peak (was 3,801 s and 54.6 GB). Dropping the loop matching contributes nothing measurable to this: the witness table of the old admission takes 0.18 s at g g > g g g g g (707 witnesses, on 3 s of families); the reduction comes from the request-scoped lowering and the program without the set of all legs but leg 1. This is within the budget of REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM H (O(hours), < 150 GB) but not within the earlier 7-leg gate (< 10 min, < 4 GB), which the coordinator moved to WP3.1 (decision D3). The program's Python tree rows (`TreeRows`, 340 s and most of the 11.8 GB of the bind at gg→gggg) serve only the per-point Python path: they are compiled on the first point (`CountertermProgram.rows`), so generation no longer builds them (`test_generation_leaves_the_program_rows_to_the_first_point`). Their stage evaluators are interpreted (`TreeRows(jit=False)`): Symbolica's JIT compile on the first point took 69 s of a 115 s CT-level point at gg→ggg and over an hour at gg→gggg (a coordinator-stopped run), while one point costs the same interpreted (0.4–0.6 s at gg→ggg). CT-level points (Born and counterterms, `LoopSlice._point`, binary64, the first two points of `tests/fixtures/loop/required/gg_gggg.json`, both routes in parallel processes on the same tree, 2026-10-23, host load 150–210; `workspace/tmp/ct-recursion/perf2/g6{r,s}{2,3}.log`): g g > g g g g, recursion against sites, generation 268 / 275 s against 245 / 243 s, first point (the program's row build included) 1,267 / 1,250 s against 1,286 / 1,256 s, generation and first point together 1,535 / 1,525 s against 1,531 / 1,500 s (1.003 / 1.017 times), second point 229 s against 422 s (0.54 times), peak 6.96 GB against 5.52 GB; after two points 1,754 s against 1,921 s. Both first points carry a common one-time cost of about 1,000 s. The CT values agree to 5·10⁻¹⁵ (R2 at the first point, −2.625658292295815·10⁻⁴ against −2.625658292295828·10⁻⁴) and 4·10⁻¹⁴ (second point). Before the two changes: recursion 333 s + 1,549 s (13.7 GB) against the sites' 169 s + 1,256 s (5.4 GB). gg→ggg under cProfile: generation 40 s against 28 s, points 31 s and 13 s against 40 s and 31 s. The earlier tree-route program (Born × CT squared-order partials, `CountertermRecursion`, Rust recurrence lane) stays as a test of the lowering: uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g in 70 s / 0.73 GB, gg→ggggg in 77 s / 0.76 GB (2026-10-22); its 200-bit uu~→dd~g stays a strict xfail (binary64 U(1) weight of the tree runtime, WP2.4), which the slice's exact weights avoid.
- **Generation cost against the site oracle (ct-perf, 2026-10-24; the user's "fix before moving on").** Measured with `LoopSlice.generate` on binary64 loop_sm requests (`MB = 0`, QCD Born order n − 2, loop order n). Stage timers and RSS sampling come from `workspace/tmp/ct-perf/measure.py`. Each run is one process pinned to an idle core without an SMT sibling, with `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` and `PYTHONHASHSEED=7`, at host load 110–135. Values are medians of two runs, all in one session (`campaign5.sh`). The columns are:
  - **sites:** the site oracle on `work/v2-ct-perf`;
  - **slice:** the recursion on the slice tip `c51d0c10`;
  - **cold / warm:** the recursion on `work/v2-ct-perf` `d5c1d89c`, with an empty or a filled lowered-model cache;
  - **A4:** the measured A4 projection. It is `d5c1d89c` merged with `work/v2-native-dag` `9f8aa885` (A3), on that branch's build `loop-native-native-dag-79674311`, with a harness patch (`workspace/tmp/ct-perf/a4proj.py`, measurement only). The patch makes `CountertermBlocks.build` call A3's `dag_input.native_counterterm_blocks` and `u1_leaking_structures` its native scan, and it decodes the Python DAG only when something reads it; generation never does.

  The CT stages are the lowering, the program and the root closure for the recursion, and the sites with their site terms for the oracle. Times are in seconds:

  | control | CT stages: sites / slice / cold / warm / A4 | generation: sites / slice / cold / warm / A4 | peak RSS (GB): sites / slice / warm / A4 |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | g g > g g g g | 11.6 / 39.6 / 25.0 / 23.4 / **9.3** | 281 / 321 / 289 / 287 / **260** | 4.21 / 4.27 / 4.21 / **4.21** |
  | u u~ > g g g g | 1.21 / 10.7 / 7.1 / 4.7 / 2.4 | 38.9 / 48.8 / 45.0 / 42.5 / 39.7 | 0.58 / 0.69 / 0.66 / 0.64 |
  | g g > t t~ g g | 1.08 / 8.3 / 6.3 / 4.0 / 2.7 | 52.7 / 62.6 / 58.6 / 55.9 / 54.7 | 0.89 / 0.90 / 0.89 / 0.89 |

  Stage split of the warm recursion on `dbe2243b` (`campaign6.sh`, u u~ > g g g g and g g > t t~ g g only):
  - lowering 0.33 and 0.30 s;
  - program 3.8 and 2.7 s;
  - bind 0.13 and 0.54 s.

  Stage split of the A4 projection:
  - g g > g g g g: lowering 0.7–1.2, program 1.7 (input tables 0.9, native build 0.45, block decode 0.3), bind 6.3;
  - u u~ > g g g g (`dbe2243b`): 0.42 / 1.49 (0.88 / 0.10 / 0.33) / 0.13;
  - g g > t t~ g g (`dbe2243b`): 0.30 / 1.52 (0.87 / 0.39 / 0.38) / 0.54.

  Whole-generation times scatter by about ±10 % (the two sites runs at g g > g g g g: 297 and 266 s), so the CT stage sums are the comparison.

  The changes, all output-identical:
  1. **Loop-request DAG compile** (`generation/dag_compiler.py`, loop-request mode):
     - the eager compiler's memoization of coupling-order checks, colour-flow combinations and per-vertex model queries (pure functions of their keys, repeated for every slot);
     - each input pair's vertex list filtered once;
     - a result's validated `CurrentIndex` memoized by its construction arguments;
     - colour-weight tuples shared between interactions.
  2. **Evaluation-reuse pass** (`dag_equivalence`):
     - the coupling provenance is memoized;
     - an interaction whose group and factor are bitwise unchanged is kept;
     - the unit factor skips the general product;
     - factor tuples are shared, and nodes are built without `dataclasses.replace`.
  3. **The bind** shares the Born closure's colour cache (`SiteClosure.share_colours`).
  4. **Content-addressed lowering cache** (`models.loading.compile_counterterm_model_ir`). The `compile_ufo_model_ir` of the lowered payload is 2.5–3.0 s of the lowering: 629 oriented kernels and their Symbolica proofs. The payload is the same for every process of one request scope; at six and seven legs the scope keeps the same 57 vertex rules. The compile is now cached like a compiled model, in the model cache.
     - Key: the payload in its own key order, the kernel variant, the projected vertices, and `_compiler_code_identity`, which is `compiler_fingerprint` without the package version, whose source-checkout check alone costs 0.2 s.
     - The IR round-trips exactly through `CompiledModelIR.to_dict` / `from_dict`. An unreadable entry is recompiled.
     - The first generation of a scope pays the compile, as the base model's compile is paid once per model.
     - Test: `test_lowered_model_compile_is_cached_by_content`.
  5. **Symbol registry** (`_internal/physics/symbols`): the pure safe-name maps are memoized. A model compile makes about 10⁵ such lookups.

  Items 1 and 2 also speed up the slice's own block DAG on both routes: 23 → 14 s at g g > g g g g.

  Output identity:
