  **Residual (for the coordinator; complete = false).**
  - **Parity on generation plus export:**
    - g g > g g g g reaches parity in time (122.5 against 122.1 s, 1.004×, within the noise) and almost in memory (1.857 against 1.810 GB, +2.6 %).
    - u u~ > g g g g does not (1.18× time, 1.29× peak), nor g g > t t~ g g (1.12× and 1.14×).
  - **`LoopSlice.generate` alone:** 1.175×, 1.088× and 1.135×, with peaks 1.19×, 1.10× and 1.23×. The oracle's closure now sits in its export (1.9, 1.8 and 19.8 s), the recursion's bind does not.
  - **What remains is two Python stages of the CT program, which the Rust phases must remove:**
    - **A4** (the native `CountertermBlocks.build`): the DAG compile at generation, 3.79 / 2.44 / 13.57 s, and the Python CT DAG kept to the export, most of the generation-peak excess (+68 / +39 / +162 MB).
    - **A5** (native emission of the CT program's tree program): `native_trees._CountertermTrees.of` in the export, i.e. the Python `TreeRows` (runtime schema) of the CT DAG and its interaction program, 4.04 / 4.73 / 11.21 s. The export-peak excess (+147 / +73 / +47 MB) is the retained Python CT DAG (A4) plus these (A5).
    - A4 alone leaves the A5 stage in place, and the export would then decode the native DAG for it (A3's `NativeDag` decodes on read). The measured stages leave a gap with A4 alone of 4.61 − 3.79 = 0.8 s at u u~ > g g g g and 3.36 − 2.44 = 0.9 s at g g > t t~ g g, plus A4's own build and decode, while the A5 stage is 4.0 and 4.7 s there: parity at those controls needs A5 as well as A4. The statement above that A5 does not affect generation holds for `LoopSlice.generate` only.
  - **Recursion-only Python costs that stay after both:**
    - the warm lowering, 0.29–0.50 s;
    - the bind, 0.13 / 0.56 / 5.65 s: the closure's colour reductions (`SiteClosure._closed` → `colour.reduce_network`), 2,290 distinct ones at g g > g g g g;
    - the closure program, 0.22 / 0.25 / 4.26 s, which is less than the oracle's 1.91 / 1.83 / 19.81 s.
  - **Not deferred: the bind, and the DAG to first use** (as G-int did for `site_terms`).
    - The export reads `program.closure.terms` and the CT DAG, so deferring would move the same work from `LoopSlice.generate` into the export and leave the artifact build unchanged.
    - It would also move refusals out of generation. `SiteClosure.vertex_terms` raises `ValueError` or `NotImplementedError` for a vertex it cannot close; in the export, `generate_process`'s `except NotImplementedError` (a plan without native trees) would absorb that.
    - The DAG is needed at generation for the CT-root sites (entries of the kernel catalogue) and for the U(1) leakage refusal (`UnprojectedU1Error`).
    - `InsertionReachability`, the inert record and `UnloweredCountertermError` need no DAG and stay where they are.
- **Spenso's threads and the package-scoped Symbolica key (fixed 2026-10-23).** `LoopSlice.generate` of g g > g g g g deadlocked in the CT lowering (`ct_line_insertions._line_factor` → `custom_propagator_components` → `TensorNetwork.execute`, no CPU, every thread in a futex wait). Cause: pyAmpliCol's library key unlocks Symbolica only for frames of the package (`symbolica.set_library_key`, `has_library_unlock_frame`); Spenso's Rayon workers carry no such frame (Symbolica's own pools carry the caller's unlock with the crate-private `InheritedLibraryUnlock`), so the first `LicenseManager::check` of each worker asks `has_library_unlock_frame`, which waits for the GIL in `Python::try_attach` while the calling thread holds it and waits for the workers. It happens whenever Spenso parallelises a network before any license check has run on a Python thread, even with a valid personal key in `SYMBOLICA_LICENSE` (that key is only validated by the first such check; test processes always make one, so the suites never saw it). The same deadlock was the earlier "unexpanded clone numerator does not finish". Fix (`pyamplicol.licensing.ensure_spenso_thread_policy`): once per process, before its first tensor network (called by the model-compilation Symbolica namespaces `compiler_symbolica._ensure_symbolica` and `tensors._ensure_symbolica`; the import itself checks no license, `test_public_import_laziness`; the built-in SM lowering builds only small networks, which Spenso's workload heuristic keeps serial, and its sources are digested into the packaged built-in model, so it is left alone), the package asks `is_licensed()` on a thread without frames of the package (which also validates a personal key) and sets Spenso's symbolic parallelism to serial without a thread-wide key. Tests: `test_symbolica_thread_license.py` (the probe's frames; the import leaves Spenso alone and the first namespace sets it serial, in a subprocess without a personal key) and, end to end, `test_six_gluon_generation_completes_without_a_personal_key` (nightly: g g > g g g g on both routes without `SYMBOLICA_LICENSE`, asserting the serial policy; its script makes no Symbolica call outside the package, since without a key such a call takes the user's one restricted Symbolica thread, which the native loop module's own Symbolica then cannot start: an abort, a separate limitation of unlicensed use). Serial costs nothing measurable here (g g > g g g g generation 322 s serial against 341 s parallel). Upstream (prepared, not yet filed; `workspace/reports/engine-v2/upstream/symbolica-library-unlock/`): a reproducer whose only use of pyAmpliCol is the import that registers its key (`repro_deadlock.py`: a Dirac chain network executed in parallel from the package's globals hangs, serial finishes; it also hangs with a personal key), the focused Symbolica fix `f39c76f` on 9cdc2949 (`has_library_unlock_frame` returns false without attaching on a thread without a Python thread state; branch `fix/library-unlock-frame-no-attach` in `workspace/worktrees/symbolica-unlock-no-attach`), verified on a rebuilt community wheel (`builds/community-unlock-fix-python`: no hang; with a personal key the parallel run completes, with the library key alone it aborts at once on Symbolica's restricted-thread rule), and the issue text (`ISSUE.txt`) with the two follow-ups: public `InheritedLibraryUnlock` so that Spenso carries the caller's unlock into its Rayon jobs, and Spenso's `Auto` resolving to serial under a library unlock alone. Filing the issue and PR (draft) is left to the coordinator. pyAmpliCol's serial policy stays until both land.
- **Production form: the closure (decision for WP3.1, 2026-10-23).** The tree-route program (form 1: complete tree programs of the lowered model, Born × CT per squared order) cannot carry the multi-trace colour state. Its colour is that of the tree runtime's sectors (`ProcessLCSectorKind` Singlet, OpenLines, SingleTrace in the Rust recurrence; single-trace and open-line words in the Python lanes) and of their full-colour contraction, so a CT amplitude with a closed trace next to its open word (the δδ part of the four-gluon R2; a d-symbol or identity vertex taking the U(1)-projected gluon of a quark line) has no sector to land in. In form 1 the four-gluon R2 and the g g g V R2 therefore stay residual (`test_lowered_couplings_are_exact_model_expressions`: the only reasons left are multi-trace colours), and every QCD request with four or more gluon lines (gg→gggg, gg→ggggg, uu~→ggggg, gg→tt̄gg, …) would be refused by a residual-free production rule. Lifting that needs new multi-trace sector kinds, their replay certificates and their colour overlaps in the recurrence's full-colour contraction: a tree-engine feature (L), not a CT one. The closure (form 2) has no residual rule for loop_sm, is graded by N_c for LC/NLC, shares the Born's root closure and kernels, and is the slice's default. **Form 2 is the production form**; WP3.1 should build its CT program natively as loop-request blocks of the lowered model (the T2 strategy, with closed traces and closed lines in the colour state of the CT = 1 currents, `BlockColourFlow.traces/lines` and `generation/dag_color.py` being the reference) plus the native root closure of I4, and keep form 1 as a test of the lowering. Under REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM H the Python DAG can generate that program at seven legs on WP3.1's T1 path (4 min at uu~→ggggg, 54 min and 55 GB peak at gg→ggggg for the program, its root-closure terms unmeasured, "Generation cost" above; the earlier "> 18.5 min and > 16 GB" of the WP3.1 plan §2.4(c) was a run killed at its 1,150 s bound), so T2 and the native colour port below are an optimisation of generation, not a precondition of residual-free counterterms at seven legs.
- **What a native builder of the CT program must carry (WP3.1 I5R/T2; coordinator decision D3, 2026-10-23).** The CT program is an ordinary loop-request program of the lowered model, so no CT-specific vertex code is needed; a native builder must reproduce the items below, each defined by its Python reference. Oracles, in this order: the block set of `ct_closure.CountertermBlocks.build` (keys with field, orders, word, chirality and auxiliary kind, plus the colour state and `Block.wick_sign`) for gg→ggg, uu~→dd~gg, gg→uu~γg, uu~→γggg, gg→tt̄gg and the two-point synthetic model; then O6 against the site closure (`test_loop_v2_ct_closure.py`, `test_loop_v2_ct_two_point.py`, `test_loop_v2_ct_cancellation.py`).
  1. *Program.* Reachability: every leg set without leg 1, except the set of all legs but leg 1 (`ct_closure.RootReachability`): no root term reads it (a root attaches at least two currents; a two-point insertion next to leg 1 is on an external line). Leaving it out removes 80 % of the program's interactions at gg→ggg (110.8 k → 21.7 k) and 18 % at uu~→ggggg (2.24 M interactions, 181 s, 4.0 GB, measured before). The sets of one leg fewer then feed no interaction, so the runtime schema gives them their propagated value anyway (`propagated_currents`, the hunk of WP3.1 I1a on `work/v2-generation-scale`, byte-identical; `ct_closure.program_propagated_currents`; the oracle `tests/unit/loop_v2_stage_rows.stage_rows` takes the same option). Order limits: `CountertermRecursion.coupling_order_limits` (physical orders bounded by the Born and loop orders, `PYAMPLICOL_CT_INSERTION` ≤ 2; slot orders unlisted, at most one per current, `ct_closure._slot`). The lowered model's full-kernel variant (`model_tree_kernel_variant`). Couplings are derived model couplings; the rule multiplicities and slot switches are integer runtime parameters (`CountertermRecursion.parameter_values`). The non-vanishing assumptions under which `CoefficientLowering` resolved `cond` and `reglog` (`CountertermModel.assumptions`) are checked against the runtime parameters before an evaluation (`CountertermModel.check_parameters`): parameters that make one vanish (a mass set to zero, say) are refused, never evaluated. Status (ct-assume-fix, 2026-10-23): a helper argument free of model parameters (numbers, builtin constants and functions: loop_qcd_qed_sm's `reglog(2π)`, whose assumption `2*𝜋` check_parameters could not decide, so every recursion-route evaluation of that model failed) is decided when lowering (`ct_recursion.decide_constant`: numbers exactly, other constants numerically at 40 and 80 digits, undecidable ones refused) and is never an assumption; an assumption keeps only its parameter-dependent factors, the untaken branch of a `cond` is not resolved, and `check_parameters` decides every assumption the same way, refusing vanishing, singular and undecidable values (`test_constant_factors_are_decided_not_assumed`). *Finite-only mode* (REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM B) must skip every current and interaction that feeds only pole slots, not only zero their switches: a CT = 1 current carries at most one slot order, so the currents of the pole slots (UV_local ε⁻¹ and ε⁻²; 18 % of the interactions at gg→ggg for ε⁻¹) and everything that reaches only them are left out of a finite-only evaluation, by a per-slot reachability mask of the one program or by a finite-only program in the same artifact. The slot switches stay the API's switch (the four-valued pole selection maps onto `parameter_values(poles=has_poles(selection))`, §12.4 "Counterterm slots"); the Python CT program (the per-point Python path) only zeroes them and skips the closure terms of the pole slots. Measured on that path (gg→ggg, recursion route, binary64, two CT-level points): 9.1 s with poles against 5.2 s finite-only; the program's rows, pole-slot currents included, take 0.2 s of either (2 %; the ε⁻¹ currents are 17 % of the program's interactions), the closure terms 4.1 s against 2.65 s, the coupling program 1 ms. Skipping the pole-slot currents is therefore worth it only natively. Owner: WP3.1 (open item of WP2.2). **As built (generation-scale-2, I5 proper, TREE_SCHEMA 4):** the counterterm program's interaction program records its slot table and per-slot reachability masks, the record A5 emits natively (`dag/program.rs`, rule 3). `counterterm_trees.slots` lists per CT slot its pseudo-order, component, ε power and N_c grade (`native_trees.counterterm_slots`, `CountertermRecursion.slots` order); `interactions.masks` lists slot sets (`masks[0]` every slot, `masks[1 + s]` slot s alone, `slot_mask_table`) and each step's `accumulator_masks` the set its result current reaches (`slot_masks`: a current with one insertion carries its slot's order and reaches that slot only; a CT = 0 current feeds every slot; two slots on one current are refused). The native runtime derives the finite-only skip per slot set (`loop_v2::trees::load_interactions`: an interaction or output whose current reaches no slot of ε power 0 is skipped), exactly TREE_SCHEMA 3's per-step pole lists: converting the new record back to those lists reproduces the stored seven-leg P-ct digests (u u~ > g g g g g `7aa25d0a…`, g g > g g g g g `28213864…`, both lanes; S1″ unchanged; new P-ct `36d23c48…` and `276d98d5…`). Reachability through the CT = 0 currents is every slot (they are shared by every insertion); a finer per-current reachability needs the root closure's reads, which A6 brings natively. Measured saving (`poles="none"` against `"all"`, B = 128, one thread on an idle core, frozen G1 streams, finite parts bitwise equal), stage-isolated (the evidence audit's nine repetitions; the totals of three repetitions spread by ±3-5 % and are no measure of it): the native trees stage 122.7 → 102.4 ms/pt at g g > g g g g (32,720 of 240,120 CT interactions skipped) and 9.51 → 7.89 ms/pt at u u~ > g g g g (10,432 of 70,968), i.e. −5.0 % and −2.0 % of the total per point (total medians −4.9 % and −2.2 %); reduction and dressing do not change. The skipped set is the one TREE_SCHEMA 3's per-step pole lists already skipped: I5 proper re-encodes the record, it adds no new skip.
  2. *Colour state of a current* (`generation/dag_color.ColorState`, rules in `ColorEngine._loop_block_flows`). It is the open word plus two sorted sets: closed traces (adjoint labels, rotated to the smallest; (t₁ … tₙ) is tr(τ^{t₁} … τ^{tₙ}), so a closing's trace is its joined word read backwards) and closed lines (strings from a fundamental to an antifundamental label). A merge unites its inputs' traces and lines. A colour-singlet input of a merge into an adjoint current or of a closing (`_loop_block_absorbing_sides`: the U(1)-subtraction auxiliary, an identity contact line, a boson of a quark pair) adds its complete strings as closed lines (`_loop_block_split_singlet_strings`). The identity alone is dropped, and the trace of one gluon current without a quark line vanishes. A colour-identity vertex closing two adjoint currents into a singlet turns the joined word into a closed trace, or into closed lines when it holds fundamental labels. Input orders (`_loop_block_trace_orientation_allowed`): a closing keeps the smaller first label on the left; an absorbed singlet stands on the right; U(1)-projection singlets keep both orders, and a closing that takes the auxiliary keeps the pattern whose replaced input has the larger first label.
  3. *Colour rules of the lowered vertices* (`models/ct_color_flow.tree_colour_source`): d-symbols (on three adjoint legs, alone or with one colour-singlet leg), d·d, d·f and f·f products over one summed index, and Identity·Identity on four adjoint legs. The U(N) contact split of d·d is d·d + (2/N)δδ, with the U(1) term subtracted in the coupling (`_without_u1_terms`). Contact splits `trilinear-identity` and `identity-singlets` (`models/compiler_contacts.py`). N_c-graded couplings (`split_color_grades`) and whole-rule continuation rows (`whole_rule_rows`).
  4. *U(1) projection* (`models/compiler_color_flow.synthesize_u1_projections`, vertices suffixed `::u1-projection`). Absorption copies have colour weight −c. Production copies have −c and the exact 1/3 in the coupling. Identity contact lines are colour-singlet clones of the adjoint contact line, read by copies of the d-symbol final stages. Closings replace their right input only. The projection is all or nothing per model (`ct_closure.u1_projected`); otherwise a leaking request fails closed (`UnprojectedU1Error`).
  5. *Signs* (`loops/v2/blocks._connection_parities`). The connection parity counts only open-word endpoints, and no merge of a singlet into a non-fermion current. The auxiliary's sign is the projection copies' weight.
  6. *Clone lines* (`models/ct_line_insertions.lower_line_insertions`). Each (X, Y, orders of Π, slot) class has an oriented clone pair with the model-supplied propagator N_out Π N_in/(D_in D_out) (engine widths in D) and records with M = W = 0. The vertex copies carry half of Π's orders and the insertion and slot orders once. A clone current's block key carries the field it is read as (`CountertermLoweredModel.line_fields`; `CountertermBlocks.clones`). The Weyl factor κ sits on the copies of C̄ (`line_factors`) and is a closure scale at a `ROOT_LINE`. Dirac-forced Weyl fields (`dirac_fields`) have model-supplied standard propagators, so their sources, currents and clone lines are Dirac spinors; the root reads them through chirality-0 kernels.
  7. *Root closure* (`CountertermClosure.build`, the CT part of I4). The terms are `ROOT_VERTEX`; `ROOT_LINE`, with the other half of Π's orders and κ; `ROOT_COUNTERTERM`, on the CT-root sites (`CountertermClosure.root_sites`: the partitions with leg 1 as one block, the rule's fields on CT = 0 currents, the loop orders, multiplicity `M_rule` from `CountertermRecursion.admission`; Laurent powers per rule `_powers`); and whole-rule continuation terms (`assemble._whole_rule_terms`). Each term has an exact colour at any N_c grade. The lowered model's cancelled cells are skipped (`CountertermModel.unlowered`). A request admitting an unlowerable rule whose vertex can enter one of its trees is refused (`CountertermRecursion.residual`, `UnloweredCountertermError`); inert rules (`CountertermRecursion.inert`) are not listed.

  8. *Admission and veto* (§6.2). `admitted_alternatives(rule, particle_veto, corrections, species_orders)` per rule (the model's perturbative species facts, no loop routing); the vetoed particles are the tree engine's `ignored_particle_ids` (both charge states, `blocks.vetoed_particle_ids`) of the program's DAG and are excluded from the lowered model's vertices.

  On the T1 path (the Python loop-request DAG lowered to the eager plan, WP3.1 §3.5) items 2–6 stay in Python generation: block colour states feed only the root closure's exact colour tensors, and the native evaluation needs the kinematic recursion with each interaction's scalar colour weight. The eager lane already evaluates the lowered model (custom clone propagators, derived couplings with runtime multiplicities and switches): the form-1 O6 tests run both lanes (`test_recursion_equals_site_closure[*-eager]`). T1 therefore carries form 2 without the native colour port, at the cost of the Python CT program ("Generation cost" above). Per point, the program costs about six times its CT = 0 part (gg→ggg: 21.7 k interactions, of which CT = 0 3.6 k, R2 10.7 k with its clone lines, UV_local ε⁰ and ε⁻¹ 3.7 k each; with the set of all legs but leg 1 it was 110.8 k). Its currents per slot are separate, so finite-only evaluation skips the currents that feed only pole slots (UV_local ε⁻¹: 17 % of the interactions at gg→ggg; item 1); a zero slot switch alone, as in the Python CT program, still computes them.

  **Native builder (WP3.1 Rust migration A3, `work/v2-native-dag`; switched on in A4, below).** `loops/v2/dag_input.native_counterterm_blocks(recursion, process, ignored_particle_ids=...)` is the twin of `CountertermBlocks.build`: the Rust loop-request builder (`rusticol-core` `loop_v2::dag`) over the CT-lowered model's tables, with `RootReachability`, the program's order limits and the vetoed particles (items 1 and 8), carries items 2-6 through the model tables and the loop-block colour engine (multi-trace states, U(1)-projection copies and closings, connection signs, clone-line blocks keyed by `line_fields`, Dirac-forced fields); the DAG scan of the U(1) leakage check runs natively (`ct_closure._u1_leaking` asks the `NativeDag` handle, `NativeDag.u1_leaking`). The CT program's closed colour components make currents share evaluation contracts, so the builder carries the value-equivalence derivation of `assign_recursive_current_evaluation_reuse` in the oracle's bit-exact binary64 arithmetic (`loop_v2/dag/exact.rs`: CPython's `math.fsum` and complex division, exact rationals where Python uses `Fraction`; term vectors in Python's key order). The inert rule stays a Python generation-side record (§6.2). Oracles (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_native_dag.py`): the derivation value by value against `_derive_current_value_equivalences` on A0's electroweak fixture, on copies scaled by complex, non-unit and inexact factors, and on guard copies in which each fail-closed guard of the oracle decides one class (the exactness proof of a projective factor, `fsum`, the round-trip check of the lookup key, the coefficient-by-coefficient scaling check; the class moves when the oracle drops that guard); the whole CT `GenericDAG`, its flat-record digest, blocks and clone lines equal on g g > g g g (default tier), on g g > u u~, u u~ > d d~ g(g), g g > u u~ γ g, u u~ > γ g g g, g g > t t~ g g, a top veto, u d~ > e+ ν g, e+ e- > e+ e- (no coloured leg: the engine's colour-sector branch) and the two-point synthetic model (physics tier, the U(1) scan with the projection gate off), and at six legs (nightly; there a child without a Symbolica key also runs `V2Dag.build`, `V2Dag.reuse` on the scaled and guard copies, whose non-unit factors take the exact-rational path, and `V2Dag.u1_leaking`, equal to the oracle). Seven legs (stage-isolated S1″, `tools/loop_v2_dag.py s1pp --blocks native`): u u~ > g g g g g 248,938 currents and 2,105,180 interactions, S1″ `8eeca050…` in both lanes, CT program 141 s (Python) against 5.4 s (native), process peaks 2.79 GB against 0.78 GB; g g > g g g g g 1,027,618 currents and 9,749,220 interactions, S1″ `ae1a90e9…`, CT program 631 s (Python) against 21.0 s (cold; 22.1 s warm), process peaks 12.0 GB against 2.99 GB, the Born included (records `workspace/tmp/native-dag/oracle/a3/`, native lane re-run on the immutable build `loop-native-native-dag-79674311`: the same digests and counts, 5.8 s and 21.8 s). Below six legs the native stage is slower than the Python one (rule 8, measured 2026-10-24): its Θ(model) input tables (`dag_input.dag_input`, `_vertex_row`) query every vertex and chirality triple of the lowered model, and `CompiledUFOModel.allowed_quantum_flows` runs a symbolic Weyl-projection check on each (`_weyl_projection_is_nonzero`, cached per model instance, which `native_counterterm_blocks` builds afresh), where the Python recursion pays it only on the vertices it reaches. u u~ > a a g: CT program 4.98 s native against 0.13 s Python (1,559 vertex rows; under cProfile 10.8 s of the native 11.4 s are the tables, 10.2 s of them the projection check); g g > g g g 2.7 s against 1.4 s (1.55 s against 1.45 s in an audit run); the gain starts at six legs (g g > g g g g 28.2 s against 2.5 s). Before the A4 switch the tables must be cached per lowered model, restricted to the request's reachable species, or built in Rust. Done for the projection check (ct-perf third increment above): `native_counterterm_blocks` builds over `CountertermLoweredModel.tree_model()`, whose Weyl-projection support comes from the lowering cache; the tables take 0.06–0.07 s at six legs. The production call sites switch in A4, after G-int.

  **Switch (WP3.1 Rust migration A4, `work/v2-native-dag`; `dag_input.NATIVE_DAG = True`).** The one flag of the stage (RUST-MIGRATION rule 1) puts the native builder in production: the loop-block DAG of `build_tree_blocks` (`dag_input.loop_request_blocks`, the switch glue; one call line in `build_tree_blocks`) and the CT program of `CountertermBlocks.build` (`dag_input.native_counterterm_blocks`) are built in Rust, and so are their queries: the Ward-kernel and coloured-width scans (`blocks.propagated_species`, read by `ward_breaking_fields` and `artifact.coloured_widths`) and the DAG scan of the U(1) leakage check (`ct_closure._u1_leaking`, which keeps its model-level gates). With the flag off the tree engine's loop-request mode runs; it stays the oracle. `TreeBlocks.blocks_dag` is then a `dag_input.NativeDag` handle standing in for the `GenericDAG`: it passes `isinstance(dag, GenericDAG)` (the per-point `TreeRows`' `StageCompilationInput`), `pickle` and `copy` take the decoded DAG, and the first read of a `GenericDAG` attribute decodes it once (`decode_count`, rule 9), after which the decoded fields are the handle's own attributes (the consumers read `dag.currents` in their loops). Once generation's queries are answered (blocks, propagated species, U(1) leakage; kept on the handle) the native columns are released; a later read rebuilds them from the kept input tables (deterministic, `rebuild_count`), so generation does not hold them to its end. The decode shares equal values as the compiled DAG does (couplings, colour weights and factors by bit pattern, ids, label tuples, words): the decoded CT DAG of u u~ > g g g g retains 48 MB against 74 MB for the tree engine's. The Θ(model) input tables cover only the species the recursion can reach (a fixed point from the legs over the usable vertices within the order limits), and the model-only part of each vertex row (the Weyl-projection probe of `allowed_quantum_flows`, colour weight, kernel equivalence, coupling identities) is kept per compiled model and runtime parameters: u u~ > d d~ g loop-block tables 0.62 → 0.036 s; the CT program's tables 0.03 s at six legs. A request with a feature the builder lists as not covered (`dag_input.NATIVE_FALLBACKS`: eleven `NativeFallback` records, each with its key, owner and follow-up; `NativeDagUnsupported.feature` is the key, the Rust builder's four refusals included: a NaN colour weight, more than 64 source states, two vertex rows sharing a kind, a leg label above 64) takes the tree engine with a warning, `TreeBlocks.dag_fallback` names it (`"<key>: <refusal>"`) and `dag_input.fallback_record(slice)` collects a slice's; a refusal of no listed feature is raised (rule 2: fail closed); no control needs a fallback. Each listed feature is refused for real in `test_native_tables_refuse_each_listed_feature` (the model's answers, the process or the native tables changed in one place on u u~ > d d~ g), and the switch sites' fallback and raise in `test_native_stage_falls_back_with_a_record`. The generation record names every fallback (rule 2; slice-status DECISIONS 2026-10-25 ~12:50 item 1): `GeneratedProcess.native_fallbacks` (the slice's `fallback_record`) is the artifact's `generation_filters["native_fallbacks"]`, written only when non-empty, so an artifact without a fallback keeps its bytes. The tables fail closed (`NativeDagUnsupported` of no listed feature, raised) for a usable reachable vertex with a negative coupling order in a limited name: the oracle bounds each current's accumulated orders, which a negative order can bring back within a limit, while the tables prune vertices by their own orders, which is exact for non-negative orders only (no model of the slice has one). The native stages report their start and finish to the request's `progress_callback` (`{"step": "native-dag", "stage": "loop-blocks" | "counterterm-program", "state": "start" | "done" | "fallback"}`, `done` with the current, interaction and block counts, or `fallback` with the listed feature before the tree engine reports its own steps; `dag_input.native_progress`). `LoopSlice.generate` and `export_plan` decode nothing; the native-trees export decodes exactly the two A5 consumers (the slice's and the CT program's tree rows and interaction programs; A5's CT emission below removes the second). The builder is sequential, so 1 and N threads give the same columns (rule 5). Cache state (slice-status DECISIONS 2026-10-25 ~06:30; `workspace/tmp/native-dag/fix2/determinism/`): g g > g g and u u~ > d d~ g generated twice from empty `PYAMPLICOL_CACHE_DIR` directories and twice warm give byte-identical plans in all four runs, and native members identical between the two cold and between the two warm runs, equal to the slice's (`8b52179b`, A4 off) cold and warm. Cold and warm differ in `parameters.bin` only, on the slice as well (not A4's): `native_trees._parameter_program` passes the scalar library's helper definitions (`library._bodies`, in the order the process history first parsed them: a fresh UFO compile and the cached compiled model, `*.pyAmplicol-model.json`, parse in different orders) to `assemble._canonical_stage`, which numbers function heads positionally, so a helper is `pyamplicol_v2_canonical::c25` cold and `c31` warm (8 bytes in 2.9 kB; the evaluators compute the same). Sorting the helpers by their canonical head name before `_canonical_stage` makes cold and warm byte-identical (checked on the slice; patch `fix2/determinism/sort_helpers.patch`, for the owner of `_parameter_program`). Re-checked after the lc-perf merge (`8126d301`, `workspace/tmp/native-dag/fix4/determinism/`): the plans are byte-identical in all five runs (two cold, two warm, and the flag-off Python route warm), the native members identical between the two cold and between the two warm runs, the flag-on warm members equal the flag-off Python route's member for member, and cold and warm still differ in `parameters.bin` only (the same cause; the patch still applies). Oracles (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_native_dag.py`): the switched `build_tree_blocks` and `CountertermBlocks.build` against the oracle stages (blocks, S1′/S1″, the Ward, coloured-width and U(1) queries without a decode, one counted decode on the first attribute read) and the listed fallbacks with their record (default tier, 19 s); the photon/Z and top-width controls (u u~ > e⁻e⁺ g g, g g > u u~ γ g, e⁺e⁻ > u u~ g, g g > t t~ g) and a recursion-route `LoopSlice.generate` whose S1, S4, S5, S8 (also with native trees), S1′, S1″, S2 and P digests equal the oracle route's (physics tier); the O6 files and the recursion-against-sites tests of `test_loop_v2_ct_recursion.py` run on native blocks. Seven legs, production stages under the flag (`tools/loop_v2_dag.py`, records `workspace/tmp/native-dag/oracle/a4/`, on the A4 head and again after the slice merge): S1′ and S1″ equal the stored Python digests (g g > g g g g g S1′ `9d274adf…`, S1″ `ae1a90e9…`; u u~ > g g g g g `198f439b…`, `8eeca050…`) with every count, no fallback and no decode. Python → native: loop-block DAG and blocks 498.9 → 10.0 s (11.9 s warm; g g > g g g g g, process peak 10.27 → 1.59 GB) and 42.2 → 0.85 s (0.96 s warm; u u~ > g g g g g, 0.93 → 0.30 GB); CT program 631.1 → 20.6 s (19.1 s warm; process peak, Born included, 12.0 → 2.66 GB) and 141.0 → 4.5 s (4.3 s warm; 2.79 → 0.78 GB).

  Parity with the site oracle (`workspace/tmp/native-dag/a4b/perf/c1`, `c2`: ct-perf's `measure2.py`, i.e. `LoopSlice.generate` and then the production export; warm lowering cache, `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1`, one run per CCX on idle cores 196, 205, 213 (217), medians of two, both routes of a column in one session; "before" = `NATIVE_DAG = False` on the same tree, `c1`; plan and native-trees sha256 and every CT-program and slice digest equal in all eight runs of each control). Seconds; peak RSS in GB:

  |---|---|---|---|
  | slice tree blocks | 0.80 / 0.82 / 0.82 (sites before 1.76) | 3.57 / 3.33 / 3.64 (5.20) | 3.79 / 3.59 / 5.69 (14.50) |

  The recursion now beats the oracle on the artifact build at g g > g g g g (0.97×; peak +1.6 %) but not at u u~ > g g g g (1.09×, peak 1.27×) or g g > t t~ g g (1.07×, 1.14×); `LoopSlice.generate` alone is 1.06×, 1.05× and 1.08× (peaks 1.05×, 1.05×, 1.12×). Since G-int the oracle's site closure is lazy and sits in the export while the recursion's root closure (the bind) stays in generation, so generation alone does not compare like with like. What remains is not A4's: the CT program's Python tree rows and interaction program in the export (A5's CT emission, which RUST-MIGRATION §3.A sequences after generation-scale-2's I5 proper record), now behind a decode of the native DAG (0.98 / 0.82 / 3.10 s); the bind's colour reductions (0.15 / 0.65 / 5.74 s; A6 keeps `reduce_network` in Python); the warm lowering (0.46–0.84 s). The process peak of both routes is reached in the export, where the CT program's Python rows carry the recursion's excess. Seven legs, `tools/loop_v2_generate.py validate --inputs native --stages --points 0` against the G-int records (`workspace/tmp/native-dag/oracle/a4/val/`; binary64, values bitwise equal to the Python route's): u u~ > g g g g g generation 354.7 → 198.0 s and 2.71 → 1.31 GB (CT program 143.0 → 6.3 s, slice tree blocks 15.4 → 7.9 s), export 177.1 → 194.6 s (the two decodes and rebuilds of the A5 consumers), process peak 9.58 → 9.01 GB, within 2.9 × 10⁻¹⁰ of MadLoop and Recola; g g > g g g g g top-free (model compiled by the merged compiler, cold lowering cache) generation 2652.0 → 2021.1 s and 14.65 → 9.51 GB (`build_tree_blocks` 111.6 → 50.0 s, most of it the Python Born DAG; `CountertermBlocks.build` 484.2 → 13.1 s at 3.9 GB instead of 12.05 GB; the scaleless companion's seed table, 1754 s, is the rest), export 440.7 → 448.0 s at 28.67 → 22.96 GB (the two decodes; 621.8 s on the A4 head before the decoded fields became the handle's attributes), whole run 3211 → 2568 s, process peak 38.6 → 32.6 GB, within 3.8 × 10⁻⁹ of NJet.

  **CT program emission (WP3.1 Rust migration A5, its counterterm part, brought forward to close parity; `work/v2-native-dag`; `dag_input.NATIVE_PROGRAM = True`).** The native-trees export builds the counterterm program's interaction program from the native CT DAG (`V2Dag.program`, `rusticol-core` `loop_v2/dag/program.rs`) instead of the Python `TreeRows` (runtime schema) and `native_trees._interaction_program` on the decoded DAG, which stay the oracle; they run with the flag off and for a CT DAG the tree engine built (an A4 fallback). Rust lays out the runtime schema of a loop-request DAG whose every non-source current is propagated (`ct_closure.program_propagated_currents`; the slice's `TreeRows(dag)` is not such a DAG): one value slot per current (source, or propagated / unpropagated by the model's propagator of its particle and chirality), the momentum slots by (bit count, mask), one stage per result size; and it emits the steps (rows, accumulators, outputs, and the rows and outputs of the epsilon-pole slots that finite-only evaluations skip), the colour weights interned in first use, and the kernels in first use. Python keeps the model algebra (rule 4). Each kernel is compiled from the representative the oracle compiles it from, by the oracle's recipe: the model's component expressions on canonical symbols, saved as exact Symbolica evaluators, with the kernel functions inlined in one `_specialize_stage_symbolica_functions` call over all kernels instead of one per kernel (built in the oracle's order, so the functions and their wildcards come out the same). The source and model-parameter records come from the runtime schema's own functions (`_source_records`, `build_runtime_model_parameter_records`), run on the sources, on one interaction per distinct (vertex, coupling) and on one current per particle. Released native columns are rebuilt for the emission and released again. Nothing is decoded: the export's only remaining decode is the slice's tree rows (A5's tree program, not in this increment).
