### 6.2 Admission: plain tree-level insertions (REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM J)

A counterterm contribution is an ordinary tree-level contribution of the CT-lowered model with **exactly one** CT vertex (the insertion order, §6.1). Its admission is

- the requested coupling orders: the program's order limits and the loop orders of the request (§6.1 item 1), which also exclude every rule above them (inert);
- a rule of a selected correction (`rule.perturbation_type`; a UVtree rule also needs a selected correction among its own orders);
- a **loop of a selected correction** (WP3.2 EWI-3, Q3): every particle of the alternative is perturbative in a selected correction (the model's `perturbative_species_orders`, MadLoop's `is_perturbating`, the eligibility of the families' loop lines; a species without facts is no loop species). The UFO declares no correction type of a CT vertex (`perturbation_type` is None for every rule of loop_sm, loop_qcd_qed_sm and G_μ), so this is what tells the QCD correction of the photon vertex (the R2 of u u~ a with a u-gluon loop, orders QCD² QED) from the QED one (its u-photon UV counterterm) when a mixed-order Born admits both by coupling orders (u u~ > d d~ with QED corrections). With the families' loop species in the catalogue (EWI-1), the photon-only QED corrections of that request generated, their bare and counterterm UV poles cancelling (ladder rung `uudd_qed_photon_uv` of `test_loop_v2_ew.py`, binary64; an identity of `poles`, which takes the field factors' UV part as the remainder); since WP3.2 EWI-5 they are refused before any tree (listed `veto-unlabelled-counterterms`, "Listed generation refusals": loop_qcd_qed_sm's electroweak counterterm labels are nominal). loop_sm's QCD admission is unchanged rule by rule. This third criterion refines REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM J's literal admission list (coupling orders, exactly once, `particle_veto`) without any loop matching; it awaits the coordinator's ratification as an implementation note of ADDENDUM J (ew-ct fix round 1);
- the **particle veto**: every loop-content alternative of the rule (its UFO `loop_particles`, |PDG| codes; an empty one is a wildcard) that holds a vetoed particle is dropped, all others are kept. `M_rule` is their number (`ct_recursion.admitted_alternatives`).

**Inert vertices** (coordinator decision 2026-10-23, REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM J). The admission does not ask whether a tree of the request can hold the rule's vertex: a lowered vertex that none can hold simply never enters the program's DAG. A rule the lowering cannot lower (§6.1 "Not lowered") is checked: when its vertex cannot enter any tree of the request's CT program it is **inert**. It contributes nothing, needs no lowering and is recorded as inert with its reason (`CountertermRecursion.inert`, `LoopSlice.counterterm_program.recursion`); the artifact's generation record keeps it as the process's generation filter `counterterms_inert` (rule name → reason, in `artifact.json` `extensions.generation.concrete_processes[].filters`, next to `particle_veto`; model names only, no path, no native schema involved). Otherwise the request is refused. "Can enter" is the tree engine's own reachability over the request's legs, order limits and `particle_veto` (`ct_closure.InsertionReachability`): the tree engine's forward species and coupling-order sweep (`generation.dag_reachability._species_coupling_orders_by_mask`, the same function the DAG compiler's closure-order reachability runs, here without its colour-engine and leg-set filters) over the CT program's model, order limits (insertion order 0) and vetoed particles gives the insertion-free currents of every leg set, and a vertex of fields f₁…f_n and orders o enters a tree iff the legs split into n blocks whose currents enter its fields with orders that sum with o to within the program's limits; a single leg is the external particle. The program reads a vertex in one of two ways, told apart by the block of leg 1. With leg 1 alone the vertex is a CT root, which reads each block by its incoming field like the Born closure: the current entering a leg of field f is the particle anti(f). Otherwise it is an insertion inside a current of the program's DAG, which the tree engine reads with the current of particle p on the UFO leg of p (`models.compiler_entry._compile_oriented_kernels`): the current entering a leg of field f is f, and the block holding leg 1 is the rest of the tree re-rooted at the vertex (its root's ordinary vertex read through its conjugate, which a Hermitian vertex set holds). The two readings differ only for vertices whose fields are not closed under conjugation: in u d~ > e+ ve g the R2 of [u~ d W+] sits inside the current of legs 1 and 5 (the kernel the DAG uses for d + W+ → u) and that of [d~ u W-] at the root. A two-point vertex needs two blocks of at least two legs: the CT program puts two-point insertions on internal lines only (a clone line is never external, and an external line takes the particle's field factor, §6.5). The full leg set is swept too, as the root of a one-point vertex, should one ever reach the check. The sweep ignores colour, helicity and flavour-flow labels, so it over-approximates the currents and never declares a vertex that can enter a tree inert; no loop, site or witness is consulted. Goldstones: a tree model that absorbs them into the massive vectors (`CompiledUFOModel.inactive_goldstone_names`, unitary gauge) holds no Goldstone vertex, so every Goldstone CT vertex of that model is in no tree of any request: a two-point term on an absorbed Goldstone is not lowered and is inert (`ct_line_insertions`), a vertex term is lowered and never enters the DAG. **The check (WP3.2 EWI-4b).** Since EWI-4b only QCD-only requests take unitary trees: every request with electroweak loop species takes the model in 't Hooft–Feynman gauge (§6.8), where the Goldstones are explicit fields and their counterterms (the Goldstone Yukawa and mass counterterms, the Goldstone–vector mixings) are ordinary rules, lowered and admitted like any other. For a QCD-only request the absorbed policy accounts for the explicit Goldstone counterterms exactly when the two gauges agree: M5 compares b b~ > t t~ QED=2 [virt=QCD] with a massive b (an internal Z between massive currents; the 't Hooft–Feynman request admits and inserts the QCD counterterms of the G⁰ b b̄ and G⁰ t t̄ vertices, the unitary one cannot hold them) in both gauges (`test_qcd_corrections_agree_in_both_gauges`, nightly). It runs only when a request admits an unlowerable rule (0.02 s at four legs). Tests: `test_insertion_reachability_is_sound_against_the_site_oracle` (every rule the site oracle closes on the slice's exact tree currents is reachable; quark vertices in g g > g g and gluon or vetoed-Z vertices in Drell-Yan are not), `test_uncancelled_cells_in_no_tree_are_inert` (an anomalous g g Z R2 is inert in u u~ > e- e+ with the cancelling model's components, refused in u u~ > e- e+ g), `test_insertion_reachability_tells_a_vertex_from_its_conjugate` (u d~ > e+ ve: [d~ u W-] enters at the root and [e+ ve W-] inside the W+ current, their conjugates do not; u d~ > e+ ve g: [u~ d W+] enters too; u u~ > d d~: [g g] enters, the two-point quark vertices of its external lines do not), `test_insertion_reachability_is_sound_against_the_program_dag` (u d~ > w+ g: every rule of a CT vertex or clone line in the production program's DAG and of its CT roots is admitted, [u~ d W+] inside a current and [d~ u W-] at the root), `test_unlowerable_charged_vertex_in_a_current_is_refused` (u d~ > w+ g with the R2 of [u~ d W+] made unlowerable is refused), `test_qed_declaring_model_names_its_blockers` (loop_qcd_qed_sm u u~ > d d~: the reachable recms rules are refused, the G0–A mixing is inert) and the nightly `test_qed_declaring_model_joint_corrections_equal_legacy` (g g > g g with QCD and QED corrections, every unlowerable EW rule inert, equals legacy).

Nothing else: no counterterm is matched to its loop counterpart. There is no witness table of divergent loop routings, no admission per routing or per leg mask, and no fallback to site closure. MadLoop's matching (`set_LoopCT_vertices`) needs the loop graphs and is complicated and costly; for a consistent model it admits the same alternatives, since a CT vertex of given fields and orders that sits in a tree of the process has its one-loop counterpart in the process too, with the loop content of each non-vetoed alternative.

`particle_veto` is a public generation option (`[generation] particle_veto`, `--particle-veto`, `GenerationConfig.particle_veto`; model names, a name vetoes its antiparticle; v2 virtual generation only). It is stored with the plan v4 request record (`SliceRequest.particle_veto`, |PDG| codes) and in the generation filters. A vetoed particle is removed from every tree-level vertex of every generation step, so the loops, the trees and the counterterms stay consistent: the Born and the loop blocks (the tree engine's `ignored_particle_ids`), the loop species and loop vertices (`families.ModelLoopCatalogue(veto=...)`), the CT program (its DAG and the lowered model's vertices), the CT terms and the external-field terms with vetoed loop content. A vetoed particle cannot be external. With loop_sm, `particle_veto = [t]` is massless five-flavour QCD (no top loop, no top decoupling term in α_s and δZ_g): g g > g g equals NJet's Nf = 5 virtual to 3·10⁻¹⁴ at ten points, and g g > g g g g at NJet's first point to 1.5·10⁻¹⁴ (Born), 6·10⁻¹³ (poles) and 1.1·10⁻¹¹ (finite part), within the references' own spread at six gluons (NJet against OpenLoops up to 7·10⁻¹¹; `test_loop_v2_particle_veto.py`, the six-gluon point nightly: an hour on the per-point Python inputs). It replaces the internal top-free switches (the NJet comparison's `ignored_particle_ids`).

The site oracle (§6.6) keeps MadLoop's matching through witnesses as an independent check of this rule: a witness is an admitted family routing with structural rank R_f ≥ 2N − 4 and signature (attached currents' fields and leg masks, loop species set, loop orders); R2 and UVmass rules need a witness whose species set is one of their alternatives, UVloop rules any witness (alternatives as veto filters), UVtree rules none. O6 (closure with the plain rule = sites with witness admission) holds on every control and slice request (§6.1 "O6 and O1").

### 6.3 Completeness

The model's UV and R2 rules are taken as declared (its NLO declaration's `uv_complete` and `model_r2_complete`): production does not check them against the loops, and nothing checks completeness at runtime (a missing R2 is finite and invisible to the poles). The IR-pole oracle (the Catani operator, O9) and the site oracle check it in tests and model development; the site oracle keeps the completeness check of design 3:

1. **Coverage rule** (`ct_sites._coverage`, oracle). Every divergent witness family must be covered by an admitted rule for each eligible loop-species group, or by a certified zero. A divergent witness family has structural rank R ≥ 2N − 4, i.e. it is superficially UV divergent, which is the only case where UV poles and R2 occur in a renormalisable theory. Otherwise the oracle fails and names the uncovered signature (the sgluon bug, assessment R2). A model whose ranks make the divergent set unbounded (Δ ≥ 2 per propagator, effective theories) is refused by the oracle.
2. **Certified zeros.**
   - *Structural certificate (implemented, `ct_sites.closed_fermion_zeros`):* a closed loop of one fermion f needs no rule when its colour factor is Tr_R(T^a) = 0 (one adjoint attachment, the rest singlets), or when charge conjugation cancels its two orientations (Furry: every vertex joining f, f̄ and an attachment is a pure vector current, the number of attachments is odd, and the colour trace is unchanged by reversing them, i.e. at most two adjoint attachments). Both need f's model vertices to be three-point with one other field. Closed quark loops with one photon and two gluons are certified this way; a Z keeps its axial part, which R2_GGZ covers.
   - *Cheap test:* the summed 1PI numerator, including both fermion orientations, vanishes exactly at random exact points (Furry-type zeros).
   - *Thorough test:* the **model certifier** built on the retained direct-R2 path (`loops/evanescent.py`, `evanescent_gauge.py`, `direct_kernels.py`). Once per model, it computes R2 and UV poles of every admitted 1PI function with k ≤ k_max per loop species, and compares them with the UFO rules, both non-zero values and zeros. This is also the structural replacement for the string whitelist (assessment R6).

### 6.4 Contraction and component split

- **Contraction.** CT = 1 amplitudes are contracted with the Born exactly like the Born square: A0† G A_CT per sector, plus rectangular overlaps for CT-only multi-trace states.
- **Kinds stay separate** through slots. This reproduces the kept component vector [full, bare, UV, R2, UV_local, UV_fields] (`rust/crates/rusticol-core/src/loop_execution.rs:188` `CompleteLoopResult` [V]) and the 19 totals, with full = bare + UV_local + R2 + fields and UV = UV_local + fields.
- **No extra loop factor.** CT couplings already contain the loop factor, so no i/(16π²) is applied (`physical_counterterms.py:848-849` [V-s]).
- **Allowed exact refactorings** (contracts §2.5): fold currents per (site class, tensor) and drop identically zero currents.

### 6.5 External fields

- **Scalar δZ:** for each external leg and correction, δZ/2 × the order-masked Born interference, keeping the semantics of `physical_counterterms.py:877-905` [V-s].
- **Chiral δZ^{L,R} and mixing** (δZ_{Zγ}, CKM): CT-tagged modified external sources in the tree recursion (CT = 1 sources). The field term is then the Born dual times that amplitude, which is generic for the electroweak members.
- **Tadpole CTs.** The VEV-subtracted scheme has no tadpole CTs; one-point rules raise at preparation, as today (`counterterms.py:259-260` [V-s]).

**Status (WP2.2).** Scalar δZ only (`loops/v2/fields.py`). Internal two-point terms of any chirality and mixing between fields of equal spin are lowered as clone lines (§6.1). Chiral and mixing *external* factors have no UFO format: the particle `counterterm` attribute carries one scalar per order key, and the NLOCT electroweak models (`loop_qcd_qed_sm`) keep it QCD-only and put the electroweak field renormalisation, chiral and mixing δZ included, into their vertex counterterms (on-shell residues are one). A model that declares such factors needs a format first (proposal: `ExternalFieldRule.chirality` ∈ {None, L, R} and a `partner` PDG). Plan:
- *Chiral, massless (Weyl) legs:* a helicity selection. δZ_L multiplies quarks of physical helicity −1 and antiquarks of physical helicity +1, incoming or outgoing (ψ → P_L ψ; ψ̄ → ψ̄ P_R; the tree engine's labels of crossed legs must be mapped), so the scalar mechanism restricted to the leg's helicity rows is exact.
- *Chiral, massive legs:* the projected source P_χ w (row spinors take the opposite projector), as a CT = 1 source.
- *Mixing:* the Born with leg i's field replaced by its partner, keeping leg i's wavefunction. For legs other than 1 this is a CT = 1 source of the CT program (a partner source current with the leg's wavefunction values); for leg 1 it is the root closure with the partner's vertex entries.
- *Tests:* loop_sm has no such rule, so they are synthetic: δZ_L = δZ_R reproduces the scalar term, and a partner with identical couplings reproduces it too.

### 6.6 Test oracle: CT site closure

The site closure was the slice form of wave 1; it is now a test oracle only (`SliceRequest.counterterms = "sites"`; plan v4 refuses it), with the witness admission of §6.2. CT contributions are computed as **sites**:

- A CT vertex V_r is applied at the root to m ∈ {2, …, k_max} CT = 0 block currents whose leg sets partition the legs (k_max from the model's CT rules).
- Partitions are unordered and assigned to the vertex's field multiset. The UFO vertex is fully symmetrised, so each assignment counts once, which is the BG rule.
- Internal two-point insertions are two-block sites with both blocks of at least two legs.
- Colour comes from exact closure of the vertex colour with the block words.

Every one-CT tree has exactly one CT vertex and can be rooted there, so the site sum is exact and complete. It stays as the oracle for §6.1 (physics-judge graft 7) and for small processes. The CT-root terms of the production closure (§6.1 item 7) reuse its enumeration with the plain admission.

### 6.7 Counterterms at LC and NLC

The loop_sm CT colour origins and their projection [V-s leading-colour report §4.3; `workspace/reports/loop-general-nc-r2-input-audit.md`]:

| CT family | Colour origin | LC keeps | NLC adds |
|---|---|---|---|
| G_UVg (β₀) | 11 C_A | C_A part | — |
| Quark-loop UV (per flavour), heavy-quark decoupling logs | 4 T_F | — | All (P−1) |
| Quark field, mass and Yukawa CTs | C_F | N_c/2 part | −1/(2N_c) part (P−2) |
| R2 3-gluon and 2-gluon, gluon loop | explicit N_c | All | — |
| R2 GQQ, QQq | C_F | N_c/2 part | Rest |
| R2 with a closed fundamental loop | T_F | — | All (P−1) |
| Four-gluon R2 (general-N_c asset) | single trace × C_A; T_F N_f; δδ | C_A single-trace part, colour-ordered | T_F N_f (P−1); δδ (P−2) |
| ggV, ggS, ggVV, ggSS | δ^{ab}/T_F | — | All (P−1) |

LC/NLC need these named invariants, which a UFO with numeric SU(3) colour does not provide (`loops/model.py:1-7` [V]). LC/NLC therefore need a formal-N_c continuation: the model's own (source-owned: `QCDR2Continuation`, `loops/qcd_r2_color.py:30` [V], for QCD; a future EW or BSM declaration otherwise) or, for loop_sm, the one pyAmpliCol ships (below); a request reaching a counterterm vertex no continuation covers is refused (`colour-declaration-coverage`). FC always works (MASTER_PLAN §2.15; judge X1).

**Shipped loop_sm declaration (M-QCD MQ-NC, as built).** pyAmpliCol ships the formal-N_c data of MG5_aMC 3.7.0's loop_sm (`models/loop_color_declarations/loop_sm.json`, written by `tools/developer/build_loop_colour_data.py`), so LC and NLC work on stock loop_sm without a hand-written declaration; full colour stays the default of loop requests (USER Q1, MQ-MODEL).

- **Coverage.** All 103 counterterm vertices of loop_sm (the source's 104th, `V_R24G`, is commented out in `CT_vertices.py`): 74 R2, 26 UV, 3 UVmass; 198 coupling entries continued by the identities C_A = N_c, C_F = (N_c²−1)/(2N_c), T_F = 1/2, N_col = N_c (with the scheme tie λ_HV = 1), the four-gluon R2 `V_R2RGA` by its two whole-rule recipes (gauge and fundamental-Dirac loop groups, `loops/qcd_r2_color.py`), and the six external-field factors (c, b, t: C_F; the gluon's three heavy-quark groups: T_F).
- **Keys.** Each vertex is keyed by a structural digest of the vertex as the source writes it (`vertex_digest`): counterterm type; particles by spin, colour, charge, mass and width parameters (never names or PDG codes); colour and Lorentz structures; coupling entries with their expressions and orders; loop-particle groups; and the definitions of every internal, counterterm and helper parameter the expressions read (external parameters by LHA address and type, never a value). Field factors likewise (`field_digest`). Vertex names, record positions, the model's name and path never enter. A restriction lives in the source's metadata (card values and the zeros it fixes), not in its declarations, so every shipped restriction has the same digests and resolves its surviving vertices (checked for all nine). Vertices of one structure (d and s quark R2 and UV) share an entry: 98 entries for 103 vertices.
- **Resolution.** `resolve_loop_color_declaration` returns the source's own declaration (metadata), else the shipped one at that source's positions (`resolve_shipped_declaration`) when the source states the data's loop scheme (its metadata's loop declaration, which the public route attaches, else its shipped `[loop.model]` profile; a model sharing a few digests, e.g. loop_qcd_qed_sm with 3 of 499, gets none), else None. LC and NLC requests naming no declaration take it, on the public route (`loop_service._prepare`) and in `LoopSlice.generate` (`assemble.resolve_request`), and record it in the artifact like an explicit one; a full-colour request's contraction choice still reads the source's own declaration only.
- **Literature colour factors.** Each entry states every coupling entry's colour factor from the literature (textbook UV renormalisation; Draggiotis et al. 0903.0356 for the QCD R2; Garzelli et al. 0910.3130 for R2 with electroweak legs and QCD loops). `test_loop_v2_nc_declaration.py` proves each source coupling with the identities equal to its factor times an N_c-independent rest in every branch of its conditionals (a coupling with C_F written as 4/3 fails), and the model preparation proves the identities equal to the source at N_c = 3.
- **Guard.** The listed refusal `colour-declaration-coverage` (`refusals.require_declared_colour_rules`, `require_declared_colour_fields`): an LC or NLC request is refused when its counterterm program can insert a vertex the declaration does not cover (reachability as for unlowerable vertices, `ct_closure.InsertionReachability`; an uncovered vertex no tree holds is left alone) or an external leg takes an uncovered field factor. Full colour reads the source couplings at N_c = 3 and is never refused for coverage, with or without a declaration. The example QCD subset (`examples/loop/source_colour/loop_sm_qcd_subset.json`) is refused on u u~ > e- e+ g(g), whose closed quark loops reach g g Z, g g g Z and g g g A; the tests and developer tools that used it there take the shipped declaration. The engine's own alpha(0) photon field term is not a source factor and is not checked (electroweak, M-SM).
- **Independent oracles** (`test_loop_v2_nc_oracles.py`; none reads the declaration; the N_c scan `tests/unit/loop_nc_scan.py` evaluates full colour at N_c = N with the declaration's couplings): (a) NJet's NJetNc (2.1.1, double-double, top-free, N_f = 5; `tests/fixtures/loop/nc/njet_nc_scan.json`) at N_c = 2, 4, 5, 6 on g g > g g and g g > g g g (direct mode, which keeps the N_c = 3 colour average) and on u u~ > g g g, u u~ > d d~ g and u u~ > u u~ g (BLHA mode, `NJetNc`, every point re-evaluated in double-double; BLHA divides by the colour average at N_c, so the fixture stores its values times avg(N_c)/avg(3)): Born and every Laurent coefficient within 1.8e-13 (gluon rows) and 3.5e-12 (quark rows) in binary64; (c) Catani's operator at N_c = N (C_A, C_F, β₀ and the colour-correlated Borns at N, the Python correlators: the native C4 correlators are contracted at N_c = 3) against the poles of the renormalised virtual, which tests the UV counterterms' N_c dependence (C_A fixed to 3 fails at 46 %); (d) the gauge-Ward check (§8.4) at N_c = 2 and 4 in double-double, which tests the R2's N_c dependence (N_col fixed to 3 fails on u u~ > g g g at 1.7e-4 of the physical scale; on u u~ > e- e+ g the R2 satisfy their own Ward identity, so that row is a consistency check only); (b) BDK's O14 for u u~ > g g g (`test_loop_v2_colour.py`, `test_loop_v2_observables_nightly.py`).
- **Campaign nc column (m-qcd PLAN §1.4 rule 7).** `test_loop_v2_nc_projection.py`, per nc row (A01, A08, A09, A12, A13, A15, A18, A21, B03, C01, D05, E01, F01) at the campaign's points: the row's binding reference fixture (`required/mqcd_references.json`) gives points 0, 1, 2 at their μ_R and point 3 = point 0 at μ_R/2, its fixed parameters (5F rows on the default restriction with no_b_mass's zero masses, '5F*', until MQ-5F) and α_s; the default tier runs points 0 and 1 of A12 and D05, the nightly tier every other point (PLAN §4.2 also marks A01, A09 and C01 default: their projection runs nightly, the user's fast-default-tier rule). LC, NLC and FC come from the production native path (native point inputs, as a stored artifact; the shipped declaration resolved from the model and handed to the request) in double-double and in native MPFR (50 digits), and are compared with the exact Laurent fit FC(N) = Σ_p c_p N^p of the N_c scan. The scan is `loop_nc_scan request` with `"dressing": "native"`: the Python point inputs at N (trees, Born, colour cells, counterterm and field scalars) dressed and reduced natively at 60 digits, one generation per N for all of a row's points; it equals the Python reference path's own dressing within 7e-53 at N = 2 and 1/2 (A08, A12, A13, B03 point 0; nightly test on A12), which instead carries a precision-independent error at B03's point 1 (bare loop 4e-25 off Catani at 60, 80 and 100 digits; the native carriers equal Catani to 1e-65; below the user's ~1e-5 threshold, a HANDOFF §9 note only). The fit window holds the powers P_top − 2n_col .. P_top, n_col being the number of coloured external legs; two check nodes inside the fit nodes' range must be reproduced, and the window's two lowest powers must vanish. Every check is per point, per squared-order sector and per coefficient (PLAN §1.4), Born included: strictly relative, except a value that vanishes identically (|r| ≤ 1e-28 A) is held absolutely, |a − r| ≤ 1e-24 A, A = F_s = |B_s| α_s/2π (PLAN's F_s rule at rule 7's tolerance; |B_s| for a Born). Within 1e-24: no power above the sector's P; on MPFR every component's LC = c_P 3^P, NLC = Σ_{P−2..P} c_p 3^p, FC = FC(3); and the poles at the scan's integer N equal Catani's at N (oracle (c) on every row). In double-double the same identity for the Born (within 1e-24) and the virtual, in rules 4 and 6's band max(1e-24, 10 E_DD) per coefficient (coordinator ruling 2026-10-29 ~10:15), E_DD that coefficient's double-double estimate with `stability.RECOMMENDED_CHECKS` over the deviation's own denominator (A for a value held absolutely): MPFR proves the identity exact (≤ 2.1e-59; C01 7.4e-37), so a double-double miss within 10 E_DD is double-double's conditioning. Only A18 goes beyond 1e-24: its full-colour virtual finite part, 1.9e-24 / 7.2e-24 / 7.2e-24 at points 0, 2, 3 against E_DD 4.2e-24 / 1.6e-23 / 1.6e-23 (0.46x). Measured at all four points of every row (native dressing): double-double ≤ 3.2e-28 on every row but A18 (1.8e-25 at point 1) and B03 (2.3e-26); MPFR ≤ 2.1e-59 except C01 (7.4e-37); fit check nodes ≤ 1.7e-64 (C01 3.3e-40); Catani ≤ 3.9e-70 except the supplied-point rows C01 and E01 (≤ 1.3e-28: their 30-digit points are on shell to 1e-30, Catani takes the model masses); the values held absolutely lie below 1e-77 A, the smallest held relatively above 4.9e-4 A. Default tier: A12 and D05, about 100 s each; nightly rows 108-507 s, A15 1780 s. `NC_COLUMN` lists each row's column: the projection ids (default rows also the nightly-points id) followed by its oracle ids.

### 6.8 Gauge and scheme

- **One gauge.** Born, blocks, loop lines and CTs all use 't Hooft–Feynman gauge (ξ = 1) with explicit Goldstones and ghosts. Ranks then stay ≤ N, whereas unitary-gauge kμkν/M² terms would break rank ≤ N + 2. **As built (WP3.2 EWI-4b):** `LoopSlice.generate` takes the model in that gauge for every request with electroweak loop species (`ew_policy.electroweak`: a correction other than QCD or a colour-singlet loop species of a requested correction; `ew_policy.feynman_gauge_model`), refused with `ElectroweakGaugeError` on a model that cannot be (its source declares no gauge 1, which the loop preparation refuses for every loop request already, or a bundle prepared in unitary gauge; `prepare_request_model`, so the dry run reports it). QCD-only requests keep the compiled model's gauge: unitary by default (their validated trees; QCD plans byte-identical), 't Hooft–Feynman for a model compiled in it. The request's gauge is its model's (`LoopSlice.compiled.ir.gauge`), recorded in the artifact's generation filters (`gauge`, off the default only); a regenerated artifact takes the same gauge from the same model and request. The tree API's default is unchanged (unitary). Loop lines: massive vectors −i g, Goldstones and ghosts i (the UFO's index-free numerator), every segment and numerator of degree ≤ 1 (`test_feynman_gauge_loop_lines_keep_rank_at_most_n`).
- **Complex-mass scheme.** Complex masses and couplings are runtime inputs from the snapshot (R7). The fast NinjaRS engine accepts complex masses with real shifts and a real S-matrix (`ninjars:fast/mod.rs:60-79` [V]). The known complex-mass master defects are risk 13 (§15). **As built (M-SM MS-CMS, `loops/model_cms.py`).** The declaration's `mass_scheme` is `"real-on-shell"` or `"complex-mass"` (`mass-scheme` otherwise; `[loop.model.scheme] mass_scheme` stated alone selects it over the model's own declaration, MadLoop's `set complex_mass_scheme True`). `"complex-mass"` generates on MG5_aMC's complex-mass model of the source: `complex_mass_scheme` ports `Model.change_mass_to_complex_scheme` (MG5_aMC 3.7.0 `base_objects.py`) on the exact UFO records, `complex_mass_model` applies the same decisions to the compiled tree payload. CMSParam = 1; per width parameter first met (source order) with a nonzero value at the card (fixed parameters, else the restriction card, else the source), an internal width takes `re()`, an external real mass M gets CMASS_M = sqrt(M² − iMΓ) with M and Γ staying real inputs, an internal mass is refused (`cms-internal-mass`: MG5's PDG-24 input-scheme switch is SM-specific and not ported, m-sm PLAN §1.7); the YUKAWA inputs become their particles' (pole) masses (`cms-yukawa-input` if a request fixes one), written in M and Γ so that no coupling reads the CMASS parameter whose sign the fermion-loop tree reading flips; a tied mass without a pole mass (a zero width at the card, as for every external top, EW-25, or loop_sm's τ) gives its particles the real line mass LMASS_M := M (step 4b, `ComplexMassScheme.lines`, MS-CMS-N's CM07 fix; not in MG5_aMC, values unchanged), so the couplings read M and the line LMASS_M, and `conventions.LeafWeights._require_mass_free_couplings` holds at every width (before step 4b a zero-width top on an EW or Yukawa loop line raised `FermionSignError`: G_μ g g > t t~ [QED], loop_sm t t~ > h h [QCD]); every other internal parameter is complex and reads CMASS_M in parameters, CT parameters, couplings and the field counterterms; every particle of a pole mass, Goldstones and ghosts included, propagates with it and width ZERO (so the [U8] width guards pass on that model); the UFO-standard principal `reglog` is bound ([U3]; `reglog_convention = "source"` keeps the G_μ override as a view). First oracle: MG5_aMC's own CMS model (`tools/loop_reference/mg5_cms_model.py`) equals ours to ≤ 3.4·10⁻¹³ in every parameter, CT parameter, coupling and field counterterm (G_μ default and `a0`, loop_sm with Γ_t; `test_loop_model_cms.py`). Python inputs: `assemble._model_masses(pole_masses=True)` returns a pole mass as an exact complex; the loop propagators (`_mass_values`: M² − iMΓ to the working digits), the kernel constants and the Python reduction lanes take it; external legs and Catani's emitters stay real (external states are stable, EW-25: `cms-external-width`). Policy keys (listed, shown by the dry run): `mass-scheme`, `complex-mass-scheme` (a model outside the transform's assumptions), `cms-internal-mass`, `cms-yukawa-input`, `cms-external-width`, `complex-fixed-parameter` (both schemes: external parameters are real), and for real on-shell requests `coloured-width` and `electroweak-width` (the latter now also on the Born DAG in `loop_service._v2_request`). The conventions record (`ew_policy.scheme_conventions`) states `mass_scheme = "complex-mass"` with the pole masses, widths, Yukawa ties, reglog and CMSParam/LogSwitch/GfRedefinitionChoice (`model_cms.convention_record`; also for a QCD-only CMS request); non-CMS records are unchanged. The native point inputs take complex plan masses (MS-CMS-N, §11.1), so the public route stores complex-mass artifacts (`model_cms.NATIVE_POLE_MASSES`; a build without them refuses a request with a pole mass, `complex-mass-scheme`). Evidence (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_cms.py`): Python inputs (`LoopSlice.evaluate`; double-double runs in the native executor, MS-CMS-N) = MadLoop CMS: CM10 (u d~ > w+ b b~ QCD=0 [QCD], loop_sm, Γ_t; QP, 4 points) AP(32) ≤ 6.8·10⁻¹⁴, binary64 ≤ 3.4·10⁻¹⁰; CM02 (u u~ > e- e+ [QCD QED], G_μ, every width; QP, 4 points, every sector) binary64 ≤ 1.0·10⁻¹³, AP(32) ≤ 8.8·10⁻¹⁵; CM05 (u u~ > e- e+ g [QCD QED]) binary64 ≤ 4.6·10⁻¹³, AP(32) ≤ 1.1·10⁻¹³; CM04 (g g > t t~ [QCD QED], Γ_t = 0: the top on its line mass, ymt := MT on the EW loop lines; QP, 4 points) binary64 ≤ 1.5·10⁻¹⁴, AP(32) ≤ 1.4·10⁻¹⁵; CM02, CM04 and CM05 check the engine's set of sectors and their sum too; CM01 (e- e+ > mu- mu+ [QED], MadLoop double exports at the continuum, three Z-resonance points, zero widths and widths × 0.001 (`electroweak-mumu-cms-small-width-control.json`), both reglog conventions) ≤ 10⁻⁸ in binary64, double-double (the native executor with Python inputs) and AP(32), DD = AP(32) ≤ 10⁻²⁰ at every point, AP(40) = AP(60) ≤ 10⁻³⁰ at a Z-resonance point; CM09 (u d~ > e+ ve a [QCD QED], a0, the photon tagged: the conversion's real part) = MadLoop CMS × MG5_aMC's tagged-photon formula ≤ 10⁻⁸ (binary64 at 4 points, AP(32) at 1) (nightly: CM01, CM02, CM04, CM05, CM09). Generation: CM02 and CM05 176 s each, CM04 110 s. Default tier: loop_sm t t~ > h h [QCD] at zero widths equals real on-shell with ymt = MT, at the card and at a run-time MT, with Python inputs and as a stored public artifact. A derived mass on an external leg (the line mass, loop_sm's MW) is read by the exact runtime's sources at the point's parameters (`symbolica_exact._particle_mass`; before, the value at generation: 2·10⁻⁴ off at MT = 172 on a card of 173, 3.8·10⁻⁴ on CM04's Born). **Generation cost:** MG5_aMC restricts the transformed model by its restriction card, fixing the card's parameters of value 1 (our loader fixes only zeros); the transform does the same (`ComplexMassScheme.ones`: the G_μ UFO's TECHNICAL LogSwitch and GfRedefinitionChoice; its pole regulators epsUV and epsIR stay adjustable, MS-POLES §12.4: read only by the counterterms' pole coefficients, they carry no dead branch, CM05 generates in 178 s against 176 s with them fixed). Left free, GfRedefinitionChoice's dead re() branch carried complex conjugates of the pole masses into every resolved coupling (the 20 largest couplings × (ab + c) expand in 20 s to 646 MB of canonical text, against 0.03 s and 1 MB fixed), which made EW CMS generation 10-100× slower than real on-shell (CT lowering 256 s vs 41 s; the catalogue of u u~ > e- e+ > 25 min). Non-CMS plans, trees, parameter programs, kernels and Python binary64 virtuals are bitwise unchanged (loop_sm g g > t t~, u u~ > d d~; G_μ u u~ > e- e+ [QCD QED]).
- **No scheme choices in v2.** The γ5 prescription and the finite terms are those of the model's UV/R2 rules: loop_sm sets lhv = 1, and nothing is added to the four-dimensional numerator (`MASTER_PLAN.md:1547-1556` [V]).

### 6.9 Direct-R2 bonus

The inherited per-diagram direct path stays, quarantined, for processes whose loops have at most four propagators. Its roles are validation and the model certifier (§6.3). v2 is model-R2 only.

The plan keeps the (k_μ², ε) monomial labels and the `MuTensorNumerator` ingress, so a future evanescent segment builder (X = Y + q·Z + μ·W, MASTER_PLAN §2.6) can feed the same tries. `r2_strategy = "direct"` is refused for other processes.

---

## 7. Colour

### 7.1 Representation

- **Colour flow.** U(N_c) colour flow with explicitly graded U(1) pieces, so the dressed kinematics never depends on N_c.
- **Born basis.** From `build_color_plan` (`color/plan_build.py:17-24` [V]): single traces, open strings or products of these; optionally the DDM basis where the tree certificate applies.
- **Seed tables.** K[c, b] = ⟨b|κ_c⟩, an exact Laurent polynomial in N_c with rational coefficients, computed by integer cycle counting (`color/adjoint_kernel.py:36-62`) or exact overlaps (`color/connections.py:492`) [V-s]. Fermion signs (§3.3) multiply the leaf weights, not K.
- **No Gram inversion.** Loop colour is never decomposed into the Born basis; only overlaps are needed. This avoids the missing partial-contraction routine (tree report §8d) and the Idenso degree limits (assessment W3).
- **Born Gram.** The Born–Born Gram matrix is needed only for the Born square and CT contractions.

### 7.2 Seed classes

- **Generic (default).** As in §3.4: a per-skeleton colour-flow vector, term-factored. Per point, W_t(h) = Σ_b ⟨b|t⟩ conj(A0_b(h)) is computed once per distinct structure t, and then U0[s] = Σ_t c_{s,t}·W_t.
  - This is correct for any model whose coloured vertices lower to colour flow (Recola2 App. A method [L-s 1705.06053]).
  - Its cost grows with #skeletons × terms. For gg→ggggg FC it would be ≈0.7–2.4×10⁹ complex MAC per point [I], i.e. +0.4–2.4 s on Table 1a. **The FC targets of the gluon-rich rows assume the certified compressions below**, which is why WP2.4 builds the DDM and trace certificates first. The effect is negligible at 6 legs.
- **Certified compressions.** Each needs a loop-level certificate: the model's colour structures in the relevant orders, plus an exact colour-space identity on the smallest instance of the species pattern, plus the cited theorem.

  | Class | Seed per |
  |---|---|
  | Gluon and ghost loops | Ordering σ: Tr(F^{σ₁}…F^{σₙ}) (DDM) [L] |
  | Closed fundamental loops | σ: Tr(T^σ) |
  | One quark line | Quark ring: one class per proportional colour vector of its generalised arc word (WP2.4; the DDM (σ, L/R) q̄q ring [L] is not adopted, see below) |
  | Several quark lines | Generic, or published decompositions when certified [L-s 1111.4193, 1310.0413, 1908.02695] |

  With compression the per-point seed cost for gg→ggggg FC is ≈1.2×10⁸ complex MAC for 4 loop classes × 360 orderings [V-s Design 1 §5.1], about 2 % of dressing.

  The quark-ring compression leaves more classes than the DDM L/R form would. uu~→ggggg keeps 7,620 classes on its 120-element basis, against 2·5! = 240 L/R primitives. Its direct seed cost is therefore ≈7,620 × 120 × 64 helicity rows ≈ 6×10⁷ complex MAC per point, against ≈2×10⁶ for L/R [I]. That is about 6 % of the modelled FC dressing of that row (9.1×10⁸, Appendix B), before the FFT takes over the 74 certified loop orbits. The L/R re-expression is not adopted because rings already share one row per colour vector and a linear re-expression adds terms per skeleton to the dressing side. WP2.8 revisits it if G1 measures the seed share above that estimate.
- **Proportional classes (WP2.4).** With compression, the seed table also merges classes whose colour vectors are proportional (`colour._ClassKeys`). These are quark rings of different arc words, and generic seeds equal to them. On uu~→ggg this cuts 315 classes to 99.
  - A quark ring is reduced once per relabelling of its external gluon labels (`_ring_relabelling`); keys, tensors and cells of the other members are relabelled. Measured compressed seed tables (Python, exact) [M]: uu~→gggg 828 classes on a 24-element basis in 10 s (53 s before); uu~→ggggg 7,620 classes on 120 elements from 103,770 skeletons in 176 s plus 11 s for the 74 certified FFT loop orbits (more than 40 min before; stopped).
- **Certificate predicate (WP2.4, interface to WP2.8).** `SeedTable.certified(combination)` is true when every class of a seed is a `Primitive` whose compression is certified in the table; `ColourSeeds.certified` applies it per skeleton and is what `ReferenceExecutor.run(..., certified=...)` takes to admit schedule M at NLC and full colour. All gg→gg skeletons pass, uu~→dd~g only partly. The Python reference executor's M still falls back to B on its structural check, because seed contents are not colour-ordered; the native schedule M splits certified skeletons into colour-ordered paths instead (`loops/v2/merged.py`, §5.6).
- **Closed-fermion orientation folding** (Tr(σ) + (−1)ⁿ Tr(σᵀ) in one class) is used only when the attached vertices carry a C-parity certificate.
  - It is not used with Z or W attachments (judge finding 11). Those classes keep both orientations, and their dressing doubles.
  - The counts of Table 1a assume folding for the closed quark loops with QCD attachments (certified for loop_sm QCD vertices). Without folding, FC dressing of the gluon processes would be ≈1.75× (gluon loop plus three closed-quark classes doubled, with a massive b).
- **Native seed stage (WP3.1 Rust migration B, `work/v2-native-seeds`; RUST-MIGRATION.md §3.B).** The seed table is built in `rusticol-core` `loop_v2::generate::{seeds, networks, arena}`; the Python stage (`colour.build_seed_table` / `extend_seed_table` with `native_seeds.NATIVE` off, `native_seeds.python_oracle()`) is its oracle.
  - *Switch* (rule 1): one dispatch at the top of `colour.build_seed_table` and `colour.extend_seed_table` (`colour._native_seeds`), so the main slice, the poles companion, the LC/NLC prune and the observables' prune all take the native stage. Rule 2: the native stage covers every input the Python stage covers, so **no feature keeps a per-request Python fallback**; any native failure fails closed.
  - *Per-skeleton work* (B2/B3, phase 0): combinations, certificate bookkeeping, class keys, ratios, rows and `class_of`, class orders, the prune liveness. Python keeps what is per distinct object: each content's compression (`CompressedColour._content`), the certificate check on the smallest instance and the cells (`seed_cells`, package C).
  - *Exact reductions in-crate* (`networks.rs`, phase 1): every distinct canonical quark ring (`_reduced_primitive`) and every generic seed (`SeedColour.seed`, the ring of the contents' `CompressedColour._exact_network`s; each content's network handed over as a product of distinct factors, its relabelled vertex structure and its attached blocks' words, `native_seeds._Networks.factored`, expanded by `seeds::factored_networks`) is reduced natively, a faithful port of `reduce_network` (`_join_strings`, `_Numbering`, `_fierz`, `_decode`, the 1/sqrt(2) parity and `ConnectedColorTensor`'s normal form) in exact rationals with unbounded integer Fierz coefficients (symbolica `Integer`, a machine word until it needs more: no size cap, as the oracle's Python integers), in parallel per need into an order-preserving collect (each result depends on its need only). The ring tensors fill `_reduced_primitive`'s cache (`colour._NATIVE_REDUCED`), so the cells read them (the Python `_PrimitiveCells` and package C's `NativeCells`, both through `Primitive.tensor` / `_reduced_primitive`); `native_seeds.NATIVE_REDUCTIONS` off calls the Python reductions instead. No Symbolica licence call; Idenso/Spenso are not used because the stage's oracle is this trace algebra and its output must be identical.
  - *Plan deviation, for the coordinator's ruling* (requirements audit of `ff8b1b0e`): RUST-MIGRATION §3.B (B3) and PLAN I10/§3.4 keep the exact ring reductions in Python, batched, and rule 4 routes colour algebra beyond counting through Idenso/Spenso. Measured: the Python reductions alone take 33.7-37.7 s at uu~→ggggg FC (stage runs `p1_uu7_fc`, `s5_uu7_fc`, `s5_uu7_fc_m2` under `workspace/tmp/native-seeds/oracle/`), above B's 20 s share of I10, against 0.36 s in-crate (`i10h_uu7_fc`); at gg→ggggg FC the companion's 43,200 generic seeds take 2.7 s in-crate. `reduce_network` is pyAmpliCol's own `Fraction` trace algebra (`pyamplicol.color.contraction_trace`), with no Idenso/Spenso/Symbolica call, so the port reproduces the oracle's normal form exactly; Spenso/Idenso would give another normal form and need a canonicalisation the Python stage does not have. Not in §3.B's list: `networks.rs`, `NATIVE_REDUCTIONS` (default on) and the three-line hook in the read-only `colour._reduced_primitive` with its `_NATIVE_REDUCED` dict (rule 3: the oracle path is unchanged while the dict is empty, which `python_oracle()` ensures, also emptying the cache; past `_reduced_primitive`'s 2^14 entries an evicted ring is recomputed by `reduce_network`, equal and slower). Either ratify them as B's (no code change), or set `NATIVE_REDUCTIONS = False` (the plan's form; I10 at uu~→ggggg then fails B's share). Package C's `cells.rs` Fierz recursion (`fierz_laurent`) contracts closed traces into N_c Laurent polynomials, a different output from `networks.rs`' open-leg tensor normal form; sharing their trace-shape step is a consolidation once both have integrated.
  - *B1, the retained arena* (`arena.rs`): `V2Generation` keeps every schedule build (`Retained`: the colour input, every skeleton of every scheduled family, the interning state, view and tries) under a handle; `generator_input.NativeBuild` keeps the Python objects decoded from it. (i) The seed stage reads a build's readings (`reference_executor.reading_skeletons`) in-crate (`SeedBuilder.from_generation`) once `NativeBuild.readings_of` has checked, by identity of the content objects, that the skeletons are exactly those readings; any other list goes as bytes tables. (ii) The LC/NLC prune rebuilds the schedules from the retained skeletons of the last complete build (`schedules::rebuild`): the keep callback is evaluated on the retained skeleton objects, or the keep flags come per reading from the native liveness (`generator_input.ReadingKeep`, `NativeSeedStage.reading_keep`: B3's liveness with the slice's degrees); the rebuilt plan reuses the retained objects (one decode per build and rebuild, counted in `NativeGeneration.decodes`, rule 9). Production `_prune_colour` passes its Python callback (it owns the slice that the native liveness reads); `native_seeds.prune_keep(slice_, observables)` returns the native flags of the stage that read the slice's complete build in-crate (or None, then the callback applies), so the switch is one line of `_prune_colour`'s owner: `keep=native_seeds.prune_keep(self, observables) or keep` in its native `schedules` call (tested equal to the callback's plan, with Python and with package C's native degrees). (iii) `NativeBuild.max_rank()` answers `MonomialLayout`'s rank bound (`Retained::max_rank`).
  - *Frame cost from the legs' helicity rows* (genericity audit m4; DECISIONS 2026-10-26 ~00:20 item 3): the native cheapest-frame policy (`schedules.rs` `FramePolicy::Cheapest { leg_rows }`) prices a segment by the product of its legs' helicity rows (checked `u128`), `generator_input.leg_rows(blocks)` counts each leg's helicities in the request's own enumeration (its source blocks' rows: 1 for a scalar, 3 for a massive vector, 2 or 5 for spin 2) and `generator_input.frame_rows(rows)` is the Python twin for `cheapest`; `NativeGeneration.schedules(leg_rows=...)` takes them (tested equal to the Python policy, `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_frame_rows.py`). **Wired (generation-scale-2, in its merge of the slice's `7e3841f3`):** the production call sites (`assemble.py` `tree_program`'s `schedules()` and `_scaleless_companion`) take `leg_rows(blocks)` in both engines (`_frame_policy(rows)` in Python; the patch `workspace/tmp/native-seeds/r3/frame_rows_wiring.patch` as written). **Required (sdk-parity, second genericity audit m8):** no count per leg is assumed: `FramePolicy::Cheapest` has no default for `leg_rows` (a payload without it is refused at parse) and `NativeGeneration._policy` refuses a cheapest policy without them; `assemble._FRAME_POLICY` (two rows per leg) is deleted and the tests build their Python oracle with `_frame_policy(leg_rows(blocks))` (the control-model tests, whose legs are massless, pass two rows per leg explicitly). Plans are byte-identical (every production call already passed the counts).
  - *Replay inputs on request:* `generator_input.KEEP_REPLAY_INPUTS` (off) keeps `NativeGeneration.input_json` and the seed stage's `spec_text`, `reduce_inputs` and `reductions_text` for subprocess replays (tests); production keeps none of them.
  - *Dependencies on `assemble.py` internals (owner generation-scale-2):* the native prune liveness (`NativeSeedStage.keep`) reads `LoopSlice._born_grades()` (as `plan_export` does), and the seven-gluon nightly seed test's cheap setup calls `_field_level_table`, `_field_level_families`, `_born_block_requests` and `_born_table`. A rename there breaks them; public accessors (the Born grades, a reachability setup) would remove the coupling.

  - *Status (phase 1, on slice `392d3f4a`, re-checked on `4e9262b5` and `79f2a5bd`)* [M]: the switch is on by default and every oracle comparison is equal; the equality tests run the native stage first on empty reduction caches, so its cells read the natively reduced rings. The seven-leg S5 digests below are unchanged with loop_sm recompiled after model-determinism (`4e9262b5`), whose vertex terms are ordered differently. Default tier: three 4-5-leg controls and g g > g g at LC (20 s). `loop_physics`: the slice at FC, LC and NLC (S5 of the main slice and the poles companion, the prune, plan bytes with `colour_contraction="direct"`) and the observables' prune. `loop_nightly`: six legs, and the seven-gluon seed test with the cheap setup (124 s; it took 15-25 min). Seven legs, stage-isolated sites-form runs: the S5 digests of the build and the extension equal the stored Python ones at gg→ggggg FC/LC and uu~→ggggg FC/NLC, main slice and companion. B1's rebuild from the retained build equals a fresh filtered build (S4 and S5) at gg→ggggg LC (main prune 16.7 s against 29.9 s) and uu~→ggggg LC. With the native liveness instead of the callback, the gg→ggggg LC rebuild takes 4.9 s of flags (mostly reading the Python cells' nonzero powers) plus 2.5 s, against 10.4 s. **I10, joint with package C's native cells** (stage-isolated runs `i10h_{uu7,gg7}_fc` on the scratch merge `fcb5d301` = native-seeds `51e73d23` × native-cells `8ef63961` + slice `4e9262b5`, seed-stage code identical to native-cells `6b5013d8`; pinned cores 192-203): the whole seed stage (main slice plus companion) takes 8.20 s at uu~→ggggg and 16.18 s at gg→ggggg FC; B's part (stage minus cells) is 6.13 s and 12.22 s. With the unbounded Fierz coefficients (native `loop-native-native-seeds-737983bf`, B alone, `r3_uu7_fc`, `r3_gg7_fc`, `r3_gg7_generic_all`): the uu~→ggggg S5 digests (main and companion) and the gg→ggggg main digest equal the stored Python ones, all 43,200 generic seeds of the gg→ggggg companion equal `SeedColour.seed` (2.69 s in-crate against 260.9 s in Python), and B's part is 5.95 s and 11.2 s (reductions 0.36 s and 2.69 s, unchanged). ### 7.3 Seeds per point and FFT

U0 = A0† K. Leading colour is diagonal.

- FC/NLC use direct exact-cast contraction, or the existing rectangular symmetric-group FFT where certified orbits exist (`color/symmetric_group.py:362`; Rust `recurrence/symmetric_group_fft.rs` [V-s]).
- The `auto | direct | fft` policy and the reported algorithm are kept (MASTER_PLAN §2.16).
- DoubleFloat and arbitrary precision use exact direct contraction.
- Correlated FFT stays unsupported, as today.
- **Implemented (WP2.4).** `loops/v2/colour.seed_fourier` finds the orbits of S_m on the external adjoint labels (all of them, or all but one when every coloured leg is adjoint) directly on the literal Born tensors and the seed classes: regular Born orbits, and complete loop orbits whose stabilisers carry signs (an adjoint ring is fixed up to (−1)ⁿ by its reflection). Each Born/loop orbit pair is certified on every exact cell, K[class(g), b(h)]·sign_g = k(h⁻¹∘g). Other pairs stay direct. The native `V2SeedFourier` (`rust/loop-reduction/src/runtime/v2_seeds.rs`, binding `_loop.V2SeedFourier`) contracts the certified pairs for a batch of helicity rows with `SymmetricGroupFftPlan`. Kernels are projected exactly per sector degree P and Born grade, then rounded once.
  - The slice's `SliceRequest.colour_contraction` defaults to `auto`: binary64 at NLC/FC, with `PointResult.colour_contraction` reporting the algorithm. `fft` requires the FFT: it refuses a table without a certified pair and LC at generation, and it refuses, instead of silently contracting directly, every evaluation of the Python stage that contracts seeds directly (exact dressing, arbitrary precision, observable columns at `ObservableSlice.of`). Under `auto`, the Python stage's exact dressing, DoubleFloat and arbitrary precision stay direct.
- **Native executor (M-QCD MQ-FFT, user 2026-10-28 ~15:35/~15:40).** The production executor contracts the certified pairs with the FFT in its own carrier (`rusticol-core` `loop_v2::fourier`, the Python stage the oracle):
  - *Record.* `plan_export.fourier_record` maps `slice.fourier`'s orbits into the plan, structurally: per job (weight column of the slice's own table over every basis element, i.e. the sector columns; Born order; loop order; Born N_c grade) the plan's Born component of each Born-orbit element (-1: no seed weight reads it, its amplitude is zero for the FFT), the plan's seed-class record and sign of each loop-orbit element (-1: no seed key reads the class at that order) and the certified pairs whose projected kernel is nonzero. It holds no weights. The plan v4 is unchanged (bytes equal under every policy); the record travels beside it (`ExportedPlan.fourier`, `V2Executor(fourier=)`) and is stored in the native process record (`native_process`, key `fourier`, the companion's under `scaleless`), which the SDKs' Rust runtime reads too (`v2_runtime`).
  - *Certification at load* (`SeedFourier::load`). The kernel k(x) of a pair is read from the plan's exact weights of one recorded class (k(h⁻¹∘g₀) = s(g₀) K[c(g₀), b(h)]), and every recorded class of the orbit is checked on every Born element, K[c(g), b(h)] s(g) = k(h⁻¹∘g), exactly (rationals); a record that fails is refused. The covered (record, column, component) weights then leave the direct seed classes; the FFT adds s(g)·w(g) at the first element g of each class, w = Σ_a f_a ⋆ k_al with f_a(h) = conj(A0_{b_a(h)}). FFT and direct are equal before rounding by construction, whatever produced the record.
  - *Transform.* The tree engine's `SymmetricGroupFftPlan` supplies the subgroup tower (Young's orthogonal form, crate-visible levels with the integer axial distance of each generator); the forward and inverse transforms are rewritten generically over the carrier (real lanes: Re and Im of each SIMD lane), with the Young factors 1/r and sqrt(1 − 1/r²) and the inverse weights d_λ/m! formed in the carrier, so binary64, double-double and MPFR contract at their own precision (`F[f ⋆ k] = F[f] F[k]`, one block product per pair). Tasks of 16 Born helicity rows per job run in parallel, the rows folded into the transform lanes (one tower walk for 16 rows × 2L reals); the pairs of a job are grouped per loop orbit at load. The exact carrier (numerators only, no square roots) contracts directly.
  - *Policy.* `V2Options.contraction` (`Contraction::{Auto, Direct, Fft}`): `auto` runs the FFT where the executor has a record, `direct` never, `fft` requires one; the report counts `symmetric-group-fft seed jobs`, from which `PointResult.colour_contraction` and the public `VirtualColorContraction` (`regular_kernels` = certified pairs) report the algorithm. `NativeExecutor` takes the request's policy (`fft` without a record is refused at construction); the certified-scaleless companion runs `auto` (or `direct`). Stored observable column sets keep the direct contraction (their own seed records). A forced FFT the FFT cannot serve (no certified pair, LC, declared observables) is the listed generation refusal `forced-seed-fft-unserved` (`refusals.LOOP_ADMISSION_REFUSALS`, remedy `auto`), never a silent direct contraction.
  - *Public route.* `color.contraction = "symmetric-group-fft"` maps to `fft` (the v2 FFT needs no colour declaration at full colour); `LoopV2ExecutionMetadata.color_contraction` is `auto | direct | fft`. `auto` is not tuned per process: every certified pair takes the FFT.
  - *Oracle of the contraction* (`V2Executor.seed_values`, `NativeExecutor.seed_values`): the seeds U0 per point and seed key under `auto`, `direct` or `fft`, in every floating carrier. FFT and direct sum the same exact weights times the same Born components, so they agree to the contraction's own rounding with no reduction behind it; the virtual's comparison bounds the amplified difference only through the stability estimates.
  - *Evidence* (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_seed_fft.py`): the seeds FFT = direct normwise per point within 2⁷ u in binary64 and double-double, and the virtual within both evaluations' rounding envelope (10 (E_fft + E_direct) + 16 u S per Laurent coefficient; Born and local components bitwise), on g g > g g and u u~ > g g g (default tier, full colour), g g > g g at NLC, and nightly g g > g g g, g g > g g g g, u u~ > g g g g, g g > t t~ g g, g g > u u~ d d~ (two quark lines, S_2), g g > g g g g g (S_6) and u u~ > g g g g g (S_5); native FFT = the Python stage's FFT; fallback (no certified pair: u u~ > d d~ g, and the three-quark-line u u~ > d d~ s s~, which has no external gluon; a three-line process with an orbit needs two external gluons, 2 → 6) reported direct; forced FFT in binary64, DD and MPFR; a record that does not certify is refused; the public route and the SDK runtime run and report the FFT.
  - *Campaign* (MQ-FFT, 2026-10-28, native `loop-native-mq-fft-b8109e3a`; G1 stream points, `validate2.py` in `workspace/tmp/mq-fft/`; worst |FFT − direct| over the envelope bound above, binary64 / double-double, points in brackets): g g > g g 0.098 [128] / 0.010 [4]; g g > g g g 0.039 [128] / 0.0061 [4], NLC 0.029 [128] / 0.0027 [4]; g g > g g g g 0.025 [32] / 0.013 [2]; g g > g g g g g 0.013 [4] / 0.0041 [1] (DD relative difference 2.7·10⁻²⁶ at E_DD = 5.8·10⁻²⁵; one DD point is 13 min per evaluation on 24 threads); u u~ > g g g 0.052 [128] / 0.0032 [4], NLC 0.036 [64] / 0.0022 [2]; u u~ > g g g g 0.017 [32] / 0.0082 [2]; u u~ > g g g g g 0.0017 [8] / 0.0008 [1]; g g > t t~ g g 0.015 [32] / 0.0011 [2]. Seeds alone (`seeds.py`, native `loop-native-mq-fft-e640d36b`; max |U0_fft − U0_direct| / max |U0| per point in units of u, against the tests' 2⁷): binary64 at 8 points ≤ 7.3 up to S_5 and 17.6 at S_6 (g g > g g g g g), 8.5 for u u~ > g g g g g; double-double at 2 points ≤ 0.81 up to S_5, 0.95 for u u~ > g g g g g and 2.4 at S_6. Per seed key (relative to the key's own largest value) the same differences reach 780 u, since a key can be a small difference of large terms. The nightly seven-leg ids take 18 min together.
  - *Cost per class at B = 128* (no gate; G1 stream points, binary64, poles none, no checks, one pinned thread, one round, indicative: shared host under load, user 2026-10-28 ~17:05; `cost.py`, native `loop-native-mq-fft-b8109e3a`; seconds per batch of 128, FFT / direct):

    | Process | S_m | pairs | weights covered / plan | seed contraction | whole evaluation |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | g g > g g | S_3 | 2 | 54 / 54 | 0.0009 / 0.0003 | 0.30 / 0.21 |
    | g g > g g g | S_4 | 2 | 864 / 864 | 0.010 / 0.0076 | 2.73 / 2.55 |
    | g g > g g g g | S_5 | 2 | 21 600 / 21 600 | 0.18 / 0.24 | 47.7 / 50.3 |
    | g g > g g g g g | S_6 | 2 | 777 600 / 777 600 | 8.3 / 17.3 | 1 229 / 1 221 |
    | u u~ > g g g | S_3 | 19 | 540 / 972 | 0.011 / 0.0057 | 0.69 / 0.80 |
    | u u~ > g g g g | S_4 | 40 | 18 408 / 37 344 | 0.38 / 0.31 | 10.6 / 10.2 |
    | u u~ > g g g g g | S_5 | 74 | 862 680 / 1 918 320 | 22.2 / 30.0 | 218 / 231 |
    | g g > t t~ g g | S_4 | 40 | 18 408 / 18 408 | 0.21 / 0.19 | 12.7 / 13.0 |
    | g g > u u~ d d~ | S_2 | 2 340 | 8 520 / 8 552 | 0.24 / 0.09 | 4.96 / 4.71 |
    | u u~ > d d~ g (fallback) | — | 0 | 0 / 324 | 0.0025 / 0.0025 (both direct) | 0.70 / 0.80 |

    The seed contraction is a small part of the evaluation at every multiplicity (13 % of the direct u u~ > g g g g g, 1.4 % of g g > g g g g g, whose dressing takes 605 s of the 1 221 s), so the whole evaluation is unchanged within noise. The FFT contracts faster from S_5 on (1.3× at S_5, 2.1× at S_6, where the direct sum reads 777 600 weights per Born row) and slower than the direct sum for small groups and many small pairs (S_2, S_3: at most 3 % of the evaluation, g g > u u~ d d~ with 2 340 pairs of S_2). `auto` is not tuned per process (user ~15:40): every certified pair takes the FFT. The g g > g g g g g row's crude per-component difference at B = 128 (cost.py's maximum over components of |FFT − direct| / max |coefficient|: 15.5) comes from stream points where binary64 itself fails: at its three worst points (89, 93, 95 of `tools/loop_benchmark/streams/gg_ggggg.json`, `worst.py`) the binary64 estimates are E = 135, 3.7 and 3.9, and |FFT − direct| is 0.6 %, 1.1 % and 0.7 % of the envelope bound. Paired A/B of the direct path, slice native `5301e24a` against this native (one core, alternating, two rounds; medians at B = 128): u u~ > d d~ g −2 %, u u~ > g g g +9 %/+10 % (head auto/direct; noise: the direct path's code is unchanged), g g > g g g +2 %/−1 %, g g > t t~ g g 0 %/+1 %; again with the final native `loop-native-mq-fft-e640d36b` (two rounds): −10 %/−3 %, −2 %/+4 %, +1 %/−4 %, +1 %/+2 %: no regression (rule ~17:05: > 25 % and reproduced).
  - `auto` has no size criterion, like the tree engine's `auto` (`tests/unit/test_automatic_fft_contraction.py`): every certified pair goes through the FFT, gg→gg's S_3 included. A measured threshold against the native direct kernel belongs to WP2.1 and G1. The Python slice keeps the projected kernels and the native plans per (sector degree P, Born grade) across points, so only the Born rows are transformed per point; WP2.1 builds them once per process as well.
  - Orbits found:
    - gg→gg and gg→ggg: one Born orbit and two loop orbits (adjoint rings and closed traces).
    - uu~→ggg: 19 loop orbits cover every cell.
    - gg→ggggg: S_6, with 1 080 classes in two orbits.

### 7.4 Projection

For each virtual squared-order sector:

1. P is the maximum N_c degree over all non-zero exact cells: seed cells for every class contributing to the sector, CT cells (with CT colour grades, `physical_color.split_color_grades` [V-s]) and field cells. P is structural and independent of helicity, ε and numerics.
2. LC keeps degree P; NLC keeps P, P−1 and P−2, odd powers included; FC keeps all.
3. N_c = 3 is substituted after projection, and the external average after that. N_f has degree 0.

Born sectors keep independent degrees. Truncation acts on the contracted cells K[c, b], never on a Gram matrix alone (contracts §2.6 caveat). Without a continuation, only FC is available (§6.7).

### 7.5 Leading colour, structurally

LC prunes skeletons before any numerics, by the face rule of the leading-colour report §4.1 [V-s, verified there against exact SU(N_c) overlaps].

| Skeleton class | Maximal degree | Kept at |
|---|---|---|
| Planar gluon or ghost ring of a leading Born word | P | LC |
| q̄q adjacent, gluons on the gluon side (A^L, p = 2); quark arc in a colourless gap | P | LC |
| Four-quark box with crossed colour, gluon outside or on a gluon arc | P | LC |
| Closed quark loop (any mass class) | P−1 | NLC |
| Flow overlap differing by one quark transposition (identical flavours) | P−1 | NLC (in seeds) |
| Two-sided ring / double trace; A^R; abelian vertex correction across an emission; gluon on a quark arc inside a four-quark box; uncrossed four-quark box | P−2 | NLC |

**Implementation.**

- **Pruning.** The trie builder applies the local rules: word admissibility through the tree engine's LC segment tables (`generation/dag_color.py:186-243, 1041-1076` [V-s]) extended by cut-terminated segments; the arc rule; no closed quark loops; no U(1) or Fierz-singlet currents. Each surviving class's degree is then certified exactly from its seed table.
- **Seeds.** LC seeds are diagonal: U0 = N_c^P·κ·conj(A0(σ, h)). There is no interference between orderings and no Gram matrix.
- **Strict-LC Born.** The strict LC Born uses the graded Fierz and U(1) pieces (§3.2 item 4).
- **Counts** in the providers' configuration [M `revision/mb0_all.log`; massive-b values in `otf/run_all.log`, `lcs/lc_structure_counts.log`]:

| Process | LC orderings | LC / FC skeletons | LC/FC dressing (structured) | LC/FC families | LC/FC time (Table 1a recipe) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| gg→gggg | 60 | 3,060 / 9,180 | 0.35 | 0.50 | 0.37–0.42 |
| gg→ggggg | 360 | 40,680 / 122,040 | 0.35 | 0.50 | 0.37–0.42 |
| uu~→ggggg | 120 | 13,560 / 91,440 | 0.18 | 0.52 | 0.22–0.28 |
| uu~→e⁻e⁺ggg | 6 | 306 / 3,990 | 0.12 | 0.35 | 0.13–0.14 |
| uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g | 4 | 144 / 2,484 | 0.19 | 0.47 | 0.21–0.23 |
| gg→tt̄gg | 24 | 1,224 / 6,888 | 0.26 | 0.35 | 0.26–0.27 |
| gg→tt̄e⁻e⁺g (QCD) | 6 | 306 / 3,990 | 0.15 | 0.28 | 0.14–0.15 |

With a massive b the gluon-process ratios are 0.26 (dressing), 0.33 (families) and 0.27–0.30 (time), because FC then has one more closed-quark class. The inherited engine measures LC/FC ≈ 1, because it projects after computing everything (`colour-measurements.json` [V-s]).

The same face rule makes seeds composition-independent within an ordering, so schedule M is valid at LC without any decomposition theorem [I] (§2.2).

**Native per-point inputs at LC (package lc-perf, 25 Oct; measured).** With the WP3.1 native inputs (`4e9b5a9e`) LC was not cheaper than FC per point on five G1 rows (LC/FC 0.98–1.28, slower on four) and slower on gg→tt̄e⁻e⁺g (QCD, 1.21) and uu~→ggggg (1.19): the LC dressing was a third of FC's, but its trees, Born and counterterm inputs cost more than FC's (gg→gggg: about 930 of 1,033 ms against 650 of 913 ms). Three causes, all in `native_trees.py` and `loop_v2::trees`, none LC-specific code:

1. **Graded counterterm slots.** The LC/NLC counterterm program carries each CT slot per N_c grade of its formal-N_c continuation (`counterterm_slot_order(..., grade)`, §6.7), so it was larger than FC's (gg→gggg: 764,800 against 499,160 interactions), and its closure formed every ket component, including those whose N_c powers lie below what the projection keeps. `prune_program` now drops the ket components no interference can read (`_live_ket`: no overlap cell the projection keeps), the closure terms left empty, then every current that no closure term and no plan block row reads (through other currents too), and the kernels no interaction uses (gg→gggg LC: ket 860 → 240, CT interactions 764,800 → 179,280; FC 499,160 → 240,120). The LC Born metric was already diagonal in the colour orderings (120 cells against FC's 14,400 at gg→gggg) and the LC seeds diagonal (§7.2), so the squaring needed no change.
2. **Whole model blocks as kernel inputs.** Every vertex and propagator kernel of both interaction programs took the whole model block of its state as inputs and copied it per row: 20 of a gluon vertex's 36 inputs in the trees, 161–189 of 177–203 in the counterterm program (its model holds every coupling, multiplicity and slot switch of the request). A kernel now reads the model parameters its expressions hold (tree schema 3: the third entry of a kernel's shape lists their indices into the state's model block, read at state component `model_offset + index`).
3. **Latency-bound closures.** The Born and counterterm root closures ran per point and Born row, each kernel monomial a serial chain of complex products. They now run over the batch in lanes of 8 points and the rest of the batch in lanes of 4, 2 and 1 (so no lane repeats a point; `lane_runs`), the lanes in fixed-size arrays, and for binary64 in AVX-512 or AVX2 when the running CPU has them (chosen at run time, never from the build host).

**Values.** By construction a lane performs one point's operations in `Complex<T>`'s order (products and sums never fused, at every lane width and vector width), a pruned component only ever met zero metric cells, and kept currents sum the same interactions in the same order. Schema 3 is the one change that is not exact by construction: a kernel's canonical stage (`assemble._canonical_stage`) now numbers its positional symbols over fewer model parameters, and Symbolica orders terms by the little-endian bytes of the symbol ids, which follows the position only below 256 symbols. A kernel whose stage had more symbols before could therefore sum its terms in another order, a binary64 rounding change within the LC oracles' tolerance; none was found. Checked bitwise:

- `test_pruned_tree_program_is_bitwise_the_unpruned_one` (LC, NLC, FC: every native input and the evaluation with and without poles; each case's program strictly smaller), `test_native_inputs_do_not_depend_on_the_batch` (eleven points in one batch against each alone), `test_closure_simd_widths_are_bitwise_equal` (below);
- native inputs plus evaluations (poles none and all) of 11-point batches against the slice `19083c10` on its own native: nine pairs up to six legs (gg→ggg LC/FC, uu~→ggg LC/NLC/FC, uu~→dd~g LC/FC, uu~→e⁻e⁺gg LC, gg→tt̄gg LC), the evidence audit's 20 comparisons at gg→gggg and uu~→gggg (LC and FC; binary64, double-double, 40-digit arbitrary precision; poles none and all), and the three 2→5 rows at seven legs, LC and FC (uu~→ggggg, uu~→e⁻e⁺ggg, gg→tt̄e⁻e⁺g QCD; head `ef400de5`, 25 Oct 06:29–06:43 UTC): byte-identical value reprs in all.

**Vector widths after relocation (§0.2).** The binary64 closure kernels take AVX-512 or AVX2 by what the running CPU reports, so a relocated artifact may run a narrower width than its build host. `RUSTICOL_TEST_CLOSURE_SIMD=avx2|baseline` caps the width (never enabling a feature the CPU lacks), and `test_closure_simd_widths_are_bitwise_equal` loads a stored native process (gg→gg LC, uu~→dd~g FC) in fresh interpreters capped at AVX2 and at the baseline: its native inputs and evaluations are bitwise those of the AVX-512 run. The Rust `wide_closure_kernels_are_bitwise_the_baseline` also passes in the release profile (`cargo test --release -p rusticol-core --lib --features loop-v2 loop_v2::trees`, the production build's codegen settings), where the kernels are vectorised.

"Trees" is the native trees, Born and counterterm inputs (report stage `trees`), "loop" seeds, factors, dressing, scatter and reduction.
