At gg→gggg the LC inputs split into the interaction programs 40.4 ms (FC 50.7), the closures 19.7 ms (FC 60.9) and the interferences 0.7 ms (FC 11.4). What is left of the LC inputs: the counterterm program recomputes the plain tree currents the trees' program already holds (2,320 of its 6,736 outputs at uu~→gggg LC, about a sixth of its finite interactions), and the closure kernels are expanded monomial sums (a three-gluon closure: 72 monomials, 288 complex products, where a contracted form needs about 30; a factorised form changes the rounding). The interaction-program kernels are interpreted Symbolica evaluators until the binary64 plane applications of artifact-portability land.

**Lane runs.** A chunk of lanes costs about its instruction count whatever its width (gg→gggg, binary64 closures per chunk of 8 / 4 / 2 / 1 lanes: FC 487 / 449 / 350 / 249 ms, LC 154 / 169 / 125 / 91 ms), so a batch runs in lanes of 8 and its last `points % 8` in one chunk of the narrowest width that holds them (`lane_runs`; padded lanes repeat the chunk's last point and are dropped). B = 128 and B = 1 run as before (re-timed at `43befe28`, 25 Oct 06:57–07:03 UTC during a test sweep, FC / LC ms: gg→gggg 398.6 / 167.0 at B = 128 (LC/FC 0.42) and 3,748 / 1,404 at B = 1 (0.37); uu~→gggg 84.8 / 39.5 (0.47) and 790 / 239 (0.30)); B = 9 at gg→gggg FC takes 764 ms of closures (8 + 1 lanes) against about 974 ms (2 × 487) with a padded second chunk of 8.

**Pruning cost at export (Table 7.5c).** `prune_program` is one linear pass: each closure block's Born rows are marked once, up to the widest slot any term reads on it, the interaction rows are filtered and renumbered in place, and the counterterm record is left untouched when every ket component is read (full colour). Its first implementation appended one read per closure term, slot and Born row (about 14 M at gg→gggg FC) and copied every counterterm term and kept row. The pruned program is identical (JSON and saved kernels equal at gg→gggg and uu~→gggg, LC and FC). The pass stays in Python: at gg→gggg it takes 0.5 s, 2 % of the export (whose median grows by 0.8 s at FC and 0.2 s at LC), with an unchanged peak, and it moves to Rust with `_CountertermTrees.of` (A5), where the counterterm program is built, so that the dropped slots are never emitted.

**Table 7.5c. g g > g g g g export with and without pruning** (s, GB; `NativeExecutor(..., inputs="native")` of the G1 harness after `LoopSlice.generate`; idle pinned cores 176–190, one thread, two paired runs each, 25 Oct 06:08–06:10 UTC; first implementation = the requirements audit's measurement on loaded cores 300–312, and its pass alone re-timed on an idle core on the dumped program).

| Colour | Export, no pruning | Export, pruned | `prune_program` | Export peak, no pruning / pruned | First implementation: pass (loaded / idle), export, peak |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FC | 22.64, 22.96 | 23.71, 23.48 | 0.51, 0.51 | 1.775, 1.774 / 1.772, 1.773 | 12.05–12.4 / 5.54 s; 32.5 → 47.0 s; 1.77 → 2.96 GB |
| LC | 30.99, 30.66 | 30.80, 31.19 | 0.57, 0.54 | 2.008, 2.008 / 2.009, 2.006 | 4.0 / 2.10 s; peak 2.01 → 2.42 GB |

The pass's own traced allocation peak falls from 1,130 to 20 MiB (FC) and from 402 to 21 MiB (LC); at uu~→gggg it takes 0.12 s (FC) and 0.13 s (LC), from 0.32 and 0.20 s.

**Plane kernels and the model subsets (merged with artifact-portability).** The binary64 plane paths of `loop_v2/trees.rs` fill each kernel's own model inputs, as `run_interactions` does: `plane_interactions` and `plane_propagator` take `program.models[kernel]` and fill state components `model_offset + index` in that order, and a propagator's value width is `inputs − 4 − models[kernel].len()`. `load_interactions` reads each kernel's (inputs, outputs, model indices) entry once: the indices must be increasing and inside the model block (`StateLayout::model_block`), and the (inputs, outputs) shapes select the plane or exact kernel per carrier. `export_trees` prunes the program first (`prune_program`) and compiles the plane applications of the kernels it keeps only, so a dropped kernel compiles no plane.

### 7.6 Next-to-leading colour

NLC keeps every class whose **exact** maximal degree over the sector's Born basis is ≥ P − 2, and truncates the cells to degrees P, P−1 and P−2. No class is dropped on a literature-based expectation; the degree always comes from the exact seed table. Structurally:

- **gluon processes:** NLC adds the quark-loop classes (P−1). The double-trace and off-diagonal weights of the same gluon-loop skeletons arrive through truncated seeds.
- **quark lines:** NLC adds A^R, mixed and closed primitives, and every class whose exact degree reaches P−2.

**Literature check (O14).** In BDK's q̄qggg colour sum [L hep-ph/9409393 eq. NLOCrossSection, l.1748-1772]:

- against the leading (N_c²−1)² A_{5;1}, the other two orderings of A_{5;1} and two of the three A_{5;3} terms enter at relative N_c⁻², i.e. degree P−2, inside NLC;
- the remaining A_{5;1}, A_{5;3} and A_{5;4} terms enter at N_c⁻⁴.

WP2.4 tests v2's exact-N_c interference for uu~→ggg against this formula, with the partial amplitudes obtained from resolved-colour seed columns on the BDK colour structures. Revision 1's claim that A_{5;3} enters only at N_c⁻⁴ was a misreading and is withdrawn.

NLC/FC cost ≈ 0.9–1.0: the value of NLC is accuracy, not speed. The tree NLC truncation (`color/contraction_factors.py:100-108`) is a different definition and is not reused for loops [V-s].

**Opt-in, labelled, non-default modes** [L 1209.0100 `njet.tex:529-540`, tab:timedsymgluons l.1231-1251; L-s 0907.1984]:

- separate LC and (FC − LC) outputs for colour-expansion sampling;
- **integrated-equivalent full colour** (NJet's de-symmetrised colour sums). Valid only after integration over a phase space symmetric in identical final-state particles:
  - v2 generates loop seed classes only for the de-symmetrised orderings, and the seeds use the de-symmetrised colour matrix;
  - NJet reports 7 gluons going from 171 to 3.07 s;
  - labelled, never the default, never reported as pointwise FC; evaluated in WP4.7.

### 7.7 Directed colour correlations

V_{αβ,k} = B†K^{αβ}_{BL}L_k + L_k†K^{αβ}_{LB}B, with K_{LB}^{αβ} = (K_{BL}^{βα})† (MASTER_PLAN §2.17).

- Each (history, direction) is a seed column. The second direction is seeded with K^{βα}, reduced, and conjugated after reduction. Results stay complex per history.
- The colour operators come from `color/correlator_matrices.py:295` [V-s], applied on the Born side.
- The complete-family LC/NLC degree conventions (`loop_runtime.rs:13-14` [V-s]) are kept.
- **Cost.** Dressing and reduction scale with 2·N_hist columns: 42 at 7 legs with 21 pairs.
  - **Directed tables by the seed-cell machinery (WP2.5).** A directed table has |classes| × |basis| exact cells K[c, b] = <bra(b)|ket(κ_c)>. When every ket step is a gluon emission (dipoles, Casimirs, ordered emission histories), <bra(b)|ket(κ)> = <ket†bra(b)|κ>: `observables.absorb` returns each emission onto the Born side (the emission on a fresh label, contracted with the emitted one by SU(N_c) Fierz, `colour.reduce_network`), and the operator images ket†bra(b) replace the Born basis in the inclusive cell builder `colour.seed_cells` (primitives by integer cycle counting with the relabelling caches of §7.2, generic classes term-factored). The caches are shared by every table of a slice (`_PrimitiveCells.rebased`), so the tables of a complete family reuse each other's overlaps, and the directed overlaps of counterterm and field cells share their literal structure overlaps (`contract_cached`). A ket with a quark-pair splitting keeps the generic contraction of the connected images (`DirectedColour.generic_table`), which is also the test oracle: the tables are equal cell by cell, exactly, in both directions, for every dipole of g g > g g, d d~ > g g and u u~ > g g g, ordered histories and emitter chains. Full colour builds one table per history (two when it is not symmetric); LC/NLC builds the |F|² tables of the complete family F plus the history's own, and contracts every Born, counterterm and field structure pair of each family table for the family degrees.
  - **Generation cost (measured, Python, exact; one dipole; generic contraction → seed cells):**

    | Process | Classes × basis | FC table | LC complete family (degrees included) |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | gg→ggg | 36 × 24 | 13.2 s → 0.44 s | 309 s → 8.7 s (25 tables) |
    | uu~→ggg | 99 × 11 | 0.5–2.1 s → 0.2–0.7 s | 35 s → 4.0 s (25 tables) |
    | gg→gggg | 180 × 120 | 1,110 s → 5.9 s | 349 s (36 tables; 1,256 s without the shared literal overlaps) |
    | uu~→gggg | 828 × 50 | T12 42 s → 7.2 s; T34 167 s → 29 s | 156 s (36 tables; 186–253 s without them) |
    | gg→ggggg (tables only: the 1,080 primitive classes against the 720 single traces, no slice) | 1,080 × 720 | 115 s (both directions) | 1,348 s for the 49 dipole-family tables, 0.3 GB |

    The seven-leg row is measured on the pure-gluon classes and basis built directly (`Primitive` adjoint rings and fundamental traces of every ordering), since the Python slice generator does not produce 2→5 QCD plans (WP3.1). Not measured, estimated [I]: uu~→ggggg (7,620 quark-ring classes × 120) needs about 5–30 min per FC table from the six-leg per-cell costs (0.17–0.71 ms per cell, ×3 for the larger rings), and its LC/NLC family about 1.5–6 h with the measured six-leg cache sharing (about four tables' worth of work per table); the LC/NLC family degrees, whose counterterm and field overlaps grow with |F|² × Born structures × counterterm structures, are estimated at hours at seven legs (gg→gggg: most of its 349 s; its 36 tables alone take 52 s on the six-gluon classes built the same way). Full colour is therefore no longer a blocker for gg→ggggg (2 min per dipole, both directions), and is one for the full dipole set of uu~→ggggg only through those estimated hours; the remaining seven-leg cost of the LC/NLC families (tables and degrees) and of uu~→ggggg moves with the seed tables into the Rust generator (proposed for WP3.1's goal and acceptance, WP2.5 open list; not yet confirmed).
  - Above the break-even, a generation-time cost model switches to schedule 3: F with amplitude-level (h, b) closures, plus a transposed NinjaRS reduction that returns each family's integrals of all monomials once (request 2 in §11.5). Every observable then becomes a dot product.
  - The break-even is ≈15–20 columns at 6 legs and ≈45–60 at 7 legs [M/I Designs 1 and 2].
  - Schedule 3 is adopted only after a measured prototype, because the owner's transposition study is a symbolic instruction count, not a timing (§11.5).

### 7.8 Caps

The loop path never passes `max_sectors` (`color/plan_build.py:21` [V]), and the 12-gluon/int64 guard of `color/adjoint_kernel.py` is removed (WP2.4). Direct contraction is always available when FFT eligibility fails.

Verified for WP2.4 [V]:
- `loops/v2` calls neither `color/plan_build.py` nor `color/adjoint_kernel.py`. Its Born plan is the tree engine's trace basis, and no caller in the repository passes `max_sectors`.
- The seed FFT has no degree bound. It enumerates S_m only when |S_m| ≤ the Born basis size, the structural condition for a regular orbit, and contracts directly otherwise.
- The native plan's own bound is its factorial and `u32` index storage.
- `color/adjoint_kernel.py` has no gluon cap. The dense DDM kernel ((n−2)! entries, sweeps over S_{n−1}) uses int64 while its exact bound N_c^n·4^{n−2} fits, and exact Python integers otherwise. Above `DENSE_ADJOINT_GLUONS = 12` an overlap is evaluated on its own (`ladder_overlap`: both ladders' 2^{n−2} traces, integer cycle counting, about 6 s per entry at n = 13), so the tree DDM plan no longer refuses more than 12 gluons. The automatic tree basis choice still prefers the trace basis above that size; this is a resource preference, not a cap. Tests: every dense entry for n ≤ 6 at N_c = 2, 3, 5 equals `ladder_overlap` (all three accuracies), and the object fallback and a 13-gluon entry are checked.

### 7.9 Native colour cells (WP3.1 package C, `RUST-MIGRATION.md` §3.C)

The exact colour-cell stages run in rusticol-core (`loop_v2::generate::cells`, `loop_v2::dipole_orbits`; PyO3 `v2_colour.rs`; glue `loops/v2/native_cells.py`, `loops/v2/dipoles.py`). Every native stage equals its Python stage exactly, which stays the oracle (HANDOFF §0.4). No Symbolica state or licence is touched: exact numbers are `symbolica::domains::{integer, rational}` only (checked statically and by a no-key subprocess, `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_native_cells.py`).

**Switches (rule 1: one flag per stage, dispatched inside the Python stage).** `native_cells.NATIVE_STAGES` (default: all five; `native_cells.native_stages(())` runs the oracle lane):

| Stage | Switch site | What runs natively |
|---|---|---|
| `"cells"` | `colour.seed_cells` (the slice's, the companion's and the prune's tables); `observables.DirectedColour._table` | `CellEngine::cells`: adjoint rings by face counts of their U(N_c) expansion, fundamental traces and quark rings (their reduced canonical tensors, `_reduced_primitive`) by exact contraction, generic classes term-factored (each distinct term structure contracted once per basis tensor, a class's cells combined from those rows in parallel; equal values share one cell, so the seven-gluon companion's 7.3 M cells hold a few hundred distinct values); relabelling caches on relabelled bras. `_PrimitiveCells.native()` is the twin engine; rebased copies share its caches both ways. Quark-ring seeds are canonicalised (`colour._ring_relabelling`) once per basis (`native_cells._relabellings`, dropped with the basis), so a table rebuilt on the same basis and classes (the degrees', the liveness', the LC prune's) does not repeat it. Directed tables: the operator images ket†bra(b) of a history whose steps are gluon emissions are formed natively (`cells::operator_image`: `connect`, then `absorb` by the completeness relation τ^a_ij τ^a_kl = δ_il δ_kj − δ_ij δ_kl/N_c, generators normalised to Tr(τ^aτ^b) = δ^ab); a Born-side quark-pair splitting keeps the oracle's images (`DirectedColour.operator`) with native cells; a loop-side splitting keeps `DirectedColour.generic_table` (Python, the reference route of §7.7) |
| `"degrees"` | one dispatch in `LoopSlice.degrees` (inclusive metric; `_prune_colour` and the companion read it) | the key sets are collected as the oracle collects them (`native_cells._DegreeKeys`; the CT program's closure terms, millions at seven legs over a few thousand shared colours, grouped by (colour, coupling or whole-rule grades, grade, orders) and expanded once per colour, cached on the slice for its program and coupling layout so the inclusive degrees and every complete family collect them once), then every overlap degree and the seed-table degrees in one batch (`cells::overlap_degrees` with the relabelling orbits, `OrderedTable::degree`) |
| `"orbits"` | `assemble._AdjointOrbits.__new__` | the relabelling classes and `rename` (`cells::AdjointOrbits`); `site_key` stays the oracle's |
| `"dipoles"` | one dispatch in `LoopSlice.dipoles` | `dipoles.build` + `dipoles.operators` (§12.4, "Dipole operators per relabelling orbit") |
| `"family"` | `ObservableSlice._family_degrees` | every history pair's directed table (above) and overlap degrees of the complete family in native batches: `cells::directed_overlap_degrees` (literal Born-key and ket structures, the images formed natively per pair) or, for pairs with a quark-pair splitting, `cells::tensor_overlap_degrees` on the oracle's connected images |

The liveness of `_prune_colour` stays Python (no switch line is given to it; `native_cells.live_classes` is its tested twin). `BornBasis.gram` and `contract_colour` keep their Python bodies (twins `native_cells.gram`, `contract_many`).

**Copies to keep in step (rule 3; `RUST-MIGRATION.md` §5.3 item 3).** The `"degrees"` and `"family"` stages collect their key sets in Python copies of the oracles' key rules, and both stages are on by default, so an edit to an oracle's key rules has no production effect until the copy follows it. The owners of the oracles edit both, and the oracle tests (QCD controls only: an electroweak field-insertion or closure-term rule change is not caught by them) compare the two:

| Copy (`native_cells`) | Oracle (owner) | Rules copied |
|---|---|---|
| `_DegreeKeys` | `assemble.LoopSlice.degrees` (generation-scale-2) | Born keys with the couplings' ε⁰ grades; one counterterm site per relabelling orbit (`_AdjointOrbits.site_key`), its `_terms_of` keys with whole-rule or coupling grades; field-insertion queries per sector (`fields`, `field_names`, the Born order and grade offsets) |
| `_closure_keys` | `LoopSlice.degrees`' loop over `counterterm_program.closure.terms` (generation-scale-2) | whole-rule grades plus the item grade, coupling grades of `LAURENT_POWERS` from the scalar plan's layout |
| `family_degrees`, `_FamilyBatch` | `observables.ObservableSlice._family_degrees` (WP2.5), i.e. `LoopSlice.degrees(overlaps=pair.overlap, table=pair.table)` per pair | the complete family's history pairs (`product(family, repeat=2)`, one `DirectedColour` each) and the maximum over pairs; per pair the queries `LoopSlice.degrees` forms without relabelling orbits (`_DegreeKeys(orbits=False)`) on the pair's images: operator images formed natively for gluon-emission histories, the oracle's `operator` / `_image` otherwise |

**Rule 2 (fail closed).** No stage has a per-request Python fallback: a native refusal raises. The one known refusal where the oracle returns a value is oracle defect 3 (a primitive class against a Born tensor with an open quark string: `color.connections.overlap_traces` drops the unglued bra string; strict xfail `test_python_adjoint_ring_against_a_quark_bra_is_refused`); no control reaches it (adjoint rings and fundamental traces arise only on pure-adjoint Born bases there). The routes that stay Python by construction (not fallbacks of a switched stage): Born-side splittings' images, loop-side splittings' generic tables.

**Rule 9 (decodes).** Consumer table: each seed or directed table built during generation decodes its own cells once (an extension only its new classes) for the Python consumers (`_prune_colour`'s liveness, `SeedTable` users, the exporter); the degrees decode no cell; the dipole operators decode their entries only when expanded (`LoopSlice.dipoles`, counted per entry). `native_cells.decoded_cells()` counts; the test asserts the generation's count equals the cells of the slice's and the companion's tables.

**C2: the batched seed-record projection for D's `_Exporter.seed_records`.** `native_cells.project_records(slice_, columns, seeds, born_components, tables=None) -> (class_json: bytes, records_json: bytes)`: `columns` are the exporter's `SeedColumn`s, `seeds` its seed keys (combination, loop order) in plan order, `born_components` its dict, extended in place in first-seen order; `tables` maps `id(column.table)` to a `NativeCellTable` (default: encoded from the Python cells). The bytes equal `json.dumps(records, separators=(",", ":"))` of the oracle's two lists exactly; a class no sector admits raises the oracle's `KeyError`. D owns the plan's call site (D3); the stored observable column sets' seed records (`export_plan(column_sets=...)`) call it since generation-scale-2's fix round 2 (`plan_export._set_records`, slice-status DECISIONS 2026-10-26 ~15:10 item 4); the Rust API is `cells::project_records` (in-crate). Since generation-scale-2's `column_weights` (merged with native-cells in its fix round 1), `seed_records` returns a third value, per column its seed-weight column (None when every column has its own): columns that differ only by their helicity rows share one weight list per class record, so D3's call site passes each weight column's first column (`plan_export.weight_columns`); `test_loop_v2_native_cells.py` checks both cases (sector columns, and g g > g g's helicity-resolved columns with shared weights).

**Measured** (2026-10-26; this package merged on slice `392d3f4a`, release native `loop-native-native-cells-8ef63961` built with `build_loop_native.py`'s default codegen, 16 codegen units without LTO, not the shipped wheel's one unit with thin LTO; loop_sm with the top, compiled once; QED = 0; one process at a time pinned to 12 idle cores of the shared host at load 120–280, `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=12`; the Python column is the oracle lane on the same inputs; evidence and stage digests in `workspace/tmp/native-cells/oracle/p3/`). Package C keeps no disk cache, so every stage time is the stage's first computation in its process (cold for C's in-process caches); the generation totals have the compiled model and the kernel caches warm (outside C), and no cold-cache generation total was taken. Every native table equals its oracle exactly where both ran; S5 of the slice and of its companion are equal in both lanes (u u~ > g g g g g) and to the earlier heads' (g g > g g g g g).

| Seven legs | Python | Native | Target (`RUST-MIGRATION.md` §3.C) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cells, g g > g g g g g FC: slice table (1,080 classes × 720) and companion (10,080) | 8.1 s and not measured as a stage: the oracle lane's whole LC generation, stopped at its 30 min bound, exceeds the native one by more than 1,155 s (a lower bound for the whole generation, not a stage time); a 13-of-10,080-class sample took 3.54 s in Python (evidence audit), ≈ 46 min if every class cost the same | 1.1 s and 2.8 s (equal values share one cell: peak 11.5 → 4.4 GB) | ≤ 10 s |
| Cells, u u~ > g g g g g FC: 7,620 × 274 = 2.09 M cells (all compared) and companion | 44.2 s and 2.3 s | 1.9 s and 0.2 s | ≤ 10 s |
| Degrees, g g > g g g g g LC (recursion counterterms) | 5.1 s (54 s in the plan's survey of an earlier head) | 1.1 s | ≤ 10 s |
| Degrees, u u~ > g g g g g FC (site closure; fix round, native `loop-native-native-cells-51c7198b`, same codegen, `workspace/tmp/native-cells/fix2/prof/`) | 11.4 s as the first caller, 4.0 s again | 7.1 s as the first caller, 1.17 s again (before the per-basis memo of the quark-ring relabellings: 7.8 s and 2.7 s, 1.6 s of it Python ring canonicalisation of the rebuilt slice table) | — |
| Dipole orbit build at generation, g g > g g g g g / u u~ > g g g g g | 198 s / 40.6 s (`LoopSlice.dipoles`, phase 0) | 2.7 s / 0.33 s; no peak above the process (4,320 / 26,384 representatives, 133 / 538 kB) | ≤ 60 s, ≤ 1 GB |
| FC directed table T12, g g > g g g g g / u u~ > g g g g g | 90.6 s / 70.2 s | 1.3 s / 1.5 s (its swap the same) | ≤ 10 s each |
| Complete family LC, T12 (7 histories, 49 tables), g g > g g g g g / u u~ > g g g g g | ≈ 74 min / ≈ 57 min (49 × 90 s / 70 s) | 70 s / 92 s (tables 59 s / 77 s) | — |
| Seed records (C2), g g > g g g g g / u u~ > g g g g g FC | 36.1 s / 62.8 s | 1.5 s / 2.75 s (phase 0, byte-identical) | ≤ 10 s |

The first caller of the degrees, in either lane, fills the slice's shared lazy caches (the site closure's `_terms_of`, about 5.9 s at u u~ > g g g g g): the evidence audit's 6.2 s native against 4.4 s Python timed the native lane first.

Generation (`LoopSlice.generate`, wall time and process peak; both lanes in one process each, the compiled model and kernel caches warm, as package C's stages keep no disk cache): u u~ > g g g g g FC (site closure) 180.4 s / 1.25 GB in the oracle lane → 137.1 s / 1.24 GB; g g > g g g g g LC (recursion) > 1,800 s (stopped at its bound, 9.6 GB) → 644.8 s / 5.9 GB; g g > g g g g g FC (site closure) 543.5 s / 4.4 GB native, its oracle-lane generation not run (the Python companion cells alone are estimated at ≈ 46 min above, past the 30 min bound of these runs). The joint ≤ 30 s seed criterion of `RUST-MIGRATION.md` §3.C needs package B's seed-table switch; package C's share inside generation is 3.95 s at g g > g g g g g FC (evidence audit). The rest of these generations is outside package C: the Python seed tables (`build_seed_table`, 69 + 341 s at g g > g g g g g, the companion's the larger; package B) and the LC prune's trie rebuild (below).

**LC pruning and loop families** (investigation of slice-status DECISIONS 2026-10-25 ~20:45 item 3: the top-free g g > g g g g g LC plan keeps all 4,707 FC families). Not a defect. A family is keyed by its propagators' momenta and masses (§4), so a closed massless-quark loop shares its family with the gluon and ghost rings of the same topology: the prune drops the quark loops' skeletons inside the family, and drops a family only when none of its skeletons is live, i.e. for closed loops of a mass class no gluon ring has (the massive top). With loop_sm's top the families halve at every multiplicity; with `particle_veto` [t] they cannot, while the tries shrink alike and the LC plans with and without the top are the same:

| loop_sm, LC prune | families FC → LC | trie leaves FC → LC | trie nodes FC → LC |
|---|---|---|---|
| g g > g g g, with the top / vetoed | 154 → 77 / 77 → 77 | 3,489 → 2,481 / 2,985 → 2,481 | 1,730 → 801 / 1,227 → 801 |
| g g > g g g g, with the top / vetoed | 1,100 → 550 / 550 → 550 | 50,850 → 38,610 / 44,730 → 38,610 | 17,360 → 8,290 / 12,550 → 8,290 |
| g g > g g g g g, with the top / vetoed (measure-g1-7leg's plans) | 9,414 → 4,707 / 4,707 → 4,707 | 810,495 → 647,775 / — | 197,539 → 97,140 / 144,986 → 97,140 |

The lower multiplicities quoted against seven gluons kept the top. LC generates slower than FC because the prune rebuilds the tries after the seed table (the native schedules with `keep` evaluated per skeleton in Python, the readings and the table extension: 5–6 s of a 63–93 s generation at g g > g g g g top-free, 38 s and 10 s for the companion at seven gluons), and, before package C, the Python degrees; the liveness itself (`class_live`, cached per class) is under a second at six legs. Building the LC tries once, with `keep`, would remove the rebuild (generation-scale-2's `generator_input`). With an observable's tables the liveness dominates instead: at u u~ > g g g g g LC with T12's family (the slice table and 50 directed tables as criteria) `_prune_colour` takes 129 s in Python, its native twin `native_cells.live_classes` on the same tables (their native handles) 1.25 s (3,120 of 7,620 classes live). `_prune_colour` has no switch line of package C (§5.2 of the migration plan); the line is the coordinator's to assign.

## 8. Helicities and spin

### 8.1 Catalogue, selection and identifiers

- **Catalogue.** The full Cartesian helicity catalogue in leg order is kept (contracts §2.1).
- **Selection mask.** Massless chirality conservation plus Born-zero rows. A row h with A0_b(h) = 0 for all b gives a zero seed and is dropped at the leaves, but only for observables whose bra is the Born (assessment R3).
- **Identifiers.** Resolved helicity IDs keep indexing the full catalogue. `structural_zero_helicity_count`, hard-coded to 0 today (`runtime/virtual.py:508` [V]), reports the dropped rows.

### 8.2 Summation

- **B and M** sum the helicities of each block at the step that attaches it: objects carry only rows of undressed legs.
- **F** keeps attached-leg rows and sums at closure. That makes it the natural producer of helicity-resolved outputs.

### 8.3 Parity halving (optional, wave 4)

**Identity.** Under three conditions, the conjugate helicity configuration gives U0(c,−h)·c_α(−h) = conj(U0(c,h)·c_α(h)) per monomial in the real monomial basis:

- real external momenta;
- real couplings and masses (no widths);
- parity-certified kernels: tree, loop-segment and CT.

Hence C_α = 2 Re C_α^{half} [I, derived; the absorptive parts sit in the integrals, which are applied afterwards].

**Implementation.**

- Restrict seed rows to h_j = + for one leg j chosen inside the last-dressed position of each family's frame, so all earlier steps carry half the rows.
- Apply 2 Re to the Born-weighted coefficients before reduction.

**Gating and scope.**

- The certificate is a loop-level re-proof: the tree engine's `global_helicity_flip_equivalence_is_proven` (`models/external_catalog.py:465-472` [V]) extended to loop kernels, plus a numerical check on random points. It is never inherited (tree report §4).
- It is invalid with chiral couplings, widths or the complex-mass scheme.
- In the required set it applies to **gg→gggg, gg→ggggg, uu~→ggggg and gg→tt̄gg only**: vector-like QCD couplings, real masses, zero widths.
- It does not apply to uu~→e⁻e⁺ggg, uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g or gg→tt̄e⁻e⁺g, which are generated with QED ≤ 2 and include Z exchange.
- Expected gain: ×1.6–2 on dressing, ×1.25–1.6 per point, for those four processes.

### 8.4 Spin sources and correlations

- **Literal sources.** Complex vectors replace the polarisation of the source legs in both the Born and the blocks (`spin_sources.py:17-40` semantics [V-s]), and the source rows replace that leg's helicity rows. Joint classes and spin-correlation tensors form seed columns. Non-transverse sources use full kernels (§3.2 item 2).
- **Asymmetric Ward mode** (validation only, judge X2). The Born bra uses physical polarisations; blocks and CT = 1 currents use ε_j → p_j with full kernels. Seeds and blocks are computed separately, so this needs no new machinery. The renormalised loop amplitude must give zero interference, which tests UV + R2 completeness and gauge invariance per point.
- **Native Ward inputs at any multiplicity** (M-QCD MQ-WARD; ADDENDUM H; user ruling 2026-10-28 ~16:10, Q4). `NativeExecutor(..., inputs="native").evaluate(..., ward=...)` runs the Ward mode on the native per-point input path (`loops/v2/ward.py`): the native trees take per point a loop-side vector per leg (`inputs["loop_sources"]`, `loop_v2::trees::TreeProgram::inputs_loop_side`) and run the tree program twice, physically for the Born bra (the seeds' Born components and |A0|²) and with the vector in the leg's vector source slots (every helicity source, times its crossing phase; the targets are read from each program's own source fill, `SchemaSources::vector_slots`) for the block rows, the counterterm program and the field insertions' Born ket (`Interference::evaluate`'s `born_ket`); `TreeInputs::loop_born` = Re ⟨A0|M|A0′⟩ per squared Born order becomes `PointResult.loop_born`. The same call with `inputs="python"` is the oracle (`_PointEvaluation(loop_sources=...)`). It needs a slice on the full tree kernels (`ward.ward_slice`; refused otherwise, and on a stored process, whose record carries no kernel variant) and refuses observable columns natively. The check (`ward.ward_check`: per point and carrier one batch of the physical entry and every leg and factor, each row judged by `ward.ward_row`) uses ε_j → c p_j / p_j⁰ with c = 1 and i (of the size of a polarisation vector) and requires the Born (≤ 1e-12 binary64, ≤ 1e-24 double-double of the physical Born) and the virtual of every sector and Laurent coefficient to vanish: in double-double ≤ 1e-25 of the physical scale (the largest physical component), in binary64 by the user's O1 rule (m-qcd PLAN §1.4 rules 2 and 3, the exact value 0 and the scale in place of the coefficient) with the Ward evaluation's own reduction-direction estimate E: E ≤ 1e-9 of the scale (O1's scope), the virtual ≤ max(1e-12 of the scale, E) and ≤ 10 E. Physically (no Ward value) the full kernels are the production slice's transverse ones (double-double: bitwise on g g > g g and u u~ > g g g, asserted to 1e-25 of the scale). The parts need not vanish alone: R2 cancels a bare violation, and with massive quarks the local UV part's mass counterterm is not gauge invariant without the bare self-energies (g g > t t~ g g, ε₁ → i p₁: 40 times the scale). A rotation check is no rounding estimate of a Ward evaluation: the leg's helicity-summed bra against a helicity-independent ket makes each lane change by little-group phases under a rotation (`ward_row` refuses it). `tools/loop_v2_ward.py` runs the check on a `tools/loop_benchmark` process (one batch per carrier: the physical entry and every leg and factor); `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_ward.py` holds the default tier (g g > g g, u u~ > g g g; native = Python inputs on the non-gauge control in double-double, bitwise) and nightly the ladder up to seven legs and the seven-gluon check. Campaign (stream point 0, every gluon leg of g g > g g … g g > g g g g, u u~ > g g g, u u~ > d d~ g, u u~ > u u~ g, u u~ > g g g g, g g > t t~ g g, u u~ > g g g g g; legs 1, 4, 7 of top-free g g > g g g g g; c = 1 and i): all pass; g g > g g g g g: generation 168 s, binary64 72 s and double-double 721 s for the batch of seven on 12 threads, 27 GB; the virtual at most 2.9e-10 (binary64, 0.5 of its estimate) and 2.8e-26 (double-double) of the physical scale, the Born ratio 9.8e-16 and 5.3e-32; the physical binary64 Born and finite part of this full-kernel slice at that point are the G1 production values (m1028a `g1-values.json`, native/full point 0) to 1e-16 and 2.8e-10 (its estimate 4.8e-9); the largest binary64 rule-2 ratio of the campaign is 0.65 (u u~ > g g g g); the check discriminates: in double-double the bare and R2 parts are each 5e-4 (g g > g g g g g) to 8e-2 (g g > t t~ g g, with the local UV part) of the scale and cancel to the virtual's 1e-26 to 1e-31, so a wrong or missing R2 or UV term fails (records `workspace/reports/engine-v2/mq-ward/records/`).

### 8.5 Resolved outputs

- **Per helicity:** schedule F, or one-hot seed columns (×H cost at 7 legs).
- **Per Born colour component:** seed columns restricted to the component's Born basis elements. Components still sum exactly to the inclusive result, and `ResolvedVirtualEvaluation.total()` equals inclusive. The `kind` is renamed if anchors change (§1.6). As built on the public surface (WP2.6, `Runtime.evaluate_resolved` through v2): colour is resolved at LC only (one component per Born basis element, ids `basis-<i>`); NLC and full colour return one contracted colour component per helicity row (id = the accuracy), as `Runtime.evaluate_resolved` documents for the legacy engine. Decision 3 of §1.6 (Born-basis anchors at every accuracy) is still for the user.
- **Leading-colour selection (MQ-SELECT, as built 2026-10-28; `loops/v2/selection.py`).** *Rule.* Tree-engine parity at LC: the helicity IDs (`h:<values>`) and flow IDs (`flow:<word>`, ordinals in the tree runtime's order: plan sectors, a folded trace's reflection after its sector) are the tree engine's, and an artifact lists and numbers the flows it computes as the tree runtime with the same selectors does (`selection.request_flows`: the tree engine's plan with the request's `reference_color_order` and `max_color_sectors`, kept as `Selection.color_order`/`max_sectors`, which select nothing; under a colour selection only its flows). A flow is a Born basis element with its outer flow, and a selection is one observable column (rows and basis elements of its bra): it equals the sum of its resolved entries, and a complete set sums to the inclusive value. Declared colour or spin correlations (or the Ward mode) with a selection are refused (`selected-correlators`), as in the tree engine (a spin source on a leg of fixed helicity would ignore the selection). A selected artifact reports `helicity_coverage`/`color_coverage` `"selected"`. NLC and full colour keep their API and bytes. *Evaluation.* `evaluate` takes `helicities=`/`color_flows=` on every runtime, and `evaluate_resolved` takes them as a filter of its rows and basis elements. Python and native slices export one selection column on first use; a stored process forms one column set from its stored resolved records without regeneration (`native_observables.selection_parts`: per sector one seed column, the selected basis elements' seed-weight columns summed on the selected rows, and one interference column; by linearity the sum of the selected resolved entries, bitwise on u u~ > g g g), whose plan keeps only the families its seeds read (`live_families`). The LC stored observables record holds the flow catalogue (`color_flows`), the one byte change of LC artifacts. A runtime keeps the 8 most recently used formed selections (`selection.SelectionCache`; `$PYAMPLICOL_LOOP_SELECTION_CACHE` sets the count, 0 keeps none); each formation leaves about 1.5 MB of native heap, as every native executor (re)formation does (open, M-FINAL, HANDOFF §9). An in-memory slice exports a new selection's column instead (g g > g g g in double-double: about 16 s), so sampling should use a stored artifact. *Generation.* `SliceRequest.selection` (the public route's `selected_source_helicities`, `selected_color_sector_ids`, `reference_color_order`, `max_color_sectors`) keeps the selected flows' Born tensors (`restrict_born_terms`: basis, seed table, Born/counterterm/field bras and the tree program follow) and the selected block rows (`restrict_rows`); the colour pruning is ring-aware: a skeleton is kept when the plan's reading of its ring (rotations and reflection) or its own reading is live for the selected elements (per-reading liveness alone lost 40 % of the double pole on one flow of g g > g g g); the inclusive pruning is unchanged. A tree-engine sector of a folded trace is two flows, the sector's and its reflection's. *Leading power.* A flow below the squared matrix element's leading N_c power (u u~ > d d~ g, each quark joined to its own antiquark) is zero at evaluation; at generation it contributes exact zeros next to a leading flow, and a selection without one is refused (`subleading-colour-flows`, `LoopSlice._refuse_inexact_flows`). Leading is read from the slice's own grading of the unrestricted Born (`_flow_degrees`: each item's N_c power plus its coupling's grades, so identical-flavour lines are graded correctly; within one Born order only a flow's own term, since two distinct flows close fewer index loops and their cross term is below the mean of their own degrees; between two Born orders every pair), not from the Born roots' U(1) grades (`colour.leading_born_flows`). With several Born orders a generation-time selection is exact only if none of its flows interferes at the leading power with an unselected flow: the restricted Born drops that term (bra and ket the selected flows), the evaluation-time selection keeps it (bra the flow, ket the whole Born; this is also the per-flow cross-order convention at evaluation). Such a selection is refused (`interfering-colour-flows`): u u~ > d d~ with QCD and photon Born amplitudes, where at QCD^2 QED^2 the flows (2,4,3,1) and (2,1,3,4) overlap at N_c^1, the leading power there (evaluation-time Born values ±8.5e-3, summing to the vanishing inclusive LC value); (2,1,3,4) alone is `subleading-colour-flows`, both together are the inclusive value. *Evidence* (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_selection.py`): complete sets (flow thirds, helicity halves) sum to the inclusive value on the public artifacts of u u~ > g g g g, u u~ > d d~ g g, g g > g g g g and u u~ > g g g g g to 1e-28 in double-double and 1.8e-13 in binary64; generation-time selections equal the evaluation-time ones in double-double (identical-flavour lines included: u u~ > u u~, u u~ > u u~ g, on the public route its sectors 0 and 2); single helicities equal MadLoop's `SLOOPMATRIXHEL` (MQ-REF quadruple precision: g g > t t~, g g > g g g) to 1e-12 per coefficient, one parity pair's single pole held by parity within MadLoop's own spread (PLAN §1.4 rule 1 (i)). Cost (binary64, one core, shared host, indicative): u u~ > g g g LC, B = 128, inclusive 2.0 ms, one (helicity, flow) 0.72 ms at evaluation and 0.62 ms at generation; u u~ > g g g g LC, B = 32, inclusive 30 ms, 4.0 and 2.9 ms; a new evaluation-time selection's setup about 1 s and 66 MB there. **Native row restriction (as built 2026-10-28).** A plan whose columns keep some Born rows only (helicity selections and helicity-resolved columns: `column_rows`) seeds zeros on the other rows, so the native loader keeps only what a kept row reaches (`rust/crates/rusticol-core/src/loop_v2/plan/live.rs`): forward, the row links from a live source row; backward, those into a row a later link or trace reads; then the split targets a kept part link writes, and the parts, groups, edges and steps left with one; each step's modelled work and each family's inputs are recounted, and the fused factors and split currents are built on the rows the kept links read (`plan::ReadRows`; not with a schedule-F or -M program, which reads them too). A tree program with observable columns of such a plan (`TreeProgram::set_columns`: the stored path; an in-memory slice's `plan_export.NativeExecutor` passes no columns and runs the whole tree program) then evaluates what those read (`loop_v2/trees_rows.rs`): the plan block rows of the read factor and current rows, each with its further terms (`block_row_terms`: a block current the tree engine splits into trees by flavour flow), and the root closures (Born and counterterm) of the kept rows, with the interactions and output slots of both interaction programs kept by backward liveness over their states; the other rows' Born and counterterm components stay zero. Plans without a row restriction (inclusive evaluation at every accuracy) are untouched (paired A/B at B = 128, head/base 0.99-1.03 on u u~ > g g g FC and NLC and g g > g g g FC, values bitwise equal). Helicity-resolved columns are row-restricted at every accuracy, so `evaluate_resolved` at full and next-to-leading colour also runs the pruned program (equal up to the sign of a zero; checked bitwise before and after at LC, every resolved entry and runtime selection of the stored u u~ > g g g artifact in binary64 and double-double; the full-colour resolved rows are held against MadLoop's per-helicity values at 1e-12). The derivation bound holds on the restricted plans (`exported_plans_stay_within_their_bound`, `row_restricted_columns_stay_within_the_bound`). Split block currents: on the stored public LC artifact of u u~ > w+ w- g (the u current of legs {1, 3, 4}) the helicity halves sum to the inclusive value and single helicities equal their resolved rows in double-double (`test_helicity_selections_keep_split_block_currents`); with the further terms dropped the halves give 1.34 times the inclusive finite part. Cost: one (helicity, flow) of u u~ > g g g LC at evaluation 1.07 -> 0.70 ms; what remains above the generation-time selection is the lane data of every Born and block row, the full states' source fill and the column interference programs. M-FINAL item: per-call row and flow masks in the native executor (one program serving every selection).
- **Cost (WP2.5).** Every resolved output is its own seed column per squared-order sector, dressed and reduced like the inclusive one: helicity resolution has H columns (Born helicity rows), colour resolution |basis|, helicity × colour H × |basis|. Measured: u u~ > g g g has 32 rows × 11 basis elements = 352 columns; the nightly check of its helicity × colour outputs at two points (Python reference executor and native, binary64) takes about 5 min, against seconds for the inclusive evaluation. Exporting their seed weights takes 3.9 s (244 seed keys; 0.9 s for the inclusive plan): `plan_export` indexes each column's admitted Born entries and projected weights per (class, loop order) once, where a rescan per seed key, column and class took 53 s. At seven legs the counts reach up to 2^7 × 720 ≈ 9×10⁴ columns for gg→ggggg and 2^7 × 120 for uu~→ggggg, far above the schedule-3 break-even of ≈45–60 columns (§7.7), and helicity-only resolution alone (≈128 columns) is above it too. Resolved outputs at seven legs therefore need schedule 3 (each family's integrals of all monomials once, every output a dot product); with schedule B they cost H × |basis| inclusive evaluations.

## 9. Flavour mappings

- **Representative-only programs.** Identity-port members are records executed by the representative on the same momenta (`AdmittedFlavorMember`, `loop_runtime.rs:467-470` [V-s]).
- **Admission** keeps `prove_flavor_relabeling` (`loops/flavor_mappings.py:186` [V]) and runs it on the prepared model before compilation (assessment R11).
- **Permutation aliases** (e.g. u~ u for u u~) become relabelling tables: momentum and label permutations on the representative plan, with Bose transport as a special case and fermion signs from the same permutation parity as §3.3. This is the same mechanism as primitive templates. Public generation rejects aliases today (`loop_service.py:70-74` [V-s]); that restriction is lifted only with this proof-free relabelling (R11).
- **Retired.** The paired-program comparison of `flavor_transport.py` (contracts §2.7).
- **Composition.** Correlations and spin sources compose with members, because member execution reuses the representative's seed columns.

---

## 10. Precision and rescue

### 10.1 Carriers

- **One instruction stream for f64, DoubleFloat and Float.** Dressing and closure are generic Rust. Segment builders are SymJIT (f64) and exact evaluators (DD/AP). Seeds and colour tables are exact rationals cast per carrier. Ingress is exact decimal → rational, and the invariant table is exact until cast (contracts K4, K6).
- **Trees.** The tree recurrence lane gains DoubleFloat and arbitrary-precision prepared kernels built from the retained `exact_expressions` (tree report §7.3). Until the loop-request mode of the Rust recurrence (WP3.1) the per-point trees, Born components and counterterm scalars of the v2 path are formed in Python (`LoopSlice._point`): in binary64, or in Decimal at the carrier's working digits for DD (32 + 20 guard digits) and AP, then cast once into the native carrier (WP2.3).
- **Native carriers (WP2.3).** `NativeExecutor.evaluate` runs dressing, seeds and reduction in f64, DoubleFloat (`Precision.double_double()`, unit roundoff 2⁻¹⁰⁴) or Float (`Precision.arbitrary(digits)`); the Python reference path has no DoubleFloat arithmetic and refuses it. Native DD and AP equal the Python 60-digit reference (gg→gg, uu~→dd~g: DD within its own estimate < 10⁻²⁴, AP < 10⁻⁵⁵; `test_loop_v2_stability.py`). Outside f64 the lanes are scalar loops, so the executor uses the smallest lane count holding the batch.
- **NinjaRS.** The fast engine for f64; the generic engine for DD/AP. Every carrier reads the same on-shell external invariants (§11.1), so the generic engine's exact on-shell tests and the fast engine see equal inputs; lanes whose inputs reach the fast engine's absolute 10⁻⁹ thresholds take the generic binary64 engine (§11.2, 'Absolute thresholds').
  - The generic engine's numerator ingress is `PolynomialNumerator`, built from a Symbolica expression (`ninjars-symbolica/src/polynomial.rs:258` [V]), one template per (N, R) and reducer, compiled on first use and cached. Measured compile cost (WP2.3; first minus second call of one lane, 7-point family; shared host): DoubleFloat 0.35–0.52 s at rank 6, 0.73 s at rank 7 (330 parameters), 2.6 s at rank 8, 10.8 s at rank 9; Float(256) 1.0–1.2 s at rank 6, 1.3–1.5 s at rank 7, 3.2 s at rank 8, 11.3 s at rank 9. It is paid once per executor, so a tensor ingress is not needed for it.
  - The generic engine's workspace grows with the lanes of one call (about 18 MB per lane of a 7-point family at 256 bits), so buckets are reduced in chunks of 16 lanes (values unchanged).
  - numerica tracks the significant bits of Float masters: a master below the provider's required bits fails its lane with `ImpreciseResult` instead of returning an unestablished value (`imprecise_float_masters_fail_their_lane`).
  - A generic-precision tensor numerator exists only in the CLI harness (`ninjars-cli/src/case_check/harness.rs:1290`, `TensorGenericNumerator<T>` [V]). Request 8 (§11.5) asks the owner to upstream it.
  - Float(128) needs Symbolica 9cdc2949 (NinjaRS guidance [V]), pinned since the coordinated re-pin (WP0.2).

### 10.2 Triggers, cheapest first

**As implemented (WP2.3; REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM C overrides the defaults below).** An evaluation runs the checks its caller asks for, `StabilityChecks(rotations, reduction_direction, rescaled)` (`loops/v2/stability.py`), and none by default; with no check no estimate exists (`PointResult.stability is None`, in every carrier), and nothing is escalated automatically.

- **Rotation checks:** any number of user rotations, each an exact rational orthogonal matrix (`Rotation.about(axis, cos, sin)`, `Rotation.from_axis_angle(axis, angle)` via a rational unit quaternion), so the rotated point is exactly on shell. Each re-evaluates the whole point (trees, dressing, reduction, counterterms).
- **One reduction check:** the reduction-direction check re-reduces every lane in the reversed frame (q → −q from the second propagator) with the coefficients reused exactly and cached masters, +0.84–1.0 reduction (§5.5); or its **rescaled** variant (`rescaled=True`, `ReductionFrame::RESCALED`), the same frame with every dimensionful input scaled by x = 7/10 (shifts by x, squared masses, μ² and invariants by x², degree-d coefficients by x⁻ᵈ) and the integrals scaled back by x^{2N−4}, exact at every Laurent order. It re-evaluates the masters at differently rounded arguments, so it also measures their own rounding, which neither the cached frame nor a rotation (same invariants) can see. Cost on uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g (7 legs, binary64, 16 points, one thread, shared host): finite only 13.4 ms/pt, + reversed check 16.1, + rescaled check 16.5 (reduction 3.2 → 5.8 / 6.3 ms/pt).
- **Budget and estimate:** per check an absolute budget per squared-order sector and Laurent coefficient that lets no two lanes cancel (Σ_f 2|Ω_f − Ω′_f|/(16π²·avg) for a reduction check; for a rotation also the local components); E = 2 × (max over rotations + reduction budget) + 16 u S, with u the carrier's unit roundoff and S the absolute sum of the contributions. `StabilityEstimate` returns E (`absolute`), E against each sector's Laurent vector (`relative`) and per coefficient (`coefficients`, `finite`), each check's own budget and the per-lane deviations; `subset` recombines fewer checks. A relative estimate ≥ 1 says the value has no correct digit.
- **Failed points:** a failed lane (status, `ImpreciseResult`, non-finite numerator), also in a rotation check's evaluation, fails its point alone: `NativeExecutor.evaluate` raises `FailedPoints` with every other point's complete result.
- The calibrated false-negative rates of every configuration are in §10.4. The IR-pole check and κ below are not wired as estimates.

1. **NinjaRS status.** Lane status ≠ Success, a master failure, or a non-finite value. This is free. The fast engine has no cancellation test (NinjaRS guidance [V]).
2. **Reduction-side re-test of every family (default).** For each (family, point, sector), the stored family polynomial is reduced a second time in a relabelled frame.
   - **Relabelling.** The propagators are rotated cyclically by a fixed per-family k_f ≠ 0. This maps q → q − V_{k_f} and permutes the shifts and the S-matrix.
   - **Re-expansion.** The polynomial is re-expanded by the exact affine map (`loops/v2/poly_affine.py`; in Rust, the same generic routine in the working precision, O(4·R·C(R+4,4)) operations).
   - **Masters.** Box and triangle masters hit the cache. Bubbles with swapped mass order miss, because they are keyed by ordered arguments (§11.2).
   - **Estimate.** |Ω_f − Ω′_f| is the per-family error estimate. This is MadLoop's production check in the form recommended by NinjaRS guidance (b). Ninja's authors recommend a global scaling or rotation test, not Ninja's internal tests, as the accuracy estimate [L 1403.1229 `main.tex:1642-1651`]; the rescaling variant below is the scaling test applied per family.
   - **Rescaling variant** (measured in WP2.3). Replace c_α by x^{d_f − |α|}c_α, scale all invariants, masses and μ by a non-dyadic x so that rounding differs, and compare after undoing x^{4−2N+d_f}. Here d_f is the family numerator's mass dimension. This is NJet's scaling test applied per family [L 1209.0100 §Accuracy]. It recomputes the masters and so also tests them.
   - **Cost.** +0.75 R (relabelling, cached masters) or +1.0 R (rescaling), included in Table 1a: +12–25 % per point at 7 legs.
3. **IR-pole check** per point and sector, kept as an additional signal.
   - The 1/ε² and 1/ε coefficients of the renormalised result are compared with Catani's operator, built from the Born and the colour-correlated Borns (tree correlators) plus UV CT poles. Massive emitters use the massive dipole form (implemented, `poles.catani_poles`, §12.4); electroweak sectors use the QED analogue (`poles.qed_catani_poles`, WP3.2 EWI-6, §12.4).
   - It is free and catches missing counterterms.
   - It is not sufficient: pole residues involve only divergent integrals, so finite boxes, the R1 rational part and Gram-determinant failures in N ≤ 5 families pass it [I].
4. **Optionally,** the generic engine's κ cancellation test (guidance (a)).

**Error budget.** The point's estimated absolute error is E = Σ_f |ΔΩ_f|, compared with the target accuracy × |Σ_f Ω_f + CT|. When the target is missed, the families with the largest |ΔΩ_f| are recomputed (§10.3) until the remaining estimate meets the target. Per-family locality makes this targeted.

**Relaxation.** The default (every family, every point) may be relaxed only after WP2.3 measures the false-negative rate of each trigger against Float(256) on ≥ 10⁴ points per required process. Possible relaxations are N ≥ 5 families, or sampled points with a reported rate.

### 10.3 Escalation

By REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM C the v2 path does not escalate: the caller re-evaluates a point (or the failed points of `FailedPoints`) in `Precision.double_double()` or `Precision.arbitrary(digits)` from its original inputs when the returned estimate misses its target. No automatic rescue exists in v2 (`loop_v2/rescue.rs` is not built). The plan below stays the reference for an optional convenience tier, off by default; the legacy statuses and `verification.rs` (and its single-level defect) stay with the legacy engine until its deletion (§13.3).

Escalation always starts from the original inputs and never promotes binary64 values (MASTER_PLAN §2.18):

1. **Per (family, point):** recompute trees, seeds and that family's trie (or its M closures) in DoubleFloat from the original decimals with the same source snapshot, reduce with the generic engine, and replace the lane. Per-family locality makes this cheap.
2. Whole point in DoubleFloat, then Float(p) per policy.

Statuses:

- They stay success / rescued / failed / accuracy-not-established, with one implementation (`rust/loop-reduction/src/verification.rs`); `loops/stability.py` is deleted.
- Lane compaction replaces the whole-batch restart (`loop_execution.rs:749-826` [V-s]).
- The single-level defect (`verification.rs:346-351` [V]) is fixed per decision 6 in §1.6.
- The R2 strategy is fixed during rescue.

### 10.4 Expected rescue load and calibration

- NJet needed quadruple precision for 1.37 % (6 gluons) and 8.63 % (7 gluons) of points with unitarity in double [L 1209.0100 `njet.tex:1161-1227`].
- Ninja's exact Laurent expansions avoid the OPP sampling part of that instability [L-s 1403.1229].
- NinjaRS 0d0d7e7 lost 5×10⁻⁹–10⁻⁵ on ill-conditioned n = 7 lanes in binary64; the fix is in the pinned main aa6a6307 (guidance [V]; not re-measured here). The guidance expects many n = 7 μ² lanes to need DoubleFloat at a 10⁻⁸ target.

WP2.3 measures on 10⁴ points per process:

- the rescue rate;
- the rescue cost;
- the false-negative rate of each trigger (relabelling, rescaling, pole check, κ).

**Measured (WP2.3, O15; `tools/loop_v2_stability_calibration.py`, report `tests/fixtures/loop/v2/stability_calibration.json`).** 2000 binary64 points per process and 400 DoubleFloat points (the first 400), native executor, against Float(256) (`Precision.arbitrary(57)`: 77 working digits); RAMBO points at √s = 1 TeV made exactly on shell and momentum conserving in rationals, p_T > 20 GeV, ΔR > 0.4, μ_R = 1 TeV; two fixed rotations (`rotations()` in the tool) and one reduction check per evaluation, every combination recombined from the recorded absolute budgets (`StabilityEstimate.subset`). A false negative is a point whose true error exceeds E, both measured against the truth's Laurent vector (E against the evaluated value would let a value with no correct digit, flagged by a relative estimate ≥ 1, count as missed). No point failed. The test bounds each rate (`test_loop_v2_stability.py`).

| Checks (false-negative rate) | gg→ggg f64 | uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g f64 | gg→ggg DD | uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g DD |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| reduction direction (cached masters) | 2.30 % | 2.75 % | 0.75 % | 1.00 % |
| reduction rescaled | 0 | 0.20 % | 0.25 % | 0 |
| one rotation | 0–0.05 % | 0.50–0.60 % | 0.25 % | 1.50–1.75 % |
| two rotations | 0 | 0.35 % | 0 | 1.50 % |
| reduction direction + rotation(s) | 0 | 0.30 % | 0 | 0 |
| reduction rescaled + rotation(s) | 0 | 0.05–0.10 % | 0 | 0 |

- The cached reduction check alone misses the masters' own rounding: the same masters enter both reductions. Its estimate/error ratio has q01 = 0.4–0.5 in binary64. A rotation keeps every invariant and so misses it too; the rescaled check sees it. A rotation re-evaluates the trees, dressing and counterterms, which neither reduction check sees (injected-numerator test). Recommended configuration: the rescaled check with one rotation (estimate/error q01 ≈ 4.6–5.4 in binary64).
- **Check choice (package mq-stab, 2026-10-29).** `stability.RECOMMENDED_CHECKS` = `default_rotation(0)` (a generic axis, as the calibration's rotations) + the rescaled check is the O1 rule's estimate (staged validation, G1 stability rows; the per-file O1 tests keep their own rotations). Two weaker choices miss errors by more than 10x, binary64 against double-double: (i) a rotation about the beam axis (`z:3/5:4/5`, the earlier O1 rotation) keeps both incoming momenta and every energy and p_z bitwise: with the cached check it gave the u u~ > u u~ e+ e- double pole 7.6e-14 at the fixture's point 2 (error 1.7e-12, 22.8x; point 0 4.7x), while x-, y-, generic-axis rotations and the rescaled check give 0.5x (every rotation keeps the masters' exact invariants, so the masters are not the cause there); (ii) the masters' own rounding with a light massive loop line (g g > b b~ h, 4FS, m_b = 4.7: finite part 23-96x the estimate of every rotation with the cached check) is seen only by the rescaled check (0.14-0.24x, 7.5x at the worst point). Regression id `test_loop_v2_fermion_lines.py::test_recommended_estimate_brackets_the_uux_uuxepem_double_pole`.
- **USER DECISION (2026-10-23, ~13:12 UTC): single-point tests of an estimate keep a one-order-of-magnitude margin.** The user's words: "You can even be looser than 0.4x, we don't want to spend time calibrating or optimizing this here, you can easily afford a one-order-of-magnitude safety margin in the relative stability thresholds." Rule: every single-point test asserting that a stability estimate E brackets the true error holds error ≤ 10 E, for the whole estimate and per Laurent coefficient, for every check configuration alike (`ESTIMATE_MARGIN` in `test_loop_v2_slice.py` and `test_loop_v2_stability.py`); no further calibration or tuning. It replaces the strict bracket, which the cached reduction check alone misses at u u~ > u u~ g's fresh out-of-sample point (`test_loop_v2_slice.py::test_stability_estimates_bracket_out_of_sample`: error 3.2054·10⁻¹³ against E = 3.1082·10⁻¹³, 1.03 E), one of its calibrated false negatives; that point was pinned by a strict xfail before the decision, and no xfail remains for it. The calibrated false-negative rates above stay as measured (each counts error > E, without the margin).
- The one miss of the recommended configuration (uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g index 1832: true error 9.6·10⁻⁹, estimate 4.8·10⁻⁹) is missed by every check alike, each seeing ≈ 10⁻⁹: an error common to all evaluations at that point (not a rounding of what any check re-computes).
- True relative errors in binary64: gg→ggg median 1.1·10⁻¹³, q90 4.2·10⁻¹⁰, q99 3.2·10⁻⁶, 0.25 % above 10⁻³ (one point with no correct digit, flagged by every check with a relative estimate ≈ 10); uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g median 8.4·10⁻¹⁴, q99 2.1·10⁻⁹, max 4.7·10⁻⁶. DoubleFloat: gg→ggg median 1.4·10⁻²⁹ (one point at 3.2·10⁻¹⁰, flagged), uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g max 5.6·10⁻²³.
- Re-evaluation load at a target accuracy, from the recommended configuration's flags (true fraction above the target in brackets): 10⁻⁶: gg→ggg 3.3 % (1.25 %), uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g 0.2 % (0.1 %); 10⁻⁹: 15.7 % (8.3 %) and 3.4 % (1.25 %). In DoubleFloat every flagged point is above 10⁻¹².
- Cost per point (native, binary64, 16 points, one thread, shared host): gg→ggg finite 15.1 ms, + reversed check 17.5 (+16 %), + rescaled check 18.3 (+21 %); uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g 13.4, 16.1 (+20 %), 16.5 (+23 %). A rotation costs one more evaluation. The pole and κ triggers are not estimates of the v2 API (§10.2); 10⁴ points per required process and the other required processes remain open.

### 10.5 Optional labelled hybrid tier (needs sign-off)

Re-reduce flagged families in DoubleFloat from their binary64 coefficients (the OpenLoops 2 hybrid-precision analogue [L-s 1907.13071 §5]). It is off by default, never reported as DoubleFloat accuracy, and has its own status.
