## 11. NinjaRS and oneloopmaster interface

### 11.1 Hand-off per family and channel

| Item | Content |
|---|---|
| Denominators | N_f lines: real shifts from leg masks; masses from mass ids (complex under the complex-mass scheme) |
| S-matrix | N_f × N_f from one canonical invariant table per event, on-shell bindings patched (below) |
| Rank | True structural rank; never over-declared, since over-declaring costs up to 981× in accuracy (guidance [V]) |
| Numerator | C(R+4,4) complex coefficients scattered into `TensorNumerator` lanes; lane-major storage, `coefficients_mut` (`ninjars:fast/tensor.rs:30-90` [V]); no text |
| DD/AP numerator | `PolynomialNumerator` (Symbolica expression) until the owner upstreams the generic tensor numerator (request 8) |
| Re-test | The same coefficients after the exact affine relabelling (§10.2), same bucket |
| Output | Raw Laurent [finite, 1/ε, 1/ε²] of μ_R^{2ε}/(iπ^{2−ε} r_Γ) ∫ N/ΠD, post-processed as 2 Re(i/16π²·Σ_f Ω_f) (`rust/loop-reduction/src/lib.rs:5-9, 59-69` [V-s]); directed cross terms stay complex |

**On-shell external invariants (package precision-ap, 2026-10-25).** An S-matrix entry whose arc is a single external leg, or whose complement is one (momentum conservation), is that leg's squared mass at the point's parameters, exactly the value its propagators take, never p(arc)² of the given momenta (`families.onshell_leg`). Python: `_PointEvaluation._groups` reads it from `assemble._leg_masses` (the exact value `_mass_values` gives the leg's mass id). Native: `ExactInvariants::square` reads `V2Batch::leg_masses` (exact rationals: `NativeExecutor._inputs` passes them with python inputs; with native inputs `TreeProgram::bind` derives them from the external parameters, or for a derived mass from its parameter-program carrier output read exactly (`TreeCarrier::exact`), by the sources' own mass lookup, `SchemaSources::leg_masses`), so the cast entry equals the cast propagator mass bit for bit. A leg whose mass is a derived parameter (loop_sm's MW, any internal mass) is bound the same way (generation-scale-2 fix round 1, genericity audit M2): its invariants are the square of the value its wavefunctions and propagators take (Python: the one mass evaluator `assemble._model_masses`, at the working digits; native: the carrier value), see §16.2 WP3.1, items 3 and 4, 'Derived masses bound on their shell'. Every other entry, the shifts, the trees and the wavefunctions use the momenta as given: nothing is projected or snapped, and no tolerance is involved. All carriers bind the same entries. The structural proof of certified-scaleless families reads the same rule: `Family.onshell_bindings` calls `onshell_leg`, and the native families stage (the default, `generator_input.NATIVE_STAGES`) mirrors it in `rusticol-core loop_v2::generate::families::certified_scaleless` (its own single-leg arc test, equal on proper arcs; `test_loop_v2_generate.py` holds native and Python `scaleless` equal; it should call `dress::onshell_leg` at the next native change of that file). The staged validation's oracle is `_PointEvaluation._groups` itself, so generation, the Python reference and the native executor share one rule (package onshell-consistency, 2026-10-26: the reference without the binding is O(1) wrong in binary64 and fails its C0 master at 40 digits on a binary64-rounded point; before precision-ap both binary64 routes agreed on that wrong value, which the staged validation could not see).

- **Why.** oneloopmaster decides on-shellness by exact tests. Rounded momenta leave a leg off its shell by about 10⁻¹⁶ relative; the generic engine (DoubleFloat, MPFR, generic binary64) then evaluated the soft and collinear masters off shell, with log(p² − m²) in place of their poles: an O(1) error in every carrier but binary64 (b b~ > t t~ QED=2 [virt=QCD], massive b: binary64 3.0434, MPFR −50.574; g g > t t~ and g g > g g g at rounded RAMBO points likewise), and a 140-digit on-shell decimal point still left a massless leg's C0 non-finite. Binary64's fast engine hid the defect behind NinjaRS's absolute 10⁻⁹ GeV² snap, which depends on units and energies (a 5 TeV massless leg rounds off shell by more).
- **Contract.** Every carrier evaluates the same function of the inputs: DoubleFloat equals MPFR within 10⁻²⁴, binary64 within its accuracy (its stability estimate where that exceeds 10⁻¹²), and the MPFR value at a rounded point equals that of its exactly on-shell, momentum-conserving neighbour to the inputs' own order, about 10⁻¹⁴ (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_onshell.py`). The legacy engine binds the same invariants (`on_shell_bindings`, `docs/LOOP_PHYSICAL_KINEMATICS.md`). Like it, this is a physical-input contract, not input repair: a point off its shells (or not conserving momentum) by δ moves the result at O(δ), so results beyond binary64 accuracy need momenta on shell and conserving to that accuracy (exact rationals or enough decimals).
- **Evidence.** b b~ > t t~ QED=2 [virt=QCD] with a massive b: MPFR and DoubleFloat at the binary64-rounded reproducer point and at a rounded RAMBO point equal MadLoop (MG5_aMC 3.7.0, `loop_sm` default restriction, widths 0, quadruple-precision stability test forced, `ImprovePSPoint` 2) at the exactly on-shell points within 10⁻¹⁵ (relative) in the finite part and the single pole (3.04337313194461 and 0.584382517487585; 66.8536902819154 and 10.5488373076888); before the fix the same arbitrary-precision evaluations gave −50.574 and −806.48. g g > t t~, u u~ > t t~ g and g g > g g g agree in every carrier at rounded RAMBO points (before: O(1) off in DoubleFloat and MPFR, the massless double pole included, and `IntegrationFailed` at 140-digit on-shell decimals with a massless leg).
- **Binary64 at the reproducer** is accurate to 6.6·10⁻¹⁰ (its reduction-direction estimate: 6.3·10⁻¹⁰ there, 4.2·10⁻⁹ at the exact neighbour): the fast engine's (0, MT, MB) triangle family (the gluon correction of the W vertex) with original Ninja's cut geometry, the momenta lying in the x–z plane. A boost along x restores 1.8·10⁻¹⁵, one along z does not (2.6·10⁻¹⁰); the generic engine in binary64 reaches 10⁻¹⁵. NinjaRS's `FastOptions::conditioned_cuts` targets this geometry; it changes binary64 values and is not adopted here (open).
- **Catani.** The single pole of this process (every carrier, and MadLoop) differs from pyAmpliCol's Catani prediction by 0.45 % (0.584383 against 0.581745): [virt=QCD] loops are pure QCD (four vertex-correction triangle families here, MadLoop's 12 diagrams), so no gluon joins the b and t lines (those boxes carry the γ/Z/W in the loop), while the operator includes the b–t colour correlations of the s- and t-channel interference. Without the W the two agree exactly. Pre-existing and independent of the carrier (owner: the `"ir"` pole prediction).

**Complex pole masses (M-SM MS-CMS-N, 2026-10-29/30).** Under the complex-mass scheme a particle with a width propagates with its pole mass `CMASS_M = sqrt(M² − iMΓ)`, an internal parameter of complex type, with width ZERO (`model_cms.complex_mass_model`, MS-CMS, §6.8). The native trees read its two components like any derived parameter. A loop propagator whose mass parameter is internal and of complex type is a `["complex", o]` plan mass of the parameter program (`native_trees._parameter_program`): not checked real and positive, bound to the square of its carrier value's exact complex rational (`TreeProgram::bind`; `BoundParameters::masses_imaginary` and `V2Batch::masses_imaginary`, empty without a complex plan mass), the M² the tree propagators' carrier value squares, and run complex by the reduction lanes. External legs keep real masses (stable states; the binder's positivity check refuses a complex one). A Python-input batch gives a complex squared mass as an `[re, im]` pair (`plan_export._mass_text`, from MS-CMS's `_mass_values`), and the kernel constants take the pole mass complex. A photon α ratio whose expression reads the imaginary unit (a complex-mass α, real by construction, e.g. the G_μ UFO's Gfbar) takes its real part, as MG5_aMC's double-precision rescale factor does; the complex conversion `eCoup_DGmua0_UV_EW` is a field coupling whose interference with the Born takes 2 Re of it, MG5's real part. The binary64 fast-threshold record of a stored artifact reads |re| + |im| of a complex square (as `v2_reduce` does). With these the public route stores complex-mass artifacts (`model_cms.NATIVE_POLE_MASSES = True`): native point inputs (`native-v2`), relocatable bitwise. Records without complex plan masses are unchanged (an older native refuses the entry); the parameter program's SymJIT plane bytes differ from run to run (pre-existing, also on `54ec03c5`), everything else of the export is byte-identical on loop_sm controls. Evidence (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_native_complex_masses.py`): CM10 (u d~ > w+ b b~ QCD=0 [QCD], loop_sm under CMS with Γ_t: resonant top in the Born, complex top loops and counterterms) with native inputs equals MadLoop CMS in quadruple precision at its four points, double-double to 7·10⁻¹⁴ on every coefficient, binary64 to 3.4·10⁻¹⁰, and the same request through the public route (`mass_scheme = "complex-mass"` on the shipped loop_sm) stores `native-v2`, relocates bitwise and its loaded artifact equals MadLoop CMS; CM08 (u d~ > e+ ve b b~ QCD=0 [QCD], G_μ UFO with Γ_t, Γ_W, Γ_Z, Γ_H) native = MadLoop CMS, double-double to 1.3·10⁻¹⁴, binary64 to 1.2·10⁻¹³ (nightly, about 18 min, 25 GB); CM09 (u d~ > e+ ve a [QCD QED], G_μ `a0` under CMS, the photon tagged: complex conversion and α ratio) native = MadLoop CMS × MG5_aMC's formula over the tagged photon with Re(`eCoup_DGmua0_UV_EW`), both sectors, double-double to 2.7·10⁻¹⁴, binary64 to 1.7·10⁻¹² (nightly, about 6 min, 7 GB). CM07, the [U4] acceptance process (g g > t t~ e- e+ g [QCD QED] s⁶e⁶, Γ_Z, Γ_W, Γ_H > 0, Γ_t = 0): with Γ_t = 0 the transform ties ymt := MT (MG5_aMC), so with the top propagating on MT the top Yukawa vertices of the EW loops read the loop line's mass, which `LeafWeights._require_mass_free_couplings` refused (the tree reading flips that mass in the whole segment kernel; with the guard disabled, a diagnostic, the finite part and single pole were off by O(1)); with the top on its line mass LMASS_MT := MT (transform step 4b) it generates (2.7 min, 6.2 GB; export 2.4 min, 7.9 GB peak) and native = MadLoop CMS at its three points, double-double to 6.9·10⁻¹³ (finite), 4.5·10⁻¹⁴ (single pole), binary64 to 4.8·10⁻⁹ (nightly, about 10 min). On a loop_sm complex-top fixture the native lane binds M² − iMΓ to the carrier's rounding in binary64, double-double and MPFR, agrees across carriers, and equals the Python-input lane at the card's width (binary64 1.8·10⁻¹⁴ of the virtual, the parameter program's binary64 rounding; double-double bitwise).

### 11.2 Batching, rank and masters

- **Buckets.** One `FastAmplitude::new(N, R, FastOptions::default())` per bucket; rank ≤ N + 2 is enforced (`ninjars:fast/engine.rs:620-632` [V]).
  - Lanes are (family, point, sector, column), and every lane carries its own shifts, masses and S-matrix (`FastInput`, `ninjars:fast/mod.rs:60-79` [V]).
  - So unrelated families share calls: 5–11 physical calls per batch instead of one per family (§11.4). The re-test reuses the same buckets.
- **Per-lane rank.** Pinned NinjaRS 41a4c00 (since aa6a6307) reduces a lane declared at rank n+2 at its own rank (`FastNumerator::lane_rank`), bitwise as declaring that rank, so buckets may merge by N. `runtime/v2_reduce.rs` still buckets by (N, true rank), with the same results; merging by N is open.
- **Master cache.** A pyAmpliCol-side `CachingMasters` wrapper implements `FastMasters` (`ninjars:fast/masters.rs:56-64` [V]) and is cleared per batch.
  - Keys are the exact bit patterns of the request arguments. This works because all S-matrices come from one invariant table per event.
  - **Canonicalisation only where the outputs are invariant:** C0 under the triangle's symmetry group acting consistently on its external invariants and masses, and D0 under the dihedral relabelling of the box acting consistently on (p_i², s12, s23, m_i²). A0 needs none.
  - **Bubbles are keyed by the ordered tuple** [p², m0², m1², μ²]. `BubbleRank2/3/4` return [B0, B1, B11(, B111, B1111)] for ordered arguments (`ninjars:fast/masters.rs:16-26`, `fast/engine.rs:1817` [V]), and the tensor coefficients are not invariant under m0 ↔ m1: B1(p²; m0, m1) = −B0 − B1(p²; m1, m0). Revision 1's mass-swap canonicalisation was wrong for unequal-mass bubbles and is withdrawn.
  - The canonicalisation is applied identically in cached and uncached runs, so they stay bitwise equal. WP1.6 tests unequal-mass bubbles on gg→tt̄ (top–gluon).
  - Integer master ids from generation are an upstream optimisation (request 4).
  - The measured redundancy at 7 gluons is 308,448 requests for 2,822 distinct masters (109×) [M recola-ninjars §5.3].
- **DD/AP.** Same structure with the generic engine and its master provider.
- **Absolute thresholds (genericity audit 2, G2-M3; package onshell-threshold, 2026-10-27).** On the pinned NinjaRS 41a4c00 the binary64 fast path compares its inputs with absolute constants in the model's units, while the generic engines (`SymbolicaMasters` with `with_onshell_threshold(None)`, `runtime/reduction.rs`) have none: `OneLoopNativeMasters` (`ninjars-symbolica/src/oneloop_native.rs:27-49`) sets squared masses and invariants with |re| + |im| < 10⁻⁹ to zero and a real invariant within 10⁻⁹ of a real squared mass onto it, and the engine (`FastOptions::infrared_eps` = 10⁻⁹) skips the bubble of two massless lines whose invariant is below it and builds its cut bases from momenta whose square is below it as light-like ones. A keV mass in GeV (m² = 10⁻¹²; or the electron in TeV units) thus became massless in binary64 alone: log m² turned into poles, and the result depended on the units.
  - **Routing (interim, exact).** `v2_reduce::FastEngine` hands every lane whose inputs reach one of these thresholds to the generic binary64 engine (`reaches_fast_threshold`): a squared mass or an off-diagonal invariant x with 0 < |re x| + |im x| < 10⁻⁹, or an invariant within (0, 10⁻⁹) of a real squared mass of the lane. The comparisons are exact on the given inputs, absolute (never relative to a scale: at s ~ 10⁶ GeV² a relative rule would move the electron mass) and keyed on no name or process; exact zeros, and invariants exactly equal to a mass (the executor's on-shell external invariants, §11.1), never route. The rule runs per bucket and frame, on the frame's own (rescaled) inputs, so a lane may take the fast engine in one frame and the generic one in the other. The routed lanes give the generic engine's bits; every other lane, and every bucket without a routed lane, keeps the fast engine's code path and bits (the loop_sm controls gg→gg, uu~→dd~g, uu~→tt̄, gg→ggg and uu~→e⁻e⁺gg are bitwise unchanged, with and without the reduction-direction and rescaled checks; no lane routes). Cost: per lane and frame, the n masses and n² − n invariants are compared with the threshold and each invariant with each of the n masses, O(n³) comparisons (no measurable cost: 'Evidence'); a routed lane costs a generic binary64 reduction, and the first routed lane of each (n, rank) in an executor pays the generic engine's one-time Symbolica template compile (measured for DoubleFloat at 0.35–10.8 s for rank 6–9, §10.1 'NinjaRS'; not measured for binary64, whose template is the same expression). **Licence:** the generic engine parses and evaluates Symbolica expressions, so a routed lane is the one binary64 reduction that touches the Rust copy of Symbolica; it runs under pyAmpliCol's native library key, taken explicitly on the reducing thread (`pyamplicol::unlock` in `FastEngine::reduce`, besides the executor workers' own guards), the exception to §12.1 (e)'s 'binary64 needs no Symbolica' (`test_routed_binary64_lanes_run_under_the_package_key`: unlicensed, permit held, completes with the key and aborts without it; the unrouted e+ e- > u u~ of the same artifact completes without it; on the caller's own thread, as the Python reference path reduces, `test_the_reference_reducer_takes_the_key_on_its_own_thread`: a light bubble on `V2Reducer('double', None, 'fast')` is routed (`take_exact_lanes` = 1), completes with the key bitwise equal to the generic binary64 reducer and aborts without it, the heavy bubble completes without it; on a native without the explicit guard (the package's `26a19255`) the keyed run aborts in restricted mode, so the test pins the guard). When the package key expires (2028-01-01) such lanes need the user's licence or Symbolica's restricted mode, like DoubleFloat. **Legacy engine** (history: the engine and its production callers were removed by MQ-LEGACY-REMOVE, §13.3; `runtime::PolynomialPlan` stays as the Rust test oracle of the v2 reducer): `runtime::PolynomialPlan`'s binary64 fast plans (`fast`, `fast-tensor`; then the default engine's) route by the same rule (`reduction.rs` `FastPlan`): a lane reaching the thresholds is reduced by the legacy generic binary64 plan of the same numerator, built on first use under the key, in chunks of 16 lanes, the rule applied to the invariants of the call's `s_matrix` (the former production callers, `physical_process.py` and `runtime/physical.rs`, removed with the legacy engine, passed one with the external legs bound to their squared masses). A call without one (`evaluate_decimal`, `evaluate_batch(s_matrix=None)`, the legacy Drell–Yan bare program) has its invariants formed from the binary64 shifts, which are not given inputs: below the thresholds they are rounding (a massless leg at a generic angle gives p² ≈ −7.3·10⁻¹¹ at E = 500), which the fast path's snap sets to zero as it always did, and the generic plan would take for a slightly off-shell leg (the massless box O(1) off, or a non-finite C0 for the rotated Drell–Yan points: the re-audit of 2026-10-28, which found the first form of this routing doing so). Such a call routes on the given squared masses alone: a lane with a light squared mass is refused with the typed status `light_mass_needs_invariants(threshold)` (an `InvalidInput` naming the threshold the rule compared with; rounded invariants cannot resolve so light a mass, and an external leg's squared mass is not given at all: a formed p² ≈ 0 may stand for a massless leg or for one of that mass; the remedy is the exact invariants or double-double), and every other lane keeps the fast plan with its snap of the formed invariants, the base behaviour and bits (the re-audit's probes, the massless box and the rotated Drell–Yan points, now give the base native's values digit for digit). Every lane neither routed nor refused, and every call without such a lane, keeps the fast plan's code path and bits (`the_legacy_fast_plans_route_light_lanes_to_their_generic_plan`; without invariants `the_legacy_fast_plans_without_invariants_route_on_the_squared_masses`: the massless box at a generic angle keeps the fast plan and equals the generic plan given the exact invariants within 10⁻¹², while the generic plan at the rounded p² fails or is O(1) off; the light line is refused, and keeps its massless neighbour's bits in one call; `PolynomialPlan::take_exact_lanes` counts, a test hook no production code reads; the legacy Python binding and `runtime/physical.rs` were removed with the legacy engine, §13.3). The legacy generation record carries no `binary64_exact_engine` entry (the record is informative; the runtime decides per lane). The executor's report counts the routed lanes (`binary64 lanes on the exact engine`; `V2Reducer::take_exact_lanes`). Routing is per lane and per point because masses are runtime parameters and invariants per point. The artifact records what the generation parameters route: `GeneratedProcess.binary64_exact_engine` lists the masses of the process (loop lines of both plans and the external legs) whose binary64 square at the model's defaults and the request's fixed parameters lies in (0, threshold), the threshold being the engine's own (`runtime::fast_engine_threshold`, the loop module's `V2_FAST_ENGINE_THRESHOLD`), and the generation record keeps it as the filter `binary64_exact_engine` (`{"threshold": "1e-09", "squared_masses": {"MT": "1e-12"}}` for e+ e- > t t~ with MT = 10⁻⁶ fixed), written only when a mass is that light, so every other artifact keeps its bytes. The record is informative: the runtime decides per lane again, on the point's invariants and on any parameter override (`test_the_artifact_records_the_light_masses`).
  - **Evidence.** e+ e- > t t~ [QCD] with MT = 10⁻⁶ (m² = 10⁻¹² in every loop line and both legs) at E = 500 GeV: the slice native (`11542fd5`) was 107 % off MPFR in binary64 with a reduction-direction estimate of 10⁻¹⁴ (silently wrong); with the routing (2 lanes) binary64 is within 1.1·10⁻¹⁵ of MPFR. u u~ > g g [QCD] (a closed light-top loop) routes 4 lanes and agrees within 1.3·10⁻¹³ (its estimate 7·10⁻¹³), but does not detect the snap: the closed light loop is smooth as m → 0, and the slice native's fast engine was as close (1.2·10⁻¹³; the evidence audit), so it checks the routed path's accuracy only; MT = 173 routes nothing. The legacy engine had its own routed check (e+ e- > t t~ with MT = 10⁻⁶ fixed, binary64 within 6.9·10⁻¹⁸ of MPFR), removed with that engine (MQ-LEGACY-REMOVE, §13.3). Routed lanes under the key: `test_routed_binary64_lanes_run_under_the_package_key` (above). `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_onshell_threshold.py` holds these (binary64 against MPFR under the user's rule, DoubleFloat within 10⁻²⁴, on the four-light-line box of g g > g g within 10⁻¹⁷ (measured 2.05·10⁻¹⁸; MPFR(32) within 2.9·10⁻³⁵ of 64 digits there), no routing at MT = 173) and the unit independence of the dimensionless virtual: GeV against MeV (momenta, MT, MZ times 10³, G_F times 10⁻⁶, the scale times 10⁶) to binary64 accuracy, the light top routed in GeV and on the fast engine in MeV (m² = 10⁻⁶). `tests/runtime_v2_reduce.rs`: light internal masses (collinear-singular triangles of light lines, a box with a light line, the light bubble of a massless leg) are routed in both frames, equal the generic engine bitwise and DoubleFloat within 10⁻¹², while the massless lines' integrals (what the snap computed) are O(1) off; fast lanes keep their bits beside routed lanes in one bucket; the pinned provider snaps exactly at the routing threshold; every lane (top, massless and light families) gives the same statuses and integrals scaled by x^(4−2N) in TeV, GeV and MeV (x = 10⁻³, 1, 10³; within 10⁻¹¹), the light lanes routed in TeV and GeV and on the fast engine in MeV; in units of a power of two (x = 2⁻¹⁰, 2¹⁰, every input scaled without rounding) the routing and every status are those of the GeV inputs exactly and the integrals agree within 10⁻¹², but not bitwise: the pinned binary64 reduction commutes with an exact rescaling only up to rounding (43 of 96 comparisons bitwise, at most 2.3·10⁻¹³ on the massless rank-4 box; `power_of_two_units_scale_the_decisions_exactly`): in that range a unit change moves the values by rounding and the routing and statuses not at all. Beyond it the fast engine is unit dependent through Ninja's light-like spinors, which the routing cannot see ('Large units' under 'Other thresholds' below: O(10 %) errors from about 10⁴ times GeV-like units). The loop_sm controls gg→gg, uu~→dd~g, uu~→tt̄, gg→ggg and uu~→e⁻e⁺gg (native inputs, reduction-direction and rescaled checks) are bitwise the slice native's (`workspace/tmp/onshell-threshold/controls/`). Binary64 runtime at B = 128 (one pinned thread, finite part, checks off, pooled medians, the evidence audit of 2026-10-28 against the slice `6da37352`): g g > g g g g 421.32 → 418.63 ms per point (0.994), u u~ > g g g g 80.28 → 79.91 (0.995), every value bitwise equal, no lane routed; after the re-audit's fix of the legacy no-invariant path (the v2 reducer's rule only split into its two clauses), against the previous head `2fc09825` (paired rounds side by side on cores 248 and 254, swapped between rounds): 418.90 → 419.01 (1.000) and 78.77 → 79.56 (1.010; the reduction stage equal or faster, the difference in the unchanged native-tree stage), every value bitwise equal; merged with the slice `f6619f34` (sdk-parity), against that slice's native: 419.77 → 419.42 (0.999) and 81.24 → 81.19 (0.999), every value bitwise equal; merged with the slice `28a16255` (identical-flavour-lc, ew-leptons, gate-b128), against the slice native `5cb5ea83` (three paired rounds, cores 248 and 254 swapped between rounds, pooled medians of 15 blocks): 396.59 → 396.20 (0.999) and 74.66 → 74.88 (1.003), every value bitwise equal, and the loop_sm controls above bitwise the slice's (630 values, no lane routed).
  - **Binary64 masters at extreme hierarchies.** A routed lane is only as accurate as oneloopmaster's binary64 masters at its arguments (the fast engine's own unsnapped masters are the same native evaluators). Measured at μ² = 10⁴ against 300 bits: A0, B0, C0 with light lines between massless legs and D0 with one or two light lines stay within 3·10⁻¹⁴ down to m² = 10⁻¹²; D0 with three or four light lines between massless legs loses accuracy about in proportion to s/m² (10⁻² and 10⁻³ at m² = 10⁻¹², the three-line figure from the review probe of the upstream PR, `workspace/tmp/upstream-ninjars-onshell-threshold/correctness-probe.log`); D0 and C0 rows with light lines beside a massive leg lose more (5·10⁻² at m² = 10⁻¹⁰ beside p² = 9·10⁴) and become non-finite at m² = 10⁻¹² (m²/p² ~ 10⁻¹⁷). Such a lane fails with the master's reason (`IntegrationFailed { … MasterFailed { integral: D0, failure: NonFiniteResult } }`), so the point fails instead of taking the massless integral: g g > g g with MT = 10⁻⁶ (a box of four light lines) fails in binary64, which the snap had evaluated (there correctly, the light-top loop being smooth as m → 0) — DoubleFloat evaluates it (to 2.1·10⁻¹⁸ of MPFR, the same box's conditioning). No escalation is automatic (requirements addendum C). Routed to oneloopmaster (stable small-mass forms). **Light external quarks, and what the checks see** (the evidence audit of 2026-10-28, re-measured on this package's native): u u~ > t t~ [QCD] with MT = 10⁻⁶, beams along z, at (E, cos θ, φ, μ²) = (64, −0.8, 5.0, 1600) and (45, 0.9, 4.0, 8100): 10 lanes routed, the fast result bitwise the generic engine's, binary64 2.52·10⁻⁶ and 1.32·10⁻⁶ from MPFR(40), while the reversed reduction check estimates 3.2·10⁻¹⁵ and 3.8·10⁻¹⁵ and one exact rotation (about z, 3/5, 4/5) 4.7·10⁻¹⁵ and 5.3·10⁻¹⁵: more than 10⁸ below the error, against the tenfold estimate rule (§10.4). Both checks evaluate the same masters (a rotation leaves every invariant unchanged; the reversed frame re-reduces with the cached masters), so neither sees a master's own error; the rescaled check, which recomputes every master at 0.7 times the inputs, estimates 4.7·10⁻³ and 2.6·10⁻³. At (320, 0.35, 1.1, 90000), (150, 0.1, 0.3, 22500) and (900, −0.45, 2.0, 250000) the point fails with the C0's `NonFiniteResult` (the audit's measurement; the generic engine fails the same way on the base): fail-closed. Routed: the masters' accuracy to oneloopmaster (above); the estimate gap to the stability owner (WP3.4/O1): an estimate that sees master errors on routed lanes (the rescaled check does, at the cost of re-evaluating every master). The user docs ('Light masses in binary64') recommend the rescaled check for light loops.
  - **Upstream.** `OneLoopNativeMasters::with_onshell_threshold(Option<f64>)` (default unchanged): NinjaRS PR https://github.com/alphal00p/ninjars/pull/9 (posted 2026-10-28 by the coordinator, open; `workspace/reports/engine-v2/upstream/ninjars-onshell-threshold/`). After the re-pin the fast engine and the legacy fast plans take `None` and the masses' and near-mass clauses of the rule go; the invariant clause stays for `infrared_eps` (it cannot be 0 while Ninja's light-like spinors divide by √(p⁰ ± p³); see the spinor note below), and with it the key dependence of the lanes it routes.
  - **Other thresholds on the binary64 path (completeness, read with this package).** `runtime/master_cache.rs` keys rows on exact bits and canonicalises by exact relabellings: no threshold. `runtime/v2_poles.rs` is exact algebra in the lane's carrier; its only test is Numerica's tracked precision (unit free). The v2 stability estimate (`loops/v2/stability.py`) is relative and nothing escalates automatically. `FastOptions::relative_tolerance` is relative; `stability_threshold` (10²⁰ DBL_MIN ≈ 2·10⁻²⁸⁸) is absolute but at the underflow scale, reached by no unit system short of 10⁻⁷⁰ GeV. The legacy engine's binary64 fast path (`lib.rs` `FastReducer`, behind `runtime/fast.rs`) is routed as above. Still unit dependent and routed to their owners: Ninja's light-like spinors (`ninjars fast/geometry.rs:62-104`, the cut bases' e3, e4): a basis vector with |p⁰ ∓ p³| < `infrared_eps` takes the z axis' spinor and drops its transverse momentum (up to (2E·10⁻⁹)^(1/2): a vector within ≈ (2·10⁻⁹/E)^(1/2) of the axis), and outside that window the spinor divides by √(p⁰ + p³), formed by cancellation near −z (relative error ≈ 2.2·10⁻¹⁶/θ² at an angle θ from the axis, unit free). No mass or invariant is near a threshold, so the routing cannot see either (the vectors are internal combinations of the loop's momenta). Measured (`a_rotation_check_sees_ninjas_lightlike_spinor_errors`: a massless rank-3 box, beams along x, the outgoing legs at θ from the z axis, E = 500, no small invariant): the fast engine is silently off by 7.1·10⁻¹⁰ (θ = 10⁻³), 6.2·10⁻⁶ (10⁻⁵, cancellation), 8.8·10⁻⁶ (10⁻⁶, branch) and 8.8·10⁻⁸ (10⁻⁸), every lane 'success'; the rotated point is within 10⁻¹⁴ and the generic engine within 6·10⁻¹⁵ in both frames; the rotation check's difference equals the error and the reduction-direction check's is 1.29–2.26 times it (both asserted, within 0.5–2 times and above 0.5 times the error), so both stability checks see it. With beams along z the same legs sit near a beam, where the box's Gram determinant dominates and the fast engine returns `Unstable` for θ ≤ 10⁻⁶. **Large units** (the evidence audit of 2026-10-28; `test_power_of_ten_units_keep_the_virtual_and_its_estimate`). The same window makes the fast engine unit dependent at large factors. A beam along −z enters the cut bases as a difference of loop shifts (sums of external momenta in the family's frame), so its p⁰ + p³ carries a rounding residue of about 10⁻¹⁶ times the energies: below `infrared_eps` (energies up to a few 10⁶ in the model's units, so GeV-like units) the axis branch takes it and drops a rounding-size transverse momentum, which is harmless; above it the general branch divides the transverse rounding noise by √(p⁰ + p³) of the same noise, e3 and e4 are wrong at O(1), and the virtual is silently O(10 %) off, every lane 'success'. Measured with every momentum and dimensionful parameter times f (the scale times f², G_F times f⁻²; nothing light, nothing routed), the virtual's Laurent coefficients over the Born against DoubleFloat in GeV: g g > t t~ (beams along z, E = 400, the top at cos θ = 0.21, φ = 0.7) is within 4·10⁻¹⁵ for f = 10⁻⁵ to 10⁵, 2.2·10⁻¹ off at f = 7·10⁵ and 9.7·10⁻² at 3·10⁷, but 7.8·10⁻¹⁶ at 10⁶ (sums exact by chance); u u~ > d d~ g (fixture points 0 and 1) is within 7.5·10⁻¹³ for f = 10⁻⁶ to 10³ (the points' own conditioning: the generic engine is 3.5·10⁻¹³ off at f = 1) and 2.6·10⁻² to 1.4·10⁻¹ off from f = 10⁴ (point 1, E = 7.8·10⁶) or 10⁵ (point 0) up to 10⁹ (point 1 fails `Unstable` at 7·10⁵). The generic binary64 engine is within 4.2·10⁻¹³ (3·10⁻¹⁵ on g g > t t~) and DoubleFloat within 2.4·10⁻¹⁴ at every factor. The estimates move with it: the reduction-direction check sees most of these points (8.4·10⁻² against 0.22) or fails them, but not all (u u~ > d d~ g point 1 at f = 10⁴: 7.9·10⁻¹³ against 6.8·10⁻²), and both reduction checks raise false alarms on exact values (the rescaled one, whose frame is 0.7 times the inputs, 0.43 on g g > t t~ at f = 10⁶; the reversed one 0.11 on u u~ > d d~ g point 1 at 10⁹). The exact rotation by 90° about x (beams along ∓y) removes all of it at every factor (within 3.7·10⁻¹⁵ and 2.6·10⁻¹³, estimates below 3.4·10⁻¹⁴ and 3.3·10⁻¹²), while the 180° rotation (the beams swapped on z) keeps it: the beam on −z is the cause, not a mass or an invariant, so no structural rule on the lane's inputs sees it, and the routing above does not engage. The upstream note's threshold-free spinor (the larger light-cone component, both copies `geometry.rs` and `bases_x2.rs`) removes it, measured with a scratch native of this head patched to it (`upstream/ninjars-lightlike-spinor/experiment-larger-lightcone-component.patch`, not a PR): g g > t t~ within 2.8·10⁻¹⁵ and u u~ > d d~ g within 7.6·10⁻¹³ at every factor, estimates below 3.2·10⁻¹⁴ and 2.9·10⁻¹², no point failing; the five strict xfails of the test XPASS. It is not bitwise: 214 of 640 loop_sm control values change, by at most 9.4·10⁻¹⁴ relative except g g > g g g point 0 (2.2·10⁻⁹), where it moves the binary64 virtual from 7.4·10⁻¹⁰ to 3.4·10⁻¹¹ of DoubleFloat (cut-basis vectors with |p⁺| < |p⁻| outside the window). Routed to the dedicated NinjaRS light-like spinor package (DECISIONS 2026-10-28 ~05:20 (3)), with this reproducer in the upstream note. Until the fix is pinned, models whose energies are of order 10⁶ or more in their units (keV or eV units; MeV with multi-TeV beams) need GeV-like units or double-double in practice (user docs, 'Light masses in binary64'). The test pins the defect with strict xfails, which fail once the fix is pinned and then become plain cases; `a_rotation_check_sees_ninjas_lightlike_spinor_errors` likewise asserts the pinned error (`assert!(wrong > least)`), to be inverted by that package. Upstream note with a threshold-free spinor (the larger light-cone component; beam-axis bits unchanged): `workspace/reports/engine-v2/upstream/ninjars-lightlike-spinor/NOTE.md`. And the tree engine's binary64 external spinors (`rusticol-core/src/wavefunctions.rs:164,210`: |m| < 10⁻⁸ in the model's units takes the massless spinor in binary64 only, while the generic carriers test `is_zero`; the dropped components are m/√(E + |p|) against √(E + |p|), a relative m/(2E) (5·10⁻¹² for m = 10⁻⁹ at E = 100), below binary64 rounding only for E > 2·10¹⁵ m; the massive form divides by √(E + |p|) alone, so an exact `mass == 0.0` test is the fix). Not this package's files (the tree engine, `rusticol-core`): proposed to the coordinator for a tree-engine follow-up in the package report ('For the coordinator'), with the measurement binary64 against DoubleFloat for an external fermion of m = 10⁻⁹ as its acceptance test; no value measured here is affected (m = 10⁻⁶ is above it).

### 11.3 Topology bookkeeping, and the user's questions

**Bookkeeping**

| Aspect | Rule |
|---|---|
| Identity | Canonical cyclic (leg mask, mass id) sequence (§3.3): Recola's (ho, hm) |
| Canonicalisation | Key = minimum over rotations and reflections, at generation. Execution frames: per-family cost-optimal for B; leg-1 rule for F; cut-ordering frames for M. Oracles map frames with exact affine transforms |
| Selection | Structural rule plus certified-scaleless drop (§3.3); the drop is logged |
| Merging in the recursion | All partial results with equal key are summed: across block orderings, colour classes, species, helicities, sectors and columns. B: within (family, cut species, undressed contents). M: across families with equal prefix sets and undressed word. Never across denominators |
| Merging at closure | Species with equal masses, all cut species and all colour classes accumulate into one polynomial per (family, sector, column) |
| Merging at reduction | (N, R) buckets as lanes; master cache; optional stage 2 below |
| Born weighting | B: seeds at the leaves. M: seeds per ordering at the cut. Every state is Born-weighted and summed over the helicities of its dressed legs, so each family closes into the Born-weighted, helicity-summed numerator NinjaRS needs: one reduction per (family, sector, column), plus the re-test |
| Pinched topologies | Separate families; no lifting (MASTER_PLAN §2.13) |

**Lifting pinched families into parents** (user guidance). Rejected in both forms:

- *Globally* (multiply by the missing D_j): +2 rank and μ² per lifted line, and at most ≈2 lifts fit rank ≤ n + 2.
  - Cost model at gg→ggggg: 0.80 s (≤ n+1) and 2.7 s (≤ n+2), against 0.79 s for one reduction per family.
  - Probe errors: 3×10⁻⁴ (n = 6) and 8×10⁻³ (n = 7), against 10⁻⁷–5×10⁻⁶ for separate reductions [M recola-ninjars §4.1; recursion-literature §4.3].
- *Inside the recursion* (merging members after their first step):
  - Even the idealised bound with no rank inflation only takes the 7-gluon dressing from 9.0×10⁸ to ≥ 4.0×10⁸.
  - The alignment it needs (OpenLoops' pinch-invariant cut) costs 2.7×.
  - Families with N ≤ 4 cannot be lifted, and lifted hosts reduce at rank n+1 or n+2 [M `otf/lift_bound.log`].

**Family-tree / integrand-sum reduction sharing cuts without rank increase** (user guidance). The mathematics is exact: Ninja's extraction is linear, triangles need no subtraction, and bubble and tadpole subtractions are linear in the parent residues [L 1203.0291 `introduction.tex:98-117`; V `ninjars:fast/subtractions.rs` per physics judge]. GoSam already reduces pinched diagrams together with their parent "with virtually no additional computational cost" [L 1111.2034 `diagen.tex:100-130`]. Three realisations:

| Realisation | Gain on the NinjaRS stage | §2.13 | API | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| (b2) per-member division / process-level cut lattice fed by family tensors: each family expanded on its cuts through NinjaRS's own jets; geometry, subtractions and masters once per cut | ≈1.15–1.3× (the expansions, 72–75 % of NinjaRS time with cached masters, are unchanged) [I, from the measured split in recola-ninjars §3.5] | Compliant | Owner extension: a **public** jets API (`fast/tensor_jets.rs` is private, `ninjars:fast/mod.rs:46` [V]; request 7) | Default stage 2, if gated in |
| (b1) cut-restricted lift into parent groups: callback returns T_i = Σ_m N_m Π L_j^(i) with affine L_j on cuts containing line i; rank class kept | 1.5× (n = 6), 2.0× (n = 7) at rotation-noise accuracy, on synthetic numerators [M recola-ninjars §4.2.3] | Needs an amendment (cut-reduced extra denominators) | None to start (`cut` is passed to callbacks, `ninjars:fast/numerator.rs:58-66` [V]) | Only with sign-off and validation on physical numerators |
| Residue lattice with pyAmpliCol jets (Design 3) | Masters once per cut; best for many channels | Compliant | New NinjaRS lattice API plus private jets | Not adopted (private jet arithmetic duplicates NinjaRS; production would wait on an API) |

**Gate.** Pursue stage 2 only if NinjaRS exceeds 30 % of the total after the master cache. At 7 legs FC the stage is 22–46 % of the target including the re-test (Table 1a), so stage 2 can bring ≈3–10 % overall with (b2) and ≈7–20 % with (b1). The master cache comes first: it alone removes 0.23–1.5 s at gg→ggggg [M].

**Recola's tensor-integral bookkeeping** (user guidance). Adopted:

- the identity (ho, hm);
- canonical cut and orientation rules during the recursion (F);
- species sharing by mass;
- the counterterm flag in the current key.

Recola's pinch sharing happens inside Collier's call-position cache [L-s 1604.06792 §4]. That cache shares tensor sub-reductions as well as scalar integrals. The NinjaRS master cache shares only scalar masters, so it reproduces the scalar part of Collier's sharing, not its tensor-reduction sharing. In integrand reduction the corresponding work (per-cut expansions) is per family by construction, and stage 2 (b2) is its analogue.

Recola's "reduce numerator-independent integrals once, contract per (colour, helicity)" is unavailable to integrand reduction, which needs the numerator first. It returns through the transposed reduction (schedule 3, §11.5 request 2) for many-observable requests [V-s recola2 `amplitude_rcl.f90:1470-1496`].

**OpenLoops on-the-fly reduction** (user guidance). Its output, low-rank polynomials over fixed denominator sets, would be acceptable to NinjaRS; that is not why it is rejected. It is not adopted because:

1. **Speed.** OpenLoops' own benchmark finds reduction after dressing (OpenLoops2+Collier) faster, depending on the process; OFR is kept there for stability [L 1710.11452 `OL_OFRED_paper_epjc.tex:3836-3846`].
2. **Private mathematics.** It would be private reduction mathematics next to NinjaRS, against the user's reuse rule.
3. **Integrand-level validity.** The del Aguila–Pittau step holds at integrand level only for N ≥ 4 [L-s 1710.11452 §4.1]. It needs Gram-determinant expansions and hybrid precision.
4. **More families.** Pinch closure adds families: uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g goes from 244 to 422 [M].

A few integrand-level steps could bring rank > N+2 effective-theory numerators into NinjaRS's domain. That belongs in NinjaRS if ever needed.

### 11.4 NinjaRS reductions per event (per squared-order sector)

Providers' configuration (massless b). The re-test doubles the reductions in the same buckets with cached box and triangle masters.

| Process | Colour | Families = reductions | Physical calls (buckets) | Distinct masters after cache | Stage-2 groups | NinjaRS stage: mass-aware model / histogram model |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| gg→gggg | FC | 1,100 | 5 | 627 (of 20,800 requests) | 120 | 40–45 ms / 44–51 ms |
| gg→gggg | LC | 550 | 5 | 310 | 60 | 17–20 ms / 22–26 ms |
| gg→ggggg | FC | 9,414 | 6 | 2,822 (of 308,448) | 720 | 0.79–0.82 s / 0.85–1.0 s |
| gg→ggggg | LC | 4,707 | 6 | 1,407 | 360 | 0.36–0.38 s / 0.43–0.50 s |
| uu~→ggggg | FC | 5,258 | 11 | 1,724 (of 146,576) | 420 loose / 971 strict | 0.19–0.21 s / 0.22–0.29 s |
| uu~→ggggg | LC | 2,712 | 11 | 1,122 | 120 / 671 | 80–90 ms / 92–124 ms |
| uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g | FC | 242 (Recola TI table: 244) | 10 | — | 39 | 2–4 ms / 1.8–3.3 ms |
| uu~→e⁻e⁺ggg | FC | 463 | 9 | — | — | — / 9–13 ms |
| gg→tt̄gg | FC | 1,086 | 9 | — | — | — / 19–27 ms |
| gg→tt̄e⁻e⁺g (QCD) | FC | 579 | 9 | — | — | — / 10–14 ms |
| gg→tt̄e⁻e⁺g (EW) | FC | ≈1,450 [I] | ≈10–12 [I] | — | — | — / ≈25–50 ms [I] (gate E0) |

Sources:

- The mass-aware model of the recola-ninjars report skips scaleless cuts [M recola-ninjars §5.2–5.3, §6].
- The histogram model applies the same constants to the (N, R) histogram without scaleless skips [M `revision/ninjars_stage.log`]. Table 1a uses it for every row.
- Reductions multiply by the number of active sectors and observable columns; physical calls stay at the number of buckets.

**On MadLoop's loop groups.** MadLoop 5's published table has 1,168 loop groups for gg→tt̄gg, which matches our 1,164 families with a massive b [L 1604.01363 timing table]. The MG5 3.7.0 exports on this host have 1.6–3.9× more groups than our canonical families: 2,459 (gg→tt̄gg), 4,241 (gg→gggg), 998 (uu~→e⁻e⁺ggg), 406 (uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g), 1,191 / 2,958 (gg→tt̄e⁻e⁺g QCD / EW) [V `NLOOPGROUPS` in each `loop_matrix.f`]. Only their EW/QCD ratio is used here.

### 11.5 Requests to the NinjaRS owner

These may be pushed to NinjaRS main under the 20 October authorisation (`MASTER_PLAN.md:96-103` [V]), in priority order:

1. **Coordinated re-pin** to the new mains with per-lane rank: done (WP0.2). Still requested: an upstream test that lanes of unrelated families give bitwise the same result batched and unbatched.
2. **Transposed / multi-numerator reduction.** H numerators share one geometry; equivalently, each family's integrals of all monomials are returned once. This enables schedule 3 for correlations and resolved outputs.
   - The owner's study (`docs/audits/transpose-probe.md` [V]) is a symbolic instruction count on a three-propagator, rank-3 toy, starting from Laurent callback heads.
   - It is a negative result at one numerator per changing point (`:21`). Its thresholds, H > 1.85 and H > 3.97 for fixed geometry, are explicitly not timing predictions (`:45`).
   - A measured prototype is required before schedule 3 is adopted.
3. **Per-(cut, lane) conditioning diagnostics:** minimal uncut-denominator leading coefficient, Gram determinant. These feed the error budget (§10.2).
4. **Optional integer master ids** in master requests, so the cache needs no hashing.
5. **Gated stage 2:** process-level lattice in tensor-provider mode or per-member division; or, after sign-off, a layout-aware affine-product builder plus a cut-reference adapter.
6. **Optional** coefficient-major `TensorNumerator` ingress, to avoid the closure transpose.
7. **Public jets API** for (b2), if stage 2 is gated in: `fast/tensor_jets.rs` is private today.
8. **Upstream the generic-precision tensor numerator** (`TensorGenericNumerator<T>`, now only in `ninjars-cli/src/case_check/harness.rs:1290`), so that DD/AP ingress needs no Symbolica expression.
9. **Opt-out of the native masters' absolute on-shell threshold** (`OneLoopNativeMasters::with_onshell_threshold(Option<f64>)`, default unchanged; G2-M3, §11.2 'Absolute thresholds'): minimal PR https://github.com/alphal00p/ninjars/pull/9 (open; `workspace/reports/engine-v2/upstream/ninjars-onshell-threshold/`).
10. **Threshold-free light-like spinors in the fast engine** (`fast/geometry.rs` `Spinor::new`: no absolute `infrared_eps` window, no √(p⁰ + p³) by cancellation near −z; §11.2 'Other thresholds on the binary64 path'): upstream note `workspace/reports/engine-v2/upstream/ninjars-lightlike-spinor/NOTE.md`, not a PR (it changes the fast engine's bits).
