## 12. Artifacts and public API

### 12.1 Artifact

As built (WP2.6, `loops/v2/artifact.py`, `generation/loop_artifact.py`, `rusticol-core/src/loop_artifact.rs`):

- **Envelope.** The schema-v3 directory, transactional writer and payload digests are kept; the runtime capability is `rusticol.loop-runtime.v4` (known to `VerifiedArtifact`, not a tree-execution capability). An artifact of the removed legacy engine (`rusticol.loop-runtime.v3`) is refused typed at load and inspection (`CompatibilityError`, `LEGACY_LOOP_ARTIFACT_REMOVED`; MQ-LEGACY-REMOVE, §13.3). No backward compatibility (AGENTS.md, contracts K9).
- **Per process** `processes/<id>/`:
  - `loop-runtime.pacbin`: the member `schedule/loop-plan-v4.json.zlib` of kind `LOOP_RUNTIME_PLAN = 11`, the zlib stream of the plan v4 JSON of `plan_export` (families, tries, merged section when present, seed weights, block-row and Born layouts, kernels). Exact seed tables and counterterm columns are exact rationals inside it. A process with certified-scaleless families also stores their companion plan (`LoopSlice.scaleless`, §12.4) as `schedule/loop-plan-v4-scaleless.json.zlib`, same kind; only the `uv` and `ir` pole selections run it.
  - `execution.json` (schema 4): runtime capability, plan SHA-256, the companion plan's SHA-256 (`scaleless_plan_sha256`, null without certified-scaleless families), `point_inputs` and the generation request (process, Born bounds, exact loop orders, corrections, fixed parameters, colour accuracy and formal-N_c declaration, seed contraction, the resolved correlator declarations).
  - `physics.json` (schema 2): `LoopV2ExecutionMetadata` (Born orders; sectors with their (Born, loop) pairs, each a separate partial result; families; (N, R) buckets; colour accuracy and LC/NLC degrees; seed contraction; schedule B; model R2; carriers double, double-double, arbitrary; declared colour histories, spin classes and resolved capability; the served pole selections `poles = ["all", "none", "uv", "ir"]` (exactly these: any other declaration, e.g. `["all", "none"]` of the internal pre-split format, is refused with a request to regenerate, since internal artifact formats are not kept backward compatible (AGENTS.md); a companion is stored exactly when its digest is declared; its digest and its legs, rows and sectors are checked against the process plan at admission, and at load the regenerated companion must have the stored digest); rotation and reduction-direction checks; caller-side rescue; scheme declarations), the legs and the Born helicity rows.
  - `model/compiled-model.json` at the root; `process-groups.json` lists exact flavour members (`exact-loop-flavor`, identity port) served by their representative.
- **Native admission** (`load_loop_v2_artifact`, rusticol-core feature `loop-v2`): envelope, execution and physics declarations (unknown fields refused), the plan digest, and the validated program, whose families, buckets, legs, helicity rows, sectors and spin-class legs must equal the declarations. The legacy loader (the SDK's) refuses v4 with the reason.
- **Kernels** are exact polynomial term lists interpreted natively (`loop_v2/dress.rs`), not SymJIT applications: the plan holds no compiled code (§5.5; factor builds 0.16–0.72 ms/pt). Their terms are in a canonical order (`reference_executor._term_order`: the monomial, then the constant key, both compared as exact tuples; the order of WP2.5), so neither the plan nor any summation depends on Symbolica's symbol ids. A content whose tree reading flips the sign of its outgoing massive fermion's mass (`TreeReading.mass_signs`) binds its own copy of each kernel, whose numbered constants the batch values with the flipped mass (`plan_export`; before this the native executor was wrong on every process with an open massive quark line through the loop, e.g. uu~→tt̄ and gg→tt̄, which the Python reference already handled). Referencing a prepared model bundle's compiled kernels stays a measured option, not needed for size (zlib already folds repeated kernels: gg→gggg publishes < 20 MB).
- **Portability (requirements addendum G; package artifact-portability on the integrated slice).** Every reference is relative to the artifact root; the configuration records paths by file name only (every engine, `artifact_writer._portable_paths`); no user or host name is stored. Acceptance: `tools/loop_artifact_portability.py` and `tests/unit/test_loop_artifact_portability.py`. The tool's `check` scans, copies the artifact to another path and evaluates both in fresh interpreters with other working, home and temporary directories and user names, compares bitwise and counts generation calls; `--hold-permit` adds concurrent unlicensed evaluations of the copy while a plain Symbolica process holds the restricted permit, `--arithmetic`/`--precision` select the loop carrier and `--client` the loop SDK; `tree-capi` runs a tree artifact's own C example through the tree C API at both locations; `cpu-matrix` runs QEMU user-mode CPU models. The cross-host recipe is in its docstring.
  - **(a) SymJIT IR, recompiled at load.** A v4 loop artifact with native point inputs (`point_inputs = "native-v2"`, `native_process.NativeProcess`) stores the plan (kind 11), the saved exact evaluators of its tree program (kind 2: the interaction kernels of the trees and of the counterterm program, and the parameter program) and one binary64 SymJIT plane application per saved evaluator (kind 1, `point-inputs/kernel-<k>.symjit`, `point-inputs/parameters.symjit`). They are storage-v4 IR at -O2 (`native_trees._PLANE_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL`; `evaluator.jit.optimization_level` does not apply), except a program whose -O2 code fails the plane check at a probe row, kept at level 0 if that passes (§15 risk 13); the stored IR keeps its level, which load recompiles at, so both are portable. Until a fixed SymJIT is pinned, the fallback is decided by the generating host's SIMD code: a kernel plane certified at -O2 on a CPU class whose code does not show the defect runs unchecked on one whose code may. The parameter program always compiles at level 0 (`native_trees._PARAMETER_PLANE_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL`): it runs once per batch or scale, and level 0 has no peephole pass, so the defect cannot reach it on any CPU class. `symjit_plane::SymjitPlaneKernel::load_bytes` authenticates, recompiles for the loading CPU and seals them at load (`loop_v2::trees::BinaryPlane`); the native admission requires them.
    - *Export* (`native_trees._plane_applications` → `_loop.compile_v2_plane` → `loop_v2::trees::compile_plane_application_checked`): from the saved state's binary64 evaluator (coefficients cast as the runtime casts them) through Symbolica's Rust instruction export, so registered constants and fixed-argument functions (pi, ...) keep their binary64 values. The model's helper functions of the parameter program (`pyamplicol_v2_canonical::c*`, piecewise on `pyamplicol_scalar_v1::equal` with `if_else`/`join`) are inlined (`symjit_plane::InlinedProgram`, on an explicit stack without a depth limit), and the scalar primitives are bound as SymJIT external functions by name (`scalar_primitive_defuns`, at compile and at load). Each application is checked against its evaluator at generation, at two complex points off the real axis and at the real points it runs at (the parameter program: the request's parameter values; WP3.2 status, ew-native), and refused on a disagreement. `test_loop_plane_applications_equal_their_exact_states` compares every stored one with Symbolica at the parameter program's default inputs and at random inputs (1e-12).
    - *Batch independence*: SymJIT's SIMD and scalar kernels of one application round differently in the last bits, so `BinaryPlane` pads the rows of a call to whole SIMD blocks (copies of the last row, discarded) and a point's value does not depend on its batch (`test_native_scales_may_change_per_point`).
    - *Reproducible generation*: from empty and warm model caches the plan and every native member are byte-identical (`test_native_members_do_not_depend_on_the_model_cache`; the parameter program names its helpers in sorted order, since a compiled and a cached model list them differently), except `parameters.symjit`. SymJIT 2.27.3 (and `v228`) tags every external call with `rand::random()`, so the IR of a program calling the scalar primitives differs between two compilations, never between loads of one artifact (strict xfail `test_plane_applications_calling_primitives_are_byte_reproducible`). A deterministic-tag patch with a regression test is prepared in `workspace/reports/engine-v2/upstream/symjit-reproducible-external-calls/` and waits for the user's go to be filed.
    - The plan's kernels stay exact term lists interpreted natively (§5.5). The tree engine's SymJIT -O2 kernels are storage-v4 IR as before.
  - **Speed of the binary64 plane path.** The interaction program fills a plane kernel's input planes directly from the states (`plane_interactions`, `plane_propagator` in `run_interactions`). It works one plane at a time with contiguous stores, in calls of at most 1,024 rows (`PLANE_ROWS`, so a call's planes stay in L2), on a buffer that only grows (plane stride = padded rows plus one cache line; `PlaneRows::{planes, run}`). Padding and the row-major accumulation order are unchanged, so the values are bitwise those of a row-major fill. A/B on one native and tree, the exact-state arm with `native_trees._plane_applications` patched to return nothing (`tools.loop_benchmark pyamplicol <process> --inputs native --batches 128 --reps 3`, full colour, `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1`, both arms at once on two idle cores, then swapped; median ms/pt; release cu16 without LTO, so not the report's absolute values):

    | Native | Host load | Process | Plane applications | Exact states | 'other' stage (plane / exact) |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | `loop-native-artifact-portability-1ef77d80` | ~120 | u u~ > g g g | 8.35 / 9.19 | 13.43 / 15.44 (−38 %, −40 %) | 3.8-4.5 / 8.9-10.8 |
    | same | ~120 | g g > g g g | 39.1 / 40.3 | 61.1 / 64.2 (−36 %, −37 %) | 23.8-24.5 / 45.5-48.6 |
    | `loop-native-artifact-portability-ae704879` | ~160 | u u~ > g g g | 12.15 / 10.85 | 16.53 / 12.90 (−26 %, −16 %) | 5.0-5.2 / 8.5-10.8 |
    | same | ~160 | g g > g g g | 60.2 / 43.4 | 80.9 / 59.9 (−26 %, −28 %) | 25.1-36.1 / 44.1-58.2 |

    Records: `workspace/tmp/artifact-portability/r5/` (`runs/`, `logs/`, `ab.py`, `abpair.sh`).
  - **(b) What is scanned.** Every file and every PACBIN member (zlib members decompressed) for absolute paths under common roots, the generating host's home, working and temporary directories, the artifact's own location, user and host names; native binaries; the member kinds and SymJIT storage version; the manifest target (`portable-64le`, no CPU features). The v4 artifacts of (f) scan clean.
  - **(d) No regeneration.** A sentinel counts calls of generation code (`GENERATION_CODE`, `GENERATION_FILES`: slice and plan generation, the model compile, `GenerationBackend.generate`, `api.Generator.generate`, the native generator's and native DAG's inputs, the native-trees export with its plane compile, the observables' slice and column plans and the export of the stored observable columns; `test_sentinel_entries_name_existing_generation_code` resolves every entry) from before the first pyAmpliCol import to the end of the evaluation. On native-v2 artifacts it counts zero for every inclusive call: all four pole selections (the process stores the companion of its certified-scaleless families with its own tree program, `native_process.NativeCompanion`, PACBIN members `point-inputs/scaleless/*`; generation-scale-2), stability checks, double-double, `set_model_parameters`, per-order partials, and an exact flavour member served by its representative (the identity-port member listed in `process-groups.json` was proved at generation, `loop_service._v2_member`; the load reads it as `artifact._stored_member`). It counts zero for every observable call too (generation-scale-2, resumed run): colour-correlated (directed histories, also in "uv" with the companion's columns, with stability checks, in double-double), spin-correlated and helicity-resolved calls and the correlation catalogue, with and without declared observables, and a member's directed history. The process stores its observable column sets (`native_observables.StoredObservables`, PACBIN members `point-inputs/observables*`, every native-v2 process): per set (`correlated`: the Born metric and every declared history; `resolved`: per Born helicity row, and per Born basis element at LC) the seed records of its plan over the stored plan (`export_plan(column_sets=...)` forms them on the plan's own exporter after the plan, so the plan is unchanged; a set equal to the plan's own columns stores none, a set on the plan's own seed-weight columns stores only its column fields), the plan's Born components in the tree program's numbering, the lanes of its columns, the same for the stored companion; the column interferences (`loop_v2::trees::TreeProgram::set_columns`: exact overlaps of the Born basis with the CT ket tensors under the column's metric, the counterterm interference's own cells for the Born metric (stored as null), a directed history's operator and its swap, with the column's LC/NLC degrees, kept rows and bra basis elements, entry lists and directed metrics stored once per set); and the vector spin-source slots of the trees' and the CT program's states with their crossing phases (a source vector replaces the leg's wavefunction natively; the leg keeps its fixed row). A load builds `StoredObservableSlice` (no slice) and the observables package assembles the requested columns on the native interferences (`observables._Assembly`, `ColumnPlan.checked`). A declared process keeps every CT ket component in its tree program (`keep_kets`: a history can read a component the Born metric does not). **Plan v4 `column_weights`:** columns that differ only by their helicity rows have equal seed weights, so a plan may name per observable column its seed-weight column (`plan_export.weight_columns`; `loop_v2::plan` validates it, `loop_v2::seeds` reads each column's weights through it) and its seed class records hold one weight list per weight column; the exporter writes it only when columns share (every inclusive plan's bytes and digest are unchanged). Without it the six-gluon resolved interferences took 22.6 MB and the seven-gluon resolved set's seed records were killed after 7 min at 38 GB. Cost for a process without declared observables (FC; both sets: the Born metric and 32, 64, 128 resolved columns): g g > g g g 1.8 kB of observable members (zlib; the native members 249 kB), generation 14.0 s against 14.1 s without them; g g > g g g g 2.6 kB (3.47 MB), 47.8 s against 47.9 s; g g > g g g g g (top veto) 5.5 kB (69.8 MB), its observables' work in `generate_process` (583 s, process peak 11.3 GB) 0.12 s for the export and no seed records beyond the plan's and its companion's own (33.6 s and 5.7 s; one run, `workspace/tmp/generation-scale-2/r2/obs7/`). **Cost with every dipole declared** (fix round 2; FC, all T_i·T_j as directed histories, `post_build_validation = false`, loop_sm, one process per run, `workspace/tmp/generation-scale-2/obs-cost/`). Three stages made declared dipoles expensive; each is now native or gone, with its Python form kept as the oracle. (i) **Metrics.** A directed history's metric (Born basis × CT ket tensors under its operator and its swap) was one Python contraction per (b, t) pair (`DirectedColour.overlap`, now the oracle), 96 % of the export. It is now one native table per direction (package C's colour cells: `NativeCells.directed` images of the literal basis against every ket tensor, `native_observables._Metrics`). A metric is stored as its distinct cells with one index per (ket tensor, basis element) (schema 2, `MetricCells::Table`: the interference sums each distinct cell once per metric class; 29 distinct cells over 120 × 265 pairs at six gluons, 552 over 720 × 1,854 at seven). (ii) **Column records.** The column sets' seed records were the exporter's Python projection (`_Exporter.seed_records`, now the oracle). They are now projected natively by package C's C2 `native_cells.project_records` on the directed tables' native cells (`plan_export._set_records`, `NATIVE_COLUMN_RECORDS`, the slice-status DECISIONS 2026-10-26 ~15:10 item 4 route; the plan's own seed records keep D3's call site). The bytes are identical: every PACBIN member of the six-gluon artifact equals the Python projection's (the nondeterministic `parameters.symjit` aside), and `test_stored_metrics_equal_the_oracle` checks the bytes of the correlated set and its companion twin. (iii) **Self-adjoint dipoles.** A dipole took two seed columns (direct and swapped) and two metrics. A history with one gluon emission on each side, off two different legs and with the same emitted gluon, is self-adjoint when its swapped seed table equals its direct one with a nonzero cell (`observables.DirectedColour._self_adjoint`, compared on the native tables' distinct cells and ids). The direct cells are s_i <b|T_i T_j k> and the swapped ones s_j <b|T_j T_i k>, with T_i† = s_i T_i (a colour charge is hermitian up to its convention's phase) and T_i T_j = T_j T_i. Equal tables therefore give s_i = s_j, and the two directions agree on every tensor, the CT kets included. A self-adjoint dipole takes one seed column (2 Re, as a symmetric history) and stores one metric without evaluating its swap. The companion takes the slice's decision, so the lanes are numbered alike, and checks its own tables against it. `SELF_ADJOINT_HISTORIES = False` keeps the two-column form (the oracle). Every correlated output of u u~ > g g g with all ten dipoles, at FC and NLC, in every pole selection, binary64 and double-double, is bitwise the two-column form's (`test_self_adjoint_dipoles_take_one_column`, NLC nightly), and the stored swapped metrics equal the oracle's per-pair overlaps. A correlated call reads half the lanes: the ten dipoles of u u~ > g g g at 128 points, finite only, one thread, take 5.56 s against 11.45 s (best of five). **Six gluons** (g g > g g g g, 15 dipoles; cores 232-239, `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=8`, warm caches, two alternating runs per arm): `generate_process` 309.6 s at the audited head (12 threads), then 66.3 s after (i) (12 threads), 41.6 s after (ii), 39.7 s after (iii), and **38.5 s** with the native comparison. Without declared histories it takes 35.0 s, so the dipoles cost **+10 %** (the whole generation: 45.7 s against 41.8 s, +9.4 %). The remaining 3.5 s are the directed seed tables (0.5 s), the swapped tables and their comparison (0.46 s), the column records (0.62 s; 27.2 s in Python) and the metrics (1.8 s for 15 tables; 3.6 s with the swaps, 240.8 s per pair in Python). Process peak 3.18 → 1.42 GB (1.35 GB undeclared); process PACBIN 14.9 → 5.83 MB (4.40 MB undeclared); the correlated-columns member 9.25 → 0.31 MB. g g > g g g (10 dipoles): the export takes 0.06 s of metrics (5.0 s per pair in Python). **Seven gluons** (g g > g g g g g, top veto, 21 dipoles; same cores and threads, one run per arm, one seven-leg job at a time): `generate_process` takes **784.1 s** with all dipoles against 418.6 s undeclared (+87 %), and the whole generation 894.9 s against 518.2 s. Process peak 17.2 against 11.2 GiB, reached in the native projection of the correlated set. Artifact 182.6 against 88.3 MB: the observable members are 94.2 MB of the 177.3 MB PACBIN (correlated seed records 56.9 MB, companion seed records 17.1 MB, metrics 20.2 MB, all zlib). For comparison, fix round 2's head (12 threads, before (ii) and (iii)) took 2,264.7 s at 30.7 GiB; its Python column records alone took 1,278.7 s (1,029.6 s for the process and 249.1 s for the companion twins). The extra 365.5 s at seven gluons are the directed seed tables (28.8 s, 21 × 1.3 s), the swapped tables with their comparison (28.8 s), the column records natively (34.5 s) and **the metrics (252.6 s)**: 21 native tables of 720 × 1,854 generic cells at 10.4 s each (482.6 s with the swaps), which do not speed up from 8 to 12 threads because the engine's serial phases over the 1.33 M (structure, basis) tasks of a table dominate (task registry, cache lookups and stores, value interning; `loop_v2::generate::cells`, the engine's `cells`). *Open, routed to package C (native-cells phase 2):* the directed metrics at seven legs (all histories in one batched call, unit literal ket tensors by cycle counting as the inclusive CT cells are, `native_trees._counterterm_program`, or one metric per dipole orbit relabelled with C4's orbit representatives), and the size of the correlated seed records (D's and C's record format, one weight list per class and column). Every PACBIN member but the correlated columns (and the nondeterministic `parameters.symjit`) is byte-identical to the oracle's; the correlated calls of u u~ > g g g at FC and NLC (11 histories, all/uv/ir/none, double-double) are bitwise those of the per-pair list format (`test_stored_metrics_equal_the_oracle` checks the tables against the oracle). Binary64 observable calls run without a licence while the restricted permit is held, with pyAmpliCol's native key disabled (`test_binary64_observable_calls_touch_no_symbolica_state`). The public O1 checks against the legacy records (`test_loop_v2_artifact.py`: FC/LC/NLC, members, u u~ > g g g with spin, binary64, double-double and 60 digits) run through the stored columns; against the former regenerated path (Python per-point trees) the stored columns agree within 3.1×10⁻¹⁴ of each output's scale on g g > g g (all and "uv", three histories, spin, resolved). The asymmetric Ward mode is a validation tool of the generated slice and is not stored. `native-v1` (no plane applications) is refused at load (`test_loop_artifact_with_retired_or_unknown_point_inputs_is_refused`).
  - **(e) Licence.** Without `SYMBOLICA_LICENSE`, Symbolica's restricted mode admits one thread per lock directory (by default the user's): the first Symbolica state access of a process takes it and every other unlicensed process aborts. The Python package key (`set_library_key` in `pyamplicol/__init__.py`) unlocks only the Python `symbolica` module, not the Rust copy in `_rusticol` and the loop SDK; `_rusticol` touches no Symbolica state at import (the scalar primitives are registered by the decoders that need them).
    - **Binary64 needs no Symbolica, except the lanes routed to the exact engine** (§3.7): the native lane runs the plane applications and loading decodes no exact state (`test_binary64_loop_evaluation_touches_no_symbolica_state`: two concurrent unlicensed binary64 evaluations complete while the permit is held and the native key is disabled, `PYAMPLICOL_NATIVE_LIBRARY_KEY=off`). The exception (G2-M3, §11.2 'Absolute thresholds'; DECISIONS 2026-10-28 ~05:20 (4)): a lane whose squared masses or invariants reach the fast engine's absolute 10⁻⁹ thresholds (a keV-scale mass in GeV) is reduced by the generic binary64 engine, which builds and evaluates Symbolica expressions under the package key, like DoubleFloat (`test_routed_binary64_lanes_run_under_the_package_key`, `test_the_reference_reducer_takes_the_key_on_its_own_thread`); with the key disabled such an evaluation aborts while the permit is held, and after the key expires (2028-01-01) it needs a personal licence or Symbolica's restricted mode. There is no other exception at any model parameters, scale or pole selection (USER ~17:35, M-SM MS-MASTERS round 5): the parameter program's plane takes the exact evaluator's side of every branch cut at real inputs by construction (§3.7, the plane check), so nothing is checked at run time. Rounds 1–4 had checked the plane against the saved state once per new parameter key under the package key, which a width set to zero, a new mass or LogSwitch = 0 triggered; removed. The held-permit tests: `g g > g g` at αs = 0.12, MT = 172 and each of WZ, WW, WH = 0 (`test_binary64_parameter_changes_need_no_key`); the shipped EW self-test (e- e+ > e- e+ [QED] on loop_qcd_qed_sm) at MadLoop's second and third points and scales (μ_R² = 4.9·10⁵, 1.96·10⁶) for every pole selection, bitwise equal to the keyed run and equal to double-double by the binary64 rule, and at MZ = 91.19, MW = 80.5 and LogSwitch = 0, equal to double-double (`test_binary64_ew_evaluations_need_no_key`); its stored parameter plane equals its state output by output at new scales, αs, α, epsIR = 0, LogSwitch = 0, MT, MZ and MW = 100 (a cut the plane had taken on the other side; `test_ew_parameter_plane_equals_its_state_off_the_generation_values`). Under the complex-mass scheme CM05's nightly evaluates MadLoop's four points at their four scales (μ_R = 1000, 700, 1400, 500) and equals MadLoop CMS (`test_cm05_native_inputs_equal_madloop_cms`); CM01's binary64 at zero widths equals MadLoop's zero-width virtual (`test_cm01_masters_avoid_the_known_complex_mass_defects`). Against the former exact-state evaluation the binary64 values moved in the last bits (instruction order and FMA; the g g > g g Born 56.50237664591928 → 56.502376645919256, DoubleFloat 56.502376645919255).
    - **DoubleFloat and arbitrary precision use pyAmpliCol's library key in the native code** (user decision 2026-10-24 ~16:05). The workspace crate `pyamplicol` (`rust/crates/pyamplicol`, named after the package the key is issued for, `publish = false`) registers the key with Symbolica's documented `set_library_key!` into a `pub(crate)` static and gives pyAmpliCol's other crates thread-bound guards only (`pyamplicol::unlock`, `unlock_thread`). Guards are held while decoding saved evaluators, registering the scalar primitives, running exact kernels of the tree runtime (`TreeRuntime::runs_exact_states`: DoubleFloat, arbitrary precision, or binary64 on a CPU without SymJIT), in the executor's generic reduction and every worker of the v2 executor's pool (and formerly in the legacy virtual runtime, removed with the legacy engine, §13.3). A personal licence is used as before; a rejected key leaves inert guards. Tests: `test_unlicensed_extended_precision_loop_evaluations_run_concurrently` (binary64, DoubleFloat and 32 digits concurrently and unlicensed while the permit is held, agreeing at an exactly massless point to 1e-12; with the key disabled DoubleFloat aborts), `test_native_library_key_uses_symbolicas_documented_registration`, `test_native_library_key_equals_the_python_package_key` (the two copies of the key, compared by digest). The v4 SDK (`V2Runtime`, generation-scale-2) inherits the guards through `loop_v2::trees` and the executor.
    - **The permit.** Symbolica keeps the restricted-thread lock in `SYMBOLICA_LOCK_DIR`, else `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/symbolica/locks`, else the cache directory's `symbolica/locks`. `RestrictedPermit` resolves it once (`symbolica_lock_dir`, or an explicit `lock_dir`) and passes it to the holder, its control and every instance it covers, so instances with a fresh `HOME` or without `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` cannot take another permit. The tests use a private 0700 lock directory (`private_symbolica_permit`): the checks discriminate the same way, the user's real permit stays free and the file runs beside other unlicensed test files. The tool's `--hold-permit` takes the user's real permit.
    - The tree engine needs no licence at any precision (unchanged): binary64 runs SymJIT applications and no Symbolica code (Python, and the C API although `librusticol_capi.a` links Symbolica through the loop SDK); other precisions go through the Python `symbolica` module, which the package key unlocks.
  - **(c) CPU feature levels.** SymJIT 2.27.3 needs x86-64 with AVX and FMA3: its AVX generator is chosen from the AVX bit alone but emits FMA3 (`vfmsub231sd`, SIGILL on `SandyBridge-v2`), and the shared epilogue emits `vzeroupper` for the SSE generator too (SIGILL on `Nehalem-v2`, `qemu64`). The SymJIT loaders refuse x86-64 CPUs without AVX and FMA3 with a compatibility error before compiling (`symjit_plane::require_symjit_host_cpu`); the tool reads the same features from the native extension (`_rusticol._symjit_host_cpu`). Upstream: SymJIT issues https://github.com/siravan/symjit/issues/22 (the FMA3/`vzeroupper` requirement) and /23 (`Config::default()` panics on a bad `symjit.toml`), pull requests https://github.com/siravan/symjit/pull/24 and /25, merged into `v228` (`4031c1a9`, `d37160b8`), not in a release yet (crates.io 2.28.1; pyAmpliCol pins 2.27.3). The gate stays until a SymJIT release with the fixes is pinned. A v4 loop artifact holds both forms of its tree program, so on such a CPU binary64 runs the exact states (under the library key) instead of refusing the artifact; its values then follow that CPU's class. QEMU 11.1 user mode on the native-v2 g g > g g artifact: `Haswell-v4` recompiles the plane applications and is bitwise equal to the host; `SandyBridge-v2` and `Nehalem-v2` run the exact states without SIGILL or refusal and differ from the host in the last bits (at most 1.6e-15 relative), equal to each other (`workspace/tmp/artifact-portability/r5/cpu/matrix.json`). Bitwise identity across SymJIT classes is not expected (FMA contraction). AArch64: SymJIT generates AArch64 code, but no relocation check has run on AArch64 (expected to hold, untested). The Python API itself needs x86-64-v2 here (numpy 2 raises SIGILL on `qemu64`).
  - **Environment independence.** SymJIT's `Config::default()` reads `./symjit.toml` or `$SYMJIT_TOML` and panics on a malformed or unreadable file; our direct-application and plane loaders build their configuration explicitly (`symjit_plane::explicit_loader_config`); the SymJIT-internal call is fixed by pull request 25.
  - **glibc floor (user decision 2026-10-28, slice-status DECISIONS ~07:40 and ~13:30; package glibc-floor).** On Linux a native library needs the glibc symbol versions it links (ELF version needs, `readelf -V`), and the dynamic loader refuses it on a glibc that does not define one. The release natives (`_rusticol.so`, `librusticol_loop_sdk.so`) built by `tools/developer/build_loop_native.py` with the repository's toolchain need at most `GLIBC_2.38` and no ABI marker: **the floor is glibc 2.38** (pinned by the tests, stated in the user docs, the SDK headers, Fortran module and README). *Build:* on x86-64 Linux the C dependencies (GMP/MPFR/MPC through `gmp-mpfr-sys`, and `cc`-crate objects) are compiled with `CC="<cc> -mtls-dialect=gnu"`, after any flag of the compiler wrapper. TLS-descriptor objects (`-mtls-dialect=gnu2`, which some nixpkgs cc wrappers add in `nix-support/cc-cflags-before`) make the linked natives need `GLIBC_ABI_GNU2_TLS`, an ABI marker that only glibc builds carrying it define (BZ #33129: glibc 2.43, and the 2.38-2.42 stable branches after the backport, not the 2.42 release tarball), so no release number decides it; slice natives `fb54246c`..`6e123fdb` needed it because `builds/cargo-v2-slice` held GMP/MPFR compiled through such a wrapper (the native digest does not cover the C compiler). `gmp-mpfr-sys` neither reruns its build script when `CC` changes nor keys its cache by the actual compiler (only by the `CC` string), so a target directory whose recorded C settings (`pyamplicol-c-dependencies.json`) differ first drops its `gmp-mpfr-sys` outputs (`cargo clean -p gmp-mpfr-sys`); the first build on a host compiles GMP/MPFR/MPC from source (`make` and `m4` on `PATH`). `build-info.json` records the C settings and each library's `glibc_version_needs` (by release). Measured 2026-10-28: `loop-native-glibc-floor-fd7838e4` (fresh target dir, package profile, C dependencies compiled through the gnu2-default nixpkgs `gcc-wrapper-15.3.0`) has 0 TLSDESC relocations in libgmp/libmpfr/libmpc (1,684 traditional TLS ones in libmpfr.a, against TLSDESC in `builds/cargo-v2-slice`'s), and both natives top at `GLIBC_2.38` without marker; on a real glibc 2.40 (Nix python3 3.13.9) its SDK `.so` loads while `loop-native-v2-slice-6e123fdb`'s is refused for `GLIBC_ABI_GNU2_TLS`. `loop-native-glibc-floor-91a4dd94` (the same target dir, env.sh's cc, the slice profile: one codegen unit, thin LTO) exercised the drop of the stale `gmp-mpfr-sys` outputs and gives the same requirements. *Python* (`pyamplicol/_native_loader.py`, installed first by `pyamplicol/__init__.py`): a `sys.meta_path` finder hands `pyamplicol._rusticol` to an `ExtensionFileLoader` subclass whose `create_module`, before `dlopen`, looks each `GLIBC_*` version the file needs up in the version definitions of the glibc library it is needed from, as mapped into the process (`/proc/self/maps`): the loader's own check (weak needs, `VER_FLG_WEAK`, which ld.so does not enforce, are skipped), so markers are decided by the running glibc build. A library not loaded yet is left to the loader. A missing version, or a process not on glibc (`os.confstr('CS_GNU_LIBC_VERSION')`, else `gnu_get_libc_version`), raises `NativePlatformError` (a `DependencyError`, not an `ImportError`, so `except ImportError` sites do not reword it; the one broad `except Exception` site, the direct-arena P-kernel compiler, re-raises it) with `library`, `missing`, `required` (the newest missing numbered release, None for markers only) and `found`. A loader error naming a missing glibc version at the two wired sites — the guarded `pyamplicol._rusticol` import (for the extension or a library it needs) and the Symbolica import in `pyamplicol/__init__.py` (the workspace's `community-release-python-9cdc2949` Symbolica itself needs `GLIBC_ABI_GNU2_TLS`) — becomes the same error with the loader's text, and a dependency's library gets its own remedy; other native code (numpy, the libraries of C++/ASM artifacts) keeps the loader's error. Cost: the module import ~0.4 ms (warm), the check ~0.9 ms once per process (mostly `/proc/self/maps`), against ~0.27 s for `import pyamplicol`. *C/C++/Fortran:* a C/C++ client of the release shared library that defines `RUSTICOL_VIRTUAL_SHARED` stops at compile time on an older glibc toolchain (`RUSTICOL_VIRTUAL_GLIBC_FLOOR_MAJOR/MINOR`; exact, since every glibc release defines the numbered versions before it; omitted for an SDK built from source on an older host). Otherwise the floor is a numbered version that real symbols bind (`__isoc23_strtol@GLIBC_2.38`, `hypot@GLIBC_2.35`), so GNU ld, whose default for executables is `--no-allow-shlib-undefined`, fails a client's link on a toolchain older than glibc 2.38 (``undefined reference to `__isoc23_strtol@GLIBC_2.38'``; only the symbol-less `GLIBC_ABI_GNU2_TLS` marker linked silently), and a client built on a newer glibc and run on an older one gets the loader's ``version `GLIBC_2.38' not found``: both messages are documented. *Tests:* `tests/unit/test_native_glibc_floor.py`. The pin asserts the staged natives' measured requirement exactly (~13:30 ruling (b)): no marker, and the newest numbered need equal to the documented floor `GLIBC_2.38`, so a raise, a drop or a marker fails and names the docs to update; the other checks use stub libraries and stub `libc.so.6` files linked by the test.
  - **(f) Results** (AMD EPYC 9754, binary64; wall times on a shared host, load 130-300; release cu16 natives without LTO):

    | tree g g > g g g g (recurrence, -O2, API bundle) | 1.3 MB | 13 s | 0.3 s | clean | bitwise | 0 | tree C API (`tree-capi`): all 7,680 resolved values and both totals bitwise = Python at both locations; 2 concurrent C instances |
    | v4 g g > g g FC (`loop_physics` tests) | 5.4 MB | 13-14 s | 1.1-1.2 s (18 plane applications recompiled), 2 points 0.09 s | clean | bitwise | 0 | binary64 ×2 with the native key off; binary64 + DoubleFloat + 32 digits with the key |
