Earlier records are in this file's history.
  - **Open (owned).** (1) Python twin of the Rust inliner: the tree engine's SymJIT export (`symbolica_adapters._JITSymbolicaEvaluatorAdapter`) inlines helper bodies with `symbolica_helpers._InlinedProgram` and refuses registered constants read as 0 with `_verify_symjit_plane_constants`, because it exports through Symbolica's Python instruction export; routing it through the Rust exporter (`compile_symbolica_evaluator_to_plane_application_bytes` on the evaluator's saved state, a new `_rusticol` entry) would remove both. A Symbolica getter `EvaluatorInstructions.constant_functions` (reproducer and tests, on upstream `main` `1a1829b`) is prepared in `workspace/reports/engine-v2/upstream/symbolica-python-export-constant-functions/` for the coordinator's review; with it the Python check could read the constants instead of evaluating. Until then `test_symjit_plane_helper_functions.py` checks both inliners bitwise against Symbolica. Owner: a follow-up of artifact-portability (tree-engine export, §0.4), not scheduled. (2) The SymJIT call tag: the prepared patch, after the user's go. (3) The regeneration residuals of (d): generation-scale-2. (4) AArch64 evidence: needs an AArch64 host.
- **Point inputs** (as built by WP3.1's sixth increment and artifact-portability: `point_inputs = "native-v2"`, the process PACBIN also stores `native_process.NativeProcess`, its tree program with the Born, counterterm and field components, its saved exact evaluators and their binary64 SymJIT plane applications (§12.1 (a); `native-v1`, without them, is refused with a request to regenerate by the writer and the loader, `LOOP_V2_RETIRED_POINT_INPUTS` in `generation/loop_artifact.py` and `loop_artifact.rs`), so no call regenerates (every pole selection, with the stored companion; flavour members; correlated, resolved and catalogue calls on the stored observable columns, §12.1 (d); generation-scale-2). A process whose native programs or observable columns cannot be exported is refused at generation (`artifact._not_storable`, the export's reason), and the former `point_inputs = "regenerated-at-load"` (per-point Python inputs, §5.5, from a `LoopSlice` regenerated at load from the artifact's compiled model and request, the regenerated plan's digest checked against the stored one) is a retired declaration, refused at admission like `native-v1` (generation-scale-2). Generation does not depend on the interpreter's history: tree stages use canonical symbols (`assemble._canonical_stage`, WP2.5) and kernel terms a canonical order, and the plan digest and the binary64 Born and virtual values are bitwise equal after 0, 130, 141 and 300 unrelated Symbolica symbols (re-measured after the merge of the slice's (monomial, key) order, `42c6117d`; before the canonical kernel order, 141 symbols reordered 14 of 20 gg→gg kernels, so the load was refused, and 130 changed the last bits of the Born components; `test_fresh_interpreter_loads_native_inputs_without_generation` loads after 141). Other Symbolica normal forms in the point inputs would still be history-dependent in principle (the tree engine's own stage compile, WP2.5 open list); native point inputs store the point-input bits. The "uv" and "ir" pole selections also run the companion of the certified-scaleless families, stored with its own tree program (`native_process.NativeCompanion`; it forms its own block rows natively, `NativeExecutor.scaleless`).
- **Sizes:** gg→gggg (1100 families) publishes 7.4 MB: 45 MB of plan JSON stored as a 2.1 MB member (the schedule-F oracle section, 52 MB more, is not stored; the runtime dresses by B), 5.3 MB of compiled model (nightly test, < 20 MB). With the companion plan of its 6 certified-scaleless families (WP2.10, §12.4) it publishes 8.8 MB (PACBIN 3.5 MB). 20–80 MB at 7 legs [I], against 394 MB for the legacy engine at 6 legs. With the native point inputs and the counterterm program of the recursion (WP3.1 seventh increment) the six-gluon artifact is 11 MB.
- **Binary plan member (proposed by WP3.1 D, native-lowering, for generation-scale-2's I6 writer; RUST-MIGRATION.md §3.D, slice-status DECISIONS 2026-10-25 ~15:45 item 3).** `ExportedPlan.binary()` (`loops/v2/native_lowering.py`, `rusticol_core::loop_v2::plan::binary`, format 3: format 2 plus generation-scale-2's `column_weights`, the carry of DECISIONS 2026-10-26 ~05:10 item 2) is the plan's columnar encoding: one column per record field at its narrowest width, repeated per-content lists stored once, plan data only (no path, host or build information), deterministic, decoding to the model whose JSON is the plan JSON byte for byte. It is read by `loop_v2::plan::read_binary`, the member's one reader, through the loader's validation and derivations (bounded decode and derivations, fail-closed; the schedule-B row links stay derived at load). Proposed storage:
  - **Kind** `LOOP_RUNTIME_PLAN_COLUMNS = 12`, a new `pacbin-v1` member kind (`PacbinMemberKind::LoopRuntimePlanColumns`, `evaluator_container.PacbinMemberKind`), so a reader never mistakes it for the JSON of kind 11.
  - **Members** `schedule/loop-plan-v4.bin.zlib` (the main plan), `schedule/loop-plan-v4-scaleless.bin.zlib` (the companion's, §12.4) and, for I6's stored observable columns, `schedule/loop-plan-v4-columns-<k>.bin.zlib` (the column plans of `observables.ColumnPlan`, `export_plan(..., columns=)`), each the zlib stream of `ExportedPlan.binary()`. zlib on top of the columns is cheap and large: g g > g g g g 1.23 MB → 0.17 MB (7.0×, inflated in 2 ms), u u~ > g g g g 0.40 MB → 0.07 MB (6.1×).
  - **Inflation is bounded:** the loader inflates at most the member's length recorded in `execution.json` (`plan_member_bytes`, covered by the payload digest) and refuses a longer stream, then `read_binary` bounds the decode and the derivations by that length (`DECODED_PER_BYTE`, `DERIVED_PER_BYTE`).
  - **Digests:** `plan_sha256` stays the SHA-256 of the plan JSON (the member decodes to it byte for byte: `PlanModel::json`), so the regeneration check and the S8 digests are unchanged; the member's own bytes are covered by the PACBIN payload digest.
  - **One plan encoding per artifact:** once I6 stores kind 12, the JSON member (kind 11) is no longer written and `load_loop_v2_artifact` reads the member (`read_binary`); a v4 artifact with kind 11 only is then refused with a request to regenerate (no backward compatibility, AGENTS.md). **Not stored yet (open, generation-scale-2's next run, I6):** the SDK v4 runtime landed in generation-scale-2 reading the JSON member (kind 11), as every loader does; storing kind 12, switching `load_loop_v2_artifact` and the Python loader to `read_binary` and refusing kind 11 alone is I6's next step.
  - **Size** at g g > g g g g g FC (one sector column, `forward=False`): 23.0 MB of member for 193.6 MB of JSON (the I11 criterion ≤ 40 MB excluding bundles holds before D3's lowering), u u~ > g g g g g FC (QED = 0) 14.7 MB for 69.2 MB (WP3.1 D status below).
- **SDK (Rust/C/C++/Fortran): plan v4 through `rusticol_virtual_open` (generation-scale-2, from the parked `wip/v2-generation-scale-2-sdk`).** The C, C++, Fortran and Rust ABIs open a plan v4 artifact with native point inputs through `rusticol_loop_reduction::v2_runtime::V2Runtime`: `loop_artifact::read_loop_v2_native_inputs` reads the record, the tree program, the saved evaluators and their plane applications (the admission does not inflate them; the Python runtime reads its own copy), the tree program binds the external parameters (the caller's over the defaults; the scale parameter is √μ_R² per point), forms the trees, Born, |A0|² and counterterm and field components through the binary64 plane applications, the plan's executor dresses and reduces (the Python binding's binary64 backend: fast engine, both master poles), and the components are assembled per sector in the Python API's binary64 operations and order. Ordinary binary64 virtuals with model R2 in every pole selection of REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM I (`rusticol_virtual_set_poles(handle, "all" | "none" | "uv" | "ir")`, also in the C++, Fortran and Rust wrappers and `evaluate_v2_artifact`): finite-only skips the pole-only work natively and returns the poles as quiet NaNs; the split selections (fix round 2) reduce every lane with its UV residue (`Poles::Split`) and run the stored companion of the certified-scaleless families (its plan and tree program, `LoopV2NativeInputs.scaleless`, PACBIN members `point-inputs/scaleless/*`, built on first use), then split every component by origin as `poles.split_sector` / `select_sector` do (`v2_runtime::split_virtual`), in the Python API's binary64 operations and order (the companion's lanes after the process's, only their UV residues kept). Catani's operator is not part of a selection's values (it is the Python API's separate IR-pole prediction, `PointResult.ir_poles`), so the SDK does not form it; plan v1-v3 handles take "all" only. Resolved, correlated, verified and extended-precision calls of a plan v4 process are refused with a message (they go through the Python API; open, not in this package's scope). Binary64 touches no Symbolica state, so SDK instances need no licence, with §12.1 (e)'s exception: light-mass lanes. **Parity with the Python API (sdk-parity, second genericity audit M2, m3 and the SDK advisories).** Model parameters (at open and `update_parameters`, every client) follow `V2VirtualRuntime._checked_parameters`, whose rule is now one function per language (`assemble.check_model_parameters`, `v2_runtime::checked_parameters`): the whole mapping is validated before anything is applied, and an unknown name (not an external parameter of the artifact's model, `model/parameters.json`, the Python API's `compiled.ir.parameters` of nature external), the renormalisation scale's parameter (the native record's `scale_parameter`, resolved at generation as the Python API resolves it, not by name) and a parameter fixed at generation at another exact value are refused with a typed `ModelParameter` error carrying the Python API's message (C `RUSTICOL_VIRTUAL_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, C++ `VirtualError::status()`, Fortran `ierr` and `error_message`, `evaluate_v2_artifact` `ValueError`); a refused update changes nothing. The fixed set is every external parameter fixed at generation (`LoopSlice.fixed_parameters`: the request's, as given, and the model's external parameters the preparation fixes besides, from the prepared model's `structure.fixed_parameters`, e.g. a colour declaration's external scheme constants; the names an applied source restriction removed from the model are unknown names to every client already), recorded in the native record's `fixed_parameters` and applied by every runtime; before (fix round 1), the record held the request's alone, so an external scheme constant at another value entered trees whose counterterm colour continuation was built at the fixed value (toy: loop_sm with `lhv` external, `loop_v2_ufo_variants.external_scheme_constant_ufo`). Every client refuses an empty name first (the Python API's `TypeError`; Rust `InvalidArgument`, C `RUSTICOL_VIRTUAL_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, `evaluate_v2_artifact` `ValueError`, same text), reads the values next (as Python's `Decimal` of ASCII text: no fractions, an infinity or NaN not finite, other digits or whitespace than ASCII refused, by the Python API's `runtime.virtual._number` too; `v2_runtime::decimal_parameter`), then the names, both by name, so a mapping with several refused entries gets the same message everywhere; a complex value is the Python API's `CompatibilityError` (Rust `Compatibility`, C `RUSTICOL_VIRTUAL_RUNTIME_ERROR`, as `"direct"` R2). The stored-process view (`native_process._ProcessView`, `NativeProcess.executor(..., externals=...)`) applies the same rule; the native record carries the model's external parameter names (`externals`) and the names of the fixed set the model preparation fixed (`preparation_fixed_parameters`), so a stored view alone refuses unknown names (before, `NativeProcess.executor` without `externals`, the benchmark's `--load` path, dropped a misspelt name) and every client says who fixed a refused fixed value (the request's `[loop.preparation] fixed_parameters`, or the model preparation: a scheme constant, not a run-time parameter; `assemble.fixed_refusal`, `v2_runtime::fixed_refusal`); a record of an older build has neither, and its views check no names. Before, the SDK silently replaced a caller's value of a fixed parameter by the fixed one and overwrote the scale parameter, and applied an update value by value. The slice's per-point evaluation (`LoopSlice._point_parameters`, the reference executor's twin) applies the same rule. The default R2 strategy `"auto"` (the C ABI's and C++ wrapper's default) selects the model R2 of a v4 artifact as the Python API does (`"direct"` is refused); before, `open_v4` refused every strategy but `"model"`. The tree and closure stages run on the executor's pool (`V2Executor::install`), so the runtime's `threads` bound covers them (before, the global rayon pool); the Python API's native executor (`plan_export.NativeExecutor`, the PyO3 `V2Executor`'s `Trees::fill`) binds its batch and runs the same stages on its own pool too (before, 2 -> 11 threads at `threads=1` with `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=8`). Scale-dependent batches: see §12.1's parameter program. Tests: `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_sdk_parity.py` (g g > t t~ with WT fixed: the Python API, `evaluate_v2_artifact`, a C client and the slice refuse WT = 1.4915, MU_R and an unknown name with the same text, a mapping with one refused entry changes nothing, and accepted values (WT at zero, an unread MH, αs, MT = 150 at a point on shell there) are bitwise the Python API's; the C++ and Fortran wrappers compile and report the refusal; on the renamed-model harness's `scale` variant `muR` is refused as the scale and `MU_R` as unknown; `threads=1` starts no global rayon pool), `rust/loop-reduction/sdk/tests/v4_parameters.rs` (the safe Rust `Runtime`: an ignored test run with `--ignored` and `PYAMPLICOL_V4_GGTT_ARTIFACT`, failing without it; error kinds checked; the parity file's opt-in nightly id `test_the_rust_sdk_follows_the_python_api` runs it on its artifact given a private `PYAMPLICOL_SDK_CARGO_TARGET_DIR`) and the unit test of `checked_parameters`. Evidence: the C client (`tools/loop_artifact_portability.py`, `CLIENT_SOURCE`) equals the Python API bitwise at both locations of a relocated g g > g g artifact, and two unlicensed SDK instances run while another process holds the restricted permit (`test_loop_artifact_portability.py`, the former strict xfails); the C client equals the Python API bitwise in all four pole selections, the finite part identical in all four (`test_loop_v4_sdk_selects_poles_like_python`), and `evaluate_v2_artifact` does at g g > g g and d d~ > g g with the default and a caller-set αs (`test_rust_runtime_equals_the_python_api`). A v2 artifact still carries no API bundle (recorded as a configuration adjustment).

### 12.2 Public API and configuration

As built (WP2.6). The planned changes below are kept in the history of this file; what exists is:

- **Surface.**
  - `Generator(RunConfig)` with `process.loop_coupling_orders`: `generate` publishes plan v4 (`loop_service._generate_v2`) and `plan` is the v2 dry run (`_plan_v2`, `loop_graph_count` 0 per process). v2 is the only one-loop engine (MQ-LEGACY-REMOVE, §13.3): `loop.engine` (TOML, override, CLI `--loop-engine`) is refused with the reason, and so is a `rusticol.loop-runtime.v3` artifact at load and inspection.
  - `Runtime.load` dispatches a v4 process to `loops.v2.artifact.V2VirtualRuntime`, which implements the `RuntimeBackend` calls `evaluate`, `evaluate_resolved`, `evaluate_correlated(_many)`, `set_model_parameters` and `set_spin_correlation_vectors`; a v3 process still loads `VirtualRuntimeBackend`.
  - The result types (`api/virtual_results.py`), with the additions below.
  - The C, C++, Fortran, Rust and UMAMI SDK ABI is unchanged; `rusticol_virtual_open` dispatches a plan v4 artifact to the native `V2Runtime` (ordinary binary64 virtuals, §12.1 SDK), legacy artifacts as before.
- **Options added.**
  - Pole selection `PoleSelection` (`pyamplicol.config`, re-exported by `pyamplicol.api`), one string-valued option across TOML (`evaluation.poles = "all" | "none" | "uv" | "ir"`, default `"all"`), CLI (`--poles {all,none,uv,ir}`), API (`poles=` on `evaluate`, `evaluate_resolved`, `evaluate_correlated(_many)`), result types (`VirtualEvaluation.poles`, `ResolvedVirtualEvaluation.poles`) and the artifact declaration (`physics.json` `poles`). `"none"` is the finite part only: poles `None` (`LaurentCoefficients`), also for correlated and resolved outputs, from the same artifact; the finite part is bitwise the same. Booleans and unknown selections are refused. `"uv"` and `"ir"` (WP2.10, §12.4) return the selected origin's pole parts, and `pole_parts` (one `VirtualPoleParts` per point: `uv` and `ir`, each component a Laurent triple per sector, and per helicity and colour component for resolved output; `pole_part(origin, component)`) both origins by component, for inclusive, correlated and resolved calls.
  - `[evaluation.stability_checks]` and per call `stability_checks=VirtualStabilityChecks(...)` (REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM C: any list of exact rotations or a rotation count, and the reduction-direction check, optionally `rescaled`; none by default, and then no estimate; recommended `VirtualStabilityChecks(1, True, rescaled=True)` = `stability.RECOMMENDED_CHECKS`, §10.4): one `VirtualStabilityEstimate` per point (per helicity row and colour component for resolved output); CLI `--stability-rotation SPEC` (repeatable), `--stability-rotations COUNT`, `--stability-reduction-direction`, `--stability-rescaled`. Escalating the arithmetic stays the caller's decision; the `[evaluation.stability]` rescue policy (and its CLI options) was removed with the legacy engine: any non-default value is refused at configuration time (`config.models.EVALUATION_STABILITY_REMOVED`) and a Python `VirtualStabilityPolicy` at load. Invalid scales raise for the whole call, also with `on_failure="return"`: a non-positive `mu_r2` and one at which a counterterm coupling is not finite in the lane's carrier (`mu_r2 = "1e-400"` in binary64: `ValueError` "a counterterm coupling's diagnostic is not finite"); returning those points one by one is open (pre-existing v2 behaviour).
  - `on_failure="return"`: a failed point is `None` with its reason, the other points are unaffected.
- **Diagnostics as built.** `VirtualColorContraction`: `requested` is the request's `"auto"`, `"direct"` or `"symmetric-group-fft"`, `algorithm` is what the native executor ran: `"symmetric-group-fft"` (the certified pairs of the stored seed Fourier record, `regular_kernels` their count; MQ-FFT, §7.3) or `"direct"` (no `"diagonal"` was added). `ProcessPhysics` reports `helicity_coverage = "all"` and `structural_zero_helicity_count = 0`: the plan keeps every Born helicity row, structural zeros included (u u~ > d d~ g keeps all 32), and does not count them yet. Per-point diagnostics carry the carrier, precision and colour contraction; the schedule, per-family estimates, rescue tier and stage times are not reported (WP2.7, with the live report).
- **Not built:** `[loop] schedule` and `[loop] reduction_grouping` (the runtime dresses by schedule B; M is selected per class by WP2.8/WP2.7), `[loop] colour = "integrated-full"` (§7.6). `r2_strategy` is unchanged; v2 artifacts carry model-supplied R2 only.
- **Request checks at the public boundary** (`loop_service._v2_request`, before any loop generation): the Born amplitude orders are read from the Born DAG (`artifact.born_amplitude_orders`) and the loop orders follow them, one per Born amplitude order and correction (`v2_loop_orders`); a process without a Born amplitude is refused; the renormalisation-scale parameter is resolved (`_v2_scale_parameter`: UFO/SLHA code (LOOP, 1), else the only external parameter of block LOOP); coloured widths in tree currents are refused (WP2.6 status, widths). The dry run also runs the slice's model preparation (`assemble.prepare_request_model`, the function `LoopSlice.generate` calls; LC/NLC without a declaration is refused there too) and reports its refusal; loop admission (counterterm sites, fermion signs of loop vertices, the loop-side tree currents) is checked only by the generation (`loop_admission_checked: false` per process).
- **Delivered path = measured path.** `virtual_runtime.rs:210-216` hard-wires `"interpreted"` [V]. As built (WP2.6): the v2 runtime interprets the plan's exact kernel term lists natively (§12.1, not SymJIT applications); SymJIT serves only the per-point tree stages, compiled in memory at load, until WP3.1.

### 12.3 Commands and the live report

- `inspect` shows family, skeleton and trie counts, certified-zero families, buckets and degrees. As built (WP2.6, `pyamplicol inspect`, v2 loop artifacts; a legacy one is refused): engine and runtime capability, Born orders, sectors with their pairs, families and (N, R) buckets, colour accuracy and degrees, schedule, poles mode, stability checks, carriers, declared colour histories, spin classes and resolved capability, point inputs and the plan digest. Skeleton and trie counts and certified-zero families are not stored in the artifact yet (WP2.7, with the live report).
- `profile` and `benchmark` report numerator dressing, NinjaRS, the re-test and masters separately, as the live benchmark report requires (`MASTER_PLAN.md:1405-1451` [V]).
- The report quotes providers in both production and protocol modes, labelled.
- The report is updated at every gate.

### 12.4 Pole selection: `poles = "all" | "none" | "uv" | "ir"` (user request 2026-10-23)

The user asked that the UV/IR pole selection of NinjaRS 41a4c00 and oneloopmaster 5b710437 reach the user settings of the one-loop matrix elements, so that a user can compute or access only the IR or only the UV poles, not just no poles. The mode is a runtime option of an evaluation; one generated artifact serves all four modes, as it does finite-only (REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM B, I). Code: `loops/v2/poles.py`, `runtime/v2_poles.rs` (an interim local copy of the UV-residue functional proposed upstream as NinjaRS `fast::tensor_uv`, see Cost), `runtime/v2_reduce.rs` (`Poles::Split`).

**Regulators.** Every pole of the Born-virtual interference is split by origin with two regulators: P/ε = P_UV/ε_UV + P_IR/ε_IR, and D/ε² = D/ε_IR². At one loop the double pole is always infrared.

| Mode | Poles returned | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `"all"` (default) | P_UV + P_IR and D (ε_UV = ε_IR) | The existing result, unchanged bit for bit (re-pin checked bitwise, §12.4 validation) |
| `"none"` | none (absent, not zero) | Finite-only mode (`poles="none"`; a bool is refused) with all its savings: one Laurent column per lane, no pole CT slots, no Catani check |
| `"uv"` | P_UV of the BARE loop (the unrenormalised amplitude, the reduction's UV poles); the double pole is 0 | User decision 2026-10-23 ~19:20 (REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM I). The counterterms' UV poles are the selected `uv` and `r2` components and `pole_origins`; bare + counterterm UV = 0 where renormalisation is complete |
| `"ir"` | P_IR and D of the RENORMALISED virtual | Catani's operator; the poles of `"all"` where renormalisation is complete |

**Invariant.** The finite part is identical, bit for bit, in all four modes and every carrier. The selection never touches a finite term: the reduction runs exactly as in `"all"` (its combined columns are those of `"all"` bit for bit), and the R2, counterterm and field finite parts are those of `"all"`.

**Origin of each component** (§6.4; per squared-order sector and pole coefficient):

| Component | UV part | IR part |
|---|---|---|
| bare | The UV residue of every reduction lane, summed like the lanes | combined − UV; the double pole |
| r2 | its poles (zero for loop_sm: R2 is finite) | 0 |
| uv_local (model UV vertex and internal two-point CTs: couplings, masses) | its poles | 0 |
| uv_external_fields | Z_UV = −(bare_UV + uv_local + r2 poles) | model factor − Z_UV |
| r2, uv_local, uv_external_fields of a model with pole regulators (M-SM MS-POLES) | the counterterm program at ε_IR-regulator 0: CT(epsUV, 0) | CT(epsUV, epsIR) − CT(epsUV, 0) |
| uv, virtual (`pole_origins`) | uv_local + fields; bare + uv + r2 = 0 (UV finite, to rounding; with pole regulators a sum of independent parts) | = the combined poles of `"all"` (with pole regulators: bare_IR + CT_IR, the same where renormalisation is complete) |
| virtual selected in `"uv"` | the bare loop's UV poles, with the renormalised finite part (`poles.select_sector`) | — |

- **Certified-scaleless families.** A scaleless family (§3.3: the massless bubbles {k², (k−p_i)²} with a quartic partner in QCD) integrates to zero, B0(0;0,0) = 1/ε_UV − 1/ε_IR, so the combined result never needs it. Its UV and IR parts are local but not separately zero, and without them the bare UV pole is not local: at g g > g g the generated families alone give 35.47 (α_s/4π)·Born, a point-dependent number. With them the bare UV pole is 10 (α_s/4π)·Born, the Feynman-gauge value −[(n/2)δZ_3 + (n−2)δZ_g]·2 for n = 4 and six loop flavours. Generation therefore keeps them as a companion slice, `LoopSlice.scaleless`, with its own schedules and seed table and shared blocks, Born, counterterms, kernels and colour degrees (LC/NLC pruned like the main slice). The `uv` and `ir` selections dress and reduce it: on the Python path in the same reducer call, natively with a second plan v4 (`NativeExecutor.scaleless`, schedule B). Its lanes' combined Laurent values are set to exact zero and only their UV residues enter, so `"all"` and `"none"` never dress it and the combined bare sum is unchanged. The plan v4 artifact (WP2.6) must carry this second plan, so that one artifact serves every mode.
- **Bare loop.** The UV residue of a lane, the 1/ε_UV coefficient of μ^{2ε}/(iπ^{d/2} r_Γ) ∫ N(q)/Π_i((q+V_i)² − m_i²), depends only on the integrand's large-q behaviour. `runtime/v2_poles.rs` expands 1/D_i = Σ_k (−1)^k (2q·V_i + c_i)^k/(q²)^{k+1} (c_i = V_i² − m_i²) to total degree −4 and integrates the angles exactly: ⟨q^β⟩ = Π_μ (β_μ − 1)!! g_μμ^{β_μ/2}/(2^k (k+1)!) for |β| = 2k. A degree-r block contributes only if r ≥ 2N − 4. It costs O(C(R+4,4)) per lane from the coefficients already in the lane, in any carrier, and in the re-test frame too. It equals the sum of the reduction coefficients times oneloopmaster's UV master residues: A0 and the tadpole tensors (UV only), B0 and the longitudinal bubble moments ((−1)^r/(r+1)), scaleless bubbles included (B0(0;0,0) = 1/ε_UV − 1/ε_IR); C0, D0 and dB0 have none. `tests/runtime_v2_reduce.rs` checks this lane by lane against NinjaRS's generic engine with `PoleSelection::UV` and `::IR`, at binary64 (10⁻¹⁰) and DoubleFloat (10⁻²⁶), massive and massless families, including a scaleless bubble whose combined integral vanishes. This is master-integral algebra, which belongs upstream (HANDOFF §1.7): `v2_poles.rs` is an interim local copy of `ninjars::fast::tensor_uv`, proposed to NinjaRS with the same signature (Cost, last item), and is replaced by it at the re-pin after that change lands on NinjaRS main.
- **Local counterterms** (`r2`, `uv_local`): UV by origin. They renormalise couplings and masses: loop_sm has δg_s (MS-bar, top decoupled) and the on-shell δm and δy. These are IR finite; δm^OS has no IR pole, only its derivative does.
- **External fields.** The model's external-field factors (UFO `particle.counterterm`, δZ/2 per leg) are on-shell field renormalisation constants written with one ε. A massless leg's δZ = Z_UV(1/ε_UV − 1/ε_IR) vanishes, and loop_sm omits it. A massive leg's δZ_Q = −(α_s C_F/4π)(1/ε_UV + 2/ε_IR + …) appears as 3/ε. A UFO model does not state the origin. The renormalised virtual is UV finite (bare_UV + uv_local + Σ_legs Z_i^UV·Born = 0), so the UV part of the field factors of a sector is fixed as above and the IR part is the rest. This does not hide a missing counterterm: an incomplete UV renormalisation makes the `"ir"` poles miss Catani's operator, exactly as `"all"` does. It is also not a mere definition: for loop_sm in Feynman gauge (§6.8) the field UV part equals the textbook constants of every external leg, Z_g^UV = (α_s/4π)(5/3 C_A − 4/3 T_F n_f) with the six loop flavours and Z_q^UV = −(α_s/4π) C_F for massless and massive quarks. `test_loop_v2_poles.py` checks this per sector against the Born, independently of the residue, on g g > g g, u u~ > g g g, u u~ > u u~ (four quarks), Drell-Yan (no δg_s) and the massive g g > t t~ and u u~ > t t~. The bare loop's UV/IR split of massless legs depends on the gauge (Z_i^UV does); its sum does not. Consequently the cancellation bare_UV + uv_local + fields_UV + r2 = 0 that the result shows (the user's renormalisation check) holds by construction of fields_UV. Its falsifiable content is three independent checks: the bare-loop UV residue against NinjaRS's generic engine with UV-selected masters, bare_UV + uv_local + Σ_legs Z_i^UV(textbook)·Born = 0 (the test above), and the `"ir"` poles against Catani's operator. A model-generic, independent field-UV part would need each external leg's self-energy UV residue, which the recursion does not generate (external-leg corrections are replaced by the field factors, §3.3); it is not attempted.
- **Models with pole regulators (M-SM MS-POLES).** MadLoop's electroweak UFOs (loop_qcd_qed_sm and its G_μ variant) state the origin: their CT parameters are linear in two external parameters, `epsUV` and `epsIR` (UFO/SLHA TECHNICAL 1 and 2, default 1), e.g. `(epsUV − epsIR)` for a massless δZ and `(epsUV + 2 epsIR)` for a massive one; no finite part reads them. `poles.pole_regulators` finds them by code (not by name) when both are adjustable external parameters of the request that only pole coefficients read (`poles.model_pole_regulators`: a model that uses those codes for a parameter a tree coupling or a finite part reads has none); the native record stores them (`pole_regulators`), and every lane that sets the IR regulator refuses one its tree program does not bind (`plan_export._regulated`, `V2Runtime` at load). The complex-mass transform keeps them external (`model_cms` step 5 exempts them from the restriction card's ones: linear, no dead branch), so the split holds under both mass schemes. In `"uv"` and `"ir"` the counterterm program is then also evaluated with the IR regulator at 0 at the point's parameters (`poles.regulated_parameters`; MadLoop's `epsIR = 0` run): its r2, uv_local and field components are their UV parts, and the IR parts are the rest, `CT(epsUV, epsIR) − CT(epsUV, 0)`, for every component and sector (`split_sector(..., counterterms_uv)`), with no remainder. An evaluation at `(0, epsIR)` instead would count twice a pole term that reads neither regulator (the UFOs' `dMB_*` massive-b corrections of their EW CT parameters, UV in MadLoop's `epsIR = 0` reading). Every lane forms the second evaluation: native point inputs (`NativeExecutor.reduce`, a second native tree pass), Python inputs (`_PointEvaluation.counterterm_twin`, the point's trees in Python at the regulated parameters), the observable columns (`observables._Assembly._local_uv`; stored columns: one more pass of the tree program for the column interferences, `V2Executor.tree_columns`, no loop dressing or reduction, `StoredColumns._evaluations`) and the SDK's plan v4 runtime (`v2_runtime.rs`, bitwise the Python API). bare + counterterm UV = 0 is then a test of the model's counterterms and the bare residues, and the `"ir"` virtual is bare_IR + CT_IR. `"all"` and `"none"` never form it (their values are unchanged bit for bit). Oracle: the counterterms' UV part equals MadLoop's UV-CT component at `epsIR = 0` and their IR part MadLoop's epsIR = 1 minus epsIR = 0 virtual, on loop_qcd_qed_sm_Gmu's `restrict_with_technical` card at its own masses (u u~ > e- e+ and g g > t t~ with QCD and QED corrections, MS-REF's `*_ew_tech` and `*_ew_tech_ir0` runs), and bare + CT UV vanishes to 10⁻¹⁶ of the bare UV pole, asserted at 10⁻¹² (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_ew_pole_split.py`, nightly). Open: the difference reading and an evaluation at `(0, epsIR)` differ only by the `dMB_*` terms, which vanish at MB = 0, where the oracle runs; the choice rests on MadLoop's `epsIR = 0` definition, not on a massive-b comparison. Without regulators (a model without them, one of them in `fixed_parameters`) the split is the remainder definition below; an electroweak request records which one applies (`scheme_conventions`' `pole_split`: "regulators" with `pole_regulators`, or "remainder"), and an electroweak artifact written before MS-POLES (no `pole_split` entry, no native `pole_regulators`) carries the remainder: regenerate it.
- **Selected values (user decision 2026-10-23).** In `"uv"` the virtual's poles are the bare loop's UV poles, the unrenormalised amplitude's (P_UV of `bare`, double pole 0), with the renormalised virtual's finite part. The other components carry their UV parts: `uv` (UV_local plus the field UV part) and `r2` are the counterterms' UV poles, so virtual + uv + r2 poles is the renormalised virtual's UV part, zero where renormalisation is complete. `ir_poles` is `None`. In `"ir"` the virtual's poles are the renormalised virtual's IR poles, those of `"all"` (Catani's operator), and the components carry the IR parts: bare_IR, the field IR parts, and UV_local and R2 IR parts that are zero for a model without pole regulators (with them, their `CT(epsUV, epsIR) − CT(epsUV, 0)`, e.g. the `(epsUV − epsIR)` vertex terms). Both rules are `poles.select_sector`, shared by the inclusive assembly (`assemble._PointEvaluation._assemble`) and the observable columns (`observables._Assembly.result`).
- **Richer access.** In `"uv"` and `"ir"` both parts cost the same, so `PointResult.pole_origins[origin][component][sector]` returns both origins' Laurent values of every renormalised component (with the combined finite part) besides the selected components: the strict origin split, so the two origins of each component add up to its poles in `"all"`, and `pole_origins["uv"]["virtual"]` is the renormalised virtual's UV part (not the `"uv"` selection's virtual, which is `pole_origins["uv"]["bare"]`'s poles). The selection pays for this split in both modes: the `"ir"` values alone equal the `"all"` poles, so `"ir"`'s extra cost buys `pole_parts`.

**Catani's operator** (`poles.catani_poles`; design §10.2 item 3, oracle O9) is the IR-pole prediction in `"all"` and `"ir"`. It now covers massive emitters (Catani, Dittmaier, Trócsányi 2001). Per ordered pair (j, k) of coloured legs it adds (1/C_j)[C_j (μ²/s_jk)^ε V^(S)_jk + Γ_j]⟨T_j·T_k⟩, with:

- V^(S) = 1/ε² for two massless legs; 1/(2ε²) + ln(m²/s_jk)/(2ε) for one massive leg of mass m; ln ρ_jk/(v_jk ε) for two massive legs, where v_jk = (1 − m_j²m_k²/(p_j·p_k)²)^{1/2} and ρ = ((1−v)/(1+v))^{1/2};
- Γ_j = γ_j/ε for a massless leg and C_j/ε for a massive one. γ_j follows the leg's spin and representation (`poles.strong_gamma`): 3/2 C(R) for a fermion, 2 C(R) for a scalar, and β₀ for the gluon (the massless adjoint gauge vector), with β₀ = 11/6 C_A − Σ_s w_s T(R_s) over the request's eligible species (`poles.strong_beta0`, `assemble._strong_beta0`): the massless coloured species perturbative in the strong order and not vetoed, one entry per particle-antiparticle pair, weighted 2/3 (Dirac fermion), 1/3 (Majorana), 1/6 (complex scalar), 1/12 (real scalar); for QCD this is 11/6 C_A − 2/3 T_R n_f with n_f the light flavours of the request (a vetoed b gives four). Another coloured vector, a representation without C(R) or T(R), or a species the weights do not cover gives no prediction (genericity audit m3).

The massless arithmetic is unchanged. The massive form reproduces the provider poles of g g > t t~ (MadLoop, Recola, OpenLoops) and the virtual's poles of u u~ > t t~ and g g > t t~.

**Dipole operators per relabelling orbit (WP3.1 C4, placement (a) of `RUST-MIGRATION.md` §10 item 6).** The colour correlators ⟨b|T_i·T_j|b′⟩ at N_c = 3 over the Born basis are built at generation in rusticol-core, stored, and expanded at load with index arithmetic only (no Symbolica, no licence, no basis tensors). For a permutation g of identical external adjoint labels that maps the basis onto itself, g·b = s_b b″, the entries obey C_{gi,gj}[gb, gb′] = C_ij[b, b′]/(conj(s_b) s_b′); the group is generated by the transpositions of identical labels verified exactly. Seven legs: g g > g g g g g 4,320 representatives (133 kB) for 21.8 M entries, u u~ > g g g g g 26,384 (538 kB) for 3.2 M (phase-0 measurement).
- *Rust API* (`rusticol_core::loop_v2::dipole_orbits`, for generation-scale-2's I5c in-crate): `DipoleOrbits::build(legs: &[Leg], basis: &[Tensor], classes: &[Vec<Label>])` (one-term basis tensors without √2 normalisation; `classes` are the candidate label sets, the external adjoint legs of one particle; colour conservation Σ_{j≠i} T_i·T_j = −C_i checked exactly); `to_json()` / `from_json(&str)` (`from_json` runs `validate()` before any table of the index space is allocated: format; coloured legs with strictly increasing labels and representations 3, 3̄ or 8; c²B² and B² by checked arithmetic; scales starting with one and holding no zero; generators permuting the legs and the basis; representatives in range and off the diagonal; and the necessary cover bound Σ_reps |O(i)||O(j)||O(b)||O(b′)| ≥ c(c−1)B² from the generators' leg and basis orbits, with its Born-metric analogue; a crafted member is a typed `InvalidArgument`, never an abort); `tuple_count()` / `entry_count()` (checked c²B², c(c−1)B²); every table of the index space is allocated fallibly (`try_reserve_exact`), so a host that cannot hold the expansion gets a typed refusal too; `expand(visit(i, j, b, b′, &Rational))` (every entry once, i ≠ j coloured-leg indices; fails on overlapping or incomplete orbits); `expand_dense(zero, cast)` (dense `[i][j][b][b′]` in any carrier: binary64, DoubleFloat, MPFR) and `expand_f64()`; `pairs()` (the (label_i, label_j) order of `LoopSlice.dipoles`); `expand_gram` (the Born metric ⟨b|b′⟩ at N_c = 3); `check_conservation()`; `digest()` (SHA-256 in `LoopSlice.dipoles` order).
- *Stored form* (`FORMAT = "pyamplicol-dipole-orbits-1"`, JSON, portable: no path, user or host state): `{"format", "coloured": [[label, representation], …] (coloured legs in leg order), "basis": B, "generators": [{"legs": [k′ per coloured-leg index k], "basis": [[b″, scale index] per b]}, …], "scales": [[re, im] exact texts; index 0 is 1], "representatives": [[i, j, b, b′, "p/q"], …], "gram": [[b, b′, "p/q"], …]}`.
- *Python binding* (`loops/v2/dipoles.py`): `build(slice_)` (at generation), `stored(orbits) -> str`, `load(text)` (licence-free), `operators(orbits)` (= `LoopSlice.dipoles()` exactly), `matrices(orbits)` (binary64, keyed and ordered as `operators`), `slice_operators(slice_)` (the `"dipoles"` switch).
- *For I5c (generation-scale-2)*: the artifact writer stores `dipoles.stored(dipoles.build(slice_))` as a member at generation; the native runtime reads it with `DipoleOrbits::from_json` and expands once at load (`expand_dense` in the carrier); no load-time regeneration and no Symbolica. Not wired into the artifact by package C (the writer and `NativeExecutor` are generation-scale-2's).

**QED operator and joint requests** (WP3.2 EWI-6, `poles.qed_catani_poles`). The same form with T_j·T_k → Q_jQ_k (all-incoming charges), C_j → Q_j², γ_f = 3/2 Q_f², γ_s = 2 Q_s², massive legs soft only, and the prefactor α/2π with α of the model's photon couplings (`poles.qed_coupling`: α_Gμ on the G_μ UFO, the coupling of the loops, not α(0)). The correlator is Q_jQ_k times the colour-summed Born interferences the sector's QED correction dresses (`poles.dressed_born`: squared Born orders plus QED² equal to the sector, cross orders included). A request's prediction is the sum of one operator per correction (`_PointEvaluation._ir_poles`, `poles.sector_sum`: QCD with α_s/2π and ⟨T_j·T_k⟩ over the Born orders plus QCD², QED as above); a correction without an operator, or one operator without a prediction, gives `None`, never a partial sum (a joint QCD + QED request got 0 before). The strong and the electromagnetic corrections are the coupling orders that the gauge bosons' vertices carry, never their names (`ew_policy.correction_orders`, genericity audit m1): the strong order is the one every minimal gauge vertex of the massless adjoint vector carries (with a coloured particle-antiparticle pair or two more of itself), the abelian one the one every three-point vertex of the photon with a charged particle-antiparticle pair carries; a model whose orders are spelt otherwise takes the same operators, the same electroweak classification and the same α_s rule. The slice request has no order-name default either (second genericity audit m7): `SliceRequest.corrections` left unset (None) is the model's strong correction (`ew_policy.request_corrections`, resolved by `assemble.resolve_request` when the slice is generated or its model prepared), and a model without one is refused with a typed `GenerationError`; the public routes name the corrections (`[process] loop_coupling_orders`). **Sectors the operators predict** (`poles.predicted_sectors`, `assemble._predicted_sectors`). The poles of a complete one-loop amplitude of order L are Σ_c I_c A0_{L−2c} over every coupling order c with L_c ≥ 2, but a request computes the loops of its corrections only (MadLoop's loop selection: a QCD correction has no gluon-photon box). A sector is therefore predicted only if, for each of its loop orders L and each such c, either c is requested and the tree of order L − 2c is a Born amplitude of the slice or provably absent, or c is not requested and that tree provably does not exist; and if every Born interference a requested correction dresses in the sector pairs with a requested loop order (the operator sums over all of them: the cross-order correlators of a joint request). A tree of an order outside the Born program is absent when the order lies within the request's Born bounds, or when an external leg has no admitted vertex whose orders fit within it; otherwise one tree DAG of the process within that order decides (`artifact.born_amplitude_orders`, the request's veto, once per slice at the first prediction: e⁺e⁻ → tt̄h has no tree of order g_s²e). Sectors without a prediction are absent from `ir_poles` (`None` when no sector is predicted). Nor is a sector predicted where a requested correction's loops leave out soft exchanges its operator sums over the Born amplitudes it dresses (mq-ewjoin, MQ-VALIDATE finding F1; `poles.separated_exchange`): such an exchange between charged legs runs through the lines that join them in a Born tree, and a line of a species no requested correction circulates (the W or Z between the quark lines of u d > u d h at QCD = 0) is not in the computed loops. Grouping the charged legs of each tree by the other lines gives its partition (`assemble._born_partitions`, a recursion over the Born DAG's currents, skipped when no such line separates charged legs). With one partition for every tree a non-abelian operator matches the computed loops (the cross-component ⟨B|T_j·T_k|D⟩ vanish: u b > d t, u c > u c h), with several it does not (u d > u d h: Z and W exchange, single pole 0.1–0.3 off while the virtual equals MadLoop; u g > d t b~ and u b > d t g: the gluon on either line); an abelian operator needs one component per tree. Every unpredicted sector has a typed reason (`poles.sector_predictions`: `uncorrected-exchange`, `uncomputed-born-pair`, `separated-exchange`; `LoopSlice.unpredicted_sectors`). This fixes the misleading prediction of mixed QCD × EW sectors of QCD-only requests routed from ew-lines (slice-status DECISIONS 2026-10-25 ~13:30): on u u~ > d d~ with gluon and photon Borns and QCD corrections the operators' sum in QCD⁴QED² was twice the virtual's single pole (the gluon Born's QED poles that the left-out gluon-photon boxes carry); QCD⁶ stays predicted and equal to the virtual (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_catani_scope.py`). precision-ap's b b~ > t t~ at QED² with QCD corrections (0.45 % from the b-t boxes with a photon or a W in the loop) is no longer predicted either. **External photons** (WP3.2 EWI-6b; the user's decision [U9] of 2026-10-25) couple with α(0) in electroweak requests: see `ew_policy.external_photon_scheme` below; under that convention they add no γ_γ (`qed_catani_poles`, convention `"alpha0"`), and the conversion term's pole is IR. Before EWI-3 opens electroweak generation the operator is checked on stand-in points (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_qed_catani.py`): the providers' poles of g g > t t~, u u~ > e⁻e⁺g, g g > t t~ e⁻e⁺ and g g > t t~ e⁻e⁺g at O(α) (MadLoop ≤ 10⁻¹², every validated provider within the fixtures' 2·10⁻⁸) and of the MadLoop G_μ controls e⁻e⁺ → μ⁻μ⁺ and e⁻e⁺ → uū (≤ 10⁻¹²). The joint g g > g g on loop_qcd_qed_sm, which generates, has its QCD⁶ poles equal to the prediction. The ε_UV/ε_IR split of the counterterm poles on the G_μ UFO waits for EWI-3.

**Electroweak guards** (WP3.2 EWI-0b, `loops/v2/ew_policy.py`, called from `LoopSlice.generate`). An electroweak request (a correction other than the model's strong one, identified by the gluon's vertices; or a perturbative colour-singlet species) is refused with a typed `GenerationError` if its Born or loop-side trees propagate a particle with a non-zero width, coloured or not (`ElectroweakWidthError`, listed key `electroweak-width`; the user's decision [U8]: fix the widths to zero, or use the complex-mass scheme, §6.8). On-shell external photons (WP3.2 EWI-6b, `ew_policy.external_photon_scheme`) couple with α(0), the convention MG5_aMC applies outside the UFO (1804.10017 §2; `export_fks.write_rescale_a0gmu_file`, the G_μ branch) and the user's decision [U9]: every output of the point is multiplied by (α(0)/α)^n for n such photons (`_PointEvaluation.average`, which divides the slice's spin, colour and symmetry average by it: Born, loop lanes, counterterm and field components, Catani's correlators, stability budgets and the observable columns all take it), and each photon leg carries the field-like factor −(δZ_e^{Gμ} − δZ_e^{α(0)}) of the electromagnetic correction, the model's `eCoup_DGmua0_UV_EW` (`assemble._alpha0_field_rules`; the field component adds −2 n DGmua0 Born, MG5_aMC's term). α is the photon couplings' (`poles.qed_coupling`, α_Gμ), α(0) the adjustable 1/α(0) at SMINPUTS 1 (aEW0M1 of MG5_aMC's G_μ loop models under `restrict_a0`; the default card fixes it to zero). The conversion parameter has no structural marker in a UFO: MG5_aMC applies it by that name, so v2 does too. **Per leg (MS-PHOTON; m-sm/PLAN.md Q1 (a), MG5_aMC's `!a!`).** Only the *tagged* photons take the convention: `SliceRequest.external_photons` (`[loop] external_photons`) is `"final"` (default: final-state photons, isolated photons), `"all"`, `"none"` or a list of 1-based legs (initial legs allowed; a listed leg that is no on-shell photon is refused, listed key `external-photon-legs`), resolved by `ew_policy.photon_tags`; `ExternalPhotons.legs` holds the tagged legs only, so (α(0)/α)^n, the conversion field insertion (filtered to the tagged legs, not the species) and the native binder's `photons` leg list run over them, and a request without a tagged photon needs neither 1/α(0) nor the conversion counterterm (the refusal, listed key `external-photon-scheme`, applies to tagged photons only and names the remedy for the model: untagged photons, or `restriction = "a0"`). An untagged photon keeps the loops' coupling: the QED operator adds −γ_γ/ε per untagged photon (`poles.UNTAGGED_PHOTON_CONVENTION`, γ_γ over the massless, non-vetoed charged fermions at the point, `_PointEvaluation._photon_gamma`), so the joint prediction stays complete. Default rationale: an initial photon is a PDF photon (α(0) would shift it by ~3.3 % per photon and give the wrong IR pole); MadLoop standalone converts none, MG5_aMC converts exactly the tagged ones, and only with the electromagnetic correction (QCD-only requests convert nothing). `scheme_conventions` records `tagged_photon_legs`, `untagged_photon_legs` and the option; a request at the default records no option (request records keep their bytes). Acceptance: PI01–PI04, PI01's `"all"` twin, VJ03, VV05, VV06, LL08 = raw MadLoop × MG5_aMC's formula over the tagged legs with Python and native inputs, rule 4 per leg (`test_loop_v2_external_photons.py::test_photon_rows_equal_madloop_with_the_formula_over_tagged_legs`, nightly). A model or request that cannot take the convention is refused (`ExternalPhotonError`, naming the reason): no electromagnetic correction requested, no conversion parameter, no adjustable 1/α(0) the loops' coupling leaves free, 1/α(0) fixed to zero, or a `particle_veto` of a species perturbative in the electromagnetic correction (the conversion is one parameter over the whole electroweak content). The native point inputs take the convention in the photons' sources (WP3.2 EWI-N; until then the listed refusal `external-photon-alpha0` of package fail-closed, G2-m4): the parameter program's output α(0)/α (`native_trees._parameter_program`'s `photons` record, on the inputs 1/α(0) and those of the photon coupling, checked real, finite and positive per point by the native binder, which names 1/α(0) otherwise) and every source of the photon legs (helicity, spin and loop-side sources, in the trees and the counterterm program) multiplied by its square root (`loop_v2::trees::TreeProgram::scale_photons`, after the spin and loop-side sources are written). Every amplitude is linear in each leg's source, so every output (Born, loops, counterterm kets, field insertions, observable columns) takes (α(0)/α)ⁿ and the plan's average stays the slice's: `_NativePoint`, V2Runtime and the SDKs divide by it; the conversion is a field insertion, exported as every field coupling. 1/α(0) is a runtime parameter of the stored process. Native = Python inputs within 1.1·10⁻¹⁵ (u u~ > a g) and 3.4·10⁻¹⁵ (u u~ > a a) of the virtual, and the native virtual = MadLoop's converted one within 6.4·10⁻¹³ and 4.4·10⁻¹³ per coefficient (`test_loop_v2_external_photons.py::test_alpha0_native_inputs_equal_madloop_and_the_python_inputs`). MadLoop references on loop_qcd_qed_sm_Gμ (`tests/fixtures/loop/required/uux_ag_ew.json`, `uux_aa_ew.json`; pure G_μ standalone, converted with MG5_aMC's formula and MadLoop's own DGmua0) agree to 6.3·10⁻¹³ (u u~ > a g) and 2.7·10⁻¹³ (u u~ > a a) per coefficient, with the IR poles equal to Catani's without γ_γ and cancelling UV poles; the photon's Ward identity holds on u u~ > a g (O10's asymmetric mode, ε_3 → c p_3 on the loop side: every renormalised component ≤ 2.1·10⁻¹² of the physical scale in binary64, the α(0) field term 6·10⁻¹⁷; a literal vector gives every part ≥ 10⁻² of it) (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_external_photons.py`, nightly). The width check runs on the Born and block DAGs before their kernels and the counterterm lowering. QCD-only requests are not guarded and their plans are unchanged.

**Listed generation refusals** (package fail-closed, 2026-10-27; second genericity audit, `genericity-audit-2/REPORT.md`). An unsupported structure is refused with a typed error naming a listed rule, decided by a structural criterion (never process strings, names, PDG codes or leg and line counts), and the dry run lists both tables. Loop admission (`loops/v2/refusals.py`, `LOOP_ADMISSION_REFUSALS`, `LoopRequestUnsupported`, a `GenerationError`): `colour-accuracy-nc-exact` (§3.3: a guard only since package identical-flavour-lc; LC and NLC compile with the N_c grade split, so their blocks have one grade, identical-flavour lines included, and the rule refuses only blocks that still mix grades, i.e. a loop-request DAG compiled without the split), `veto-unlabelled-counterterms` (WP3.2 EWI-5 interim, item (3) of EWI-5's "Open (found here, outside the lepton signs)": a `particle_veto` removing a loop species of a requested correction on a model whose counterterm terms of that correction are not all labelled with their loop content, refused in the model preparation `prepare_request_model`, before any tree, so the dry run reports it) and `fermion-line-representation` (G2-m1): the blocks sign coloured fermion lines by their colour flow and colour-singlet ones by their Wick order (`generation.fermion_ordering`), so a vertex joining fermions of different sign ownership (a leptoquark u e S vertex: one line changing colour representation) has no sign; `LoopSlice.generate` refuses, before any tree, a request that can use such a vertex: an oriented kernel admitted as the DAG admits it (veto, order limits of the Born and the blocks) whose inputs are both currents producible from the legs (a tree vertex) or one such current and a species perturbative in a requested correction while its result is such a species or a contact auxiliary (a loop vertex: the loop line in and out), the producible currents the fixed point of the admitted kernels (an over-approximation of the trees, so a vertex any tree or loop uses is found), naming the vertices; the native DAG builder refuses the same merge, and a block whose coloured fermion ends do not pair, as `DagError::Unsupported` with the listed key `FERMION_LINE_REPRESENTATION` (`loop_v2::dag::ADMISSION_REFUSALS`), which `dag_input` raises as the same typed error; and `loop-vertex-outside-tree-model` (fail-closed-2, a guard): loop lines read their vertices' colour and Lorentz structures from the request's tree model, so a vertex of the loop model whose couplings at an order vanish identically at the model's restriction is no loop vertex there: `ct_sites.restricted_to_zero` (the counterterm root closure's rule of WP3.2 EWI-5, now with the source helpers a coupling calls inlined with their exact bodies, `ScalarFunctionLibrary.inline`, before the internal parameters are resolved; decided from the coupling values, never names) gives `ct_sites.vanishing_loop_vertices`, which `families.ModelLoopCatalogue(vanishing=...)` leaves out (0.08 s per generation on the G_μ UFO; on its default restriction, Wolfenstein inputs zero: the six W vertices of the off-diagonal CKM elements, each a `complexconjugate` of a zero element that the prepared native model keeps, and their charged-Goldstone Yukawas); any other loop vertex the tree model does not hold at the order a loop position takes it is refused when the position is used (`colour.SeedColour.states`, naming the vertex, its particles and orders), where it raised an untyped `ValueError` (g t → t u u~ with QCD and QED corrections at the G_μ default restriction, slice-status DECISIONS 2026-10-28 ~03:50: its loops now pass the catalogue and the slice generates and evaluates, `LoopSlice.generate`: 150 families, 282 s, 8.7 GB, its poles the Catani operator's; the stored-process path, `artifact.generate_process` behind `pyamplicol generate` and `Generator`, then refuses it at native export with the listed `plane-mismatch` at its `UV_GTTLEW` counterterm, epsilon power 0, after about 355 s and 8.8 GB, the open EWI-N refusal of g g → t t~ [QCD QED], measured by the fail-closed-2 evidence audit). Evidence: `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_restricted_zero.py` (the vanishing set at the default restriction equal to the loop model's tree vertices of which the compiled tree model holds no term, the tree compiler's own zero test, and of the native model's vertices exactly the six off-diagonal W⁺ vertices and t̄ d G⁺, t̄ s G⁺; on the renamed `ckm` variant of `loop_renamed_models`, CKM elements, Wolfenstein inputs and vertices renamed: the same vertices by particles and orders, and no W vertex at `restrict_ckm`; the reproducer's generation stopped after its loop schedules, its catalogue without them; the refusal with the rule disabled; the reproducer's slice, nightly); the loop_sm byte pins below are unchanged (no loop_sm vertex vanishes), and so is the plan of u u~ → t t~ with QCD and QED corrections on the G_μ UFO; the plans of the G_μ controls with closed light-quark loops (g u → g u, g g → t t~, u u~ → e⁻ e⁺ g, e⁻ e⁺ → u u~ with their QED corrections) change on purpose: the closed s and b loops, whose catalogue entries differed only by the vanishing W vertices, now form one closed-loop class of multiplicity 2 (§4.3; g u → g u: 108 → 100 reading skeletons, 82 → 74 plan contents), and every component (Born, bare, R2, UV and its parts, virtual) is bitwise identical in binary64 at a point for g u → g u, g g → t t~ and e⁻ e⁺ → u u~, while for u u~ → e⁻ e⁺ g the Born, R2 and UV components are and the bare part and the virtual move by at most 1.2·10⁻¹⁵ of their largest coefficient (the merged class's summation order). Native export (`native_trees.NATIVE_EXPORT_REFUSALS`, `NativeExportUnsupported`, a generation `GenerationError` through `artifact._not_storable`): besides the generation-scale keys, `plane-mismatch` (G2-M4: the SymJIT plane check's disagreement, `loop_v2::trees::compile_plane_application_checked` → `PlaneMismatchError(message, output, row, imaginary, real_axis)`, names the output: a kernel by its class and index (vertex or propagator kernel of the tree or counterterm program), a parameter-program output by its key, a derived coupling `derived_coupling_<term id>` by its UFO coupling, vertex and particles, a counterterm coefficient with its epsilon power and N_c grade (`native_trees._PlaneOutputs`); it refused g g → t t~ [QCD QED] on `loop_qcd_qed_sm_Gmu` (a counterterm coefficient `UV_GTTLEW` of the parameter program) and the MW-card-input loop_sm variant of the renamed-model harness (a W derived coupling) until WP3.2 EWI-N made the check independent of the parameter declaration order: complex rows off the real axis and the request's parameter values as the real point, with the native counterterm couplings on MadLoop's side of real cuts (DECISIONS 2026-10-26 ~21:10 item 6, confirmed by the user 2026-10-27 ~19:20; WP3.2 status, ew-native); `external-photon-alpha0` (G2-m4, above) was listed until EWI-N exported the α(0) convention natively. Evidence: `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_fail_closed.py` (both rules through `LoopSlice.generate`, the public API and the CLI, each export layer, the toy leptoquark UFO `loop_v2_ufo_variants.leptoquark_ufo` in both DAG lanes and the native key), `test_loop_v2_ew_policy.py::test_stored_external_photon_requests_reach_their_trees`, `test_loop_genericity_renamed_models.py::test_plane_mismatch_names_the_parameter_program_output` (at the former real probes as the real point), `test_loop_v2_ew_native.py` (the check's rows, EWI-N); `test_loop_v2_fail_closed.py::test_plans_the_guards_do_not_refuse_keep_their_bytes` pins the plan, companion and native-member digests of six of the controls below (measured on the slice `6da37352`; equal at `59d47cb3` and `11542fd5` except the native trees of g g → u u~ u u~, whose counterterm program takes ew-epsilon's root-closure reading signs at `6da37352`: 192 four-block term signs); on the eleven loop_sm controls the guards admit (u u~ → d d~ g at LC, NLC and full colour, u u~ → d d~ s s~ at LC and NLC, u u~ → u u~ at LC, g g → g g g at LC, and full colour of u u~ → u u~ g, g g → u u~ u u~, u u~ → e⁺ e⁻ g and d d~ → g g) the stored plan, the companion plan and every native PACBIN member are byte-identical to `11542fd5`'s (the six pinned ones also to `6da37352`'s), except the parameter program's SymJIT plane (`parameters.symjit`), which differs between two runs of `11542fd5` itself.

**Stock loop_sm on the public route** (package mq-model, m-qcd PLAN MQ-MODEL, 2026-10-28). (1) Source repair: `loops.covariant_model.repair_source_declarations` moves a vertex (tree or counterterm) whose particles are all scalars from a constant Lorentz tensor of other (scalar) slots to the model's unique tensor of its spin signature and the same exact constant (loop_sm's four-G⁰ `V_1001`: `SSS1` → `SSSS1`), recorded under `pyamplicol_source_repairs` in the derived source and in the structure's diagnostics; it runs in `prepare_loop_request_model` on the vertices the request reaches (so a source whose reached vertices have no defect, e.g. loop_sm under a QCD-only Born scope as in the frozen legacy captures, is prepared byte for byte as before; idempotent, a no-op on the checkout's scalar-arity view, `tools/loop_reference/model_compatibility.py`, which stays the independent oracle: the same derived collections) and on the compiled model the public route publishes (`with_source_repairs` in `generation.loop_service._prepare`); any other slot mismatch is the listed `lorentz-slot-arity` when a request reaches the vertex. (2) Shipped model and declaration: MG5_aMC 3.7.0's loop_sm and all its restriction cards ship verbatim (`assets/models/ufo/loop_sm`, PROVENANCE, MANIFEST); a bare shipped model name is a model source when no such path exists (`_internal.packaged_models`); `loops.shipped_profiles` holds loop_sm's `[loop.model]` declaration keyed by the sha256 of the source's declarations (schema version and record collections after the repair: one digest for the shipped copy, the MG5_aMC directory and every restriction), used when neither the card nor the source declares one. (3) A one-loop request without `color.accuracy` is full colour (user ruling Q1; `config.ColorConfig.accuracy = None` resolves in `RunConfig`). (4) Listed QCD refusals (user rulings 2026-10-28 ~15:25): `loop-induced` (no Born amplitude in the limits), `coloured-width` (naming `restriction = "no_widths"` and `WT = "0"`), `dirac-weyl-closure` (the tree DAG's closure guard; lifted for loop requests by MQ-5F, whose loop Born records the class as a marker root, and since then the tree lanes' `dag_ordering.DiracWeylClosureUnsupported`, no loop admission refusal), `lorentz-slot-arity`, `majorana-fermion` and `multi-fermion-contact` (model preparation); and product-wide process refusals (`processes.refusals`, `ProcessRequestUnsupported`, a `ValueError`, listed as `process_request_refusals` in the dry run): `decay-process` (one initial-state slot, `ProcessRequest.parse`) and `madloop-syntax` (NLO brackets, order constraints, `/`, `$`, decay chains, `@N`, naming the run-card setting). (5) `pyamplicol self-test` generates u u~ → e⁻ e⁺ / Z [virt=QCD] on the shipped loop_sm-no_b_mass through the public route and holds it to MadLoop (`_internal.selftest_virtual`). Evidence: `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_stock_loop_sm.py` (the public route on `model.source = "loop_sm"` with no `[loop.model]` and no colour setting bitwise equal in binary64 to the tools-view route for u u~ → e⁻ e⁺ and, nightly, the eleven further QED-Born requests of the MQ-MODEL list on the shipped copy and the MG5_aMC directory; Drell–Yan / Z and e⁺e⁻ → tt̄ against MadLoop: double-double within 2.8·10⁻¹³ and 1.3·10⁻¹⁴, binary64 within 10⁻¹² of double-double; every key in a dry run or at model preparation). Campaign rows on the public route (generation through `Generator` on `model.source = "loop_sm"`, evaluation through `Runtime.evaluate` with the O1 checks, judged by MQ-VALIDATE's §1.4 rules against MQ-REF's MadLoop references; driver and records in `workspace/reports/engine-v2/mq-model/public-route-campaign/`): D01, D05, D12, D15, D16, D18, D21, F01 and F03 on the 5F* recipe (default restriction, MB = ymb = WT = 0) pass rules 1–4 (and 6 where the row lists it) at every reference point, double-double within 1.4·10⁻¹³ of MadLoop; the binary64 out-of-scope listing of D16 is route specific (the public route's estimates put its single pole in scope, within E); D19's single pole differs from Catani's prediction by the same amount on both routes while equal to MadLoop's (MQ-VALIDATE); F03 on `no_b_mass` was `dirac-weyl-closure` until MQ-5F, which admits it (records in `workspace/reports/engine-v2/mq-5f/`). The multiparticle row M01 (`p p > t t~` on loop_sm-no_b_mass, `m01_public.py`): one public generation (37 s) holds the six concrete processes (d, u, s, c, b q q̄ and g g → t t̄; `p` is the model's massless partons), the set of MadLoop's SubProcesses P0_uux_ttx and P2_gg_ttx; each, loaded from that artifact by its expression, is bitwise equal to its own single-process generation (Born, the six components per sector and the totals, binary64 and double-double, at four reference points), and the gating variants M01@P0 (u u~ → t t~) and M01@P2 (g g → t t~) read from it pass rules 1–4 at every point (double-double within 1.2·10⁻¹⁴ of MadLoop). A loop artifact records the model's spelling (loop_sm's gluon is `G`), so `loops.v2.artifact._select` follows the public spelling rule of requests (`processes.model.resolve_model_particle_name`): the exact spelling first, then a unique letter-case-insensitive match (as the tree selector's lower-cased tokens, rusticol-core `normalize_process_expression`), and a folded match that is not unique names the stable ids. (6) Installed wheel. The committed built-in-sm prepared-model pair was stale against `models/builtin` (f355f343 after the f9874045 regeneration, on `loop` too), so `stage_packaged_prepared_models` refused every wheel build in both modes; it is regenerated with the release flow of `.github/workflows/release-prepared-models.yml` run on x86_64 Linux (`tools/release/prepare_release_prepared_models.py bootstrap-wheel`, the bootstrap runtime on PyPI dependencies, `tools/ci/eager_portability.py produce --asset-mode release`; the HEFT pair was current), and the one portable SymJIT storage-v4 O2 bundle fills both architecture slots of both stores through the backend's writer (`write_release_packaged_prepared_model_asset`; the portability contract audits no producer/consumer architecture equality). A mass alias that resolves to an exact zero (loop_sm's `Me`, `MC`, `MB` where a restriction or a fixed parameter zeroes them) is named ZERO in the covariant structure (`build_covariant_structure`): the published Community 3.0.2 HepKit, the wheel's pinned Symbolica, keeps the alias's source symbol where the integration kernel's (9cdc2949) exports ZERO, and the v2 families refused it as a derived mass of value zero (`_model_masses`); `native.to_json()` is now byte for byte the same under both (loop_sm under seven restriction and fixed-zero cases; unchanged under 9cdc2949; `test_structural_zero_mass_alias_is_named_zero_under_either_hepkit` emulates the published HepKit). Evidence: a release-mode host wheel (`pyamplicol.host-wheel=true`) of the branch head installed into fresh venvs, one with only its pinned PyPI dependencies and one with the Community 9cdc2949 Symbolica, passes `pyamplicol self-test` (python, model-assets, rusticol-python, native-sdk, physics-f64, physics-virtual: MadLoop to 2.8·10⁻¹³, generated in 9 s) under `env -i` (`workspace/reports/engine-v2/mq-model/wheel-selftest/`).

**The QCD+QED loop models on the public route** (package ms-model, m-sm PLAN MS-MODEL, wave 1 part, 2026-10-29). (1) Goldstone-flag source repair: the legacy UFO object library's `Particle.anti` binds `goldstoneboson` to its `line` argument, so MG5_aMC's loop_qcd_qed_sm and loop_qcd_qed_sm_Gmu declare G⁻ without the flag of its partner G⁺; `repair_source_declarations` (rule `antiparticle-goldstone-flag`, found by the library fingerprint `_lost_goldstone_flag`, never by a name; not restricted by `within`, particles belong to every request) restores it, records it under `pyamplicol_source_repairs` and is idempotent, so the public route needs no `compatibility = "gmu_particle_anti"`, which stays accepted (satisfied as it is on a repaired source). loop_sm has no such antiparticle: its declarations digest `e2512823…` is unchanged. (2) Shipped models (Q5 (a)): loop_qcd_qed_sm as downloaded (MG5_aMC model database, `loop_qcd_qed_sm.tgz` sha256 `7b7b9b2c…`) and loop_qcd_qed_sm_Gmu as the Python-3 conversion of its Python-2 download (`…_Gmu.tgz` sha256 `7655cb63…`; package-relative imports, `.iteritems()` → `.items()`, a Python-3 `write_param_card.py`; no declaration changed: the converted copy has the declarations digest of the workspace reference copy), every restriction card byte for byte, without caches, backups, AppleDouble or hidden files and the SMWidth tool (PROVENANCE `ufo-python3-conversion-v1`). (3) `loops.shipped_profiles` declares both by their declarations digest after the repair (`1b9f6550…`, `588cb07a…`): joint QCD and QED corrections, complete UV and R2, the HV / four-dimensional / on-shell electroweak scheme; the scheme differs from loop_sm's, so MQ-NC's scheme-gated N_c data never attaches (EW LC/NLC keep `colour-declaration-coverage`). A modified copy (massive-muon, b-mass variants) has other declarations and keeps a declared `[loop.model]`. (4) `ufo-python2` (`models.source_refusals`, a `ModelError`): a UFO directory whose import fails is Python 2 when one of its modules is not Python-3 source (compiled, not imported) or the failure names a Python-2-only attribute or builtin; the model loaders raise it, and the dry run, which compiles no model, finds the first case without importing. (5) Tree API: the NLO UFO scalar helpers `arg`, `crecms`, `grreglog`, `recms`, `regsqrt` are registered (the models call them in counterterm expressions only), so the tree models compile as supported; tree MEs from their prepared kernel packs equal MadLoop's Borns of e⁻e⁺ → e⁻e⁺, e⁻e⁺ → tt̄h, gg → tt̄, uū → e⁻e⁺g and gg → tt̄e⁻e⁺ to ≤ 2.3·10⁻¹⁵.
