Evidence: `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_ew_public_models.py` (nightly: the public route on the shipped models, no `[loop.model]`, no compatibility profile, no `tools/` import, is bitwise equal in binary64 to the tools view for e⁻e⁺ → μ⁻μ⁺, uū → e⁻e⁺, uū → dd̄, gg → tt̄, ud̄ → W⁺g, uū → ZZ and uū → γg (a0), and gg → tt̄ equals MadLoop's `gg_ttx_ew` rows).

**UFOmodeldb as the source of the QCD+QED loop models** (package ms-ufodb, m-sm PLAN MS-MODEL follow-up, user rule 2026-10-30: https://github.com/mg5amcnlo/UFOmodeldb is the ground truth). The shipped loop_qcd_qed_sm and loop_qcd_qed_sm_Gmu are UFOmodeldb's (commit 6ca4b120; `loop_qcd_qed_sm.tgz` sha256 `b6a60603…`, `loop_qcd_qed_sm_Gmu.tgz` sha256 `9f3668e7…`; the G_mu model with the same Python-3 conversion). Against the MG5_aMC online-database copies of item (2) above the one declaration that changed is the coupling R2_ZZHH (CT_couplings.py, `(2+2*cw**2 …)` → `(1+2*cw**2 …)`; UFOmodeldb fix 1f13c48f / 6ca4b120), which enters the R2 vertices Z Z H H and Z Z G0 G0 only. The shipped profiles list the new declarations digests first (`580c029a…`, `b580d14a…`) and keep the downloads' (`1b9f6550…`, `588cb07a…`): same scheme, one coupling value. A counterterm vertex is a tree vertex inserted once and the bare loops use tree couplings only, so a request none of whose trees holds those vertices has the same plan on both (artifacts of VV02, SC07, VH01 on either model: byte-identical but for the compiled model and the run-to-run `parameters.symjit`); of the M-SM rows only SC10 (z z > h h, new, MadLoop QP on both models) reaches them (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_ufodb_models.py`); Recola, whose R2 terms are its own, equals MadLoop on the UFOmodeldb model to 2.2e-11 of the finite part and differs from MadLoop on the download by 3.7 % to 2.7 times it, so the fix is right. Tests and the reference toolkit load the UFOmodeldb copies under `workspace/reference/models/ufodb-6ca4b120/`; the shipped EW self-test artifact is regenerated from the new model (same values bitwise).

**The UFOmodeldb issue-1 model patch in the shipped QCD+QED models** (package ms-ufo-patch, user rule 2026-10-30 ~12:45; gist ValentinHirschi/81a26a3c…, `ufomodeldb-issue-1.patch` sha256 `4b5b1ef0…`, applied with `patch --fuzz=0 -p1`; PROVENANCE `ufomodeldb-issue-1-patch-v1`, `-python3-v1`, `-python3-fold-v1`). (1) R2_G0tt_{d,s,b} split by loop content ([W+, G+, q] and [G+, q], coefficient sum kept): the engine inserts every R2 vertex once, so its values do not move (bitwise on TT04, SC09, LL10; one ulp of the R2 component, ≤ 3e-17 of the virtual, on TT05 and TT06: the vertex coefficient is the same sum formed from other terms), while MadLoop's exports now call R2_G0tt_b_WG and equal Recola (the rule-1 `madloop_omitted_r2` correction is gone). (2) Exactly unitary CKM: on the `ckm` restriction (SC03) values change by O(lambda^4); on every other restriction the CKM entries are exactly 1 and 0 as before, but the unpatched UFO's `CKM33 = '1'` of type complex resolved to a float (`1.00000000000000`) and the patched `CKMc23*CKMc13` resolves to the exact 1, so rows with V_tb in W couplings or counterterms move at the last bits of those constants (cancellations amplify it to ≤ 2.1e-11 of the virtual: VV07, V301, VV01, CM08; a CKM-only variant reproduces it, the R2 split alone is bitwise neutral there). (3) The G_mu charge counterterm's b-mass term: `eCoup_a0_UV_EW + eCoup_DGmua0_UV_EW = eCoup_Gmu_UV_EW` with MB ≠ 0 (ST04, SC04, TT06, SC03 move, = Recola), unchanged bitwise at MB = 0. The patch writes it as module-level `X.value[k] += '...'` statements, which ufo-model-loader 1.0.1 does not certify (it then certifies no value of the model: "source helper complexconjugate lacks a certified body"); the shipped G_mu model and the reference copies fold them into the constructors (`tools/developer/fold_ufo_value_appends.py`, every UFO value string identical by import). (4) `restrict_with_b_mass.dat` of G_mu gains `SMINPUTS 1 = 0` (aEW0M1's default; no value changes). The new term `( 0 if MB == 0 else … reglog(MU_R**2/MB**2) … )` exposed a lowering defect: with MB a fixed parameter the dead branch formed 0^-2 and no evaluator could be built ("infinity coefficients"; SC08 and ST03 hit it on the unpatched model already). `ScalarFunctionLibrary` now forms only the taken branch of a conditional its bindings decide (`decided_number`: exact numbers, the library's predicates and `symbolica::if`), as Python's lazy conditional does; values and exported plans are bitwise unchanged on the rows that evaluated before (tools route). The shipped EW self-test artifact, regenerated because its compiled model changes with the patch, has a 14 % smaller runtime (`loop-runtime.pacbin`, the dead branches of the `MB == 0` conditionals gone) and one of its twelve values moves by 6.4e-16 (a single pole; the same on the unpatched model, so the reshaped scalar evaluator's rounding, not the patch); it still equals MadLoop to 5.4e-15. Evidence: old-vs-new generations of 65 M-SM rows on the QCD+QED models (all but the composite MP rows, the CM01 and LL02 controls and the loop_sm rows SC05, CM10), bitwise equal except the rows named above; MadLoop QP and Recola on the patched model for LL10, TT04, SC09, TT05, TT06, ST04 and SC04's e^6 sector agree to ≤ 5.3e-11 and with pyAmpliCol (`workspace/reports/engine-v2/upstream/ufo-patch-validation.md`); SC03's IR single-pole remnant is exactly the documented tau-mass residual MTA^2/(2 sw^2 (s − MW^2)) (α/2π) Born (the EW counterterms omit the tau mass, a retained limitation). UFOmodeldb 60ee58bb (2026-10-30) took the b-flavour R2 split alone; the shipped models keep the full patch (coordinator ruling ~13:40). Shipped profiles: patched digests first (`0dc17624…`, `97f39eeb…`), then UFOmodeldb 60ee58bb's (`1555635e…`, `14ddd943…`), 6ca4b120's and MG5_aMC's; tests and the toolkit read `workspace/reference/models/ufodb-6ca4b120-patched/` (the unpatched copies remain as conftest controls).

**Listed keys of every refusal a one-loop request reaches** (package ms-model, m-sm PLAN MS-MODEL items 4, 5 and 7, after EWI-7, 2026-10-30). Three tables in `loops/v2/refusals.py` share `LoopAdmissionRefusal` (key, feature, rule, remedy, owner, follow-up) and the dry run lists each (`estimated_coverage.loop_admission_refusals`, `loop_configuration_refusals`, `loop_internal_refusals`): (1) admission keys, now also `external-photon-scheme` (`ew_policy.ExternalPhotonError`, a `LoopRequestUnsupported`; conditions and remedy per leg stay MS-PHOTON's), `feynman-gauge` (`ElectroweakGaugeError` and the model preparation's no-gauge-1 refusal), `antiparticle-metadata` (`covariant_model._validate_pairs`, the generalisation of the repaired Goldstone-flag mismatch) and `anomalous-veto` (`ct_recursion.AnomalousVetoError`, [U2]); (2) configuration keys (`LOOP_CONFIGURATION_REFUSALS`, decided from the run card and the model's declarations before any tree): `loop-model-declaration`, `loop-model-corrections`, `loop-model-completion` (`models.loop_declaration`), `undeclared-corrections`, `scale-parameter`, `veto-external-particle`, `tree-only-model`, and the `_feature_blockers` / `_v2_blockers` texts `fft-basis-trace`, `colour-selectors`, `process-aliases`, `process-group-analysis`, `direct-r2`, `relation-discovery`, `evaluator-backend`, `evaluator-tuning`, `covariant-multiplets` (each a `ListedBlocker`, a `str` with its key: the dry run shows the listed text, generation raises the first as `LoopRequestUnsupported`); the LC/NLC "explicit source colour data" text is `colour-declaration-coverage`; (3) internal keys (`LOOP_INTERNAL_REFUSALS`): engine conditions met during the generation of an admitted request, carried by the error classes as `refusal_key` (`fermion-sign`, `reading-sign`, `unlowered-counterterm`, `counterterm-coupling`, `loop-kernel-catalogue`, `counterterm-completeness`) and typed at the generation boundary (`refusals.keyed_error`: `LoopEngineRefusal`, a `GenerationError` with `key`); a native export refusal surfaces as `NativeExportRefused` with the export's feature key (`artifact._not_storable`), not as text in a plain `GenerationError`. The dry run also calls the checks of an electroweak request that need only the model and the Born DAG (`loop_service._plan_policy_checks`: `external_photon_scheme` and the width policy `ew_policy.require_zero_widths` on the Born currents; the width and complex-parameter policy and their keys are MS-CMS's). Multiparticle requests (Q2 (a), `loop_service._born_members`): a concrete process of a multiparticle request with no Born amplitude is skipped with a typed `loop-induced` record (the dry run's `skipped_members`, the artifact manifest extension `loop_skipped_members`); a single process, or a request with no member left, is refused (`loop-induced`). Plan export (`plan_export._trace_groups`): a trace step reads its sources at the largest source storage and contracts at the largest content degree, so a family whose rings peak at different positions (an N = 2 family of u ū → ZZ [virt=QED] on G_μ: rank 1, a trace step at 1 + 1) was refused at load ("a trace step above the family rank"); such a step is now one step per source storage, summed by the accumulator, and a step within the rank is unchanged (every plan the loader admitted before is exported byte for byte as before). Evidence: `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_refusal_keys.py` (every configuration key in the dry run and as the typed generation error; the model-level keys on loop_sm, the modified-model keys, the EW keys on the shipped QCD+QED models; the multiparticle dry run and generation, a stored member bitwise equal to its own generation; internal keys and `NativeExportRefused`; the trace-step split); user docs `docs/user/electroweak-and-madloop.md`.

**Engine defects found by the M-SM campaign** (package ms-validate, m-sm PLAN MS-VALIDATE, 2026-10-30; `docs/performance_reports/loop/data/msm-findings.json` F1, F2, F9). (F1) A schedule-B step's rank is its largest source storage plus its largest content degree, which can exceed every single ring's summed degree (`trie.rank`) where a trie node joins rings of degree-0 and degree-1 vertices (Higgs, Goldstone, W and Z rings: h h > h h, u u~ > z h, w+ w- > w+ w- [virt=QED]); `plan_export._Exporter.family` writes the family rank as the largest of both, so the loader admits the steps (a family whose steps stay within the trie rank is written as before; a raised family is reduced at the higher rank, where ms-model's `_trace_groups` split by source storage keeps the rank when it applies). (F2) A UFO conditional whose test reads only exact numbers (a parameter fixed at generation against a literal) is decided at lowering and only its taken branch lowered, as Python evaluates it: '0 if MB == 0 else .../MB' at MB = 0 no longer builds 1/0 in the untaken branch, which the evaluators refused as an infinite coefficient (`c_UVtxbWp1EW`: t t~ > w+ w-, g b > t w-, b b~ members of p p > ...); ms-ufo-patch landed the same fix independently (`ScalarFunctionLibrary.decided_number`, above), which the merge keeps (this package's `decided` and its test are dropped). The decided conditionals change the parameter programs of the QCD+QED UFOs, so their binary64 EW values move in the last bits (on the unpatched model the EW self-test's single pole -0.004058402964848439 -> -0.004058402964848442; Born and finite part unchanged): the shipped EW self-test artifact is regenerated with it and stays the public route's output. (F9) The exact runtime's massive Dirac source takes |p| = |pz| on the z axis (`symbolica_exact._massive_dirac`): the residue of sqrt(pz^2) at 50 digits put an on-axis leg along -z on the branch with pp3 = |p| + pz > 0 and zero transverse momentum, a vanishing spinor (the binary64 carrier's Python inputs of b b~ > w+ w- with MB = 4.5 gave a Born of 1e-49). Evidence: the campaign rows (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_msm_nightly_*.py`), `test_loop_scalar_frontend.py::test_conditional_decided_by_its_bindings_forms_only_the_taken_branch`, `test_source_wavefunction_metadata.py::test_massive_dirac_sources_on_the_beam_axis_do_not_hang_on_rounding`.

**Counterterm slots** (S_slot of the CT recursion, §6.1; WP2.2, integrated at `a18b7599`). `"all"`, `"uv"` and `"ir"` evaluate every slot. `"none"` switches the pole slots off and, in the native CT program, skips the currents that feed only them (WP3.1 item 1). `"uv"` needs the UV_local pole slots for the counterterms' UV poles, and `"ir"` cannot drop them either, because they fix the IR part of the field factors. So the switch is `has_poles(selection)` for every pole slot (`poles.has_poles`): `_PointEvaluation` passes it to the CT program, which sets the slot switches with `CountertermRecursion.parameter_values(poles=has_poles(selection))` (a bool derived from the selection, not a selection; `ct_closure.py`, `assemble.py`); the site closure of the slice binds the counterterm couplings at every ε power except in `"none"` the same way.

**Masters and caches.** In every mode the fast engine's per-batch cache holds the combined masters of its never-switched provider. NinjaRS refuses a UV- or IR-selecting provider in the fast engine, and `MasterCache` and `ProfiledMasters` forward that capability (`requires_scaleless_bubbles`). The adapter itself refuses a master selection with the fast engine. The generic engine's selection (`ReduceOptions::masters`, binding `master_poles`) applies to its uncached `SymbolicaMasters` and is the oracle and cost reference below.

**Carriers and estimates.** The residue is computed in the lane's carrier (binary64, DoubleFloat, MPFR), and in the reduction check's frame (rescaled frames scale it back like the integral). In MPFR it first lifts its inputs to the engine's working precision (`BucketEngine::precision`). An evaluator's exact coefficients and masses can carry a low binary precision under Symbolica's loose tracking, and an MPFR product rounds to the smaller precision of its factors. Without the lift, the native MPFR residues at a rotated g g > g g point kept 2–3 significant bits: bare UV 53.27 at 32 digits and 49.88 at 50 digits, against 43.22. The split-mode rotation estimate in MPFR found it (`test_split_estimates_in_the_extended_carriers`), and a unit test with 2-bit exact inputs covers it (`v2_poles.rs`). Binary64 and DoubleFloat inputs are borrowed unchanged. Stability estimates are formed on the selected coefficients: the reduction check compares the selected lane values of both frames, and rotations re-evaluate the mode.

**In `"uv"` the reduction check is blind to numerator errors; bracket `"uv"` with a rotation.** The selected single pole is the bare loop's UV residue, an exact functional of each lane's numerator coefficients, shifts and masses that does not depend on the reduction frame. The reduction-direction check (reversed or rescaled) re-reduces the same coefficients, so for this coefficient it sees only the residue's own rounding, not an error of the coefficients themselves (tree rows, dressing, kernel constants). Measured at u u~ > d d~ g (first point of `tests/fixtures/loop/required/uux_ddxg.json`, binary64, one rotation and the reduction check, true error against DoubleFloat per sector and coefficient): the reduction check alone underestimates the `"uv"` single pole's error 7.7× (rescaled 3.9×; 1.4× at the second point), while it brackets `"all"` and `"ir"`, whose poles contain the reduced masters (error/E ≤ 0.15). A rotation re-evaluates the numerators and brackets every mode there (error/E ≤ 0.17 in `"uv"`, ≤ 0.07 in `"ir"`, alone or with the reduction check). So a `"uv"` estimate needs at least one rotation check; with the reduction check alone it is a lower bound of the residue's rounding only. `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_poles.py::test_a_rotation_brackets_the_split_poles_in_binary64` checks that the rotation's estimate alone and the combined one bound the true error of every coefficient and sector at that point, in `"uv"` and `"ir"`, with no margin; the reduction subset alone fails it in `"uv"`. Flooring the `"uv"` single-pole estimate by the lanes' single-pole reduction budget, so that the reduction check alone brackets it, is open (WP2.10).

**Cost** (`tools/loop_v2_poles_cost.py`; ms per point, complete native evaluation, reduction in parentheses). Setup: binary64, schedule B, one thread pinned to an idle core, 16 lanes, 16 exactly light-like rational points, random block rows, Born components and kernel constants. Best of 5 runs (gg→gggg: of 3). Plans exported at `ba7d52f7` (companions without massless tadpoles; the plan export is unchanged since). Build: release, codegen-units 1, thin LTO, `loop-native-poles-uvir-a0cea095` (build-info `CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_CODEGEN_UNITS=1`, `LTO=thin`, the slice builds' profile), measured 2026-10-23 20:31–20:37 UTC. Later commits change binary64 only by a per-lane precision scan (the residue's inputs are borrowed, not lifted). The host was shared, at load 80–220 of 384 cores during the six-leg runs. A second gg→gggg run gave none / all / split 282.8 / 310.3 / 326.9 and companion 42.2, so differences below about 10 % are noise there.

Earlier figures in this section's history were labelled "cu1 thin-LTO" but came from `loop-native-poles-uvir-ba7d52f7`, a codegen-units-16, no-LTO build (audit finding). Its ratios agree with the table below. The evidence audit re-measured it on an idle core: gg→ggg 13.97 / 13.97 / 14.05, companion 0.947 (+7 %); uu~→e⁻e⁺gg 3.54 / 3.36 / 3.37, companion 0.027 (+1 %); gg→gggg 283.9 / 281.2 / 286.2, companion 31.2 (+13 %).

| Process | Families (main + companion) | `none` | `all` | Split columns (main plan) | Scaleless companion | `uv` / `ir` total | Generic engine, BOTH / UV masters |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| gg→ggg | 154 + 5 | 14.05 (2.47) | 14.10 (2.47) | 14.14 (2.54) | 0.99 | ≈ 15.1 (+7 %) | 36.2 (24.6) / 36.5 (24.8) |
| uu~→e⁻e⁺gg (photon) | 69 + 2 | 2.96 (0.68) | 2.97 (0.69) | 2.99 (0.70) | 0.028 | ≈ 3.02 (+2 %) | 9.87 (7.57) / 9.86 (7.56) |
| gg→gggg | 1100 + 6 | 285 (50.0) | 287 (50.6) | 308 (57.9) | 31.3 | ≈ 340 (+18 %; +13 % idle, audit) | 716 (463) / 731 (455) |

**Generation and load cost of the companion.** `LoopSlice.generate` builds the companion for every process, and `artifact.generate_process` exports it, so the cost is paid even when `uv`/`ir` are never used. Loading a v4 artifact currently regenerates the process (WP2.6), until native evaluation without load-time regeneration lands (DECISIONS 2026-10-23 18:40), so the build cost recurs at load. Measured with a probe at `ba7d52f7` on the same host:

| Process | Slice generation incl. companion | Companion build | Main plan: merged graph + export | Companion export | Plan JSON, main / companion |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| uu~→e⁻e⁺gg | 9.0 s | 0.02 s | 0.1 + 1.5 s | 0.05 s | 0.74 / 0.02 MB |
| gg→ggg | 31.1 s | 3.5 s (11 %) | 1.3 + 2.4 s | 0.40 s | 3.8 / 0.85 MB |
| gg→gggg | 372 s | 56 s (15 %) | 23 + 33 s | 16 s | 64 / 14 MB (zlib in the artifact: 7.4 → 8.8 MB) |

Seven legs (gg→ggggg, uu~→ggggg; §0.1 targets) were not measured. From five to six legs the companion build grew 16× and the slice generation 12×. If the same growth held, an estimate for WP3.1 planning (not a measurement) would be about 15 min of companion build at gg→ggggg, 15–20 % of the generation, plus a few minutes of export. The companion's quartic partner positions take every split of the other legs. **Decision (coordinator, DECISIONS 2026-10-23, under the user's relaxed generation budget):** the companion is kept as built, always generated and stored, so that one artifact serves every mode; its evaluation cost falls only on `uv`/`ir`. Folding it into the main plan, which would share seeds and factors (open, below), remains the optimisation if WP3.1's seven-leg generation measures the share as too large; a generation option without the companion (artifacts refusing `uv`/`ir`) was not chosen.

- `"all"` and `"none"` are unchanged: same fast engine and same plan, and the companion is never run.
- The UV residue column costs less than this host's noise: 0.1–0.2 ms/pt at five legs on a quieter host (build `0af1e5d3`). This is after the monomial tables are built once per bucket; built per lane they cost +0.9 ms/pt at gg→ggg.
- The companion dominates the `uv`/`ir` overhead: 31 ms/pt for its 6 families at gg→gggg, against 65 ms for 7 families before massless tadpoles were dropped. Its seeds, factors and dressing are repeated outside the main plan. Folding it into the main plan v4 (families flagged and skipped unless split) would share seeds and factors; that is an open optimisation, measured here before it is done.
- The upstream route, NinjaRS's generic engine with UV- or IR-selected masters, costs 9–12× the fast engine's reduction and 2.5–3.5× its complete evaluation (table; gg→gggg 467 against 51 ms/pt of reduction). It returns one selection per reduction, so `ir` (which needs the combined poles for the field split) would also need the fast run. It would still need the companion, and its binary64 finite part is not the fast engine's bit for bit. It is therefore the oracle of the residue, not the production path.
- **Upstream extension (brief trigger: generic engine much slower).** The fast route needs the UV residue of every lane next to the fast engine's combined poles. That residue is master-integral algebra, so it is proposed to NinjaRS as `ninjars::fast::tensor_uv` (`TensorUvResidues`, `tensor_uv_residue`; any Symbolica float carrier). Branch `pyamplicol/fast-tensor-uv-residue`, commits `40dca4c` and `68945a3` (the multiprecision input lift, as in the local copy) on main 41a4c00, in the local clone `workspace/tmp/poles-uvir/upstream/ninjars`. Both are also kept as `workspace/reports/engine-v2/ninjars-fast-tensor-uv-residue-68945a3.patch` (`git am` onto 41a4c00), with the passing test logs `ninjars-fast-tensor-uv-residue-40dca4c-tests.log` and `-68945a3-tests.log`: `tensor_uv_residue` 2/2 and `fast::tensor_uv` 4/4, then 5/5. It is not pushed: pushing it or opening the PR waits for the coordinator's go, and NinjaRS main is not touched. Its minimal reproducer `crates/ninjars-symbolica/tests/tensor_uv_residue.rs` shows that the fast engine's combined poles can be split without selected masters. It checks the functional against the generic engine with `PoleSelection::UV` on tadpoles, bubbles (massive and scaleless light-like), a triangle and boxes (massless and massive) with dense integer numerators, and checks that finite shapes have no residue. The README section on separate UV and IR poles states the split IR = combined − UV, which includes the dropped scaleless bubbles. `runtime/v2_poles.rs` is the interim local copy; the re-pin that brings the function onto NinjaRS main deletes it.

**Validation** (one point per process; `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_poles.py`, `rust/loop-reduction/tests/runtime_v2_reduce.rs`):

- `"all"` is bitwise the result before the re-pin. Checked on g g > g g, u u~ > d d~ g and u u~ > t t~: Python path, native binary64 and DoubleFloat, finite only, and the re-test estimates. After merging the slice tips `42c6117d` (observables) and `45875b87` (artifact-api), and with the public wiring and the observable-column split, `"all"` is again bitwise each tip's result with its own native (same three processes; plus g g > g g observable outputs: native inclusive, dipole and directed columns, the Python path's, and finite-only helicity-resolved outputs).
- The finite parts are identical in all four modes, in binary64 and DoubleFloat (g g > g g, g g > t t~) and with MPFR at 32 digits (g g > g g; `test_finite_parts_are_identical_in_every_mode`).
- UV + IR parts give the combined poles of every component, to 10⁻¹². The double pole is IR. The renormalised virtual's UV part vanishes, and its IR poles (the `"ir"` values) are those of `"all"`, also at LC and NLC and for correlated, directed and resolved outputs. The `"uv"` values are the bare loop's UV poles, and the counterterms' UV poles cancel them. For a model without pole regulators these identities hold by construction of the field UV part, and the three items below are the independent evidence; with them (MS-POLES, "Models with pole regulators" above) the UV cancellation is itself a test.
- `"ir"` equals Catani's operator on g g > g g and u u~ > g g g (massless) and on g g > t t~ and u u~ > t t~ (massive, stable top), to 10⁻¹⁰. The massive operator and the virtual reproduce the provider poles of g g > t t~ (MadLoop, Recola, OpenLoops) to 2·10⁻¹⁵.
- −(`"uv"` values + uv_local) = −(bare_UV + uv_local) equals Σ_legs Z_i^UV·Born (Feynman gauge, textbook constants) on g g > g g, u u~ > g g g, u u~ > u u~, Drell-Yan (photon vertex, no δg_s), g g > t t~ and u u~ > t t~, to 10⁻¹⁰ (observed ≤ 4·10⁻¹⁵ by the evidence audit, also on u u~ > d d~ g and u u~ > u u~ g). In units of (α_s/4π)·Born, bare_UV is 10 (g g > g g), 58/3 (four quarks) and 8/3 (Drell-Yan).
- The residue equals the generic engine's UV-selected reduction lane by lane: binary64 to 10⁻¹⁰ on synthetic lanes and 3·10⁻¹⁴ on g g > g g, DoubleFloat to 10⁻²⁶.
- The Python reference path equals the native executor in split mode. The stability estimates exist in every mode and carrier (binary64; `"uv"` and `"ir"` with a rotation and the rescaled reduction check in DoubleFloat and MPFR at 32 digits).
- A relocated copy of an artifact gives bitwise the original's `"uv"` and `"ir"` values and pole parts (the stored companion plan, §0.2; `test_artifacts_hold_no_host_paths_and_load_relocated`).

Found on the way (pre-existing, not a pole-selection defect): at massive external legs the native executor's family integrals differed from the reference path's (g g > t t~, top width 0: native double pole −0.0666 against −0.1133; the reference path equals MadLoop to 2·10⁻¹⁵). The slice's mass-flipped kernel copies (`reference_executor.mass_flips`, WP2.6) fixed it: after merging `45875b87` the native executor equals the reference path at g g > t t~ in `"all"` and `"ir"` (`test_native_executor_at_massive_external_legs`), and the massive processes of these tests now run natively (binary64, DoubleFloat). Catani's operator takes the legs' masses from the model's runtime values (a symbolic mass bound to 0 is massless), not from p² of the given momenta. Rounded inputs give a massless leg a p² of ±10⁻¹⁷ (the post-build validation's points) or O(10⁻¹¹) (decimal momenta of a 500 GeV point), which the massive branches read as a mass: the log of a negative argument, or v = 1 and log 0. With MB symbolic and bound to 0 that made `"all"` and `"ir"` raise at g g > b b~ (audit finding). It is fixed and tested by `test_catani_takes_the_masses_from_the_model` and `test_symbolic_mass_bound_to_zero_at_rounded_momenta`. Since the CT recursion became the default CT route (WP2.2, merged at `a18b7599`), the latter generates with `counterterms="sites"`: the recursion's lowered couplings refuse a symbolic mass bound to 0. The latter is natively and on the Python path equal to Catani's prediction, and natively bitwise equal to the slice tip `45875b87` in `"all"`. ρ² is computed as m_j²m_k²/(p_j·p_k)² / (1+v)², so 1 − v does not cancel at small masses. Where the operator makes no prediction (a vanishing invariant, a massive pair at or below threshold, a coloured leg whose mass is not a runtime parameter), `ir_poles` is None rather than an error. A top width in the loop propagators (complex mass, no CMS counterterms: WP3.2) also moves the single pole of g g > t t~ by 10⁻³ relative to Catani's, so the IR-pole prediction is made only for stable coloured legs.

**Public settings** (WP2.6 surface, §12.2). `PoleSelection` has `ALL`, `NONE`, `UV`, `IR`; TOML `evaluation.poles`, CLI `--poles` and the API `poles=` take any of them, a runtime option of one artifact. `V2VirtualRuntime.evaluate` passes the mode to `NativeExecutor.evaluate` (the reducer's `Poles::Split` and the companion plan in `uv`/`ir`):

- `VirtualEvaluation.values` holds the selection (finite part bitwise that of `"all"`; `"uv"`: the bare loop's UV poles, the double pole exactly zero, equal to `pole_part("uv", "bare")`'s poles; `"ir"`: the renormalised virtual's IR poles, the poles of `"all"` to rounding);
- `VirtualEvaluation.pole_parts[point]` (`VirtualPoleParts`, `None` for failed points and under `all`/`none`) holds both origins for the components `virtual`, `bare`, `uv`, `r2`, `uv_local`, `uv_external_fields` (`PointResult.pole_origins`); `pole_part(origin, component)` reads one as `[point][sector]` Laurent triples; `for_order` keeps them; the CLI prints them as a table (human output) or `pole_parts` (JSON);
- stability estimates are formed on the selected coefficients (the `"uv"` double pole is exactly zero and so is its estimate, 0.0; `relative` is the whole-sector bound); in `"uv"` the reduction check alone does not see numerator errors, so a rotation check is needed to bracket the selected single pole (Carriers and estimates); tested in binary64 (a rotation brackets `"uv"` and `"ir"` against DoubleFloat), DoubleFloat and MPFR;
- the artifact stores the companion plan (§12.1), so loading needs no extra generation for `uv`/`ir`; an artifact written before the split (poles declared `["all", "none"]`) is refused at admission and load with a regeneration message (AGENTS.md: no backward compatibility; tested by rewriting a current artifact's declaration);
- correlated and resolved outputs (`V2ObservablesRuntime`, `observables.NativeObservables` and the Python path) take all four. Their columns also run on the certified-scaleless companion: `ObservableSlice.scaleless()` is the same observables on the companion slice (at LC/NLC with directed histories rebuilt unpruned and pruned for them, as `of` prunes the slice), and `ColumnPlan.scaleless()` its column plan, which numbers its lanes as the main plan does (checked). The assembly (`observables._Assembly.result`) splits every output's poles per column with `poles.split_sector`: the UV finiteness of the renormalised amplitude holds for every linear functional of it, so the field-factor rule applies per column. `ResolvedVirtualEvaluation.pole_parts` and `.total()` (summed parts) follow; `_sum_results` sums the origins. Checked: native = Python (10⁻¹¹), UV + IR = all and finite parts bitwise for the inclusive, a dipole and a directed history at full colour and a directed history at LC; resolved sums = inclusive split (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_poles.py`, `test_loop_v2_artifact.py`).

The slice-level option stays `LoopSlice.evaluate(poles=...)` and `NativeExecutor.evaluate(poles=...)`, with one spelling: a `PoleSelection` or its value (`poles.pole_mode`; a bool is refused), and the binding's reducer option is `"all"`, `"none"` or `"split"`. The SDKs take the same four selections for plan v4 artifacts (`rusticol_virtual_set_poles`, §12.1 SDK); plan v1-v3 handles take "all" only.

### 13.3 Deleted (MQ-LEGACY-REMOVE, 2026-10-29)

The user ruled (2026-10-28 ~16:10, M-QCD Q3) that v2 is the only one-loop engine and the legacy per-diagram engine is removed now, not after gate R3. Package MQ-LEGACY-REMOVE did so; the table below is the original plan (line ranges [V-s contracts §5], now gone). What was removed, decided by a def-level static reachability graph from the production entry points, `loops/v2`, the retained tests and tools: `LoopEngine`/`loop.engine` (refused with the reason in TOML, overrides and CLI), the legacy branches of `loop_service` (graph plan, compile, flavour transport, directed preparation and validation), `LoopProcessArtifact`/`LoopExecutionMetadata`, `runtime.virtual.VirtualRuntimeBackend`, 37 modules under `loops/` and the legacy names of the shared ones (CT matching in `counterterms`, `PreparedLoopRequestModel.compile` and the primitive certificates in `compiler`, scale bindings, field-CT matching), the PyO3 legacy runtime (`NativeLoopProgram`, `PortableLoopPlan`, `NativeVirtualRuntime`, `CoefficientPlan`, `DualBornWeights`, …), the loop crate's `VirtualRuntime`, physical backend, schedule, dual weights and state batches, the SDK's plan v1-v3 engine (a v3 artifact is refused typed; resolved/correlated/spin/verified SDK calls refuse with the plan v4 reason) and the core's plan v3 loader. Kept as test oracles: FeynKit's diagram catalogue (`tools/loop_reference/feynkit_loop_graphs.py`, used by O7), the Rust `PolynomialPlan` (the v2 reducer's Rust tests), `control_models.py`; the legacy engine's values are frozen captures under `tests/fixtures/loop/legacy/` (O1, O2 and the captures of the former live comparisons: `reduction/`, `ew_families_*`, `gg_gg_non_default_parameters`, `gg_ttx_model_defaults`, `gg_gg_qcd_qed_sm`). The plan v3 runtime inside `rusticol-core` (`loop_runtime` descriptor/loader, `loop_execution::LoopExecutor`/`LoopBackend`/`evaluate_inner`, `source_bindings::SourceOwner`) went too, as did the Python `PolynomialPlan`/`CoefficientPlan`/spinor bindings of `_loop_reduction` and their crate tests (the legacy family reductions are frozen captures compared with `V2Reducer`).

## 14. Validation strategy and oracles

| # | Oracle | What it isolates | When |
|---|---|---|---|
| O1 | **Frozen legacy outputs** (double, AP ≥ 200 bits) for DY, uudd, mixed uudd, tt, qq→gg, gg→gg, uu~→ggg, uu~→dd~g, uu~→e⁻e⁺gg, uu~→dd~ss~, ee→μμ, sgluon, **uu~→uu~, uu~→uu~g**. Covers Born, 6 components × sectors × poles, 19 totals, LC/NLC degrees, directed, spin, resolved, flavour, CT insertion multisets | Everything, end to end | Wave 0 freeze; every gate |
| O2 | **Strong per-family oracle.** Legacy Born-weighted coefficient vectors (capture hooks `physical_process.py:1030, 1362` [V-s]) against v2 polynomials, after the exact affine map from FeynKit `IntegralFamily.find_mappings`, modulo certified-zero families. Error norm: the coefficient max-norm with the coefficients of rank r weighted by √s^r (the raw max-norm mixes mass dimensions); bounds as in §16.3 | Numerator, colour, helicity, fermion signs; isolates reduction | S1 and wave 2 |
| O3 | **Weak per-family oracle.** Reduced Ω_f per family | Frame-independent check | When frames differ |
| O4 | **Schedule equality.** F = B = M per family, exact in rational arithmetic (M on LC and certified classes) | Merging and sharing logic | Every change to schedules |
| O5 | **Random-q family oracle** (Design 3 A′). Evaluate the family numerator at random exact q by direct per-skeleton evaluation (kernels with numeric q) and compare with the polynomial | Polynomial algebra, raise tables, layout | Unit level |
| O6 | **CT site closure against CT recursion.** Equal CT amplitudes per (site class, kind, ε); multisets equal legacy `match_counterterms` | Counterterm recursion, symmetry | Wave 2 |
| O7 | **FeynKit structure oracle.** Family sets (modulo logged certified-zero families: gg→gg 48 + 4 = 52 in the control model), signs and symmetry factors from overall factors on gg→gg, gg→ggg, uu~→gg, uu~→dd~, uu~→dd~g, uu~→e⁻e⁺gg, uu~→uu~; Recola tensor-integral sets on 5 processes [M] | Conventions (judge X4) | S1 |
| O8 | **Provider references.** `workspace/reference/runs/*` controls, `tools/loop_reference/*.json`, and `tests/fixtures/loop/required/<process>.json` from the required-references campaign. gg_gggg passed Recola–OpenLoops at max rel 7.3×10⁻¹¹ [V `required/watch.log`] | Physics truth | All gates; mandatory, never env-gated |
| O9 | **IR poles** against Catani from the Born and colour-correlated Borns; renormalised poles | Every process without a reference | Per point, always |
| O10 | **Asymmetric Ward identity** (ε → p on loop and CT sides only, full kernels) | Gauge invariance, UV + R2 completeness | Validation runs |
| O11 | **Precision convergence:** double vs DoubleFloat vs Float on frozen points | Stability, genuine carriers | Wave 2 |
| O12 | **Counts and costs:** families, skeletons and trie nodes equal Appendix B; measured operation counts within ×2 of the recalibrated model | Generator and cost model | G0, G1 |
| O13 | **Fermion signs:** uu~→uu~, uu~→uu~g (identical-flavour pairings), ee→μμ and e⁺e⁻→e⁺e⁻ at NLO EW (lepton lines in the loop) against O1/O8 and FeynKit overall factors | The §3.3 sign rule | S1 (QCD), wave 3 (EW) |
| O14 | **BDK NLC check:** exact-N_c interference of uu~→ggg against hep-ph/9409393 eq. NLOCrossSection, with partial amplitudes from resolved-colour seed columns | NLC degree classification | Wave 2 (WP2.4) |
| O15 | **Re-test calibration:** false-negative rates of relabelling, rescaling, pole and κ triggers against Float(256) on ≥ 10⁴ points per required process | Accuracy claims | Wave 2–3 (WP2.3) |

**Gates**

| Gate | Criterion |
|---|---|
| G0 | Rust segment kernels on real trie shapes (gg→gggg, uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g); throughput and dispatch measured; recipe recalibrated; required QCD targets within their gates with ×1.3 headroom (§5.4). |
| S1 | First vertical slice (§16.3). |
| G1 | Measured end-to-end throughput on gg→gg, gg→ggg, uu~→ggg, uu~→dd~g, gg→gggg, uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g, uu~→e⁻e⁺ggg; model recalibrated; providers re-measured in production and protocol modes; gg→gg faster than MadLoop at B = 128; B-vs-M decision recorded. *Status 2026-10-24 (§5.7):* REQUIREMENTS_ADDENDUM A/D replace the gg→gg criterion (2→2 is reported without a gate; measured 2.22 ms against MadLoop's 2.02 ms) and set the gate from 2→3 upward in production mode; measured on the 2→3/2→4 set including uu~→e⁻e⁺g and uu~→e⁻e⁺gg; model recalibrated (Table 1c); B-vs-M recorded. | 10 | Dependency coordination (re-pin, Symbolica pin for Float, upstreaming the generic tensor numerator) | Medium / medium | WP0.2 with the NinjaRS owner; re-pinned together to the announced mains (NinjaRS aa6a6307, oneloopmaster 8ec256fc, Symbolica 9cdc2949); later bumps only on owner announcements, again in one coordinated rebuild: NinjaRS 41a4c00 and oneloopmaster 5b710437 (UV/IR pole selection, 2026-10-23; default results bitwise unchanged) |
| 11 | Three executors (F, B, M) diverge | Low / low | F kept small; O4 in CI |
| 12 | Asymptotics: B's Θ((n−1)!·4ⁿ) seed term grows at n ≥ 8 | Low (required set n ≤ 7) | M (WP2.8) is the planned path and the default for n ≥ 8 |
| 13 | Complex-mass master defects (NinjaRS guidance [V]): one-/two-mass boxes with wide widths wrong, also in OneLOop (fixed in oneloopmaster 8ec256fc, pinned); still open: C0(0,0,0; m_complex,0,m_real) wrong on all routes, generic-engine small-\|p²\| unequal-mass bubbles fail, opposite-mass two-mass boxes near collinear limits ≈10⁻⁸, real negative m² not the −i0 limit | Medium / high for CMS processes | Re-pin done; the CMS controls pass on it in double, DoubleFloat and AP80, zero width included; per-defect regression points in WP2.3; affected master signatures refused or forced to the re-test plus rescue; no silent use. **Checked once on the masters of CM01–CM10 (M-SM MS-MASTERS, 2026-10-30;** `tools/loop_benchmark/master_census.py`: every subset of at most four propagators of every family at the rows' points, exact invariants and bound masses; per row `tools/loop_benchmark/master_census_rows.py`; `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_master_census.py`): no C0 at vanishing invariants is reached (a triangle's three momenta partition the legs, so beyond three legs one of them is a multi-leg invariant, zero only at exceptional points); the smallest unequal-mass bubble momentum is 0.43 of its largest mass (binary64 loss ~1e-15, the V9 scaling); no real negative m²; the complex-mass boxes on the `Float` route agree: AP(40) = AP(60) to ≤ 1.0·10⁻⁵⁴ and DD = AP(60) to ≤ 6·10⁻²⁶ on CM01–CM04, CM08 (4 points each) and CM10 (2 points), AP(40) = DD to 1.1·10⁻²⁷ on CM07 (one point; its 2457 complex D0 masters outnumber all other rows'; AP(40) 277 s, 15 GB), AP(32) = MadLoop CMS on CM05 and CM09 (MS-CMS, §6.8); oneloopmaster's SymJIT masters are not on any reducer's route. None bites, so no upstream PR. What did bite is the same signature in pyAmpliCol's own planes: a width set to zero at run time on a pole mass generated complex (CM01's zero-width control) made the binary64 SymJIT parameter program take the other side of the electroweak UV counterterm couplings' branch cuts than the exact evaluator (signed zeros; its plane check certifies the generation's parameter values only): finite part 3.9·10⁻⁵ against MadLoop's 1.8·10⁻³, while DD equals MadLoop. The plane translation now makes the zero imaginary part of every cut argument +0 (`symjit_plane::canonical_cut_row`: + 0 on each non-constant argument of a call other than the continuous ones and on a slot-exponent power's base; −0 + 0 = +0 and x + 0 = x otherwise), the side exact arithmetic, Symbolica's evaluators, DD, AP and MadLoop take (MadLoop's REGLOG, REGSQRT and GRREGLOG normalise a signed zero to +0 too); every carrier then equals MadLoop at zero widths with nothing checked at run time. (Rounds 1–4 of MS-MASTERS checked the plane against the exact evaluator once per new parameter key instead, through an abstract interpretation of which inputs can move a side; that called Symbolica in binary64 and was removed in round 5, USER ~17:35.) At 0 < |Im m²| < 10⁻¹⁵⁰ Re m² the DoubleFloat C0 turned non-finite (typed `MasterFailed`, CM01 at 10⁻¹⁵⁵, 10⁻¹⁶⁰ and 10⁻³⁰⁰ of the masses, not at 10⁻²⁰⁰): double-double refuses such a width (`complex-mass-master`, a model-parameter error naming the pole mass by its value), a deliberate refusal of unphysical widths with no upstream track; binary64 and AP(32) equal MadLoop's zero-width value there (CM01 at 10⁻¹⁵²). CM05's native-input QCD⁴QED⁴ finite part was NaN at every point (`uv_local`; MS-POLES had excluded its native lane as a master defect). This is not a master defect but a SymJIT SIMD defect: one output of the binary64 parameter program was NaN at the request's point in a block of equal lanes, as every batch runs it, while its scalar code and the exact evaluator agree (−3.03·10⁻⁵ − 0.0130i). The plane check had evaluated that point in one block with the off-axis probes, which SymJIT replays in scalar code. Cause (SymJIT 2.27.3 through the v228 head `c0ccfa4`): the AVX and AVX-512 `times_complex` decides aliasing on logical registers, but `Reg::Left` and `Reg::Ret` share a physical register. A complex product that the peephole optimizer (level ≥ 1) writes into a call's argument registers therefore overwrote its first factor; a 7-instruction reproducer, `log(a·b)` at a = b = 1, gives −∞ in SIMD. An upstream PR is prepared (`upstream/symjit-simd-complex-times-alias`: the fix, a regression test, suite 2262 passed and 0 failed); with it, all 2509 outputs of CM05's program agree between SIMD and scalar. Until a fixed release is pinned, `verify_plane` runs every probe row in a SIMD block of its own, and `compile_plane_application_checked` compiles a failing program again at level 0 (no peephole pass) and keeps it if it passes. CM05's parameter program takes that plane, and its native lane equals MadLoop CMS (`test_cm05_native_inputs_equal_madloop_cms`). A second bite (fix rounds 6–7): at run-time partial zero widths, WZ = WT = 0 with WW ≠ 0, CM01's virtual depended on rounding in every carrier. The G_mu UFO's MZ⁴ − 4MT²MZ² (and MH⁴ − 4MH²MZ² at WH = 0) reaches its square root through cw² = MW²/MZ², so its imaginary part, zero in exact arithmetic, came out as a residue of either sign once WW ≠ 0 (Symbolica's binary64 evaluator −3.7·10⁻⁹ at the card's WW, DD −8.3·10⁻²⁵; AP at 300 bits −8·10⁻⁸⁷ at another row). A seeded fuzz of CM01's masses, widths and scale (54 rows at its first finite point, 26 with WZ = WT = 0 and WW ≠ 0; MadLoop CMS from a recompiled copy of the control export) had DD wrong at 13 rows, AP(32) at 6 (flipping with its digits up to 120) and binary64 at 1, and MadLoop's own double and quadruple precision differ at 3; round 6's textbook complex products made binary64 equal DD and were reverted. Such an argument, not manifestly real but real in its rational normal form wherever some of its widths vanish (`ct_recursion._RealArguments.zero_width_cases`), now reads its real projection when those widths are exactly zero, in the native parameter program and the counterterm coefficient plan (a test on exact inputs; the value changes only by the residue). On the fuzz binary64 = DD = AP(32) to ≤ 3·10⁻¹³ at every row, AP(32…120) and DD agree to 12 digits where they flipped, and the value equals MadLoop at 53 rows in double precision and at the remaining one in quadruple precision (at each row where MadLoop's two precisions differ it equals one of them); widths down to 10⁻²⁰ GeV with the others exactly zero agree in every carrier too (`test_cut_arguments_real_at_zero_widths_read_their_real_part_there`, the CM01 nightly on `required/msm/cm01_zero_widths.json`). While localising it, a separate latent SymJIT defect turned up: its common-subexpression elimination (`Block::elimination_pass`, 2.27.3 to 2.28.2 and the v228 head `c67777e`) collects the subexpressions shared by straight-line statements but rewrote every occurrence, those inside a branch too, assigning the shared value at its first occurrence, which can lie in a branch the inputs do not take, so later statements read an unset temporary (not CM01's cause: its count of wrong outputs was unchanged without it). Both plane translations drop the jumps (every branch evaluated, the `Join` selects; `symjit_plane::is_control_flow`, `symbolica_helpers._symbolica_instruction_program_repr`; `test_symjit_plane_helper_functions.py::test_untaken_branches_define_no_shared_values`), and an upstream PR is prepared (`upstream/symjit-cse-across-branches`: the second pass rewrites only at depth 0, a regression test, suite 2264 passed and 0 failed). An artifact written before these fixes has a parameter plane without the canonicalisation, the zero-width projections and the flattening, certified by the check at the generation's values only: regenerate it before evaluating it in binary64 at other parameter values that put a parameter-program argument on a cut (a width set to zero under the complex-mass scheme); nothing refuses such an artifact (none was released: `origin/loop` holds M-QCD only, whose loop_sm QCD parameter planes equal their states at quark masses set to zero or changed, at a new αs and a new scale, before and after the change). The shipped EW self-test artifact is regenerated and equals MadLoop to 5.4·10⁻¹⁵. Not pursued (oracle path, unphysical widths): `NativeExecutor(inputs="python")` in binary64 at widths 10⁻¹⁰⁰ of CM01's masses did not finish in 5 min (native inputs: 0.1 s). |
| 14 | Electroweak family proliferation (gg→tt̄e⁻e⁺g EW: 2.5× QCD families by MadLoop's ratio, heptagons through the lepton line) | Medium / medium | Gate E0 with exact counts before EW timing work; species sharing by mass; M for certified EW classes |
| 15 | Provider baselines drift (MadLoop exports rebuilt with production flags during this revision; Recola cache defect at N = 7 with COLLIER 1.2.9/1.2.10) | Medium / low | One protocol for all providers at G1 and R3; production and protocol columns both reported; provider versions frozen in the report |

## 16. Implementation plan

Sizes are in agent-days: S ≤ 3, M 4–8, L 9–18. Every work package owns its files exclusively; shared files are split by function as stated. Dependencies on the assessment's no-regret packages (test harness WP01, release builds WP02, massive-quark CT admission WP08, R6/R7) are external.

### 16.1 Waves

| Wave | Content | Exit gate | Wall time with 4–6 agents [I] |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Oracles, references, NinjaRS coordination, kernel gate | Fixtures frozen; **G0** | ≈1–2 weeks (parallel with wave 1) |
| 1 | Vertical slice in Python (F, B, M) plus the reduction adapter | **S1** | 3–4 weeks |
| 2 | Native Rust core, CT recursion, precision and re-test, colour, observables, API, measurement, M in Rust | **G1**, **W2** | 5–7 weeks |
| 3 | Scale and required set (QCD, then EW after E0), deletion | **E0**, **R3**, **D** | 4–6 weeks |
| 4 | Gated optimisations | Each ≥ 15 % measured or dropped | As needed |

Total ≈ 270 agent-days [I].

**Critical path:** WP1.2 → WP1.3 → WP1.4 → WP1.5 → WP1.8 (S1) → WP2.1 → WP2.7 (G1) → WP3.1 → WP3.3 (R3). WP0.3 (G0) runs in parallel with wave 1 and must finish before WP2.1 fixes the kernel form and lane count.

### 16.2 Work packages

  - *Goal:* freeze O1 and the per-family O2 captures (double, AP 200 bits) for the control set, including uu~→uu~ and uu~→uu~g. Produce three-provider references for uu~→dd~g, uu~→uu~ and uu~→uu~g with the existing required-references toolkit (`tools/loop_reference/{exact_points.py, madloop_export.py, references.py, run_required.sh}` on branch `work/required-references`). Add the shim for the stale `_loop_reduction` gate and make provider comparisons mandatory.

At 200 bits within 1e-40 (deviations in Decimal arithmetic); in binary64 every output runs in the Python reference executor and natively (representative and member, directed, spin and resolved) with one rotation and the reduction-direction check. A directed or spin output is held relative to the largest Born and, per virtual sector, the largest Laurent coefficient; a helicity-resolved row relative to its own Born and per-sector scales, a structural-zero row (legacy value below 1e-40 of the point's largest) relative to the point's largest.
    - binary64 O1 (gate S1), **the user's rule (signed off 2026-10-23, ~07:30 UTC; an approved requirement, not a loosened tolerance)**: a binary64 value whose own stability estimate E (one rotation and the reduction-direction check, run with it) is at most 1e-12 is held to 1e-12 of its 200-bit legacy value; a value with E > 1e-12 is held to E, capped at 1e-9, and its output must also agree natively in DoubleFloat within 1e-24 of the legacy value at every point. The user chose this ("1e-12 unless E > 1e-12") among three options: the threshold E > 1e-11, the threshold E > 1e-12 (this rule), or a strict 1e-12 with binary64 taken off the ill-conditioned point. A value is an output at a point in one executor (directed, spin and helicity-resolved rows; representative and members); the Born is held to 1e-12 always (E estimates the virtual). A structural-zero helicity row, Born or virtual sector (legacy value below 1e-40 of the point's largest) is held to 1e-12 of the point's largest whatever its E, as the option put to the user said ("structural-zero rows keep the point's scale, their relative E is meaningless": it is measured against the row's rounding residue, up to 3.4e16 measured), and a resolved row's E is taken over its virtual sectors that are not structural zeros (`_resolved_estimate`), so a structural sector loosens neither itself nor the row's other sectors. In the measured records no row mixes structural and non-structural sectors, so the second clause changes no E today. The rule is applied to every value alike (`_o1_bounds`); there is no list of expected misses, so the outcome does not depend on which values are ill-conditioned in a build or on the process history.
      - Census (all binary64 O1 values: both executors at both points of every record, every member; one census process per record, which gives the same bits as the test processes since generation does not depend on the process history (below); re-run with the canonical kernel order, which moved 377 of the 860 values in the last bits and changed only the four-leg resolved rows' figures below and the structural rows' largest E above): directed and spin, 284 values, 28 identically zero in both engines, 27 with E > 1e-12 (all u u~ > g g g, 19 at its first point), E at most 1.6e-10. Where E ≤ 1e-12 the values are within 6.5e-14 (every four-leg value within 1.6e-14, four-leg E at most 3.0e-13). Six values exceed 1e-12, all at the first u u~ > g g g point and all with E > 1e-12: spin 3 Python 8.45e-12 (E 1.6e-10) and native 2.05e-12 (E 1.3e-10), FC T12 Python 4.48e-12 and native 3.62e-12 (E 1.2e-10), NLC T13 native 3.82e-12 (E 2.1e-11), NLC T12 Python 2.0e-12 (E 6.3e-11); every deviation is at most 0.22 E. Helicity-resolved, 576 row values, 392 structural zeros (v2 within 1.1e-17 of the point's largest; held to 1e-12 of it, whatever their own E), of the other 184 53 with E > 1e-12 (43 on u u~ > g g g, 10 four-leg rows with E ≤ 2.4e-12), E at most 2.2e-10; where E ≤ 1e-12 within 4.7e-14 (four-leg within 8.0e-14); seven rows exceed 1e-12, all at the first u u~ > g g g point with E > 1e-12 (1.6e-12 to 1.14e-11), every deviation at most 0.27 E. No E reaches the 1e-9 cap. Natively in DoubleFloat the 148 values of the outputs with E > 1e-12 are within 7.1e-27. Born values are within 5.7e-15. (The legacy engine's own binary64 misses 1e-12 at that point too: T12 3.4e-12, spin 3 3.0e-12, three resolved rows up to 1.06e-11.)
      - The point is ill-conditioned in every engine (rotated copies, measured while generation still depended on the process history: their bits differ from today's, not their statistics): at ten exact rotations of it spin 3 missed 1e-12 in 9 of 10 copies natively, 10 of 10 in Python and 9 of 10 in the legacy engine; NLC T13 in 3, 2 and 7 of 10; FC T12 in 8 and 8 of 10 (up to 2.5e-11), NLC T12 in 9 and 6 of 10 (up to 1.1e-11), always within 0.31 E; T35, the Born and spin (4,5) never (E ≤ 7e-12).
    - Generation does not depend on the process history (v2 tree stages and loop kernel records):
      - Cause. Symbolica numbers symbols in creation order and sorts the terms of a sum by the bytes of their packed ids (`AtomView::cmp_terms` compares `get_term_cmp_slice`; ids are packed little-endian in 1, 2 or 4 bytes). The normal form of a tree stage, and the evaluator built from it, therefore depended on the absolute ids of its slot and parameter symbols, i.e. on every symbol created before it in the interpreter (the model compile, earlier generations), and so did the binary64 rounding of `TreeRows` (350 of 866 rows of u u~ > g g g differed in the last bits between histories). The loop kernel records had the same cause (next items); counterterm scalars and model values were identical across the measured histories. A list of expected binary64 misses then held only in the process history it was measured in. Minimal reproducer (`workspace/tmp/observables/determinism/repro/repro.py`): create N unrelated symbols, then `a`, `b`, `c`; `E("a + b + c")` normalises as `a+b+c` for N = 0 and as `c+a+b` for N = 141 (this build defines 369 symbols at start, so `a`, `b`, `c` get ids 510, 511, 512, packed as FE 01, FF 01, 00 02).
      - Mechanism (`assemble._canonical_stage`). Each stage is renamed onto canonical symbols allocated in whole blocks of 2^16 whose first id is a multiple of 2^16 (padding symbols fill the gap, about 0.2 s per block), so canonical symbol k sorts by k's own bytes in every process. A positional renaming onto consecutive ids would not suffice: ids that straddle a byte boundary reorder. The stage inputs are positional (`ParamBuilder.add_parameter_list`), so input k becomes canonical symbol k by swapping the namespace in the id-independent canonical string and parsing it back, linear in the stage's size (0.6 s for u u~ > g g g, 3.7 s for g g > g g g's stages of up to 12 MB). Stages that retain model functions (above their inlining size; no tested process has one) take the general path: one `replace_multiple` renames the symbols, then a second the function heads, because a replacement does not descend into a call whose head it replaced. Function heads that carry Symbolica attributes keep their ids (none in the current models; WP3.1's deterministic generator owns that case, open list).
      - Loop kernel records (`reference_executor.ExactKernel`, `_term_order`). A kernel output is read term by term from `expand()` of the catalogue's exact expression, whose term order follows the ids of the kernel's input and parameter symbols; both executors sum the terms in record order, and the plan numbers the kernel constants in order of first use. The records therefore moved with the history too: after 250 unrelated symbols 685 of the 1594 exported kernel outputs of u u~ > g g g changed (term order in 517, constant numbering in the rest), and 6 of its 19 binary64 totals moved by up to 7.4e-13 relative; u u~ > u u~'s changed after 77 symbols (measured after 0, 77, 250, 300, 1000 and 3000 unrelated symbols on the legacy controls g g > g g, u u~ > d d~ g, u u~ > u u~, u u~ > u u~ g, u u~ > g g g and u u~ > t t~). Each output's terms are now sorted by (monomial, constant key), both built from exact numbers, parameter names and Symbolica's canonical strings only, so the records, the constant numbering and the binary64 sums are independent of the ids (the term sets were already equal across histories). In a process that loads the compiled model and generates one control first, the canonical records equal the former ones for the first five controls (input symbols are created in position order) and differ for u u~ > t t~; in the binary64 O1 census processes (below) 377 of the 860 values moved in the last bits, with unchanged statistics but for two more four-leg resolved rows above E = 1e-12.
      - Guards. Each canonical block is checked when it is created (`_check_canonical_block`): a sum of probe symbols at offsets 0, 1, 255, 256, 257, 511, 65280 and 65535 must normalise in the order of the offsets' bytes, which every misalignment by 1 to 2^16 − 1 ids breaks (checked for all of them), so a wrong id assumption or a change of Symbolica's term order raises instead of silently restoring history dependence. Tests: `test_canonical_stages_do_not_depend_on_the_process_history` and `test_misaligned_canonical_blocks_are_refused` (fast tier: both renaming paths on a synthetic 400-input stage, with and without a retained model function, after 0 and 300 unrelated symbols: equal normal forms and binary64 evaluator bits, while the raw stages' normal forms differ), and `test_generation_does_not_depend_on_the_process_history` (three fresh interpreters, after 0, 250 and 300 unrelated symbols: identical exported plan programs, kernel records included, identical kernel constants and bitwise identical binary64 totals of u u~ > g g g, while the probe's kernel records in Symbolica's own order still differ after 250; without the canonical stages the totals gave three different results after 0, 77 and 300, without the canonical kernel order they differed after 250).
      - Stability estimates do not depend on `PYTHONHASHSEED`: the observables and the inclusive rotation budgets (`assemble.rotation_budget`) sum their lanes in insertion order (before, in a set union's order, 24 of 63 binary64 estimates of g g > g g and d d~ > g g LC at their first legacy point, directed, spin and inclusive, both executors, differed in the last ulp between seeds 1 and 2; none now).
      - Evidence. u u~ > g g g generated alone (model loaded or compiled in the process), after d d~ > g g, and after g g > g g, d d~ > g g and u u~ > d d~ gives bitwise identical tree rows and binary64 T12 (native and Python) on both renaming paths. Binary64 bits of v2 evaluations moved once with the canonical stages; accuracy did not (true binary64 errors of the 16 slice points against 200 bits: geometric mean 8.5e-15 with the raw stages in one process history, 9.3e-15 canonical; only u u~ > u u~ g's values change, two up and one down; its fresh point gives 3.21e-13, and 1.59e-13 to 3.21e-13 with the raw stages, by history).
      - WP2.3 test (decided by the user, 2026-10-23, §10.4). The cached reduction-direction check alone has a documented binary64 false-negative rate of 2.3–2.75 % (§10.4), and u u~ > u u~ g's fresh point is one of its misses: 3.2054e-13 against an estimate of 3.1082e-13, the same after 0, 77, 300, 1000 and 3000 unrelated symbols, and after 0 and 250 with the canonical kernel order. On the slice's raw stages the strict bracket failed after 77, 141, 300 and 3000 unrelated symbols (3.12–3.21e-13 against 3.05–3.11e-13) and passed after 0 and 1000 (2.74e-13 against 3.06e-13, 1.59e-13 against 3.40e-13): it passed in the suite only through the suite's process history. By the user's decision every single-point bracket test holds error ≤ 10 E, as a whole and per Laurent coefficient, for every check configuration (`ESTIMATE_MARGIN`); `test_stability_estimates_bracket_out_of_sample` is again one test over every configuration and all four processes, with no xfail. The binary64 O1 rule above is unchanged (it uses E itself, without the margin).
    - also: Recola dipoles and spin source; MadLoop split orders natively and in Python; `.total()` = inclusive on g g > g g (FC, LC, NLC) and u u~ > g g g (FC; binary64 nightly), native = Python there; O10 on gg→gg and uu~→ggg on every gluon leg with ε_j → c p_j for c = 1 and c = i (Python on the first gluon leg, natively on every leg; per Laurent coefficient relative to the cancellation's scale, the larger of the physical components and the R2 part, which is the physical scale for c = 1: 200 bits and MPFR within 1e-45, DoubleFloat within 1e-25, measured at most 8.8e-27 of the physical scale for c = 1 and 1.35e-26 of the R2 part for c = i; binary64 within 1e-11 of that scale plus 1e-12 of the run's family lanes' absolute sum, the conditioning of the cancellation; 200 bits and DoubleFloat nightly); O14 with resolved seed columns at 200 bits (exact Gram, tree and BDK matrices, 1e-40; nightly); native = Python for directed, spin, resolved and member outputs; M = B on observable columns (g g > g g, 32 digits 1e-25, binary64 1e-12); directed tables by seed cells equal the generic contraction exactly.
    - O10, what it shows (gauge invariance, UV + R2 completeness). The Ward interference vanishes as a complex number; an inclusive output projects it to its real part, so the test runs c = 1 and c = i (`ward=(j, c)`). With c = 1 alone the test would see only that real projection, in which R2 and UV can appear to vanish on the QCD controls. Measured at the test point (200 bits, Python): on u u~ > g g g the bare interference for c = i is 3.3 to 20 times the physical scale (by leg and Laurent coefficient) and the R2 part cancels it to 1e-50 (c = 1 + 2i: the same picture), so the bare amplitude alone is not gauge invariant and R2 restores the identity; the test asserts R2 above the physical scale there, so a missing or wrong R2 fails it. On g g > g g R2 vanishes for every gauge vector (exactly zero, c = 1, i, 1 + 2i). The UV parts (`uv_local`, external fields) are built on the Ward-sourced trees and vanish with the tree Ward identity (below 1e-77 of the physical scale at 200 bits, 1e-14 in binary64); the test holds each to the Ward bound on its own, so a UV part read with the physical source fails it. Non-gauge control (`ward=(j, v)`, a literal complex vector on the loop side; binary64, both executors): the virtual is of order of the physical scale and R2, `uv_local` and the fields are each above 1e-2 of it (measured 0.1 to 0.7), so every part sees the loop-side source. g g > g g family lanes vanish one by one; u u~ > g g g lanes cancel across families (their absolute sum is 1.2e3 to 2.7e3 times the physical scale); binary64 leaves up to 3.5e-10 of the physical scale (Python, leg 5, c = 1), within the bound.
    - test tiers (HANDOFF §0.3): `test_loop_v2_observables.py` is the default tier, in binary64 with one 200-bit legacy record (d d~ > g g NLC) and the Python executor at the first legacy point (the native batch keeps both points). Its one-process run takes 10.5 min at machine load about 100 (628 s; 55 min before the split, 16 min with every four-leg record). Every other configuration runs nightly, in parallel files: `..._legacy_nightly.py` (five-leg directed and spin O1, both carriers), `..._precision_nightly.py` (four-leg O1 at 200 bits and the remaining binary64 points and records; the configurations come from `o1_nightly`, the complement of the default ones), `..._native_nightly.py` (O10 at 200 bits and in DoubleFloat, Recola at 200 bits, native = Python and the stability checks in DoubleFloat and at 40 digits, resolved sums at 200 bits, spin bilinearity at LC/NLC, the pure alias) and `..._nightly.py` (five-leg resolved O1 and sums, O14). `..._fast.py` (`loop_fast`, seconds) holds the pure-logic checks and the canonical-stage checks (both renaming paths after two process histories, refused misaligned blocks).

Resolved outputs at seven legs need schedule 3 (§8.5).

Python today: 2 min per gg→ggggg dipole at full colour, 22 min for its 49-table family without the degrees, hours estimated [I] for uu~→ggggg and for the family degrees.

The public `symmetric-group-fft` setting was refused at v2 generation until MQ-FFT (2026-10-28), which serves it (§7.3).

Since MQ-FFT the native executor runs it in every floating carrier (`NativeExecutor` refuses `fft` only for a plan without a certified pair), and the public mapping is `_V2_CONTRACTION[SYMMETRIC_GROUP_FFT] = "fft"` (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_seed_fft.py`).
      - Counterterms from the tree recursion (WP2.2, `work/v2-ct-recursion`, `counterterms="recursion"` by default there): `CountertermProgram.point` evaluates the CT program's tree rows with `self.rows.evaluate(point, values, numbers)`, without the spin sources and the Ward loop-side sources that this branch threads through `_PointEvaluation(sources=..., loop_sources=..., fixed=...)`. Whichever of the two branches is integrated second must (1) give `CountertermProgram.point` a `sources` argument passed to `TreeRows.evaluate(..., sources=sources)`; (2) call it from `_PointEvaluation.__init__` with the loop-side sources, `program.point(decimal_point, parameters, numbers, poles, sources={**(sources or {}), **(loop_sources or {})} or None)` (counterterm amplitudes are loop-side objects, like blocks, counterterm currents and field insertions); (3) build the CT program's trees on the slice's tree kernel variant (`lowered_counterterm_model` must keep `compiled.ir.tree_kernel_variant`: spin and Ward slices are generated on the full kernels, `compile_tree_kernel_variant(compiled, "full")`, and a transverse kernel drops the non-transverse part of a source); (4) keep the `_closed_counterterms` call inside the per-helicity-row loop of `_born_and_counterterms`, which after the merge keeps a source leg's single row (`fixed`); (5) take both new dataclass fields (`colour_pruned`, `counterterm_program`) and `site_vectors[row] = dict(loops)` after `_closed_counterterms` in the `assemble.py` conflict. Checks: the O1 spin outputs (`test_directed_and_spin_outputs_equal_legacy`, records with spin classes; nightly `uux_ggg_spin`) and O10 (`test_asymmetric_ward_identity`, whose UV parts must vanish on their own and whose u u~ > g g g R2 must cancel the bare part) with `counterterms="recursion"`, plus spin- and Ward-mode outputs equal between the recursion and the site closure at 200 bits on g g > g g and u u~ > g g g.
      - Open, generation determinism beyond the v2 tree stages and loop kernel records (owner: generation, WP3.1 — the native generator must be deterministic): the tree engine's production stage compile (`generation/stage_artifacts.py`, `_compile_symbolica_outputs`) builds Symbolica evaluators from expressions whose normal form depends on absolute symbol ids in the same way, so a tree artifact generated after other generations in one interpreter can round differently in binary64 (not affecting §0.2 relocation, which reloads one artifact); the v2 loop kernel records are canonical since `reference_executor._term_order` (above). Reproducer: the probe `workspace/tmp/observables/determinism/probe.py` (u u~ > g g g generated alone or after d d~ > g g; digests of kernels, tree rows, counterterm scalars and T12; for the kernel records, the history probe of `test_generation_does_not_depend_on_the_process_history`) and the Symbolica one above. Either canonicalise as `assemble._canonical_stage` does, or (upstream request, not a defect: Symbolica documents id-ordered normal forms) compare symbol ids numerically in `cmp_terms`, after which a positional renaming with consecutive ids would suffice.
      - Trees are still replayed in Python per point (the slice's architecture).
      - Compiled models do not depend on the process history (package model-determinism, 2026-10-25; slice-status DECISIONS ~16:45 item 3). Cause: the same id-ordered normal forms reached the model compiler's records. A compile of loop_qcd_qed_sm_Gmu after a tree evaluation wrote V_35's colour as `f(c1,c2,c3)`, a cold one `f(c2,c3,c1)`, with the index bindings, LC transition/closure proof digests and oriented-kernel colour expressions derived from it; the kernels' Horner forms (`collect_factors`, `collect_horner`) followed the symbol order; Spenso contracts a tensor network in an order that follows the ids, so scalar_gravity's three-graviton kernels came out in other (equal) nestings when the index symbols had been created in another order; and ufo_model_loader evaluated internal parameters and couplings in binary64 in the history's order (loop_sm's `yb` one ulp apart). Mechanism (`_internal/physics/canonical_order`, the general form of `assemble._canonical_stage`'s pool): `CanonicalRenaming` renames an expression's symbols, in the natural order of their canonical names, onto canonical symbols from 2^16-aligned blocks (a second block of Spenso tensor heads, tagged `spenso::tensor`, shares the renaming's index k, so the two never tie); `canonical_plain` prints colour and Lorentz records (`models/tensors.py`), `canonical_apply` runs the kernel pipeline (`compiler_kernels._canonicalize_oriented_kernel_component`), `compiler_kernels._execute_dense_network` runs every model-compiler network (oriented kernels, contact partials and finals, contact trees direct and staged, custom propagators; their tensors collected in `DenseTensors`) on canonical symbols, and `loading._canonical_loader_values` re-evaluates the loader's internal parameters and couplings with its own function on canonical symbols. The compiled-model payload (`CompiledModel.to_dict`: cache file, an artifact's compiled-model member and model digest, the prepared bundle's compiled-model digest) no longer carries the wall-clock `conversion_seconds` and `phase_timings`; the compile that measured them keeps them on its object, a loaded model has none. Evidence (`workspace/tmp/model-determinism/`): loop_sm, loop_qcd_qed_sm, loop_qcd_qed_sm_Gmu, MG5 sm, the bundled sm, scalars and scalar_gravity UFOs, the massive-muon Gmu variant and the built-in SM, each compiled cold and after ten histories (a second cold process, 100 and 65,300 unrelated symbols, a tree evaluation, a v2 loop generation, index symbols created in reverse, another model's compile plus 65,300 symbols plus reversed indices, a compile into another cache in the same process, 1 and 32 threads): byte-identical cache files in all 90 comparisons; g g > g g and u u~ > d d~ g (LC and FC) cold in three histories, with 16 threads and warm: identical plans, native members (but `parameters.symjit`, SymJIT's random tag) and cache files, bitwise-equal FC values, all equal to the base `18ed6674`'s plans, members and values; S1/S4/S5/S8 of g g > t t~, u u~ > d d~ g, g u > w+ d and g g > g g unchanged. Bytes that moved against the base, once: the colour and Lorentz record strings and what derives from them, 9 Z f f̄ kernels' Horner forms, and binary64 parameter/coupling values that the base rounded in its history's order (loop_sm 3 values, 1 ulp; loop_qcd_qed_sm_Gmu 76 values, up to 8 ulp; EW values move in their last bits accordingly). Cost: about 0.45 s and 170 MB (Symbolica symbol table) once per process for the two blocks; a cold loop_sm compile 3.1 → 4.2 s, u u~ > d d~ g generation with its model and CT compiles 25.0 → 29.3 s (both pinned, cold cache). Tests: `tests/unit/test_model_determinism.py` (two fresh interpreters compile the bundled sm UFO cold and after a perturbing history: byte-identical cache files; a three-graviton network summand whose Spenso network differs with the index creation order while `_execute_dense_network`'s does not; the renaming's round trip; misaligned blocks refused). Open: `loops/v2/catalogue.py` runs its loop-kernel networks on the original symbols (spin-2 loop species only would show it); `assemble` keeps its own pool (the shared one would save a block per generation process; assemble's owner); attributed heads other than tensor heads keep their ids (none in current models); prepared model bundles (`.pyamplicol-model`) were not measured.

  - *Goal:* §12.

  - *Size:* M (7).
