    - done: v2 generation (the `[loop] engine = "legacy" | "v2"` switch of this package was removed with the legacy engine, §13.3) (`loop_service._generate_v2`) publishes each process as plan v4 (§12.1). Post-build validation loads every staged process and flavour member through the public runtime and compares the inclusive and every declared colour history with the Python reference executor, in binary64 at `generation.validation.relative_tolerance`; a point whose binary64 pair misses it is checked again, alone, against the reference at `DOUBLE_DOUBLE_DIGITS` digits in both carriers (`loop_service._revalidate_v2_point`; package onshell-consistency, 2026-10-26): native double-double at the same tolerance, the point's binary64 Borns at the tolerance and its binary64 virtuals at max(tolerance, E), E the point's binary64 estimate from the recommended checks (one generic exact rotation and the rescaled reduction check, §10.4; one rotation about z and the cached check before package mq-stab), refused when absent or above 1e-9 (the user's O1 rule of 2026-10-23); an accepted point is logged (`pyamplicol.generation` warning) with its deviations and estimates. A binary64 virtual off by more than the tolerance thus passes only where binary64 estimates it cannot resolve the tolerance, and a binary64-only defect (SymJIT plane applications, the fast engine) is refused as before (`test_loop_generation_service.py`: 1e-6 and x2 injections, beyond E, at a well-conditioned point, without an admissible estimate). The binary64 pair is two routes rounded their own way: at the d d~ > g g NLC validation point the casimir virtual's estimate is 1.5e-11 (the reduction direction alone 6.6e-12, and only 3.1e-13 for T12, whose error is 1.0e-12: hence the rotation), the native value is 2.7e-12 of its sector off the 32-digit value and the pair 1.3e-12 apart, while double-double and 40 digits agree to 1.4e-27. Cost per re-checked point: one double-double and one checked binary64 evaluation, and the reference at 32 digits, which dominates: 7.8 s against 0.7 s in binary64 at d d~ > g g NLC (five outputs), 60 s against 6.9 s at u u~ > g g g NLC (four outputs; native double-double 9 s, checked binary64 1.7 s). Validation is opt-in; at 2->5 the binary64 reference is not measured, and binary64 errors there reach the 1e-9 cap (the onshell-consistency requirements audit reads 2.9e-10 to 5.6e-9 maximal errors from the G1 2->5 records), so a 2->5 validation needs a `relative_tolerance` that binary64 resolves (the cap is max(tolerance, 1e-9)). `Runtime.load` dispatches v4 to `V2VirtualRuntime` (`loops/v2/artifact.py`): `evaluate` natively in binary64, double-double and MPFR; `poles="none"` (`PoleSelection`; finite only, poles `None`, the same artifact; `"uv"`/`"ir"` added by WP2.10); `stability_checks` (`VirtualStabilityChecks`: rotation count, exact rotations or text, reduction direction, rescaled) with one `VirtualStabilityEstimate` per point (`None` without checks); `on_failure="return"` keeps a failed point as `None` with its reason (`FailedPoints`). Correlated (`evaluate_correlated(_many)`: declared colour histories, spin classes, joint), resolved (`evaluate_resolved`: per Born helicity row, per Born basis element at LC, one contracted component at NLC/FC as documented; per-entry estimates) and flavour-member calls are delegated to `runtime.virtual.V2ObservablesRuntime` over the regenerated `ObservableSlice`, with the same options. Generation takes `correlators` (resolved catalogue stored in the request) and `process.flavor_blind` (identity-port members proved by `admit_member` on the representative, `process-groups.json`, re-proved at load). `Runtime.evaluate_resolved` and `evaluate_correlated(_many)` forward `poles` and `stability_checks`; `ResolvedVirtualEvaluation` has `poles` and per-entry `stability`. Native admission `load_loop_v2_artifact` (§12.1); `pyamplicol inspect` summarizes v2 loop artifacts (engine, orders, sectors, families, buckets, degrees, observables, plan digest, point inputs). TOML `evaluation.poles = "all" | "none" | "uv" | "ir"` (WP2.10), `[evaluation.stability_checks]`; CLI `--poles {all,none,uv,ir}`, `--stability-rotation(s)`, `--stability-reduction-direction`, `--stability-rescaled` (§12.2).
    - acceptance: through the public generator and runtime, the inclusive virtual of gg→gg, uu~→dd~g, uu~→tt̄ (open top lines through the loop), uu~→dd~ and uu~→uu~ (identical-flavour signs) at full colour equals legacy O1 within 10⁻¹² (binary64, Born and virtual), 10⁻²⁴ (double-double) and 10⁻⁴⁰ (60 digits, nightly: HANDOFF §0.3, binary64 and DD in the default tier); gg→gg and uu~→tt̄ at LC and uu~→dd~g at NLC within 10⁻¹² and 10⁻²⁴; uu~→uu~g and uu~→ggg at full colour nightly (binary64 under the user's rule below, double-double 10⁻²⁴). Every legacy directed history, joint complex spin source and helicity-resolved row of g g > g g (FC, LC) and d d~ > g g with s s~ > g g as a member (one artifact process) equals the frozen legacy observables in binary64 (virtuals 1e-12, or the value's own estimate E ≤ 1e-9 where E > 1e-12, the user's rule of 2026-10-23; Borns 1e-12) and in double-double (1e-24); every other record (NLC, LC/NLC members, uu~→ggg FC/LC/NLC and spin) nightly, and the full-colour ones (g g > g g, d d~ > g g with its member, uu~→ggg directed and spin) also in 60 digits (1e-40, nightly; HANDOFF §0.3). Non-default parameters: every other public-path test runs at loop_sm's defaults (aS 0.118, MT 173, the fixtures' values), where a runtime ignoring its parameters would pass; at aS 0.13 and MT 150, g g > g g (inclusive and the T12 colour correlation, one point) equals a legacy artifact at the same parameters evaluated in double-double (binary64 under the user's rule, double-double 1e-24), parameters passed at load and through `set_model_parameters` give bitwise-identical binary64 values, the resolved rows sum to the inclusive value and the Born scales as aS² (`test_public_v2_runtime_at_non_default_model_parameters`, 2.2 min alone; one history, since the legacy generation with all of the record's correlators takes 4.5 min against 55 s with T12 and 23 s inclusive). The round-3 evidence audit's probe on the branch measured, against legacy at 40 digits, 5.3e-15 (binary64) and 1.9e-30 (double-double) for the inclusive value, the Casimir, T12, T34 and spin classes 3 and 3,4. Finite-only correlated and resolved from the same artifact; estimates bound the true errors; no host path, user or host name in any artifact file, a relocated copy loads in a fresh interpreter with bitwise-identical binary64 values, and so does a load after 141 unrelated Symbolica symbols; gg→gggg publishes < 20 MB (`tests/unit/test_loop_v2_artifact.py`; uu~→tt̄ also in `test_loop_v2_executor.py`).
    - coupling orders: the service reads the Born amplitude orders from the Born DAG before any loop generation (`artifact.born_amplitude_orders`, compiled as `build_tree_blocks` compiles the slice's Born) and derives one exact loop order per Born amplitude order and correction (`loop_service.v2_loop_orders`), passed to the slice as a sequence; a correction the source model does not expand perturbatively is refused as the legacy graph generator refuses it ("requested corrections require declared NLO model support"; loop_sm declares QCD only — the slice would otherwise admit no QED loop species and return a vanishing QED correction). The earlier derivation (the Born *limits* raised by two) produced {QCD:4, QED:2} for u u~ → d d~ with limits {QCD:2, QED:2}, the loop amplitude of no Born order, and failed in the catalogue with a misleading reason; it is gone, and so is the refusal of several Born amplitude orders after generation. Evidence (public path, `test_loop_v2_artifact.py`): mixed Born orders, u u~ → d d~ with QCD and photon Born amplitudes (Z, W and Higgs excluded through the slice request, as the slice tests do, since the public syntax has no exclusion), equal the MadLoop split-order control `mixed-uudd-qcd-control` per sector (QCD⁶, QCD⁴QED², QCD²QED⁴) and Born order, in binary64 after regeneration at load (measured ≤ 6.9e-15 of each sector's largest coefficient, 6.94e-15 again under the slice's kernel order after the merge of `42c6117d`, at the third point in the finite part of QCD⁴QED²; 4.4e-15 in QCD⁶; every Born order within ≈ 1e-15 of its own value, the colour-forbidden QCD²QED² Born exactly 0; test bounds 1e-12 of each Born order's own value, of the point's largest Born for the structural zero, and of each sector's largest coefficient, the reference being MadLoop binary64); in the SM (photon, Z and W exchange) it generates since WP3.2 EWI-2 (the W read through its conjugate rule) and equals MadLoop's full-SM control with the Wolfenstein CKM (`test_loop_v2_ew_lines.py`). Joint corrections with a QED-declaring model, MG5_aMC's loop_qcd_qed_sm (with `[loop.preparation] compatibility = "gmu_particle_anti"`, which v2 now forwards to the model preparation as `SliceRequest.compatibility`, as the legacy engine does; without it both engines refuse "antiparticle metadata mismatch for G+: goldstoneboson", reported by the dry run too): g g → g g with QCD+QED corrections equals legacy in binary64 (1e-12) and at 60 digits (1e-40; nightly test, 2.7 min), and with QCD corrections alone at 40 digits (probe); the QCD⁴QED² sector is a structural zero (no one-loop g g → g g amplitude of order QCD²QED²) that v2 lists and legacy omits, and u u~ → d d~ with QCD+QED stops in counterterm admission ("divergent witnesses without a counterterm rule or certified zero": QCD¹QED² vertex witnesses with a photon in the loop, WP3.2/WP3.5). Before this round the public v2 path could not serve loop_qcd_qed_sm at all: its MU_R sits at LHA code (LOOP, 666) and the slice looks for (LOOP, 1); `_v2_scale_parameter` now resolves it (else refuses with the reason).
    - widths: v2 virtuals are real on-shell (loop propagators carry no width; the model counterterms renormalise the real mass). A coloured particle that a tree current propagates with a width other than a compile-time or fixed zero (the t-channel top of g g → t t̄ at loop_sm's WT = 1.4915) mixed two schemes: the Born moved by 1.5e-6–3.7e-6 relative while the single pole over the Born moved by up to 2.7e-3 and the finite part by 2.8 % (the colour-correlated Borns should move at O(Γ²) only), against a legacy engine that ignores every width. Such a request is now refused, by the dry run and before any loop generation on the Born DAG, and after generation on the loop-side tree currents (`artifact.require_zero_coloured_widths`), naming the fix: fix the width to zero in `[loop.preparation] fixed_parameters` (the legacy convention). A resonant coloured particle needs the complex-mass scheme (WP3.2). A width that only loop propagators would carry (u u~ → t t̄) does not enter. The runtime refuses unknown parameter names, the scale parameter (`mu_r2` sets it) and a value other than the one fixed at generation (before, `WT = 1.4915` passed to an artifact generated with WT fixed to zero was silently replaced); other known parameters are accepted even where the process does not use them, as by the legacy engine (e.g. WT or WZ for u u~ → t t̄: bitwise-identical results). Test (`test_coloured_widths_in_tree_currents_are_fixed_to_zero`): g g → t t̄ refused at the default WT by the dry run and the generation; with WT fixed to zero the public v2 virtual equals the legacy engine evaluated at the model's default parameters (WT = 1.4915) at the three exact points and scales of the gg→tt̄ provider reference, in binary64 (1e-12) and double-double (1e-24) against legacy in double-double, and at 60 digits (1e-40) nightly (`test_coloured_width_fixed_to_zero_equals_legacy_at_sixty_digits`); runtime WT ≠ 0, MU_R and unknown names are refused. Currents of all legs but one carry an external momentum and are amputated, so they do not count (u u~ → t t̄ is admitted at the default WT and equals legacy there, as the audit measured). Integrator: WP2.2 (`work/v2-ct-recursion`, adopted at 09d70e76) selects full tree kernels for coloured widths and model-supplied propagators by its own criterion (`blocks.ward_breaking_fields`, which does not exist on this branch or the slice); keep one criterion when both are integrated: the public path fixes coloured widths to zero (this refusal), and the full-kernel branch remains for custom propagators. Make DESIGN WP2.2/WP2.6 and `docs/user/configuration.md` agree.

The photon-only forms the slice tests use are requested publicly with `[generation] particle_veto` (ct-simplify, §6.2; e.g. `["z"]` for Drell–Yan through the photon); the process syntax itself has no exclusion ("u u~ > e- e+ / z" is refused by the parser).
    - generation cache: a generation run retains every process it generated until its staged validation has loaded them (`artifact.retained`), so validation reuses them instead of regenerating the processes beyond the cache's four entries.
    - counterterms: until WP2.2 (`work/v2-ct-recursion`, `SliceRequest.counterterms = "recursion"` by default there) is integrated, the public v2 path takes its counterterms from the slice's site closure, which the user rules reserve for test oracles in production paths; the integrator of WP2.2 makes the public path use the recursion (its default; the request record stores every `SliceRequest` field, so the field is recorded and restored without an edit) and adds a public-path assertion that a published v4 artifact records `request["counterterms"] == "recursion"` (`test_public_generation_publishes_plan_v4`). `git merge-tree` of this branch (after its merge of the slice `42c6117d`) with `work/v2-ct-recursion` (`78a7c6ef`, which does not contain `42c6117d` yet) conflicts in `loops/v2/assemble.py` only (the known observables × ct-recursion conflict, WP2.5 open list; this branch also adds `SliceRequest.compatibility` next to `colour_contraction` and passes it to `prepare_loop_request_model`). Integration condition, whichever of the two branches lands second: (1) `loop_service` builds its `SliceRequest` without a `counterterms` argument, so the public path takes the recursion as soon as `SliceRequest.counterterms` defaults to it; nothing else in this package names the CT form; (2) `test_public_generation_publishes_plan_v4` asserts `execution["request"]["counterterms"] == "recursion"`; (3) the configuration.md sentence on site closure ("a development stage") is removed; (4) the observables × ct-recursion source threading of the WP2.5 open list (spin and Ward sources into `CountertermProgram.point`) is done and checked on the public correlated path (the spin records of `test_public_correlated_and_resolved_calls_equal_legacy_o1`); (5) artifacts written before carry no `counterterms` field and are refused at load with "regenerate the artifact" (`request_from_record`; no backward compatibility).
    - precision label (WP2.3/WP3.1 owner, recorded here): in double-double and arbitrary-precision calls the Born comes from the Python tree replay at `Precision.working_digits`, above the carrier, while `VirtualLaneDiagnostic.precision_bits` reports the carrier of the virtual (106 bits for double-double). Measured on the g g → g g correlated record: Born within 4.1e-51 of legacy at 200 digits, virtual 8.1e-31. Nothing is numerically wrong; WP3.1's native point inputs evaluate the Born in the carrier and make the label exact.
    - known oracle limitation (legacy engine, unchanged by this package): a legacy generation of g g > g g with the gg_gg correlators (Casimir, T12, T34; spin classes 3 and 3,4) fails its own post-build validation at the default `[generation.validation]` tolerances (relative 1e-12, samples 1): "saved virtual execution differs from source execution for 'gg/casimir', virtual coefficient 0 (absolute difference 2.660e-10)" (`loop_service._compare_validation_result`). The legacy observables capture (`tools/loop_reference/legacy_observables_capture.py`) validates with relative 3e-9 / absolute 2e-12, and the non-default-parameter test generates its legacy oracle without post-build validation and evaluates it in double-double. Legacy users of correlated outputs need the looser validation tolerances; the legacy engine is retired by WP3.6, so this is recorded, not fixed.
    - leanness (AGENTS.md): the writer no longer re-reads and re-hashes the staged plan member; a generated process serialises and hashes its plan once (`GeneratedProcess`, frozen, is what the generation cache keeps; `NativeExecutor(plan_json=...)` takes its plan JSON at load and at `clear()`), and a regenerating load digests the compiled model once (`generate_process(..., model=...)`). The v4 admission, flate2 and `read_loop_program_v4_json` are behind rusticol-core's `loop-v2` feature; the plan-v4 runtime capability itself is defined in the ungated `engine.rs`, so rusticol-core alone compiles in every feature configuration (`just rust-test`'s `--no-default-features --features f64-compiled` included). Still parsed twice per load: the admission validates the stored plan against the declarations, and the native executor parses the regenerated plan (digest-equal) for each carrier it builds. The second parse disappears with WP3.1, when the admitted program is the executed one (no regeneration).
    - integration of WP2.2 with this branch (`loops/v2/assemble.py`): the request model preparation is `assemble.prepare_request_model` (one function, called by `LoopSlice.generate` and by the dry run); ct-recursion's `LoopSlice.generate` uses the locals `fixed` and `declaration`, which become `model.fixed_parameters` (bound as `_fixed` here) and `request.colour_declaration`.
    - interim (§12.1): loading a v4 process regenerates its slice (≈ generation time: gg→gg 9 s; reused within one interpreter) until WP3.1 stores native point inputs; the SDK evaluates v4 only after WP3.1.
    - proposed for WP3.1 (WP2.6 proposes, the coordinator confirms; WP3.1's goal and acceptance are not edited here): *Goal* add "native programs for the per-point inputs of a v4 process (tree recurrence in loop-request mode, Born components, counterterm scalars) stored as further PACBIN members under a new `point_inputs` value, their SymJIT applications as IR recompiled at load (storage-v4 plane kernels), and the SDK (Rust/C/C++/Fortran) evaluating v4 artifacts"; *Accept* add "a v4 artifact loads without regenerating its slice and gives bitwise-identical binary64 values after relocation with the same native build; SDK smoke tests on the v2 controls equal the public runtime".
    - superseded (user ruling 2026-10-28 ~16:10, M-QCD Q3: the legacy engine was removed instead, MQ-LEGACY-REMOVE, §13.3); the former default-switch criteria: (1) R3 passed for QCD (required set against ≥ 2 providers, O9 per point, rescue rates reported, MASTER_PLAN §5.8 speed gates against production-mode providers); (2) every public capability the default serves today runs through v2 or is refused with its reason (EW and mixed orders via WP3.2; aliases); (3) native point inputs (WP3.1), so loading needs no regeneration and the SDK evaluates v4; (4) the whole unit suite green with `engine = "v2"` as default, legacy-only tests pinned to `engine = "legacy"`.

  - *Goal:* fix the tree-lane electroweak defects that the MadGraph split-order checks pinned as strict xfails, and the recurrence refusals that remain:

    - done: the Python Born (eager lane, the loop engine's Born) built four-point colour-singlet contact vertices joining four quark lines once per sink-dependent orientation (pure-EW uu~→dd~ss~cc~ 1.48× MadGraph) — fixed in the final merge of the contact vertex (tree-ew-followup; uu~→dd~ss~cc~ matches MadGraph pure EW, at QCD≤2 and at all orders at FC and LC, also relabelled, and NLC pure EW; uu~→dd~dd~ss~, dd~→uu~ss~cc~ and uc~→ds~dd~ match pure EW at FC and LC; five W-paired lines with a massive b are labelling-invariant at FC, NLC and LC);
    - done (fails closed in the eager lane): a chiral vertex that takes a fermion line between massive and massless fermions, the massive one internal or external, can bring a Dirac (chirality-0) current to the line's massless, Weyl (chirality ±1) sink, which no closure contraction joins; the eager lane dropped those diagrams without an error (§3.3, at on-shell points: ν_τν̄_τ→W⁺W⁻ 982–3030× MadGraph; with a massless b, bb~→W⁺W⁻ 314–671×, tt~→bb~ 1.15–1.75×, e⁺e⁻→bb~W⁺W⁻ 0.24–0.74×; five W-paired lines relabelling-dependent) — it now raises (tree-ew-followup, `generation/dag_ordering.py`);
    - done (fails closed in the recurrence lane, m-qcd MQ-5F): the Rust recurrence dropped the diagrams of the massive fermion's exchange without an error — ν_τν̄_τ→W⁺W⁻, W⁺W⁻Z and W⁺W⁻γ in the default SM; with a massless b (`sm-no_b_mass`, the providers' `loop_sm-no_b_mass`), bb~→W⁺W⁻, W⁺W⁻Z, W⁺W⁻γ and W⁺W⁻g, tt~→bb~ and e⁺e⁻→bb~W⁺W⁻ (§3.3) — it now refuses them (`dirac-weyl-closure`, `construct.rs` `DiracWeylClosureGuard`; the strict xfails are refusal tests); the loop engine admits the class (§3.3, "Loop engine");
    - open: the Dirac–Weyl closure itself — a closure contraction of a chirality-0 with a chirality ±1 current (or a Weyl current where a chiral vertex projects the line back) in the model's contraction records and both lanes. Without it, with a massless b, no lane builds tW (gb→tW⁻, bg→tW⁻, gb→W⁻t, bg→W⁻t: the eager lane raises the Dirac–Weyl error, the recurrence finds no closure root), the eager lane builds t-channel single top only as bu→dt or bu→td (it refuses ub→dt and ub→td), and the recurrence refuses all four orderings;
    - open: colour-singlet contacts with more than four legs that join three or more quark lines fail closed at NLC and full colour (`ColorEngine._require_four_point_singlet_contact`), and four-point contact trees (`ufo-contact-tree`) take the four-point rule without a reference; the SM has neither, but generic BSM models (SMEFT contacts) can, so both need a test model with such a vertex and a reference;
    - open: the recurrence fails closed (generation error) on colour flows without roots at the requested orders, which it should take as zero amplitudes — uu~→dd~ss~, ud→udss~, ud~→ud~ss~ at QCD=0, uu~→dd~ss~cc~ at QCD=0 and QCD≤2, uc~→ds~dd~ at all orders, and single top bu→td, ub→dt and ud~→tb~ at all orders with a massive b (default SM) — and its full-colour helicity selector refuses uu~→dd~ss~ and ud→udss~ at QCD≤2 and uu~→dd~ss~cc~ at QCD≤4; the eager lane builds and matches all of them.

**Wave 3: required set**

- **WP3.1 Generation at scale.**
  - *Goal:* loop-request mode in the Rust recurrence builder (template catalogue: all-subset blocks with loop-aware reachability, CT = 1); Rust family, trie and M-state generator.
  - *Owns:* `generation/recurrence_projection.py`, `models/recurrence_template.py`, the loop mode of `recurrence/construct.rs`, `loop_v2/generate.rs`.
  - *Depends:* WP2.1, WP2.2.
  - *Accept:* gg→ggggg FC generation < 10 min and < 8 GB; plans equal the Python generator's on the 5–6-leg controls.
  - *Size:* L (14).
  - *Plan:* `workspace/reports/engine-v2/wp31/PLAN.md` (increments I0–I13; its §10 records the deviations).
  - *Status (branch `work/v2-generation-scale`, first increment):*
    - **Native families and schedule-B tries** (I8/I9): `rusticol-core` `loop_v2::generate::{families, schedules}` (feature `loop-v2`), binding `V2Generation`, inputs and decoders `loops/v2/generator_input.py`. The inputs are Θ(model) tables plus the field-level block table, with every name a rank in a sorted table, so id order is Python's object order; colour states come from the Python `ColourModel` once per distinct position. `LoopSlice.generate` runs both stages natively (`generator_input.NATIVE_STAGES`), the LC/NLC prune builds only each family's stored frame. The decoded objects equal the Python stages' (same objects, same order: `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_generate.py`) on the control model (five processes, two colour models, the prune pass), on loop_sm slices (gg→gg FC/LC/NLC, uu~→dd~g, uu~→uu~g), and at 6–7 legs (gg→gggg, uu~→ggggg, gg→ggggg FC). One thread: gg→ggggg families 0.09 s (+0.4 s decode; Python 3.2 s; 9,414 families, 52,682 routings, 109,179 prefix states); schedules gg→gggg 1.5 s (Python 17.4 s), uu~→ggggg 6.5 s (Python 60.9 s), gg→ggggg 31–33 s, of it 17.5 s native (Python 357 s; 810,495 leaves, 197,539 nodes, 44,926 contents).
    - **Loop-aware reachability** (§3.2 item 1, I1a): the tree program builds only the block currents of the field-level families' admitted positions (`ModelBlockTable` honours ignored particles); where the pruned table's families differ from the field-level ones (photon processes: the closed-loop flavour classes read every field of the table) every block is built. The runtime schema gains `propagated_currents`: a pruned DAG has requested blocks no tree interaction consumes, and the loop engine reads every block with its propagator. Families, schedules, seeds, the plan and the complete virtual are unchanged on gg→gg, uu~→dd~g, uu~→ggg, Drell-Yan, gg→tt̄, uu~→uu~g (stage digests, `loops/v2/snapshot.py`); gg→ggggg trees 169 s and 2.5 GB with the reuse fast path below (1,088 s and 11.2 GB unpruned, plan §2.2).
    - **gg→ggggg FC** (§4.4 table): generation 7.4 min and 4.2 GB peak (the WP3.1 gate), plan export 6.0 min with a 949 MB JSON and a 9.3 GB peak (not yet: I11). Before this increment the stages to the seed table alone took 13.8 min and the export about 19 min [E] (plan §2.2, §2.6).
    - **Output-identical Python fixes** (I1): TreeRows and the slice's counterterm site terms are built on first use (190 s of the gg→ggggg generation; the full-colour scalar plan reads only whole-rule terms); the exporter indexes the admitted seed entries once (uu~→gggg export 67.8 s → 7.4 s, plan JSON byte-identical); certified seed combinations are memoised (gg→gggg seeds 14.2 → 12.0 s); the tree engine's evaluation-reuse pass takes the identity classes when no two currents share an evaluation contract (the loop blocks and Borns of QCD processes; DAGs identical, gg→gggg unpruned trees 56.6 → 33.6 s). In the per-point Python inputs each Born vector is projected once per helicity onto the colour basis keys, so an interference costs one pass over its ket instead of a B × B pair loop (uu~→gggg 79 → 41 s per point profiled).
    - **Two-level seeds in plan v4** (part of I11): the plan stores the projected K[c, b] once per (seed class, loop orders) and per seed key its combination of classes, and the executor forms U0_c = Σ_b K conj(A0) per class, then U0 = Σ_c x_c U0_c per key. Before, one dense vector per key: at gg→ggggg FC its exact products dominated the 44 min export and the 1.53 GB JSON. gg→gggg seed records 7.3 → 0.64 MB, export 44.6 → 25.7 s.
    - **Stage digests** (I0, lean): S1/S4/S5/S8 and the complete virtual (L3) with a hash-seed independent encoding; `tools/loop_v2_generate.py` `measure` (time and peak memory per stage), `diff`, `validate` (native evaluation against `tests/fixtures/loop/required`).
    - **2→4 and 2→5 against providers** (binary64, per-point trees, Born and counterterm inputs still Python; Catani's check skipped, its exact dipole matrices cost hours at 7 legs; `tools/loop_v2_generate.py validate`): gg→gggg against Recola and OpenLoops ≤ 9×10⁻¹² at points 1 and 3, point 2 unstable in binary64 (8.4×10⁻⁹, within the fixture's 2×10⁻⁸ and flagged by the stability estimate at 3×10⁻⁶) and 7×10⁻¹¹ in DoubleFloat; **uu~→ggggg against MadLoop ≤ 2.6×10⁻¹² and Recola ≤ 6.1×10⁻¹⁰ at all three points** (the providers agree to 6.2×10⁻¹⁰), all three Laurent coefficients. The fixture `uux_ggggg.json` is the existing MadLoop + Recola reference. uu~→ggggg generation 6.4 min and plan export 5.9 min (245 MB JSON), peak 4.3 GB; 23–26 min per point, where the Python counterterm site terms dominate (MadLoop 558 s, Recola 182 s per point; not a speed comparison: the native per-point inputs are open).
  - *Status (second increment):*
    - **Native per-point trees and Born** (I3/I4 part; §5.5): `NativeExecutor(inputs="native")` forms block rows, Born components and |A0|² natively from the slice's tree stage program and Born root closure, and evaluates the bare virtual without Python per point. The lane reuses the Python block DAG and builds one Symbolica program per stage (gg→ggggg: 2.7 min, 18 GB peak), a stopgap until the loop-request trees (I2/I3).
    - **Derived row links** (I11 part): the plan carries the Born rows as a product of per-leg helicities, per content its legs and row helicities and per step its open legs; the loader derives every schedule-B step's rows and row links (`loop_v2::plan::links`; `export_plan(links=True)` still writes them and the loader checks its derivation). gg→gggg export 14.1 → 6.3 s and 40.8 → 16.1 MB; gg→ggggg export 5.4 → 2.0 min, JSON 949 → 185 MB, peak 9.3 → 5.8 GB.
    - **Colour metrics per relabelling orbit** (I5c part): the Born metric and the IR dipole matrices contract each orbit of the external adjoint labels once (identical matrices). Born metric gg→gggg 8.6 → 0.16 s; dipoles gg→ggg 27.9 → 1.3 s, uu~→gggg 62.8 → 5.9 s, gg→ggggg 5.6 min (the direct form costs hours).
    - **Native colour overlaps** (I10's overlap engine): `loop_v2::generate::cycles` counts each literal overlap, a product of adjoint traces, as the sum over the labels taking the U(1) term of their Fierz identity of ±N_c to its index loops; identical to `color/contraction_trace.py` on 20,000 random products and identical seed tables, plans, metrics and dipoles on seven controls. Seeds uu~→ggggg 4.2 → 2.4 min; with the hash-once objects gg→ggggg seeds 3.3 → 1.6 min.
    - **gg→ggggg FC: generation and plan 7.9 min, 5.8 GB** (§4.4), inside the former 10 min / 8 GB target (ADDENDUM H now allows O(hours) below 150 GB).
    - **Seven legs validated**: uu~→ggggg with native trees and Born against MadLoop and Recola, Born ≤ 1.6×10⁻¹⁴ / 1.3×10⁻¹³ and double pole ≤ 7.0×10⁻¹³ / 4.4×10⁻¹¹ at all three points (6–10 s per point with the rotation and reduction-direction checks, estimates 4–7×10⁻¹¹; finite parts and single poles need the native counterterms). gg→ggggg (no provider reference, ADDENDUM F): the bare double pole over the Born equals the universal −(α_s/2π)·7·C_A within 5.4×10⁻¹² at five points (rotation estimates 1.6×10⁻¹⁰ … 2.9×10⁻⁶). One gg→ggggg point with Python inputs and counterterms (3.0 h, 18–27 GB): the complete virtual and its rotation agree to 5.8×10⁻⁹ (finite), 2.6×10⁻⁹ (single pole) and 2.5×10⁻¹³ (double pole), and its single pole equals Catani's prediction (orbit dipoles over the native Born amplitudes) within 1.2×10⁻⁹, its double pole within 1.0×10⁻¹².
    - **Massive fermion loop lines natively** (bug fix): the tree reading evaluates a segment whose loop line leaves as a massive fermion with the opposite mass sign (`conventions.TreeReading.mass_signs`); the Python executor applied it per content, plan v4 valued every kernel constant once per batch without it. Kernel records and constants are now keyed by the content's mass flips (`plan_export._Exporter._kernel`). Before, gg→tt̄ family numerators differed by up to 2.0 of their scale and the native virtual missed the providers by 6–60 % (closed massive loops with vector couplings are even in the mass, so gg→gggg, uu~→ggggg and gg→ggggg were unaffected). After: gg→tt̄ equals MadLoop, Recola and OpenLoops at all three points, and **gg→tt̄gg** too (Python inputs, binary64: MadLoop ≤ 2.1×10⁻¹¹, OpenLoops ≤ 2.1×10⁻¹¹, Recola ≤ 1.0×10⁻¹⁰, all three Laurent coefficients; stability estimates 5×10⁻¹¹–5×10⁻¹⁰; about 45 s per point). `validate` passes every fixture parameter (gg→tt̄ needs WT = 0).
  - *Status (third increment):*
    - **Chiral loop lines (I-AX), no new sign rule.** The tree reading of a loop fermion line is its transposed Dirac chain: each vertex tensor contracted on swapped spinor slots, each propagator the antifermion propagator S(−k)ᵀ (`models/expressions.py`). The chain therefore equals the families' reading with S(−k) = −(k̸ − m) for S(k): −1 per fermion propagator and the flipped mass (`conventions.TreeReading.mass_signs`), whatever the vertex. The planned C-twist M′ = −C Mᵀ C⁻¹ (PLAN I-AX) was not needed and would have reversed every axial coupling. `LeafWeights` now refuses only vertices coupling through the mass of the line they emit (and colour-singlet lines, vertices with two fermion lines, as before). Oracles: Drell-Yan with the photon and the Z equals the universal massless form factor to 10⁻¹⁵; uu~→e⁻e⁺gg with the Z equals MadLoop's bare, UV, R2 and full components to ≈10⁻¹² at three points, and reversing the top's axial coupling inside its closed loops moves the finite part by 6×10⁻⁴ to 7×10⁻³ (the closed-loop axial sign is tested). Scalar and tensor couplings follow from the same identity; the scalar's first process oracle is e⁺e⁻→tt̄h against MadLoop (`top-pair-higgs-model.json`, WP3.2 EWI-1: the colour-singlet line at the root takes the tree engine's merge sign).
    - **Certified zeros of closed fermion loops** (§6.3, `ct_sites.closed_fermion_zeros`, part of WP3.5): Furry and traceless-colour certificates inside `generate_counterterm_sites`; the tests' hand-made Furry zeros are gone.
    - **Levi-Civita counterterm kernels**: the catalogue admits UFO `Epsilon` in counterterm sites (R2_GGZ, R2_GGGVa) as a dense tensor with ALOHA's components (ε^{0123} = −1, contravariant); its `ct-site` kernels equal compiled ALOHA routines to rounding (`test_loop_v2_levi_civita.py`, `tools/loop_reference/levi_civita_aloha.py`). The tree model compiler then still refused Epsilon; it lowers it since WP3.2 EWI-7b (the same tensor, in every vertex of every model: ordinary tree vertices with `Epsilon` take the same lowering but have no reference check yet), and the root closure of these vertices carries the reading sign of §6.1 "Root closure reading", which the kernel-level ALOHA comparison could not see (EW-20 found it).
    - **The three Z-inclusive 2→5 targets against MadLoop, Recola and OpenLoops** (Python inputs, binary64, all three Laurent coefficients, three points each): uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g ≤ 8.5×10⁻¹³, uu~→e⁻e⁺ggg ≤ 6.9×10⁻¹³, gg→tt̄e⁻e⁺g (QCD) ≤ 3.6×10⁻¹² at two points; at its third point the binary64 finite part is off by 5.7×10⁻⁹ (inside the fixture's 2×10⁻⁸, stability estimate 1.7×10⁻⁹) and DoubleFloat gives MadLoop to 1.9×10⁻¹⁴ (OpenLoops 5.8×10⁻¹³; Recola's 10⁻⁸ is its default accuracy). Generation 10.2–10.5 s, export 3.4–3.7 s, 0.6–7.8 s per point, peak 0.29–0.43 GB.
    - **Generation per process** (this build, one thread, shared host at load 50–80; `tools/loop_v2_generate.py measure --export`): uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g 9.5 s + 3.4 s export, 1.1 MB, 0.20 GB; uu~→e⁻e⁺ggg 9.9 + 3.7 s, 2.3 MB, 0.22 GB; gg→tt̄e⁻e⁺g 10.1 + 3.6 s, 2.0 MB, 0.20 GB (structure counts equal PLAN §2.4(d)'s cost-only rows); gg→tt̄gg 20.9 + 2.9 s, 5.0 MB, 0.29 GB; gg→gggg 26.7 + 5.8 s, 15 MB, 0.55 GB; uu~→ggggg 193 + 69 s, 70 MB, 2.4 GB; gg→ggggg 358 + 109 s, 185 MB, 5.8 GB (second increment). Recola generates the first three in 8–29 s and MadLoop in 56 s–2.7 min (MASTER_PLAN §0.1 table).
    - **Leading colour is not cheaper to generate.** gg→gggg LC 46.5 s (FC 26.7 s), gg→ggggg LC 12.9 min and 6.5 GB (FC 6.0 min): LC first builds the FC seed table, then `_prune_colour` computes the sector degrees P from every counterterm site term and every Born × counterterm colour overlap (6 legs: 17.6 s site terms, 16 s overlaps; 7 legs: 415 s in all) and rebuilds the tries; it keeps 647,775 of 810,495 skeletons at gg→ggggg. Exact relabelling-orbit reductions of the overlaps (as for the Born metric and dipoles) are the obvious lever.
    - **gg→6g (ADDENDUM H stretch) not attempted: estimate.** Scaling the measured 6→7-leg stage growth (trees ×18, schedules ×16, seeds ×18, LC prune ×24, export ×16, peak memory ×12) gives ≈ 4.5 h and ≈ 70 GB for LC generation [E], and the native per-point tree lane (T0, 18 GB at 7 legs) ≈ 270 GB [E] above the 150 GB budget. The blockers are the tree lane (I2/I3 loop-request trees) and the LC degree computation, then the Python tree DAG and seeds (I10).
  - *Status (fourth increment):*
    - **Top-free requests, NJet's scheme.** `SliceRequest.ignored_particle_ids` now also removes the particle's loop lines (`ModelLoopCatalogue(ignored_particle_ids=...)`: |PDG|, both orientations; the tree compiler gets both signs). Before, ignoring the top removed its decoupling counterterm and field insertion but kept the closed top loops, so the single pole exceeded massless N_f = 5 QCD by (α_s/4π)(4/3)B at every multiplicity. Top-free loop_sm (MB = 0) now equals NJet double-double at pyAmpliCol's exact points and three scales, all three Laurent coefficients (`njet_nf5_topfree.json`, `test_loop_v2_topfree.py`):
      - gg→gg ≤ 2.1×10⁻¹⁴ at 10 points;
      - gg→ggg ≤ 2.3×10⁻¹³ at the stable points; one ill-conditioned binary64 point is flagged by the rotation check and gives 3×10⁻¹⁴ in DoubleFloat;
      - gg→gggg 1.0×10⁻¹¹ at point 0 (estimate 1.4×10⁻¹⁰); point 1 is 1.6×10⁻⁷ in binary64 (estimate 2.7×10⁻⁶) and 4.7×10⁻¹⁵ in DoubleFloat.
      - gg→ggggg top-free, bare virtual on the native trees (binary64, three fixture points) against `gg_ggggg_njet.json`: Born ≤ 4.9×10⁻¹⁴ and double pole ≤ 5.4×10⁻¹². The single pole, minus the counterterm pole (α_s/4π)(n−2)β₀(5)B of a top-free request, agrees to 3.4×10⁻⁹ and 5.2×10⁻⁹ at points 0 and 1 (rotation estimates 1.4×10⁻⁸ and 5.0×10⁻⁸) and to 1.4×10⁻¹¹ at point 2.
      The gg→ggggg finite part against `gg_ggggg_njet.json` now needs only the native counterterms (I5).
    - **LC/NLC degrees per relabelling orbit** (`assemble._AdjointOrbits`). Identical external adjoint legs whose transpositions leave the Born key sets invariant (checked exactly) form classes. Two consequences:
      - the leading degree over the Born keys is constant on the orbit of the other tensor, so one relabelled representative is contracted;
      - a counterterm site's terms are those of a site in its orbit, relabelled, so one site per orbit is closed (also for the scalar plan's whole-rule parameters).
      The degrees equal the direct computation on 13 controls, and the S1/S4/S5/S8 digests are unchanged on eight LC/NLC controls. gg→gggg LC prune 18.3 → 4.9 s. gg→ggggg LC generation 772 → 412 s (prune 415 → 54 s), plan export 47 s, 107 MB, peak 4.7 GB: LC now costs about what FC costs (6.0 min).
    - **Born orders that the root closure misses are refused.** Loop-aware reachability (I1a) did not build the electroweak block currents of a mixed-order Born that no admitted loop position consumes. Example: uu~→dd~g without a QED bound has a QCD 1 QED 2 Born (γ, Z and W exchange). Its root closure then had no terms, and the interference sector (QCD 6, QED 2) was silently zero, also in `test_loop_v2_native_trees` (now QED = 0). Without reachability the closure refuses the W vertex (§3.3). Generation now raises when the Born program has roots at an order without closure terms.
    - **Native trees compiled in chunks** (`native_trees.CHUNK_OUTPUTS`). `TreeRows` builds its stage blueprint only on a Python replay. The native export compiles each stage of the runtime schema in chunks of whole output currents (≤ 2¹⁵ outputs) and saves and releases them one at a time. At gg→ggggg the export peak falls from 13.0 to 7.0 GB at equal time (390 s with generation's 6.4 min before it) and equal per-point cost (0.79 s of trees and Born, 12.2 s with the bare loop). The process peak falls only from 18.2 to 17.5 GB, because the native tree runtime now sets it: about 5.6 GB at load for 265 MB of saved evaluators, plus 5 GB during the evaluation. The runtime now keeps the saved stages and decodes, casts and drops one at a time per carrier, which lowers the peak to 16.3 GB. What remains is the binary64 stage evaluators (≈ 4.3 GB) and the evaluation (+5 GB). For 8 legs T0 therefore stays near 200 GB [E]; the interaction program (below) replaces it.
    - **Native trees as an interaction program** (`native_trees._interaction_program`, `loop_v2::trees`; the default `TREE_LANE`). The blocks DAG is evaluated interaction by interaction. In each runtime stage, every interaction adds its exact colour weight times its vertex kernel to its result current; each output slot then copies the sum or applies the propagator kernel. The kernels are the model's own component expressions (`vertex_component_expression` on `[left, right, p_left, p_right, model]`, `propagator_component_expression` on `[value, p, model]`), one saved exact evaluator per shape (gg→gggg: 4 vertex and 1 propagator kernel; u u~ > d d~ g: 25 and 10). They run batched over the interactions and points that share them, in every carrier, so the program grows with the DAG instead of with T0's per-output expressions.
      - Rows equal the Python inputs: `test_loop_v2_native_trees` (binary64, DoubleFloat, FC, LC, NLC) and a Python interpretation of the program on four processes (≤ 7.7×10⁻¹⁵).
      - gg→ggggg FC: export with trees 166 s instead of 390 s, a 72 MB table and no stage evaluators. Load peak 10.3 GB, process peak 15.0 GB (T0: 16.3 GB), 0.95 s of trees and Born per point.
      - gg→ggggg LC poles: export with trees 86 s instead of 305 s, peak 12.7 GB instead of 15.1 GB. The double pole equals the universal value within 1.2×10⁻¹¹. The values agree with T0's within the rotation estimates (1.9×10⁻¹³ at the stable point; 3.7×10⁻⁹ and 9.9×10⁻⁹ at the two points estimated at 1.1×10⁻⁸ and 2.7×10⁻⁸).
      - Quark lines at seven legs (`validate --inputs native`, Born and double pole against the providers at three points): uu~→ggggg against MadLoop's Born ≤ 1.6×10⁻¹⁴ and double pole ≤ 6.8×10⁻¹³, against Recola's ≤ 1.3×10⁻¹³ and ≤ 4.4×10⁻¹¹; export 81 s (T0: 219 s), 6–15 s per point with the checks, peak 5.1 GB. uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g against all three providers ≤ 1.0×10⁻¹³; export 4.8 s, 0.1–0.4 s per point.
      - The tree part of an 8-leg evaluation is no longer the memory wall. The remaining peak is the plan and the executor (+3.6 GB at load, +4.8 GB in the evaluation at 7 legs).
    - **Native Born at LC and NLC.** The tree program carries the Born metric over Born components (basis element, Born order, N_c power) per squared Born order. Each cell keeps the N_c powers that the projection keeps below the order's leading degree, as in the Python inputs' interference. `NativeExecutor` therefore returns the Born at every accuracy, equal to the Python inputs (g g > g g LC, u u~ > d d~ g NLC; `test_loop_v2_native_trees`).
    - **LC poles check.** `loop_v2_generate.py poles --accuracy lc`: at LC the double pole over the Born is −(α_s/2π) Σ_i C_i with the Casimirs' leading parts (C_F → N_c/2). At NLC this holds only with adjoint legs, and the tool refuses quark legs. gg→ggggg LC (native trees and Born): the double pole over the LC Born equals −(α_s/2π)·7·N_c within 1.2×10⁻¹¹ at three points. The rotation estimates are 4×10⁻¹¹ to 2.8×10⁻⁸, at 11–25 s per point with the check. Generation takes 6.9 min, the export with trees 5.1 min, and the peak is 15.1 GB.
    - **Ring seed combinations per label renaming** (`CompressedColour._ring_combination`). The combination of an adjoint ring or closed quark loop is computed once on its key with the external labels renamed in reading order. Each primitive is then renamed back and made canonical again, with its sign. Stage digests are unchanged (eight controls, gg→ggggg LC). Seeds: gg→gggg 6.2 → 4.9 s, gg→ggggg 102 → 94 s. The remaining seed cost is the per-skeleton exact arithmetic (I10).
    - **gg→6g LC generation attempted (ADDENDUM H), measured to the seed stage.** One thread, from a frozen worktree (`tools/loop_v2_generate.py measure "g g > g g g g g g" --born QCD=6 --accuracy lc --export`; per-stage records stream to stderr). Times per stage, with the growth over seven legs:
      - tree blocks: 66 min, 44 GB peak (×22);
      - native schedules: 409 s, 52 GB;
      - counterterm sites: 95 s;
      - readings: 23 s;
      - Born terms: 163 s.
      The seed table was running at 1 h 48 min (55 GB; 75 GB at 1 h 55 min in a first attempt) when the process received SIGTERM from outside its timeout. The first attempt stopped at the same stage when this session staged a new native build under it.
      Estimate for the rest [E]: seeds ≈ 30 min, LC prune ≈ 15 min, plan export ≈ 15 min, so generation and plan take ≈ 2.7 h with a peak of ≈ 75–90 GB. That is inside ADDENDUM H's budget.
      With the interaction program the native trees are no longer the evaluation's memory wall. The plan and executor memory at eight legs are not measured.
  - *Status (fifth increment):*
    - **Slice `45875b87` merged** (observables WP2.5, artifact-api WP2.6). Resolutions: `LoopSlice.degrees(overlaps=, table=)` keeps the relabelling-orbit reduction (overlap degrees and one counterterm site per orbit) for the inclusive call only, since a directed colour history is not invariant under the relabellings; the tree program bounds blocks by `SliceRequest.loop_order_bounds` (several exact loop orders); one mass-flip implementation (`reference_executor.mass_flips`, `signed_constants`); two-level seed records per observable column (`SeedColumn`: each column its own seed table on the slice's classes, pairs, degree and Born basis elements; the class records are shared by the seed keys); `NativeExecutor(plan_json=, inputs=, born_squared=)` with the explicit-FFT refusal, and `_evaluated` over `reduce`. Two merge defects were caught by a one-point poles smoke run before any long job (a property spliced out of `SliceRequest`, a kernel binding without its mass flips) and fixed. All 26 default-tier v2 test files pass after the merge (one process per file); one public test changed its expected refusal: the SM u u~ → d d~ request with QCD and electroweak Born amplitudes now fails closed at the W exchange of its root closure ("not closed under conjugation"), past the chiral Z that I-AX supports.
    - **I2 decision: the interaction program (T3) is the native tree lane.** The stage lane (T0) is removed (`TREE_LANE`, `CHUNK_OUTPUTS`, `_stream_stages`, `_chunked_stages`, the Rust stage records and their execution; tree-program schema 2). T0's per-stage evaluators grew with the currents' outputs (16.3 GB at gg→ggggg, ≈ 200 GB extrapolated to 8 legs); T3 holds a table of the DAG's interactions and a few saved kernel evaluators in every carrier. T2 (the Rust recurrence in loop-request mode, binary64 only) stays the possible binary64 fast lane; it is not needed for the 2→5 set.
    - **History-independent native trees.** The interaction kernels are compiled on canonical symbols (`assemble._canonical_stage`): with 300 unrelated Symbolica symbols created first, 7 of the 15 saved kernels of u u~ → g g g differed and the native rows changed in their last bits (measured); now program, kernels and binary64 rows are identical (`test_native_trees_do_not_depend_on_the_process_history`, three fresh interpreters).
    - **Native parameter binding of the native lane** (I4's binding, the SDK's prerequisite). Each tree program carries a parameter program: one saved exact Symbolica evaluator (canonical symbols) from the external model parameters (real, the tree runtime schema's externals) to every parameter-dependent value: the tree program's derived model parameters (the model's own definitions, as `TreeRows._model_values` evaluates them), the Born couplings per N_c grade (the slice's scalar plan coefficients) and the plan's kernel constants (`KernelConstants` keys with their mass flips; the bases a fractional power was distributed over are checked positive natively). Masses (external spinors, loop propagators) are the exact mass parameters. A native batch now carries only momenta, μ_R² per point and one list of external parameter values (`native_trees.batch_parameters`); `loop_v2::trees::TreeProgram::bind` derives the rest in the carrier, the scale parameter as √μ_R² of each point, and checks that the couplings and kernel constants do not vary across the batch. **Scale dependence (sdk-parity, second genericity audit m3):** a batch shares its Born couplings, kernel constants and masses, which encodes the NLOCT convention that only counterterms read MU_R. Generation now analyses the parameter program structurally (`native_trees._scale_outputs`: an output depends on the scale when its expression on the program's inputs contains the scale input; the helper functions are functions of their arguments) and records every dependent output with its name (`scale_outputs` in the parameter record; the native `ParameterFile` reads it, absent in older artifacts). Names are the plane refusal's (`native_trees._output_label`, one helper for both refusals): a Born coupling with its N_c grade, a counterterm coefficient with its epsilon power and N_c grade, a kernel constant by its value's form (coefficient and model factors), a mass by its parameter. The record changes the stored tree programs (`tree-program.json`, the companion's too) and so the native-member digests of every artifact generated since (fail-closed's `CONTROL_BYTES` were re-pinned with it and the native record's two parameter fields; plans, companion plans and values are bitwise unchanged). `TreeProgram::scale_dependent_shared` / `native_trees.scale_dependent_shared` list the batch-shared ones (empty for loop_sm and every NLO model whose scale enters the counterterms only). For a running-coupling or MSbar-mass model they are not empty: the SDK's `V2Runtime::evaluate` and the Python API's `V2VirtualRuntime._run` partition a batch by exact μ_R² (each point bitwise its single-scale value), and `TreeProgram::bind`, which takes a batch as given (the slice's native executor, the correlated and resolved calls of such a model), refuses a batch of several scales with a typed error naming the dependent outputs instead of the former untyped per-point mismatch (an older artifact, without the record, gets the mismatch typed and named by output). Tested with a toy running-coupling loop_sm (`G` times `1 + αs (MU_R − MZ)/(4π MZ)`, `loop_v2_ufo_variants.running_coupling_ufo`, g g > g g) in `test_loop_v2_sdk_parity.py`. Against the Python values at αs = 0.13, MT = 171.5 (g g → t t̄ g): model parameters and Born couplings bitwise equal in binary64, kernel constants within 2.5×10⁻¹⁶; the native lane equals the Python inputs there within 10⁻¹² (`test_native_lane_binds_the_external_parameters`). At seven legs (g g → g g g g g LC, `loop_v2_generate.py poles`, two points with a rotation check, one thread): generation 378 s and 4.0 GB, export with the tree and parameter programs 80 s (plan 112 MB, tree program 60 MB), 11–23 s per point with the check, process peak 13.5 GB; the double pole equals −(α_s/2π)·7·N_c times the native Born within 6.7×10⁻¹³ and 5.5×10⁻¹² (rotation estimates 1.3×10⁻⁸, 2.4×10⁻⁸). Open for the SDK: storing the tree program in the artifact and the native assembly of Born, bare and (after I5) counterterms per sector.
    - **Poles tool with stable coloured particles.** `loop_v2_generate.py poles` sets every coloured width to zero, as the public path requires (`artifact.require_zero_coloured_widths`): with loop_sm's WT = 1.508 the tree propagators carry the top width and the loop's do not, which moved the double pole of g g → t t̄ g off the universal value by 6×10⁻⁷ of the Born in binary64 and in double-double alike (g g → t t̄: 5×10⁻⁸); with WT = 0 it agrees within 1.2×10⁻⁹ and 4×10⁻¹² at the two points.
    - **gg→6g stopped by the coordinator (18:10 and 18:40 UTC):** no 2→6 generation or evaluation until the 2→5 plan is complete (every §5.7 process incl. EW, native evaluation without load-time regeneration, the SDK v4 path, the top-free gg→ggggg finite part against NJet). The 18:40 poles run had reached the tree blocks stage (12 min, 18.7 GB).
    - open (PLAN.md §5): generic-precision trees (I7; T3 already runs DoubleFloat and MPFR), CT scalars natively (I4 rest, with I5), native counterterms and fields (I5, after WP2.2; the Python counterterm sites are 95 % of a per-point Python-input evaluation: 546 of 574 s at gg→gggg), the native IR-pole operator (I5c), regeneration-free artifacts and the SDK (I6, after WP2.6), Rust seeds and plan lowering (I10, I11), schedule M (I12), and the residual counterterm lowering of the recursion form (I5R: four-gluon multi-trace R2, the gggV d-symbol, R2_GGZ in the CT-lowered model). The gg→ggggg finite part against NJet needs the native counterterms with `ignored_particle_ids=(6,)`. Mixed-order Borns with W or Z exchange (QCD corrections without a QED bound) need the root closure of colour-singlet and flavour-changing lines (§3.3).
  - *Status (sixth increment):*
    - **Native counterterm and external-field components** (I5's native assembly, built before WP2.2 integrates; PLAN.md §10 item 21). `native_trees._counterterm_program` exports every counterterm closure term (a counterterm vertex closed on tree blocks, `ct_sites.SiteTerm`: the slice's route until WP2.2 is integrated; WP2.2's leg-1 root terms are `SiteTerm`s on its counterterm program's blocks) as a closure term like the Born's, accumulated per (component, ε power, loop orders) into ket vectors over the ket tensors (the Born basis, then the closures' other literal tensors); the field insertions as Born × Born interferences; whole-rule colour continuations (LC/NLC four-gluon R2) as closure terms whose kernel is their polynomial in the block rows. `loop_v2::interference` forms each output (component, sector, ε power) as 2 Re(c ⟨A0|M|ket⟩), M the exact overlaps of the Born basis with the ket tensors per N_c power (the Born Gram, the other tensors counted natively in one batch), projected per sector degree as `_interfere` does; `z = A0† M` is formed once per row, Born order, N_c power and metric class. The parameter program also yields the counterterm and field couplings per point (they read log μ_R²), with the scalar library's helpers (`cond`, `reglog`) as function definitions and their diagnostics checked finite natively. Closure terms run in parallel over the Born rows, share interned input layouts and one colour-weight table, and finite-only batches skip the pole-only couplings and outputs (every finite part bitwise unchanged).

    - **Loop-aware reachability is the recursion route's only.** Site closure attaches counterterm vertices to the tree currents of every leg partition, which no loop position need consume. Example: the synthetic two-point counterterms of `test_loop_v2_ct_two_point` at u u~ > e⁻e⁺ lost their `uv_local` site (4.8 % of the component) on pruned blocks. The site route therefore builds every block current. When the recursion leaves rules to site closure, the tree program is rebuilt unpruned; the families do not change.
    - **The Levi-Civita R2 kernels of I-AX meet WP2.2's cancellation.** On this branch the site catalogue closes `R2_GGZ` and `R2_GGGVa` with their Levi-Civita kernels (validated against ALOHA, `test_loop_v2_levi_civita`). The site route therefore keeps those cells, and their up- and down-type terms cancel numerically; the recursion still leaves them out where they cancel. Where they do not cancel (the anomalous model of `test_loop_v2_ct_cancellation`), the recursion leaves the rules to sites, and both routes now evaluate the cell and agree, where they used to refuse the request (the tests changed accordingly).
    - **I5 proper, the interim production route** (coordinator decision 4; without it the merge would have turned every default-route artifact back to `point_inputs = "regenerated-at-load"`). The CT program of the recursion (`ct_closure.CountertermProgram.rows`) is exported as a second interaction program on a state of its own (`native_trees._CountertermTrees`; `loop_v2::trees` `counterterm_trees`, whose kernels follow the trees'). Its root terms (`ct_closure.ClosureTerm`) close like the site terms into the counterterm vectors, with their multiplicity and the inserted current's N_c grade; a root term with a Weyl-line factor (no loop_sm process has one) is refused natively, so the writer keeps the plan alone and loads regenerate. Coupling slots now record the ε power of the terms they weight: a root vertex's coupling is ε⁰, but its term is the slot's power. The parameter program also yields the CT program's model parameters: the model's, plus the request's CT multiplicities and slot switches, all slots on. Currents of pole slots are flagged per step, and finite-only evaluations skip them; every finite part stays bitwise unchanged. Closure blocks past the trees' state read the CT program's state. The site route (`counterterms = "sites"`) stays exported as before.

Unpaired runs on the shared host are not compared (see the third item).

        | Stage | before s | after s | peak GB before → after |
        |---|---:|---:|---:|
        | `build_tree_blocks` | 111.3 | 111.7 | 2.44 → 2.43 |
        | native schedules | 15.8 | 15.6 | 2.85 → 2.85 |
        | `CountertermProgram.build` (its `CountertermBlocks.build`) | 610.0 (603.6) | **474.3 (468.6)** | 14.03 → 12.06 |
        | `generate_counterterm_sites` | 0.2 | not run | |
        | `build_loop_kernel_catalogue`, `reading_skeletons` | 4.9 | 4.9 | |
        | `CountertermClosure.build` | 52.5 | **65.5** | 9.10 → 7.86 |
        | `_born_terms` | 8.1 | 8.0 | |
        | `build_seed_table` (main) | 88.2 | 69.5 | 9.71 → 8.62 |
        | `seed_fourier` | 5.4 | 5.3 | |
        | **sum of the stages above** | **898.1** | **756.4** | |

        `CountertermProgram.build` is 22 % faster (ct-simplify's plain admission and the inert rule leave fewer CT-program currents). The main seed table ran 21 % faster on unchanged code and input (not investigated). **`CountertermClosure.build` (+25 %) is the one stage that got slower**. Measured reason: Python's cyclic garbage collector, not the closure's work (probe `workspace/tmp/generation-scale/gint2/probe/closure_probe.py`, the same paired cores). The closure's code is unchanged by the merge and its work shrank: 601,920 → 566,640 root terms, 8,035 → 4,948 current combinations examined, 287 → 231 CT-program currents in the closure's table (41,256 → 33,204 blocks); at g g > g g g g both trees take 5.6 s for the same 76,360 terms. With the objects alive before it frozen (`gc.freeze()`) the same build takes 39.7 s before and 39.1 s after, with the collector off 38.5 and 38.4 s; the rest, 12.8 s before and 26.1 s after, is full collections. The after process holds fewer long-lived objects when the closure starts (22.4 M tracked against 25.2 M, the smaller CT program), so CPython's full-collection trigger (new survivors above a quarter of the long-lived objects) fires earlier while the closure's terms accumulate, and each full collection scans the whole heap. Freezing the generation's long-lived structures at stage boundaries would remove this cost on both sides; it is listed for generation-scale-2 below. The poles companion (69 % of the generation), whose code and work the merge leaves unchanged, ran only in the unpaired runs. **The merge's generation-level effect is therefore the paired −142 s, about 5 % of the seven-gluon generation, almost all of it in `CountertermProgram.build`.** An independent paired audit run (other cores, other load) measured the same pattern: `CountertermProgram.build` 805.0 → 623.3 s, `CountertermClosure.build` 66.5 → 85.6 s (49.7 and 48.1 s with the heap frozen), other stages equal.
