- **WP3.1 Rust migration D, native-lowering** (branch `work/v2-native-lowering`; `workspace/reports/engine-v2/wp31/RUST-MIGRATION.md` §3.D; phase 0 accepted 2026-10-25 ~01:05, phase 1 part 1 here; D3, the lowering in Rust from B's arena, is next):
  - *Plan model, JSON writer and binary member (D2, phase 0).* `rusticol_core::loop_v2::plan::binary::PlanModel` holds a plan v4 in the loader's file form; `PlanModel::json` writes `json.dumps(program, separators=(",", ":"))` byte for byte (keys in the exporter's order, `ensure_ascii` escapes; the plan holds no float), `PlanModel::binary` the columnar member of §12.1 (proposed kind 12), which `loop_v2::plan::read_binary` reads with a declared, bounded decoded size (no decompression bomb), a derivation bound refusing what the derivations would trust beyond the plan's tables, fallible probes of the decoded model and of the derivations' peak, and fallible copies: never a panic or an allocation abort, an error instead.
  - *Loader hardening (phase 1).* `LoopProgramV4::load` (the production JSON load of the executor and of `load_loop_v2_artifact`) goes through the same bound: a fused entry's q-degree above its content's is refused before the segment layouts (whose recursive monomial index overflowed the stack), a decimal power of ten of 2^32 or more before numerica's panic, vertex ids and ranks before the tables they size, and the derivations' peak must be granted (a probe); the scalar-sized tables (row links, content rows, the vertex table) are reserved fallibly. A JSON is not bounded per byte (it cannot be padded). A read decodes on one thread wherever an allocation can fail before the host's memory is exhausted (an address-space or data limit, strict overcommit): the parallel decode's per-thread state (lazily created malloc arenas, rayon's queues) escapes the probes, and under an `RLIMIT_AS` with the default pool it aborted on valid members. Elsewhere the chunks' state is allocated fallibly and a section whose chunks cannot run is decoded again sequentially; a pool that cannot start leaves every read on one thread instead of refusing it. **The probe is conservative (§0.2 portability):** at g g > g g g g g FC the bound is 6.60 GB and the probe, with its margin, 7.4 GB, against a measured derivation peak of 3.29 GB (peak/bound 0.39-0.66 over the exported plans, `tests/loop_v2_plan_derive_bound.rs`); a host whose address space for the load lies between the real peak and the probe (an `RLIMIT_AS`/`ulimit -v`, common in batch jobs, or RAM plus swap under Linux's heuristic overcommit, which refuses one allocation beyond it) refuses with an error an artifact it could load before. This grows at 2→6; tightening the bound (or probing the derivations in pieces) is a D3 / phase-1 part 2 item. **Cost (rule 8; the build profile matters: the package's natives are `build_loop_native.py`'s default, 16 codegen units without LTO, the slice's natives the shipped codegen, one unit with thin LTO).** Same profile (the slice `392d3f4a` rebuilt at 16 units without LTO by the evidence audit, `V2Executor` construction, cores 216-227, 12 rayon threads): the g g > g g g g plan loads in 0.5566-0.5573 s against 0.5485-0.5512 s (+1.0-1.6 %), the u u~ > g g g g plan in 0.1428-0.1433 s against 0.1384-0.1394 s (+2.8-3.5 %). Across profiles (the package's native against the slice's thin-LTO one, so the profile's share is not separated): u u~ > g g g g g FC (QED = 0, 69.2 MB, best of 3, alternating rounds) 2.575-2.592 s against 2.431-2.462 s (+5-6 %; at six legs the cross-profile cost is +3-4 % and +7 % where the same profile gives +1.0-1.6 % and +2.8-3.5 %), of which the derivation bound is 0.09 s (the probe is one mmap/munmap; a fixed glibc mmap threshold changes neither side, so the probe's large free does not slow the derivations); g g > g g g g g FC 12.21-12.36 s against 12.22-12.24 s (the bound 0.10 s of it). No gate is affected; the load's own cut is the derive (Open, below).
  - *D1, leaf weights* (`conventions.LeafWeights`; Python, output-identical). Generation asks eps_s for every reading (F and B: the refusal loop) and the export for every F leaf; `fermion_sign` now computes it once per distinct contents tuple (`_fermion_sign`, the full rule unchanged; a refusal is raised again at every call) and takes a fast path for a tuple without a fermion line (per content, once: no fermion loop line, no fermion block, no block holding a coloured fermion end; the sign of a closed ghost loop or +1). The fast path copies the inputs of the full rule (`_content_kind`: fermion loop lines, fermion blocks, blocks holding a coloured fermion end): any new input of `_line_parity` or `_require_mass_free_couplings` (WP3.2 EWI-5, e.g. Koszul signs of colour-singlet lines through the loop) must make `_content_kind` fermionic too, and `check_leaf_weights` of the package's test file is the guard (both methods' docstrings say so). Every reading's sign and every leaf weight equal the full rule on the controls (main and companion readings, refusals included) and at seven legs. Measured (sites form, cores 216-227, the two loops replayed on the generated slice): g g > g g g g g 1,620,990 readings, 1,333,047 distinct tuples, 1,295,550 readings on the fast path, 285,840 signs kept; refusal loop 33.3 → 8.6 s, the export's weight calls 19.4 → 7.6 s, **36.5 s saved**. u u~ > g g g g g (QED = 0): 207,540 readings, no fast path (the quark line runs through every loop); 5.5 → 5.1 s and 3.1 → 0.3 s, 3.2 s saved.
  - *D2' switch* (`plan_export.ExportedPlan`). `ExportedPlan.json()` is written by the native model, read from the exporter's Python records (`V2PlanModel.from_program`: a `serde` deserializer over the Python objects that accepts only records the writer writes back as `json.dumps` writes them, i.e. the loader's types, every record's keys in the writer's order (the loader's file form declares its fields in that order), every key the writer always writes present (an absent option as `None`, which it writes as `null`) and no `None` under a key the writer omits when absent (a step's `rows`, an edge's `links`, the plan's `column_rows` and `column_weights`, the only keys a record may lack; `binary::OMITTED_WHEN_ABSENT`, qualified by record), an error naming the record otherwise, so an accepted program's native JSON is `json.dumps`'s byte for byte and an exporter change of key order or of an optional key's presence fails loudly instead of being normalised; the writer (`json::write`), the member's traversal (`visit_plan`) and the derivation bound (`derive::bound`) destructure the file form without `..`, so a field added to the loader does not compile until all three carry it), and `ExportedPlan.binary()` is its member; `native_lowering.NATIVE_JSON` (default on) switches the JSON back to `ExportedPlan.python_json()`, the oracle. The switch is inside `ExportedPlan`, so the main plan, the companion's plan and the observables' column plans take it alike; byte identity holds on every control of `tests/unit/test_loop_v2_native_lowering.py` for all three (schedules B, F and M, written and derived row links, mass flips, helicity-resolved columns, the stored form with the tree program, electroweak and six-leg controls). Seven legs (sites form, `forward=False`, QED = 0, the companion skipped for stage isolation):

    | Process | S8 (= phase 0's stored oracle) | JSON | `python_json` → `json()` | member (= phase 0) | decode JSON → member | derive (JSON / member) | load ratio |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | g g > g g g g g FC | `2b0e45c9`, identical | 193.6 MB | 6.37 → 3.35 s (records 1.90 s, both encodings 1.01 s) | 23.0 MB (`9d59b38d`) | 1.08 → 0.26 s (4.15×) | 10.79 / 10.62 s | 1.09× |
    | u u~ > g g g g g FC | `8e7d99d6`, identical | 69.2 MB | 2.79 → 1.58 s | 14.7 MB (`da1fdc88`) | 0.46 → 0.10 s (4.66×) | 2.01 / 1.91 s | 1.23× |

    The program loaded from the member equals the JSON's (program digests `b3b49eb5`, `8b881fc2`), so dressing, which reads only the program, is unchanged: the evidence audit's same-profile timing (both natives at 16 codegen units without LTO, g g > g g g, 96 points, schedule B, binary64, cores 216-227, 12 rayon threads, 90 repetitions per side) gives bitwise identical numerators and a mean of 0.9736 s against 0.9850 s on the slice (−1.2 %; median 1.0729 s against 1.0775 s), and +1.0 % in mean against the slice's thin-LTO native, within the 3 % criterion either way. Records: `workspace/tmp/native-lowering/p1/oracle/{gg7,uu7}.json`. **The poles companion at seven legs** (u u~ > g g g g g, QED = 0, its 5 certified-scaleless families; generated with the main slice in 185 s, through the strict records reader): S8 `fe7e0635` (11.0 MB of JSON; equal to phase 0's companion), native JSON identical, member 1.47 MB (`d0825b21`, no padding), decoding to the JSON and re-encoding bitwise, equal program digests from the JSON load, the member load and `read_binary` (`e0a2f4ed`); the main plan in the same run keeps S8 `8e7d99d6` and member `da1fdc88` (`workspace/tmp/native-lowering/p2/oracle/uu7c.json`). The g g > g g g g g companion stays out of budget (its generic seed table is about 70 % of a 45-min seven-gluon generation on the slice, §0.1; generation-scale-2 item 5).
  - *generation-scale-2's `column_weights` (for the coordinator: generation-scale-2 edits the loader's `PlanFile`, `_Exporter.seed_records`, the lowering body of `export_plan` and `ExportedPlan`, D's after G-int by RUST-MIGRATION §5.2).* The writer, the member and the bound name the file form whole, so the merge of the two branches does not compile until they carry the new field (no silent drop from `ExportedPlan.json()` or the member). The carry is `workspace/reports/engine-v2/wp31/carry/native-lowering-column-weights.patch`, for whichever branch integrates second: the key written after `column_rows` when present and omitted when absent (`OMITTED_WHEN_ABSENT`), three member columns (`ColumnWeightsFlag`, `ColumnWeightsLen`, `ColumnWeight`; member format 3), the loader's per-column weight indices in the bound, and the test (the resolved-column control shares weight columns). Validated on the merge with generation-scale-2 `84cef31a` (rusticol-core lib and derivation-bound tests, this file's default and physics tiers, `test_loop_v2_observables.py`, `test_loop_v2_stored_process.py`). A plan whose columns share weight columns stores its weights once while the loader still expands the row links over every column, so its member may be padded (g g > t t~ g, helicity-colour columns: 12,984 bytes); every other plan still needs none. S8 (`snapshot.py`, generation-scale-2's) hashes `ExportedPlan.json()`, the native writer since the D2′ switch; with the records reader's refusals and the whole-form writer a dropped or added key cannot pass unnoticed, but hashing `python_json()` would keep the stage oracle independent of D's writer (the owner's call).
  - *Open (D3 and phase 1 part 2).* The Rust lowering from B's arena (D3; it removes the Python records the JSON is now written from and brings the export below 15 s). The load's ≥ 5× target (RUST-MIGRATION §10, 15:45 of 24 Oct) needs the shared derive cut, which is 82-98 % of either load at seven legs: at six legs it is `LoopProgramV4::family` 11 %, SipHash of the integer keys of its maps 14 %, `links::RowSpace::restrict` 7 % (perf, `workspace/tmp/native-lowering/p1/perf/`); faster hashing and per-family parallelism (with the derivation bound counting every thread's temporaries) are the candidates.
- **WP3.2 Electroweak enablement and gate E0.**
  - *Goal:*
    - Feynman-gauge massive vectors, explicit Goldstones, ghost loop lines;
    - complex-mass-scheme integration (R7); generic CT admission (R6); species sharing for vector/Goldstone masses;
    - **gate E0**: exact counts (families per mass assignment, skeletons, trie nodes, buckets, NinjaRS reductions) for gg→tt̄e⁻e⁺g (EW) and uu~→e⁻e⁺ggg (EW), with the G1-calibrated prediction;
    - EW lepton-in-loop sign oracles (O13 EW part).
  - *Owns:* `models/external_evaluation.py`, `models/external.py`, `models/compiler_gauge.py`, `models/recurrence_catalog_builder.py`, `loops/model_cms.py`.
  - *Depends:* R6 and R7 packages; WP1.1; G1 calibration for E0.
  - *Accept:* Feynman = unitary gauge on gauge-invariant trees; ee→μμ, massive μμ, eeuu and the CMS fixtures through v2; gg→tt̄ EW against MadLoop; O13 EW; gate E0.
  - *Size:* L (17).
  - *Plan:* `workspace/reports/engine-v2/wp32/PLAN.md` (revision 2, adopted 2026-10-25; increments EWI-0 … EWI-11).
  - *Status, ew-ct (EWI-0, EWI-3; measurement M1; `test_loop_v2_ew.py`, `workspace/reports/engine-v2/wp32/M1-ew-ct.md`):*
    - **EWI-0.** The EW harness (`tools/loop_v2_generate.py validate` on EW fixtures: corrections, compatibility profile and scale parameter from the fixture), the ladder of strict xfails naming each request's blocker (`test_electroweak_ladder_reaches_its_blocker`, nightly), a per-tier persistent test cache (`tests/conftest.py`: `PYAMPLICOL_CACHE_DIR` under pytest's cache, a removed temporary directory without it, never `~/.cache`) and the CT-lowering hygiene M2 (no branch not taken is formed; a non-finite coefficient, expression or binary64 value at the default card, is refused).
    - **EWI-3.** B1 is lifted: CT scalars whose bodies need the model's scalar library are reference parameters (§6.1), with one helper semantics (`standard_helpers`: loop_sm's `cond`/`reglog`/`complexconjugate` resolved by name, so its plans are byte-identical; every other body a scalar-library function), never inlined into a vertex; `CMSParam` decided (`decide_constant`, undecidable constants still refused); the CT admission takes loops of the selected corrections (§6.2). Oracles: the UFO's own Python evaluation of `parameters.py`/`CT_parameters.py` (binary64) and an mpmath transliteration (double-double, 60 digits; nightly), both on G_μ and loop_qcd_qed_sm, the helpers against `function_library.py` in every quadrant, MadLoop's own CT values in double and quadruple precision (every piece its Fortran model library writes, both UFOs: the convention, including the +0 side of exactly-on-cut arguments, which matters at real masses, §6.1), the referenced couplings against the UFO couplings, both reglog conventions (the UFO oracle and `loops.model_cms`'s rebinding through the production lowering), Python = native parameter program (§6.1), and Q3's UV-pole cancellation. Every EW request of the ladder reaches B3 or later; none fails in the CT lowering.
    - **M1** (PLAN §9): the target's CT lowering took 262 s cold and 23.6 s warm (pinned cores 240-255; 258.7 s and 22.9 s after the fix round, 259.2 s and 23.4 s with MadLoop's runtime side; 286.9 s on the slice tip `005ab8f8`, after EWI-4b's Feynman-gauge clone lines), above the 30 s threshold, so EWI-3R was released (DECISIONS 2026-10-26 ~00:35).
    - **EWI-3R** (option 2: a referenced CT coupling is one reference pair per Laurent coefficient; option 1: the model compiler's kernel-equivalence keys and proofs in rusticol with rayon, the Python stage as the byte oracle, proofs shared by the two input orders of an orientation; output-identical memos and canonical-text renamings of the compile's per-structure stages): the target's CT lowering (`lowered_counterterm_model`) takes 24.2-27.4 s cold and 10.0-11.1 s warm on 8 pinned cores (≤ 30 s, PLAN §9; implementer and audit runs at host load 130-160), the base models and loop_sm's CT programs byte-identical to the slice's, the CT model IR of the target, of G_μ g g > t t~ EW and of loop_qcd_qed_sm u u~ > d d~ QED equal to the Python oracle's with the native proofs on 1 thread and on 8 (target) or 2 (controls), the CT values equal MadLoop's (`test_counterterm_scalars_equal_madloop`, all three carriers) and the couplings' references an mpmath transliteration of the UFO couplings in double-double and at 60 digits (`test_referenced_couplings_equal_mpmath`, both UFOs). Only the 30 s half of §9 is met: the 5 % criterion is not measurable at the target, whose generation stops at the colour-singlet fermion-line refusal (`FermionSignError`, EWI-5) after 260 s (the lowering is at most about 10 % of that), and on the measurable G_μ g g > t t~ EW the lowering is 14.0 s of a 166.5 s cold generation (8.4 %); remainder and causes in `M1-ew-ct.md`.
    - **Open.** Q3's finite part was never validated: loop_qcd_qed_sm writes the photon and Z parts of the u u~ g R2 in one coupling labelled [[u, Z]], which the photon-only veto drops whole; such vetoes are refused since WP3.2 EWI-5 (`veto-unlabelled-counterterms`) until the [U2] guard (EWI-7) handles them. Under `loops.model_cms` (CMSParam = 1) the two reglog conventions differ at zero widths (eCoup_Gmu_UV_EW, eCoup_DGmua0_UV_EW): EWI-10. `models/compiler_entry.py`'s lazy kernel proofs (output-identical) and the admission's third criterion await the coordinator's ratification.
  - *Status, ew-lines (EWI-1, EWI-1b, EWI-2; QCD corrections with electroweak lines, `test_loop_v2_ew_lines.py`):*
    - **Loop species from the families (B2).** The kernel catalogue takes the species the families route (`assemble._family_loop_species`: the cut species and loop lines of the admitted and certified-scaleless families' routings, with the members of merged closed-loop flavour classes), not the loop particles of the root counterterm rules (none at a lepton leg 1; NLOCT UV rules declare none); species no family routes get no kernels (a unitary-gauge W with joint corrections on g g > g g). An admitted rule naming a loop particle that is no eligible loop species of the request (vetoed, or not perturbative in its corrections) is refused (`_check_declared_loop_species`, NotImplementedError).
    - **Colour-singlet line at the root (B4-root).** History (WP3.2 EWI-1, superseded by EWI-5): a lepton line closing at the root vertex took the tree engine's merge sign of its two ends (`FermionOrderingSign.tree_sign`, then `SiteClosure._singlet_root_sign`), and a process with more than one colour-singlet fermion line (more than two lepton legs) was refused before any tree was built, wherever its lines sat (`require_one_singlet_line`, `FermionSignError`; also in `SiteClosure`): the tree engine signs every merge of currents holding singlet fermions with their Koszul sign, which the blocks' rows carry inside a block but neither the root closure nor the loops' leaf weights then applied between blocks. With interleaved lines (uū→e⁻μ⁻e⁺μ⁺: supports {3, 5} and {4, 6}, sign −1) the Born closure was 0.53 times the tree runtime's amplitude (uū→e⁻e⁻e⁺e⁺: 0.34), and so were the Born and the virtual, silently, on `8b52179b` too; the earlier check refused only singlet lines attached directly at the root. Both helpers and the refusal are gone: the root closure (`SiteClosure._root_sign`) and the loops' leaf weights (`LeafWeights._line_parity`) apply the Koszul signs between blocks with one rule, `conventions.colour_singlet_sign` ("Koszul signs of colour-singlet fermions", below). The Born closure's currents enter the loop-aware reachability (`assemble._born_block_requests`; within the Born's own orders when they exceed the loop orders' cap, an order the request leaves open bounded by its largest tree power from the model's vertices, `_tree_order_bounds`: n − 2 for renormalisable vertices, a two-point vertex carrying the order refused) and the tree blocks' order bound takes the Born's where larger (`_block_order_bounds`).
    - **Vertices not closed under conjugation (B5, B6).** The tree engine reads the current of tree particle p on the UFO leg of p; a W vertex has no leg for the tree particles of the families' (incoming-field) attachments, so the root closure (Born, CT roots) and loop segments read it through the model's conjugate rule, whose couplings and Lorentz structures are the vertex's own complex-conjugated with the chiral projectors exchanged (`ct_sites.ConjugateVertices`, `conventions.TreeReading.vertex`; the colour stays the vertex's incoming reading; the conjugate must be unique at the same coupling orders with one colour structure each, and a ghost on such a vertex is refused, by both readings). The counterterm-site oracle (O6) reads its open sites the same way, so the site scalar plan computes the conjugate rules' couplings (`SiteClosure.read_entries`); a site term whose coupling the scalar plan does not compute is refused (`LoopSlice._terms_of`) and the evaluations read the couplings strictly: before, the R2 of every open W site vanished silently on the sites route (ud̄→e⁺ν_e: R2 0 against −3.08·10⁻⁵, 67 % of the virtual's scale). Reading the vertex's own couplings on swapped spinor legs (the first attempt) is wrong for complex or chiral couplings: with loop_sm's Wolfenstein CKM and a massless b, g u > w+ b missed Catani by 59 % and the Born closure of u b~ > e+ ve differed from the tree runtime by the phase V_ub*/V_ub; with the conjugate rule both agree (≤ 4·10⁻¹⁶, a real global sign). Closed vertices keep their reading (bosons and ghosts on their own legs; since WP3.2 EWI-7b each kernel leg also takes the momentum of the block whose row it takes, with the reading sign of §6.1 "Root closure reading": before, a closed vertex with a conjugate pair, W⁺W⁻V, had rows and momenta on swapped legs); twelve QCD controls (five to six legs, FC/LC, top veto, sites route, the Z, the photon-only mixed Born) are byte-identical to `19083c10` (S1/S4/S5/S8, plan and native-trees digests, Born and CT terms, L3; uū→Zg's in-memory catalogue holds six unused kernels fewer). Generation time is unchanged (uū→gggg 18.9/20.4 s against 19.7/19.0 s warm, pinned; the Born block requests take 6 ms at seven legs).
    - **Oracles.** e⁺e⁻→uū and ud̄→e⁺ν_e equal the massless form factor (≤ 1e-15); e⁻u→e⁻u (lepton root) equals ue⁻→ue⁻; W/Z production (gu→W⁺d, ud̄→W⁺g, uū→Zg, ZZ, W⁺W⁻), t-channel W (bu→dt), 5F single top (gb→tW⁻) and bb̄→W⁺W⁻ (massless b with y_b = 0, the top t-channel exchange: the chiral W vertex joining a Weyl b and the massive top in the Born and in the loop, on the default restriction with MB = y_b = 0, where the b is a Dirac field everywhere, so no Weyl b meets a Dirac current; MB = 0 with the default y_b is not unitary; the Weyl b of `loop_sm-no_b_mass`, the Dirac–Weyl class, is admitted since m-qcd MQ-5F, §3.3 "Dirac–Weyl closures") and lepton legs equal the public legacy engine at stored points (`tests/fixtures/loop/v2/ew_lines_legacy.json`, widths zero; ≤ 8.5·10⁻¹⁴); the mixed-order Born uū→dd̄ with the W exchange equals MadLoop per squared order (`tools/loop_reference/uudd_mixed_sm_qcd.json`, loop_sm-full with the Wolfenstein CKM; ≤ 1.5·10⁻¹⁵); e⁺e⁻→tt̄h (the lepton root and the top Yukawa on loop top lines, the first process oracle of the scalar coupling of the tree reading) equals MadLoop's virtual at three points (≤ 3·10⁻¹⁴, nightly); e⁻e⁺→uūg: Catani and O6 (nightly); O6 with W sites: ud̄→e⁺ν_e (default tier), gu→W⁺d and bu→dt (nightly), every component equal to the recursion; uū→e⁻e⁺gg: LC and NLC are the formal N_c projections of the full-colour cells (an unpruned LC slice evaluated one N_c power at a time; the parts above each sector's leading degree vanish and the powers sum to FC; nightly).
    - **Open.** Colour-singlet widths: at the default WZ/WW the two engines differ by (MΓ/s)² (legacy and v2 width conventions; the EW width policy is EWI-0b). With QCD corrections alone, `_ir_poles` predicts QCD×EW interference sectors although the loop selection (as MadLoop's) leaves out the photon–gluon boxes, so Catani does not apply there (uū→dd̄ photon only: twice the virtual, which equals MadLoop; with loop_sm's W exchange the single poles of the QCD⁴QED² and QCD²QED⁴ sectors are 2 % off while the virtual equals MadLoop to 1.5·10⁻¹⁵): no prediction is the right answer (ew-poles' `_ir_poles`). `ct_closure.InsertionReachability` still reads a CT root through the incoming fields; with the conjugate rule the root evaluates the conjugate rule, so an inert (unlowerable) conjugate of an open root site is refused at generation (`assemble._conjugate_site_entries`) until the reachability reads roots as the tree engine does (after A4).
  - *Status, ew-gauge (EWI-4a; 't Hooft-Feynman-gauge trees, `test_loop_v2_ew_gauge.py`; not integrated before EWI-4b):*
    - **The gauge is a compile option** (`ModelCompileOptions.gauge`, `CompiledModelIR.gauge`, both recorded only off the default unitary gauge). The two gauges share every compiled record but the Goldstone dispositions (`compiler_gauge`: absorbed by the unitary vector, or explicit fields of the tree model naming their vector), so `compile_model_source(gauge=...)` caches the gauge-independent compile and applies the gauge with `with_model_gauge`, the hook of EWI-4b's request-scoped switch. Feynman gauge is admissible only for a source that declares gauge 1 (`declared_model_gauges`, from the raw source records: `[1]` for loop_qcd_qed_sm and its G_μ variants, `[0, 1]` for loop_sm and MG5's sm); a massive vector with a standard propagator then needs exactly one Goldstone partner; a prepared bundle keeps its gauge. The lowered-counterterm IR key holds the gauge off the default. QCD-only requests keep unitary trees.
    - **Two tree-model defects found by the gauge checks (pre-existing, fixed).** (1) The Lorentz lowering of a vertex put each spinor index on its partner leg in (column, row) order, so the network evaluated spinor chains in reverse; UFO `ProjM`/`ProjP` were exchanged for spenso's `projp`/`projm` to keep γ^μ P chains right, which made every lone projector the opposite chirality (the G⁰ and G^± Yukawa couplings: the G⁻ coupled to right-handed b quarks) and reversed γγ, γγ⁵ and Σ products. Vertices now keep each index on its own leg in (row, column) order with UFO's own projectors (`models/tensors._LorentzContext`); every FFV, VVV, VVS, single-γ and identity kernel of loop_sm, loop_qcd_qed_sm(_Gmu) and MG5's sm is byte-identical, only the Goldstone FFS kernels change; propagators keep their order. (2) Those UFO object libraries lose the Goldstone flag of the G⁻ (`Particle.anti()` passes it positionally into `line`), so unitary-gauge trees kept a G⁻ beside the W⁻ (b t̄ → W⁻h, b t̄ → W⁻Z wrong by O(1)); the compiled flags are closed under conjugation (`compiler_gauge.close_goldstone_flags`).
    - **Oracles (can fail; review M1).** Feynman = unitary per helicity and colour sector ≤ 10⁻¹³ on tb̄→tb̄ (massive b; it replaced μ⁺μ⁻→tt̄ on the massive-muon G_μ UFO in EWI-4b, whose Goldstone share is only 2·10⁻³), tt̄→Zh, tt̄→tt̄ (QED), tt̄→W⁺W⁻, bb̄→tt̄ (QED; the tree of M5) and bt̄→W⁻h (massive b), with the Goldstone diagrams alone ≥ 10⁻³ of the amplitude (not in tt̄→W⁺W⁻: no G⁰W⁺W⁻ vertex, and the k k/M² term vanishes on the on-shell W pair; it checks the massive Feynman propagator); Slavnov-Taylor k·M(V) = −i M_V M(G) for gg→tt̄Z, both Z of bb̄→ZZ and gb→tW⁻ (massive b), the phase derived from the UFO couplings; the G⁰tt̄ coupling flipped fails both. Amplitudes from the v2 tree replay at 32 digits (binary64 cancellations reach 3·10⁻¹³ in bb̄→ZZ). With the previous lowering 8 of the 11 checks fail; with MG5's sm UFO (gauge [0, 1]) tt̄→Zh differed between the gauges by O(1) too.
    - **Open after EWI-4a (done in EWI-4b, below).** The legacy loop path (`loops/vertex_frames.py`, removed with the legacy engine by MQ-LEGACY-REMOVE, §13.3) kept its own projector exchange for FFS currents ("as in the tree compiler"; documented as checked against ALOHA in its frame): it was re-checked with a Goldstone-sign-sensitive oracle before serving as an EW oracle (EWI-5).
  - *Status, ew-gauge (EWI-4b; 't Hooft-Feynman gauge in loops and counterterms; `test_loop_v2_ew_gauge.py`, the ladder of `test_loop_v2_ew.py`):*
    - **Gauge scope** (§6.8): every request with electroweak loop species takes the model in 't Hooft–Feynman gauge (`ew_policy.feynman_gauge_model` in `LoopSlice.generate`; `ElectroweakGaugeError` for a model that cannot be, already at the dry run); QCD-only requests keep the compiled gauge (seven QCD controls on loop_sm and G_μ, b b̄ → t t̄ QED=2 [virt=QCD] with a massive b among them: S1/S4/S5/S8 and plan digests identical to the slice `db13c260`); the gauge is recorded in the generation filters off the default. `ModelCompileOptions.gauge` is carried by the compile path (`compile_model_source`/`load_cached_model_source` validate it as options and compile the gauge-independent ones, `ModelCompileOptions.gauge_independent`).
    - **Loop lines.** `catalogue._numerator` already took the Feynman-gauge propagator of a massive vector (−i g), the Goldstones' (i) and the ghosts' index-free UFO numerator; with the model in that gauge every segment and propagator numerator of the colour-singlet bosons and ghosts has degree ≤ 1 (rank ≤ N; every family of g g > t t~ EW has rank ≤ N).
    - **Counterterms.** The Goldstone–vector mixings are vertex insertions (§6.1 two-point insertions); clone lines compose the propagators the engine applies in the model's gauge (the UFO record of a massive vector holds the unitary numerator); propagators read UFO's projectors and the composite reverses Π's spinor chain (a lone chiral projector on a clone line had the opposite chirality before; §6.1). Terms on Goldstones a unitary-gauge tree model absorbs are inert (§6.2).
    - **Oracles.** g g > t t~ with QCD and QED corrections (G_μ, real masses, widths zero) equals MadLoop on the same UFO at the fixture's point 0 (`gg_ttx_ew.json`): Born 6.6·10⁻¹⁶, finite 0, single pole 1.2·10⁻¹⁴, double pole 1.7·10⁻¹⁵ in the fixture's metric (native executor, Python point inputs; 249 s cold generation, the CT lowering of the G_μ UFO most of it); u u~ > d d~ QED (full: Z, W, H, Goldstones and ghosts in the loops) and g t > t t t~ QED (whose trees reach the G⁰–Z, G⁰–A and G^±–W^∓ mixings) are UV-finite in binary64 to the last digit; the latter, with a massive b, equals the site oracle in every component (O6, the oracle's coverage rule certified for its electroweak g t t̄ witnesses, which the field and mass counterterms cover: the rule asks for a vertex rule per witness; with a massless b the oracle's exact-species witness admission drops two (W, b) R2 rules that the UFO names for W and G^± loops, §6.1), which fixed the mixings' momentum sign (the first reading differed from the oracle by the mixings' sign, 0.65 % of the counterterms' finite part; the total UV poles are blind to it); M5: b b̄ → t t̄ QED=2 [virt=QCD] with a massive b equals in both gauges (Born 3·10⁻¹⁶, virtual 1.4·10⁻¹⁶ at 32 digits; 2·10⁻⁹ in binary64, the reduction's conditioning at this point recorded by precision-ap), the 't Hooft–Feynman program inserting the explicit G⁰ counterterms the unitary one cannot hold; A4's native counterterm program equals the tree engine's on g g > t t~ EW (DAG, blocks, clone lines, with Goldstone currents), and the native loop blocks on the Goldstone model (`g g > t t~` at QCD² QED²). M3: g g > t t~ EW takes no reachability fallback. Ladder: uudd_qed (UV-finite), uudd_qcd_qed, gmu_gg_ttx and gmu_gt_tttx_mixing lifted; gmu_uux_emepg, gmu_gg_ttxemep and gmu_gg_ttxemepg (541 s each to there) stop at a lepton line through the loop (B4, EWI-5).
    - **Open.** `validate --inputs native` on g g > t t~ EW was a strict xfail until EWI-N (the export's SymJIT plane application of its parameter program disagreed with the exact evaluator at a counterterm coefficient of `UV_GTTLEW`, refused as the listed `plane-mismatch`); it passes since (WP3.2 status, ew-native). Ghost loop segments at vertices not closed under conjugation (a W attached to a ghost loop: g b > t b t~ EW) are refused by the tree reading (`conventions.conjugate_vertex_rules`, ew-lines' guard); processes with W block currents need their reading established. A Goldstone–vector mixing line ending at the root on its Goldstone side (leg 1 coupling to the Goldstone, e.g. a massive b at leg 1 with electroweak corrections) is refused (`UnloweredCountertermError`): the root closure would need the promoted copy's momentum factor.
  - *Status, ew-leptons (EWI-5; colour-singlet fermion lines through the loop; `test_loop_v2_ew_leptons.py`, the ladder of `test_loop_v2_ew.py`):*
    - **Koszul signs of colour-singlet fermions (B4 in the loop and at the root).** A block row carries the tree engine's Koszul signs of colour-singlet fermions (`generation/fermion_ordering.py`): it is the block current's coefficient of the product of its singlet sources in source order. The loop leaf weights (`LeafWeights._line_parity`) and the root closure (`SiteClosure._root_sign`) complete them with one rule, `conventions.colour_singlet_sign`: the Koszul sign of concatenating the blocks' singlet supports in the order they are read (loop contents, or the root vertex's legs), times the merge sign of the open singlet ends each line joins outside the blocks, listed (antiparticle, particle) as the tree engine's two-fermion merge takes them (the block of incoming field f holds the external end of anti(f), `open_end_orientation`). Every singlet line is then listed (antiparticle, particle) inside the blocks and through the loop alike, so the Wick sign of every diagram is kept up to one sign per process, which the Born shares. It is the tree engine's `merge_sign` composed over the blocks (its identity is tested against an independent inversion count in every block order, and against `FermionOrderingSign.tree_sign`); no vertex property enters, and no count of lines is assumed: any number of singlet lines, mixed with any number of coloured lines (the colour flow owns their order, the ring-sign rule unchanged). `SiteClosure.fermion_ends` holds the singlet ends (`SINGLET_END`), so the D1 fast path of `LeafWeights` reads every block holding one with the full rule (`check_leaf_weights` passes on the four-lepton processes). Lifted: `ct_sites.require_one_singlet_line` and the refusal of a singlet line through the loop; still refused: a line joining a coloured and a singlet end, two ends of one orientation (Majorana-like), vertices with more than one fermion line (four-fermion contacts; the user's decision 2026-10-26 ~07:55 keeps these and Majorana fermions unsupported). The "loop fermion vertex couples through the mass of the line it emits" refusal blocks no vertex of loop_sm, loop_qcd_qed_sm or loop_qcd_qed_sm_Gmu (default and full restrictions, unitary and 't Hooft–Feynman gauge: no vertex coupling depends on the mass of one of its fermion legs; their Yukawas are written through `ymX`).
    - **Counterterm roots on a mixing line's Goldstone side.** A root vertex of the loop model that the request's tree model does not hold because its coupling vanishes at the restriction (the electron Yukawa `[e+ e- G0]` at the G_μ default restriction) has no promoted copy in the lowered model and contributes nothing, so `CountertermClosure.build` skips it instead of refusing, but only when every coupling of its loop-model entries vanishes identically in the lowered model (`ct_sites.restricted_to_zero`, the one restriction rule since fail-closed-2, which the loop catalogue applies too, "Listed generation refusals": the source helpers inlined with their exact bodies, internal parameters resolved to the restriction's external ones, `ct_sites.vanishes_identically`); any other such root vertex is refused (`UnloweredCountertermError`, also for a vertex the tree model holds, e.g. a massive lepton or top at leg 1).
    - **The tree engine's reading of colour-singlet loop segments (the Goldstone basis).** The tree engine reads every vertex all-outgoing: the current of each outgoing tree particle on that particle's leg, with its outgoing momentum (`generation.runtime_schema`: "sum all-outgoing momenta ... negate physical incoming momenta first"). The loop segments read fermion legs that way (`TreeReading`), boson legs by their incoming fields with incoming momenta, and the colour seeds read the incoming colour flows. The tree engine's value is the segment's times `L(π legs, −p)/L(legs, p)` (`L` the Lorentz structure, `π` the exchange of each boson leg with its antiparticle's leg on a vertex closed under conjugation, the identity on one read through its conjugate rule, which takes the antiparticles' legs already) times the colour tensor's sign under the transposition of its adjoint generators (`conventions.colour_transposition_sign`: −1 per `f`; 1 for `d`, `T` between swapped ends, identities and two-index traces; other tensors refused), one rule for every vertex, and the segment kernels carry it (`catalogue._CatalogueBuilder.reading_sign`; a vertex where either factor is not ±1 is refused, typed `LoopKernelCatalogueError`). On the SM's coloured vertices the two factors cancel (the momentum-odd three-gluon vertex against its `f`; the quark–gluon and four-gluon vertices have neither). It is −1 exactly on Z G⁰ H, W∓ G⁰ G± and W∓ G± H in loop_qcd_qed_sm(_Gmu), loop_sm and MG5's sm. The first version kept every coloured vertex at 1 on the SM argument; the second genericity audit (G2-M6) showed that silently wrong for a coloured vertex whose colour does not compensate its momenta (a W coupling of a coloured scalar pair, W/Z couplings of scalar leptoquarks, a chromomagnetic dipole). With the one rule such a vertex takes its colour-singlet copy's sign (`test_coloured_segments_take_the_sign_of_their_colour_singlet_copy`, a toy extension of the G_μ UFO: W sq̃~ sd̃ as W G H, −1; the gluon's and photon's scalar vertices as A G⁻ G⁺, 1; the chromomagnetic dipole as the photon's, −1; the old rule gave 1 on W sq̃~ sd̃ and the dipole), and every vertex term of loop_sm, loop_qcd_qed_sm, the G_μ UFO in both gauges and HEFT keeps its sign (QCD catalogues and artifacts unchanged). On the vertices closed under conjugation these are the field signs of G⁰ negated (s(G⁰) = −1, s(G⁺)s(G⁻) = 1, all others 1); W∓ G⁰ G± and W∓ G± H, read through their conjugate rules, take the ratio alone and are no field signs (as field signs they would contradict each other). The Goldstone Yukawas' lone chiral projectors were matched to the tree engine's readings (EWI-4a, `models.tensors`; the legacy loop path keeps the textbook readings with the exchanged projectors, which is why it agrees with MadLoop), and blocks, Born and counterterm trees come from them. A ghost vertex has no tree reading (it is read first: the gluon's ghost vertex is no sign kinematically): it takes the product of the field signs of its other legs, solved over GF(2) from the closed boson vertices of every colour that have a reading sign (`_FieldSigns`; −1 on the ghost-G⁰ vertices, whose G⁰ blocks come from the tree engine; s(g) = 1 from the three-gluon vertex, so the gluon's ghost vertex keeps 1), so the ghost lines share the basis. Before it, the loops mixed the textbook basis of these segments with the tree engine's Goldstone Yukawas and blocks: the Z–G⁰–H triangles on an open fermion line had the opposite sign of legacy and MadLoop (O2 rel 2.0 on exactly those families of e⁻e⁺→μ⁻μ⁺ with massive muons and of e⁻e⁺→tt̄; 41 of the 127 families of e⁺e⁻→tt̄h, the last one a ghost-G⁰ bubble with a G⁰* → tt̄ block), the massive-muon control missed MadLoop's bare part by 10⁻⁷ and g g > t t~ e- e+ EW missed MadLoop, Recola and OpenLoops by O(1) in the finite part. Independently, ew-epsilon (EWI-7b) found the same reading difference at the counterterm root of the Levi-Civita R2 of a Z with gluons (`SiteClosure._reading_sign`: (−1)^(momentum degree) times the colour flows' transposition sign on all-self-conjugate vertices); the two rules are one statement: the colour factor is now one helper, `conventions.colour_transposition_sign` with `conventions.ReadingSignError` (accepting the bare UFO strings of the root's entries and the namespace-qualified ones of compiled vertex terms), which `ct_sites` imports since ew-epsilon's integration (its private copy, with the same tensors, signs and refusals, is gone; `ct_sites.ReadingSignError` is the shared class). One fermion classifier as well (G2-m2): `conventions.fermion_end` / `fermion_ends` (colour singlet → `SINGLET_END`, (anti)triplet → ±3, any other representation a typed `FermionSignError`) for the external ends, the root sign and the leaf weights, which had classified a sextet or octet fermion block as a singlet at the root, dropped an octet external fermion from the ends and refused it untyped (`ValueError`) in the leaf weights (unreachable on the slice's models: the tree engine refuses such fermions too). The analysis: the segment emulation from catalogue kernels equals the textbook Feynman rules for any external spinors, the tree engine's m⁻m⁺ → Zh equals the textbook only with the G⁰ Yukawa negated (Feynman = unitary), and a VSS probe (V_59 → `P(1,2)` alone) shows the tree engine feeding the external G⁰'s outgoing momentum.
    - **Oracles.** The MadLoop G_μ controls (full EW, Feynman gauge, lhv = 1) per Laurent coefficient and component (bare, R2, UV vertex, field, all counterterms): e⁻e⁺→μ⁻μ⁺ (two scales) ≤ 1.5·10⁻¹⁴, e⁻e⁺→uū ≤ 1.4·10⁻¹³, e⁻e⁺→μ⁻μ⁺ with massive muons (their G⁰, H and G± Yukawas, the Z–G⁰–H triangles) ≤ 3.8·10⁻¹⁴ since the reading sign (the fixtures' metric, 3 and 4 rows; 5–11 min generation each under load); g g > t t~ e- e+ with QCD and QED corrections against MadLoop, Recola and OpenLoops (`required/gg_ttxemep_ew`, three points, nightly, native executor in double-double: binary64 loses 2·10⁻¹⁰ of the finite part at point 0) 1.2·10⁻¹², 1.8·10⁻⁸ (within the fixture's cross-provider 2·10⁻⁸) and 2.4·10⁻¹²; Bhabha e⁻e⁺→e⁻e⁺ on loop_qcd_qed_sm (s- and t-channel lines through the boxes; new MadLoop reference `required/emep_emep_ew`, 196 loops, ~1 min) ≤ 5.8·10⁻¹⁵ at three points; uū→e⁻e⁺μ⁻μ⁺ [virt=QCD] (two lepton lines and a quark line, new MadLoop reference `required/uux_emepmummp`) ≤ 3.9·10⁻¹⁴ (default tier, 6 s generation), and the same Born and virtual to 10⁻¹² on a loop_sm copy whose electron and muon are renamed and renumbered (G2-M5's genericity); the Born closure equals the tree runtime and Born and virtual are invariant under relabellings that change the interleaving of the lines' supports (u u~ > e- m- e+ m+, u u~ > e- e- e+ e+; without the loop's Koszul sign the virtual moved by 0.8–1.6); O2 per family on e⁻e⁺→μ⁻μ⁺ (massless and massive muons) and O13 on Bhabha (all 44 families; the legacy families are FeynKit's diagrams with FeynKit's overall factors, so the two pairings of the identical leptons into lines are checked family by family; 754 s) against the legacy engine at 60 digits ≤ 10⁻⁵³ (Bhabha ≤ 10⁻⁵⁰) (the v2 families on one legacy ring are summed: the widths' labels keep the W, Goldstone and ghost rings at M_W apart; as scratch evidence also e⁻e⁺→tt̄, 28 families, and e⁺e⁻→tt̄h, 127 families, all equal); e⁻e⁺→μ⁻μ⁺ on loop_qcd_qed_sm (full EW) has the QED Catani poles (4.5·10⁻¹⁵). g t > t t t~ with QED corrections (two massive top lines joined through the loop by Z, photon, H, G⁰, W and G^±, W–b and G^±–b boxes and pentagons on both lines) equals MadLoop's virtual with MadLoop's omitted R2_G0tt_b added (bare part, poles and finite part within 4.6·10⁻¹⁴ of the virtual at three points, 32 digits; the reading-sign fix closes the O(1) miss EWI-7b measured on a branch without it; `test_goldstone_vector_mixings_equal_madloop`, §6.1 "Scalar–vector mixing is a vertex insertion"), and its relabelling g t > t t~ t gives the same virtual (6·10⁻¹⁴, scratch). One-point Catani checks of identical-flavour lines (scratch, binary64): u u~ > u u~ and g u > u u u~ with QED corrections (≤ 7·10⁻¹⁴), t t~ > t t~ with QCD corrections (2·10⁻¹⁵), and g t > t e⁻ e⁺ with QED corrections (a massive top line and a lepton line, 5·10⁻¹⁴). The legacy loop path's own FFS projector exchange (`loops/vertex_frames.py`) was right in its frame: it gave MadLoop's isolated FFS loops of the massive-muon control (2·10⁻⁸) and, undone, missed them by O(1) (the check was removed with the legacy engine, MQ-LEGACY-REMOVE), so the legacy engine served as the second engine (L1/L2, O2); its family values are frozen captures now (`tests/fixtures/loop/legacy/ew_families_*`). The ladder's B4 rungs generate (gmu_eemm in ~1–5 min; gmu_uux_emepg, gmu_gg_ttxemep and gmu_gg_ttxemepg in ~10 min, 8 GB each); its photon-only rung qq_eemm_photon stops at the veto refusal (`veto-unlabelled-counterterms`, "Open" item (3) below).
    - **Open (found here, outside the lepton signs).** (1) e⁺e⁻→tt̄h EW (`required/emep_ttxh_ew`, nightly `test_lepton_initiated_top_pair_higgs_ew_equals_recola`): the earlier miss of MadLoop (0.58 finite, 0.88 single pole, 2·10⁻⁵ double pole) was its diagnostic points, built at MT = 173.3 and run with the G_μ scheme's MT = 173: the providers evaluate such legs off shell, the masters take every leg's invariant on its model mass shell (§11.1), so even the double pole leaves the universal identity. `tools/loop_reference/references.py` now refuses points off the scheme's mass shells in `run` and `merge` (`check_point_masses`; every required reference passes, the two diagnostic ones with a top are refused). On points at MT = 173 three causes separate, measured with MadLoop's counterterm components (`madloop_ct_selection.py`): the bare loops equal MadLoop's (total minus counterterms) and the double pole is universal to 9·10⁻¹⁵; the single pole misses MadLoop, Recola and Catani (which agree to 10⁻¹³) by 8.8·10⁻³ of the Born floor at point 0 (4–9·10⁻³ in the fixtures' metric at the three points) through the UV roots with Z G⁰ H, the root reading of G2-M7 (ew-epsilon), and with `work/v2-ew-epsilon` `c33ac24a` merged every Laurent coefficient equals Recola within 5.7·10⁻¹² at the three points (both are on the slice since ew-epsilon's integration, `6da37352`, merged here; the nightly test passes); MadLoop then still differs by 1.9·10⁻⁴ to 1.1·10⁻² of the finite part, all in R2, exactly the G⁰ t t̄ R2 of the b loops (`R2_G0tt_b`, CT vertex `V_ttG02`, loop particles [W⁺, b]): MG5_aMC adds an R2 coupling only for a loop whose propagator set equals its label (`set_LoopCT_vertices`), and in 't Hooft–Feynman gauge every b loop of that vertex holds a G± (G⁰ has no W W vertex), so the coupling is never added; Recola − MadLoop = (R2_G0tt_b/R2_G0tt) × MadLoop's R2_G0tt component (0.8688964 at lhv = 1, to 10⁻⁸ at the three points). The recursion inserts every admitted R2 vertex once (ADDENDUM J), so the fixture validates with Recola and records MadLoop as excluded with this cause (OpenLoops has no e⁺e⁻→tt̄H EW library). The omission is shown independently of the engine (coordinator DECISIONS 2026-10-28 ~03:50 and ~05:20 (2)): (a) MadLoop's own export of the process never calls it (`required/emep_ttxh_ew/madloop-export`: `helas_calls_ampb_1.f` uses R2_G0TT once and R2_HTT_B, the H t t̄ R2 of the same b loops, whose W W H vertex lets its label match, four times, R2_G0TT_B never, and `Source/MODEL` does not define it; the g t > t t t~ export the same, 16 calls each of R2_G0TT and R2_HTT_B), while the UFO's V_ttG02 carries R2_G0tt and R2_G0tt_b on one FFS2 structure; MadLoop does call it where a loop with exactly the label's propagators exists, with a massive b, whose G0 b b̄ Yukawa closes a b–b–W triangle (`required/gb_ttxb_ew_mb` export: four calls each of R2_G0TT and R2_G0TT_B), as the matching rule predicts, although the coupling's own value (∝ 1 + M_b²/M_W²) does not vanish at M_b = 0; (b) e⁺e⁻→tt̄h reaches that insertion and Recola includes it: generated on a copy of the G_μ UFO with R2_G0tt_b = 0 (every other coupling unchanged), the virtual equals MadLoop's at the three points (finite part 1.2·10⁻¹³, 7.7·10⁻¹³, 1.8·10⁻¹² in the fixtures' metric, poles ≤ 1.4·10⁻¹³) and misses Recola's finite part by 1.9·10⁻⁴, 5.6·10⁻⁴, 1.07·10⁻²; with the coupling it equals Recola's (finite part ≤ 5.7·10⁻¹², poles ≤ 1.4·10⁻¹³) and misses MadLoop's by the same 1.9·10⁻⁴, 5.6·10⁻⁴, 1.07·10⁻², so the term is the whole difference of the two providers (nightly `test_lepton_initiated_top_pair_higgs_without_r2_g0tt_b_equals_madloop`, ~10 min, 8 GB, beside `test_lepton_initiated_top_pair_higgs_ew_equals_recola`; both print the metric against both providers). (2) `poles="uv"` cannot test UV finiteness: the field factors' UV part is the remainder by construction (`loops.v2.poles`), so the ladder's UV assertions hold identically; the total poles against Catani are the test. Asserting a zero remainder in sectors without field factors is no fix (tried on the ladder's `poles="uv"` rungs): the omitted scaleless δZ of massless charged legs and the IR poles of the massive fields' δZ, which the NLOCT vertex counterterms carry and `poles` counts as UV, both land in it (u u~ > d d~ with full QED corrections leaves −4.557·10⁻⁴, exactly minus the bare UV pole of its photon loops; g t > t t t~ with QED corrections, every charged leg massive, leaves 8.9·10⁻⁹ of a 6.1·10⁻⁸ bare UV pole). (2b) The Goldstone–vector mixing sign has no physical oracle among this package's processes: g g > t t~ e- e+ EW lowers 16 mixing insertions and gives the same values to 10⁻¹² against MadLoop and OpenLoops with `_momentum_ratio` negated (the Z side meets conserved currents), and e⁺e⁻→tt̄h, whose finite part a negated `_momentum_ratio` moves by 6·10⁻⁴, tests the mixings and the Z G⁰ H roots against Recola ((1)). (3) Photon-only QED corrections on loop_qcd_qed_sm miss Catani's single pole (−3.49·10⁻⁴ against −1.84·10⁻⁴): the veto acts on the UFO's loop labels (`ct_recursion.admitted_alternatives`, an empty label a wildcard), which are nominal on its UV counterterms (none on the vertices, one representative on the two-point ones: A A [e⁻], e⁺e⁻ [e⁻, A], A Z [W⁺]), so their W and Z loops stay, while R2 couplings labelled with a Z (`R2_eeACp`, [e⁻, Z]) lose their photon part. Photon-only corrections are not expressible on this UFO. Since the B4 lift would have turned their earlier `FermionSignError` (two singlet lines) into this silently wrong virtual, the request is refused before any tree with the listed `veto-unlabelled-counterterms` (`refusals.require_labelled_counterterms_for_veto`, called in `prepare_request_model`, so `Generator.generate`, `pyamplicol generate` and its dry run refuse it alike; interim until EWI-7's [U2] guard and C1 certifier): a veto removing a species perturbative in a requested correction on a model with a counterterm term of that correction labelled with no loop content (`loop_particles = []`; 132 electroweak UV vertices of loop_qcd_qed_sm and its G_μ variant, none of loop_sm), decided over the model's whole loop IR, since nominal labels on the terms a request reaches (the u u~ g R2 and UV, [u, Z]) cannot be told from faithful ones; full EW corrections and QCD vetoes on loop_sm pass (`test_veto_of_loop_species_needs_labelled_counterterms`, `test_photon_only_mumu_on_loop_qcd_qed_sm_is_refused_before_the_trees`, `test_public_generation_cli_and_dry_run_refuse_photon_only_mumu`: the typed error, CLI exit code 2 and the dry run's unsupported feature, the complete corrections planning without it; full EW on the same model matches Catani, `test_qed_poles_of_mumu_on_loop_qcd_qed_sm_are_catani`); the Catani test of the photon-only request, `test_qed_poles_of_photon_only_mumu_on_loop_qcd_qed_sm_are_catani`, stays a strict xfail with the refusal disabled: the target of the real fix (EWI-7, PLAN M11/R6) and the guard's negative control (the Catani miss); the ladder's `poles="uv"` assertions are the pole split's identity and count as evidence nowhere (coordinator DECISIONS 2026-10-28 ~05:20 (1)); the ladder's photon-only rungs `qq_eemm_photon` and `uudd_qed_photon_uv` stop at it; `test_renamed_correction_order_gives_the_same_virtual` now takes the full QED corrections). (4) Native point inputs of e⁻e⁺→μ⁻μ⁺: strict xfail (EWI-N). (5) A Goldstone-side root mixing with a tree-model vertex at leg 1 (t t~ > z h, h z > t t~) stays refused. (6) The root closure reads boson rows on their tree particles' legs but momenta physically; ew-epsilon's fix round (G2-M7, `work/v2-ew-epsilon` `8f075f45`) reads every root vertex as the tree engine does, the conjugate-rule roots W G± H and W G⁰ G± at leg 1 included (w⁺ h > t b̄ = h w⁺ > t b̄ in both gauges), which is what e⁺e⁻→tt̄h needs ((1)).   - *Status, ew-validate (EWI-7; the EW ladder, joint selection, carriers, conventions, guards and the routed items of slice-status DECISIONS 2026-10-28 ~05:20 (1) and ~08:10 (3); `test_loop_v2_ew_validate.py`):*

  - *Status, ew-native (EWI-N; electroweak native point inputs; `test_loop_v2_ew_native.py`):*
    - **The plane check (G2-M4; the relaxed ruling, slice-status DECISIONS 2026-10-26 ~21:10 item 6, user-confirmed 2026-10-27 ~19:20; options (b) and (c) together).** `loop_v2::trees::compile_plane_application_checked` compares each saved evaluator's binary64 SymJIT plane application with its exact evaluator at two complex rows off the real axis (real parts 0.5 + frac(k/φ), imaginary parts ±(0.25 + 0.5 frac(k/ρ)), one row per half-plane) and at the real points the caller gives (`real_points`; `compile_v2_plane(state, level, compress, real_points)`). Off the axis no input-dependent argument sits on a branch cut, so the rows certify the translation (a constant or function read wrongly changes an output at O(1)) whatever the inputs' order, names and values. The parameter program's real point is the request's parameter values (`native_trees._request_points`: the generation's fixed values, else the defaults, as `batch_parameters` binds a batch without a caller's values), where it certifies the real-axis evaluation: the side of a cut an argument sits on at real masses, and the branches of the predicates. A program reading a branch cut, a power with a slot exponent or a discontinuous predicate (`truth`, `equal`, `less`, `less_equal`) of its inputs without a real point is refused (`engine::real_axis_sensitive_outputs`, read through the inlined helper bodies; constants' branch cuts such as the kernels' √2 and the continuous `exp`, `sin`, `cos`, `abs`, `conj`, `real_part`, `imaginary_part` do not count): the tree kernels, whose inputs are complex currents and couplings, hold none. The floor of the comparison is per output (1e-12 of the output's largest part over the rows), so a small output is not compared against a large one's size. A refusal stays the listed `plane-mismatch` (`PlaneMismatchError(message, output, row, imaginary, real_axis)`; row and imaginary None for the missing real point). At other real parameter values the translation adds +0 to each non-constant argument of a cut function (`symjit_plane::canonical_cut_row`; under round-to-nearest x + 0 = x except −0 + 0 = +0), so the −0 imaginary part that SymJIT's negation and ±i products give a negated real, where Symbolica's evaluator has +0, becomes +0, exact arithmetic's and MadLoop's side (CM01 with its widths zeroed at run time put the electroweak counterterm couplings' arguments on their cuts, where the plane had taken the other side: finite part 3.9·10⁻⁵ against MadLoop's 1.8·10⁻³; M-SM MS-MASTERS, risk 13). The translation also drops the jumps of every conditional (`IfElse`, `Goto`, `Label`; `symjit_plane::is_control_flow`, and `symbolica_helpers._symbolica_instruction_program_repr` for the tree adapters' text route): every branch is evaluated and the `Join`, a bitwise select, picks the value, so a branch the request's values do not take is translated as faithfully as the one they take (SymJIT 2.27.3 to 2.28.2 defined a common subexpression at its first occurrence, which could lie inside an untaken branch; risk 13). An argument real only where some complex-mass widths vanish reads its real projection when they are exactly zero, in every carrier (`ct_recursion._RealArguments.zero_width_cases`; its exactly zero imaginary part could come out as a residue of either sign otherwise, risk 13). The real points still certify the translation and the predicates' branches at the request's values (with pole regulators also with the IR regulator at 0, the split selections' regulated pass); nothing is checked at run time. The parameter program compiles at level 0, the kernels at -O2. Every probe row also runs in a call of its own, a SIMD block of equal lanes as a batch runs it: a block mixing rows that take other branches is replayed in scalar code, which hid a SymJIT SIMD NaN in CM05's parameter program. A program failing the check at level ≥ 1 is compiled again at level 0 and kept if that passes; the defect is SymJIT's AVX `times_complex` on aliased registers, upstream PR prepared (risk 13).
    - **Evidence for the choice.** (i) SymJIT gives a negated real a −0 imaginary part where Symbolica's evaluator gives +0 (its product with the real constant −1; `sqrt(-x)` at x = 2: −i√2 against +i√2, also through Symbolica's own JIT, which uses SymJIT); neither is a translation error, so (a), a SymJIT change, would only move one convention, and a real probe placed on a cut refuses a correct translation. The former real probes (0.5 + frac(0.618 (k + 1)), assigned by input index) put sqrt(1 − MW²/MZ²) on its cut whenever the probe of MW exceeded MZ's, so the verdict depended on the declaration order (loop_sm with MW a card input; G_μ: c_UVepemA1EW, UV_GTTLEW). (ii) With the off-axis rows every kernel and parameter program of g g > t t~ EW, e⁻e⁺ > μ⁻μ⁺, e⁻e⁺ > u ū, g t > t t t~ and u ū > e⁻e⁺ g EW (G_μ) passes, which (c) alone would have accepted; the request's real point then still refused their counterterm couplings (c_UVepemA1EW ε⁰ imaginary part −2.1469·10⁻³ in the plane against −3.5745·10⁻³ in the exact evaluator, UV_GTTLEW, c_UVuxuA1EW): a bisection of the coefficient found (MZ⁴(−3 + 4(1 − MW²/MZ²)))^(1/2) of the Z's B0 helpers, a negated real at the card's masses, i.e. a binary64 value on the other side of the cut than double-double and arbitrary precision and the Python lane (MadLoop's). The real-axis row is what certifies the binary64 values, so the check keeps it.
    - **Counterterm couplings on MadLoop's side.** The native lane's counterterm and field couplings (the scalar plans' columns in `native_trees._parameter_program`, values and diagnostics) take the counterterm lowering's convention (`ct_recursion._RealArguments`, §EWI-3: MadLoop's helpers take +0 for an exactly real argument) in its `signed` form: every branch-cut argument (of a logarithm, a square root, a fractional power, or a parameter a helper's body reads through one, `cut_parameters`) that is manifestly real and not manifestly non-negative (`is_nonnegative`: non-negative numbers, π, e, even powers of reals, rational powers of non-negatives, their sums and products) reads through the real projection, value unchanged, +0 in every evaluator; arguments manifestly non-negative (QCD's log(μ²/m²)), not manifestly real, or of a helper parameter its body reads through no branch cut (`cond`'s values) keep their form, so the QCD and loop_sm programs keep their bytes (`test_plans_the_guards_do_not_refuse_keep_their_bytes`, all six pins; a first form that also projected every helper argument changed four of them). The Born couplings and the tree model's derived parameters are off the cut at real masses and unchanged.
    - **Oracles.** Native = Python inputs on the same exported plan (Born and every component per sector, binary64, in the sector's virtual): g g > t t~ EW 2.2·10⁻¹⁵, e⁻e⁺ > μ⁻μ⁺ 9.5·10⁻¹⁶, u ū > d d̄ QED 1.7·10⁻¹⁵ (`test_electroweak_native_inputs_equal_the_python_inputs`; not bitwise: the Python lane forms the trees at the working digits and its couplings at 32 digits); u ū > d d̄ QED through the public API stores `point_inputs = "native-v2"` and relocates bitwise without a licence (artifact-portability's `check`); `validate --inputs native` on g g > t t~ EW and the e⁻e⁺ > μ⁻μ⁺ control through native inputs (the strict xfails of EWI-4b and EWI-5 flipped); the renamed-model harness' loop_sm MW-card-input variant takes the native route; every lifted rung of the electroweak ladder exports its native point inputs (the ladder asserts it; g g > t t~ e⁻e⁺ g EW in 379 s), and e⁻e⁺ > u ū, g t > t t t~ (the Goldstone–vector mixings), the massive-muon e⁻e⁺ > μ⁻μ⁺ (G⁰, H and G± on the lepton line) and u ū > e⁻e⁺ g EW give native = Python inputs to 1.1·10⁻¹⁵, 6.0·10⁻¹⁵, 2.2·10⁻¹⁶ and 5.1·10⁻¹⁴ of the virtual (one point each, binary64).
    - **External photons (continuation 1).** On-shell external photons of electroweak requests take native inputs: the listed refusal `external-photon-alpha0` is gone (the α(0) convention in the photons' sources, "Electroweak guards" above); u u~ > a g and u u~ > a a equal MadLoop natively (6.4·10⁻¹³, 4.4·10⁻¹³) and the Python inputs (1.1·10⁻¹⁵, 3.4·10⁻¹⁵ of the virtual), the photon's native Ward run (its loop-side vector scaled as its source) vanishes within 8.3·10⁻¹² of the physical scale with the non-gauge control equal to the Python lane's to 5.4·10⁻¹⁵ (`test_external_photon_ward_identity`), a missing 1/α(0) is refused by the native binder naming it, and u u~ > a g with QED corrections through the public API stores native-v2 inputs and relocates bitwise (`test_loop_v2_ew_native.py`).
    - **Open.** Arguments that are mathematically real but not manifestly so (e.g. a product of complex couplings) and sit on a cut at the card would still be refused at the real point (none on the controls); the plane's complex products contract with FMA, so such an argument can carry a rounding-noise imaginary part there.

**Wave 4: gated optimisations**

Each package is kept only if it measures ≥ 15 % at equal accuracy:

- **WP4.1** Parity halving (§8.3), for gg→gggg, gg→ggggg, uu~→ggggg and gg→tt̄gg only.
- **WP4.2** KK canonical pure-gluon blocks (cubic, CT-free, source-free; numerical KK identity gate).
- **WP4.3** M for the 2→6 stretch (eight gluons) and memory streaming refinements.
- **WP4.4** NinjaRS stage 2 (§11.3; only if NinjaRS exceeds 30 %).
- **WP4.5** Schedule 3: F plus transposed reduction for many observables, after a measured prototype of request 2.
- **WP4.6** Kernel specialisation and SIMD tuning (promoted into wave 2 if G0 fails).
- **WP4.7** Integrated-equivalent (de-symmetrised) full colour, opt-in and labelled (§7.6).

Sizes: S–M each; WP4.3 and WP4.5 are L.

### 16.3 First vertical slice (gate S1)

**Scope.**

- gg→gg in FC and LC with **mb = 0** as in the provider references: 24 families = 12 massless + 12 top [M `revision/mb0_all.log`]. The control model with massive c, b and t is used only for O7.
- uu~→dd~g in FC; uu~→uu~ and uu~→uu~g for fermion signs.
- QCD corrections in loop_sm, model R2 and UV, all three Laurent coefficients, double and arbitrary precision.

**Path through the new engine.** No FeynKit diagram generation is used in this path; FeynKit appears only in oracle tests.

1. **Model and kernels** (WP1.1): `prepare_loop_request_model`, then the loop kernel catalogue (gluon, ghost, light and massive quark loop lines; QCD CT site kernels; transverse and full variants).
2. **Trees** (WP1.2): all needed blocks (loop-aware reachability) and the Born per (basis element, helicity), from `GenericDAGCompiler` in loop-request mode.
3. **Families and schedules** (WP1.3): loop-prefix recursion, certified-scaleless drop, families, B tries, F graph, M states (LC).
4. **Colour** (WP1.4): generic seed classes, exact K; FC; LC for gg→gg with `QCDR2Continuation`.
5. **Dressing** (WP1.5): Python reference executor, F, B and M, exact and f64.
6. **Reduction** (WP1.6): NinjaRS v2 adapter, fast engine in f64 and generic engine in AP, cached masters, re-test.
7. **Counterterms and fields** (WP1.7): site closure, then UV_local, R2 and fields.
8. **Assembly** (WP1.8): fermion signs, six components, 19 totals, IR-pole check, per-family error estimate.

**Acceptance.**

| Check | Reference | Tolerance |
|---|---|---|
| gg→gg full result × 3 poles | `tools/loop_reference/gluon_scattering_qcd.json` (MadLoop, Recola, OpenLoops; mb = 0) | Within the providers' mutual agreement (≤ 10⁻⁹ relative) |
| gg→gg components (bare, UV, R2, UV_local = `uv_vertex`, fields = `wf`) × 3 poles | `workspace/reference/runs/gluon-scattering-component-control.json`: a single instrumented MadLoop run ("independent diagnostic component controls"), plus frozen legacy O1 | ≤ 10⁻⁹ relative to the instrumented run, and ≤ 10⁻¹² against legacy in f64 |
| uu~→dd~g, uu~→uu~, uu~→uu~g, full and components | WP0.1 three-provider references (full results); frozen legacy engine (components) | ≤ 10⁻⁹ against providers; ≤ 10⁻¹² against legacy in f64, ≤ 10⁻⁴⁰ in AP |
| Strong per-family oracle O2, all slice processes | Legacy Born-weighted family coefficients after `find_mappings`, modulo certified zeros; max-norm with rank-r coefficients weighted by √s^r | ≤ 10⁻¹³ f64; ≤ 10⁻⁵⁰ at 200 bits |
| Schedules O4 | F = B = M (LC) per family | Exact (rational) |
| Structure O7 | FeynKit families (modulo the logged certified zeros), signs, symmetry factors | Exact |
| Fermion signs O13 (QCD) | uu~→uu~, uu~→uu~g against providers and FeynKit overall factors | Exact signs; values as above |
| Counterterms | Insertion multisets equal legacy; completeness assertion passes; removed-rule fixture fails | Exact |
| gg→gg LC | Legacy LC projection and degrees | ≤ 10⁻¹² |
| Poles O9 | Catani operator from Born and colour-correlated Born | ≤ 10⁻¹⁰ relative |
| Precision | double vs AP; re-test estimate brackets the true double-precision error on all slice points | Agreement to the reducer's double accuracy |

**Deliberately excluded from S1:** Rust execution, the CT recursion, NLC, uu~→dd~g at LC (needs graded U(1)), observables beyond inclusive, electroweak, and performance (measured at G0 and G1).

## Appendix A. Literature basis and alternatives considered

The user asked for a literature pass to make sure the structure is promising. The sibling reports read the primary sources ([L-s] below). For this document I re-checked, in source:

- van Hameren's complexity statement;
- the OpenLoops 2 pole and performance statements;
- NJet's timing and de-symmetrisation tables;
- Ninja's accuracy caveat;
- BDK's colour sum.

I also searched for 2023–2026 developments [L*] and measured the providers on this host in both modes [V]. No newer program contradicts the structure:

- GoSam-3.0 (arXiv:2507.23549) is about effective field theories and speed;
- arXiv:2502.07060 is a GPU tree-level Berends–Giele code;
- there is still no production SIMD/GPU one-loop code.

| Structure | Evidence | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Born-seeded open loops with on-the-fly helicity sums and merging (OpenLoops 2) | Merging ≈2×, plus a further 2–3× from the on-the-fly treatment. Parent–child recycling is lost because the seed carries whole-diagram colour [L 1710.11452 l.1405-1411]. A-posteriori reduction is faster than OFR depending on the process [L l.3836-3846]. Defaults return I-operator poles "for performance reasons"; actual poles need multiple evaluations [L 1907.13071 `OL2.tex:4881-4885, 6455-6457`]. gg→tt̄gg 0.60 s on an i7-4790K [L tab:perfnew]; 0.49 s production on this host [V] | Adopted as schedule B, over BG blocks instead of diagrams; OFR rejected |
| Recursive loop currents keyed by propagator history (Recola) | (ho, hm) keys, canonical cut rules, colour flows [L-s 1211.6316 §2.2; recola2 source]. Generation on this host: 8–63 s for the 7-leg V+jets and tt̄ processes, 2,035 s (gg→gggg), 10,220 s (uu~→ggggg) [V]. Tensor integrals are 2.4–29 % of its time [M]. Production mode is 3.1–5.9× faster than the IR-pole protocol in steady state [V] | Adopted for generation keys and schedule F; its amplitude-level economics only through the transposed reduction |
| Born-weighted per-topology numerators into Ninja (MadLoop5) | Coefficients 36 s/helicity vs Ninja 0.78 s at gg→tt̄ggg [L 1604.01363 App. B]. Ninja tensor layout identical to NinjaRS [V `ninjars:fast/tensor.rs:1-6`]. Loop groups: 1,168 published for gg→tt̄gg; MG5 3.7.0 exports here have 1.6–3.9× our family counts [V] | Interface and checks adopted; per-diagram structure rejected |
| Grouped or cut-dependent numerators (GoSam, Samurai, Ninja) | Cut-dependent integrands are legal [L-s 1006.0710, 1403.1229 §3.1]. Ninja's master cache [L-s 1403.1229 §3.3]. GoSam reduces pinched diagrams with their parent at almost no extra cost [L 1111.2034 `diagen.tex:100-130`]. Ninja's internal tests are not an accuracy estimate; use scaling or rotation tests [L 1403.1229 `main.tex:1642-1651`] | Master cache and per-family re-test adopted; grouping only as gated stage 2 |
| Tensor recursion per primitive and helicity (van Hameren) | O(n⁴2ⁿ) per ordered amplitude and helicity, competitive with unitarity up to n = 10 [L 0905.1005 `introduction.tex:56-99`, `results.tex:142`] | On-the-fly helicity summation removes the extra 2ⁿ from explicit helicity sums |
| Unitarity over BG trees (NGluon, NJet) | n⁶ observed, n⁸ asymptotic [L-s 1011.2900]. FC 7 gluons 171 s, 2u5g 195 s for two evaluations including the scaling test; 1.37 % / 8.63 % quadruple precision at 6 / 7 gluons [L 1209.0100 `njet.tex:1161-1227`]. De-symmetrised (integrated-equivalent) FC: 7 gluons 3.07 s [L tab:timedsymgluons] | Independent cross-check; per-point scaling-test practice adopted as the per-family re-test; de-symmetrised FC as a labelled opt-in |
| HELAC-1Loop | Loop as (n+2)-point tree; R2 as one-insertion trees [L-s 0903.4665 l.790]; colour and helicity sampling | Counterterms as trees and the (n+2)-point sign convention adopted; sampling rejected (exact per point) |
| Colour decompositions | DDM ring, BDK primitives and NLO colour sum, Ita–Ozeren, Reuschle–Weinzierl, Kälin [L/L-s hep-ph/9910563, hep-ph/9409393 l.1748-1772, 1111.4193, 1310.0413, 1712.03539, 1908.02695] | Seed compressions, the LC face rule, the O14 test |
| Leading colour in production (BlackHat, NJet) | LC ≈ 10 % off for Wbb̄ [L-s 1712.05721]; subleading sampled separately [L-s 1312.7140] | Exact LC/NLC by degree; LC and FC−LC as opt-in outputs |
| Forward-limit / Q-cut sewing of (n+2)-point trees | Integrands with linear propagators or loop-momentum relabellings [L* 1509.02169, 1512.02860, 2301.08043] | Rejected: not a fixed-denominator polynomial per family without a canonical cut; with one, it reduces to the Recola construction used here |
| Colour-dressed unitarity; numerical loop integration; loop-tree duality | Colour sampling, own reduction or no master integrals [L-s 0911.1962, 1111.1733] | Rejected |
| GPU / SIMD virtuals | No production one-loop SIMD or GPU evaluation; virtuals become the bottleneck after GPU trees [L-s 2303.18244, 2609.23500]; LoopFest 2024 Kokkos tree and one-loop abstract without details [L*]; GPU tree-level BG [L* 2502.07060] | Data-independent lockstep lanes are a distinguishing lever (≥ 16 lanes, §5.4) |
| Collier cache | Pinched sub-integrals and tensor sub-reductions computed once per event by call position [L-s 1604.06792 §4] | Scalar-master cache only; tensor-reduction sharing has no integrand-reduction analogue except stage 2 |

## Appendix C. Provider re-measurement protocol (revision 2)

- **Binaries, points, settings.** These are the campaign's (`workspace/reference/runs/required/<process>/`):
  - the OpenLoops driver in `openloops-run/` (OpenLoops 2.1.5, loop libraries from the public repository, `loop_optimisation -O0` as in the OpenLoops default configuration);
  - a scratch copy of the Recola driver that adds one `irpoles` command, built against the same Recola 2.3.0 tree (self-reports 2.2.5) with COLLIER 1.2.8 (`revision/recola_driver_modes.f90`).
- **Modes.**
  - OpenLoops protocol: `truepoles 1`, which sets `coli_cache_use = 0` and `polecheck_is = 1` (`init_ui.F90:455-463`) and forces full re-evaluation in the stability rescaling (`stability.F90:479-483`). OpenLoops production: `truepoles 0`.
  - Recola protocol: `compute_ir_poles = 1`, which makes three `compute_amplitude` calls per point (`process_computation_rcl.f90:152-190`), with the campaign's cache setting. Recola production: `compute_ir_poles = 0`, cache on. A third mode, no poles and no cache, separates the two effects:

    | Process | No poles, cache on | No poles, no cache | Poles (protocol) |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | uu~→e⁻e⁺dd~g | 0.035 s | 0.061 s | 0.186 s |
    | uu~→e⁻e⁺ggg | 0.25 s | 0.32–0.36 s | 1.02 s |
- **Timing.** Single pinned idle core of this host; the driver's own timer; point 1 evaluated untimed for initialisation, then points 2, 3, 1. Recola's cached production rows are quoted from the second timed point on.
- **Equality checks.** Finite parts: OpenLoops production = protocol bitwise on all points. Recola cached = uncached to ≤ 1×10⁻¹².
- **Ratios, protocol / production.**
  - OpenLoops: 2.85–3.03 on all six processes measured.
  - Recola: 3.1–5.9 in steady state; 2.2–5.9 including the first timed point, which pays the cache set-up.
